# Surface review findings, 2026-11-15

Output of the 45 read-only reviewers behind `2026-11-15-surface-review.md`, as returned,
with absolute paths shortened. Only the points taken into that report were opened again;
treat the rest as leads with their evidence cited. M is go-deploy-utility, A is
solmq-conn-util, B is solace-util. Counts in the name tables (A, B) are uses in each tool's
Go files; the runner and kube groups counted comment mentions too, so read the reason.

## 1. Exported surface

### runner

- **Cmd** (type, type; A36 B96): keep. It is the value every builder returns and every Runner executes, and both tools keep building their own Cmd for commands M does not offer (A image pull internal/runner/runner.go:356, B sysctl internal/container/limits.go:159). All three fields are used by tools (A reads c.Stdin and c.Env in its fakes, B sets Stdin at internal/container/manager.go:474).
  - In M: Return type of every builder: containercli/inspect/inspect.go:26, kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:52, rootless/rootless.go:184, internal/build/build.go:16, plus kube, kube/rbac, podman, systemd, containercli; runnertest.Fake.Run takes it (runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:114).
- **Cmd.Check** (method, pure-helper; A0 B1): keep. A Runner wrapper written in a tool must call it before it reads Argv[0], as B's noEnv does; any third tool that wraps the runner needs the same.
  - In M: runner/runner.go:90 (CheckRun), runner/runner.go:108 (CheckAttach)
  - Reached: A reaches it through CheckRun inside Exec.Run and Fake.Run.
- **CheckRun** (func, pure-helper; A0 B0): keep. runnertest is a separate package and calls it, so it cannot be unexported; it is also the contract a tool-written Runner must apply (none of A's 11 and B's 8 hand-written Run implementations call it today, so a builder bug passes there).
  - In M: runner/echo.go:55, runner/runner.go:204, runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:115
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through Exec.Run, Echo.Run and Fake.Run.
- **CheckAttach** (func, pure-helper; A0 B0): keep. Same as CheckRun: runnertest needs it across the package boundary and a tool-written Runner should apply it.
  - In M: runner/echo.go:67, runner/runner.go:252, runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:134
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through Exec.Attach, Echo.Attach and Fake.Attach.
- **Runner** (type, type; A100 B64): keep. Design 3.1 makes it the Runner field of every client, and the tool hands it in. It stays the one seam.
  - In M: runner/runner.go:281, 290, 300 (Combined, Split, Output take one); runner/runnertest/runnertest_test.go:35. No other M package takes a Runner today.
- **Runner.Run** (method, other; A12 B12): keep. Counts are 1 call plus 11 test implementations in A, and 4 calls plus 8 implementations in B. The planned clients run every operation through it, and the output hook of design 3.2 can be built on its two writers without changing it.
  - In M: runner/runner.go:283, 292, 302
- **Runner.Attach** (method, other; A6 B10): keep; by:containercli.Client.Exec and kube.Client.Exec (interactive session, returns the child's exit code). Counts are 1 call plus 5 implementations in A, and 4 calls plus 6 implementations in B. Design 3.2 keeps the hand-over of the terminal, and both Exec client methods need it.
- **DefaultGrace** (const, constant; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Neither tool names it and neither sets Exec.Grace (only M's own tests do, runner/runner_test.go:986 and 1018), so the doc of Exec.Grace can state the two seconds. Keeping it is harmless if the owner wants the default visible to a third tool.
  - In M: runner/runner.go:156 only
  - Reached: Both tools get the value by leaving Exec.Grace at zero.
- **Exec** (type, type; A3 B10): keep. It is the real Runner both tools put into their run (A bare, B with Announce for --verbose). Announce is B only and Grace is set by neither tool.
- **Exec.Run** (method, other; A0 B0): keep. It implements Runner, so it cannot be unexported.
  - Reached: Called through the Runner interface: A stores gdurunner.Exec{} in its app (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:189), B returns it from engine.NewExec (internal/engine/runner.go:26).
- **Exec.Attach** (method, other; A0 B0): keep. It implements Runner. See the missing item on the interrupt guard, which A adds around it by hand.
  - Reached: Called through the Runner interface (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:271, B internal/engine/runner.go:174).
- **Resolve** (func, other; A0 B4): keep. B's preamble reports the binaries it will use through the same rule Exec applies (1 call, 3 comment mentions), which is a tool's own output step and plainly generic for a third tool.
  - In M: runner/runner.go:208, runner/runner.go:256
  - Reached: A reaches it through Exec.Run and Exec.Attach.
- **Combined** (func, pure-helper; A20 B0): keep. Most of A's 20 sites become client calls, but the commands M does not offer stay in the tool and still need a capture helper (A image pull, internal/runner/runner.go:356). Whether the three capture helpers shrink to one hook-based run is in signatureNotes.
  - In M: Tests only (runner/runnertest/runnertest_test.go:36).
- **Split** (func, pure-helper; A5 B0): keep. A's runParsed (internal/runner/runner.go:86) feeds 14 helpers, and the ones for tool-built commands (image inspect, top, stats) stay in A after the clients land. The can-i, secret, linger and is-active sites move to rbac.Run, podman.Client, and systemd.Client.
  - In M: Tests only (runner/runnertest/runnertest_test.go:110).
- **Output** (func, pure-helper; A0 B1): keep. B's engine.Output sits on it and serves B's own commands that M does not offer (internal/container/limits.go:187 and 387).
  - In M: Tests only (runner/runnertest/runnertest_test.go:115).
- **Echo** (type, type; A0 B66): keep. Design 3.3 rule 6 keeps the preview through runner.Echo. B constructs it in tests only (its --dry-run flag is retired, internal/cli/cli_test.go:57-60) but its production code type-asserts it (internal/engine/runner.go:127); A does not use it.
- **Echo.Run** (method, other; A0 B1): keep. It implements Runner.
  - Reached: Otherwise called through the Runner interface in B's tests.
- **Echo.Attach** (method, other; A0 B0): keep. It implements Runner.
  - Reached: Called through the Runner interface in B's tests, also promoted by embedding (internal/cli/exit_test.go:180).
- **MaskEnv** (func, pure-helper; A0 B4): keep, owner call. Only B's tests name it, to build the masked text Echo prints. Result.Argv in design 3.2 needs the same masking, so if M gains one display function for a whole Cmd (see missing), this can be unexported behind it.
  - In M: runner/echo.go:47
- **Quote** (func, pure-helper; A0 B5): keep. B's verbose trail prints through it and runnertest calls it across the package boundary. The name now sits beside compose.Quote (containerart/compose/compose.go:44), which is YAML quoting, so a rename is noted in signatureNotes for the owner.
  - In M: runner/echo.go:42, runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:55

#### runner: missing

- The output hook and the result of design 3.2: runner.Stream, the hook type, Result (masked argv, exit code, duration) and one helper that runs a Cmd, feeds every chunk to the hook, and keeps the copy a parser needs. None of it exists in the source yet.
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:86-92 (runParsed: split the streams, fold stderr into the error) and internal/runner/runner.go:150 (buffer, print at the end)
  - B: internal/engine/runner.go:133-136 (stream to the terminal) and internal/engine/runner.go:151-166 (RunTee and OutputTee: show stderr and keep a copy for kube.Forbidden)
  - Proposal: Put Stream, the hook type and Result in runner, with one exported helper the four clients, rbac.Run and nsgate.Read all call. Export it so a tool runs the commands M does not offer through the same hook and gets the same Result.
- One display form of a whole Cmd: quoted argv, stdin as a byte count, env values masked. Today it is the unexported Echo.echo (runner/echo.go:41-50), and Result.Argv in design 3.2 describes the same masking.
  - A: none (no verbose trail, paritydoc/body.md:223)
  - B: internal/engine/runner.go:32 (verbose trail) and internal/cli/cli_test.go:1453 and 3981 (tests rebuild the echoed line by hand)
  - Proposal: runner gains one function or Cmd method that Echo, Result.Argv and runnertest.Call.String share. Quote and MaskEnv can then stay or go behind it.
- Keeping the tool alive on Ctrl-C while a session is attached. The runner's doc says the terminal owns the session, but only one tool guards its own process.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:268 and 310-314 (ignoreInterruptWhileAttached, signal.Notify, not signal.Ignore)
  - B: internal/engine/runner.go:173-178 (Interactive has no guard; B's only signal handling is internal/cli/watch.go:63)
  - Proposal: runner.Exec.Attach installs the handler for the life of the session and removes it after. It is process-wide signal state inside shared code, so the owner decides whether it belongs in Exec.Attach or stays a tool step.
- A Runner wrapper that refuses a Cmd carrying Env, so only the one caller that needs child environment can pass it.
  - A: none (A adds session env to any command, internal/runner/runner.go:594-596)
  - B: internal/engine/runner.go:37-76 (NoEnv)
  - Proposal: runner.NoEnv(r), generic and about 30 lines. The parity body lists the env policy as each tool's own (paritydoc/body.md:225), so this is the owner's call; a tool would wrap the Runner it gives kube.Client.
- Telling whether a Runner is the preview runner when it is wrapped. Echo's doc tells tools to type-assert (runner/echo.go:23-24), which fails through any wrapper.
  - A: none (Echo not used)
  - B: internal/engine/runner.go:96-129 (unwrap and IsEcho, used across B's production code to skip file writes and host probes)
  - Proposal: runner.IsEcho(r) with a small unwrap convention for wrappers. Only needed if M owns a wrapper (NoEnv) or the guide tells a third tool to wrap the runner.

#### runner: placement

- Result (design 3.2) has no package in the design: runner is the one package every client, rbac.Run and nsgate.Read already import, so Result, Stream and the hook type belong in runner.
- runner.Echo stays in runner, not runnertest: no tool constructs it outside tests, but design 3.3 rule 6 names it as the preview runner and B's production code type-asserts it.
- inspect.Printable (containercli/inspect/inspect.go:195) is what A uses to clean any command's output before it joins an error (A internal/runner/runner.go:49-55), while runner has its own unexported printable (runner/echo.go:121). Once executors word the errors (3.3 rule 5), one control-character rule should serve both; flagged for the containercli/inspect review.
- The package doc's table of each tool's retired calls (runner/runner.go:11-20) is migration history naming two tools; it belongs in docs, not in the doc of a package a third tool reads.

#### runner: signatures

- Cmd.Check is a method, while CheckRun and CheckAttach are free functions that take the Cmd first: pick one form.
- Run takes a context and Attach takes none (reason at runner/runner.go:25-27). Design 3.1 shows every client method with ctx, so settle what the interactive Exec methods do with a ctx that Attach cannot honour.
- Combined and Split return strings; Output returns []byte and takes an extra stderr writer. Settle one return type, and decide whether the three stay beside the hook-based run or reduce to it.
- A non-zero exit is an error from Run (wrapping *exec.ExitError) but a plain code from Attach. Result.ExitCode is -1 when the command never started (design 3.2), while Exec.Attach turns a signal-killed -1 into exitcode.Failure (runner/runner.go:267-271): fix one meaning for -1.
- Quote(name, args...) and Exec.Announce(path, args) split the argv in two, while Cmd.Argv, runnertest.Call.Argv and Result.Argv are one slice; callers write Quote(c.Argv[0], c.Argv[1:]...) (runner/echo.go:42, runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:55).
- runner.Quote is display quoting for a shell line and compose.Quote is YAML quoting. With the one YAML writer landing, a clearer name for runner.Quote avoids two Quote functions with different rules (owner call).
- MaskEnv returns one display string, but Result.Argv is a slice with env values masked: decide the masked form once (see the display item under missing).
- Echo.W defaults to os.Stdout when nil (runner/echo.go:30-35), the one place shared code picks a process stream itself. B sets W at 32 sites and leaves it nil at 4 (internal/container/manager_test.go:2662); requiring W is the stricter rule.

### runner/runnertest

- **Call** (type, test-helper; A0 B0): keep. It is what a test reads back from the Fake, and design section 6 tests every executor's argv against it.
  - In M: runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:91 (Script parameter), 143 (Calls), 154, 166
  - Reached: Element type of Fake.Calls() and the Script parameter: B reads its Env, Attach, Stdout and Stderr fields (internal/engine/runner_test.go:73, 126, 176); A takes only the length (internal/runner/runner_test.go:2233).
- **Call.Has** (method, test-helper; A0 B0): keep, owner call. FailOn is decided to stay (CR-0001) and this is its matcher, and the one a hand-written Script uses. Unexporting it is the alternative if the owner wants the smallest surface.
  - In M: runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:168 (FailOn)
  - Reached: Only through FailOn, which neither tool calls.
- **Call.String** (method, test-helper; A0 B0): keep, owner call. No tool reaches it, but the executor tests design section 6 plans compare argv against recorded calls and this is the one-line form for a failure message. Removing it is the mechanical reading of the rule, so the owner decides.
  - In M: Tests only (runner/runnertest/runnertest_test.go:63, 72, 211)
- **Reply** (type, test-helper; A0 B16): keep. It is the answer type of every Script, and B scripts all four fields.
  - In M: runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:91, 154, 166
- **Fake** (type, test-helper; A2 B15): keep. Design 3.1 and section 6 name it as what tests fill a client with. Today A uses it in one test and B in two files, beside 11 and 7 hand-written fakes (see missing).
  - In M: Tests only (runner/runnertest/runnertest_test.go:35)
- **Fake.Run** (method, test-helper; A0 B0): keep. It implements runner.Runner.
  - Reached: Called through the Runner interface (A internal/runner/runner_test.go:2229, B internal/engine/runner_test.go:119).
- **Fake.Attach** (method, test-helper; A0 B0): keep. It implements runner.Runner.
  - Reached: Called through the Runner interface (B internal/engine/runner_test.go:97 and 122).
- **Fake.Calls** (method, test-helper; A1 B9): keep. It is the only way to read what the Fake recorded.
  - In M: Tests only
- **Seq** (func, test-helper; A0 B7): keep. B scripts by position with it in seven places.
  - In M: Tests only (runner/runnertest/runnertest_test.go:130)
- **FailOn** (func, test-helper; A0 B0): keep. CR-0001 decided it stays, although neither tool calls it. It can only fail a call, which is why both tools still route answers by argv in their own fakes (see missing).
  - In M: Tests only (runner/runnertest/runnertest_test.go:149)

#### runner/runnertest: missing

- A Script that answers by argv content with output, not only an error, and a way to combine scripts (match first, else fall through to a sequence or a default). Both tools write this by hand.
  - A: internal/runner/runner_test.go:2431-2441 (routeRunner), internal/runner/runner_test.go:43-79 (out and error by call index), cmd/solmq-conn-util/main_test.go:304-339 and 380-399 (fakeRunner, queueRunner)
  - B: internal/cli/cli_test.go:197-241 (opRunner with fail and output functions), internal/k8s/transport_test.go:173 (recRunner), internal/k8s/statusreport_test.go:45 (fixtureRunner)
  - Proposal: runnertest gains a matcher script, for example On(sub, Reply) or Route(func(Call) (Reply, bool)), and a combinator that tries scripts in order. With it the tools can drop most of their own Runner fakes, which also skip runner.CheckRun today.
- The default answer yes to a kubectl auth can-i probe, so a test scripts only the refusal it wants. Each tool detects the probe's argv shape itself.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/main_test.go:347-358 (defaultAnswer) and internal/runner/runner_test.go:65-68 and 94 (isCanI)
  - B: internal/cli/cli_test.go:236-238 and 258 (opCanI) and internal/k8s/transport_test.go:68 (isCanI)
  - Proposal: A canned script for the probe. It knows kubectl's argv, so it fits a test helper beside kube/rbac better than runnertest, which should stay free of any command family.

#### runner/runnertest: placement

- A can-i default script, if added, belongs with kube/rbac (a test helper there), not in runnertest: runnertest imports only runner and should not learn a command family.

#### runner/runnertest: signatures

- Fake.Script, Seq and FailOn all use the unnamed type func(int, Call) Reply: name it (Script) so combinators and the guide can refer to it.
- Seq matches by position and FailOn by a substring of any token; FailOn takes only an error while Seq takes whole Replies. A general matcher taking a Reply would make FailOn a special case of it.
- Call.Stdout and Call.Stderr record the writers so a test can tell a streamed call from a captured one (runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:32-33; B relies on it at internal/cli/cli_test.go:207, A at cmd/solmq-conn-util/main_test.go:6256). Under the output hook every client call passes the client's own writers, so that check stops working: decide what replaces it.
- Reply.Code applies to Attach only and Reply.Stdout and Stderr to Run only. To test Result.ExitCode for a Run, a test must build an error that carries a status itself: either let Code apply to Run or document exitcode.Exited as the way.
- Fake.Attach answers 0 for an unscripted session, while A's fakes fail loudly when an unexpected Attach arrives (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main_test.go:313-317): consider a strict option.

### Verifiers for the runner group

Usage re-count: 30 names counted, 8 corrections.

- runner/runnertest.Fake.Run: A 0, B 1. Class error (reviewer 0/0). B calls Run directly on a *runnertest.Fake: B:internal/engine/runner_test.go:69-70 (fake := &runnertest.Fake{}; fake.Run(t.Context(), withEnv, nil, nil)). A has no direct call (grep '\.Run\(' over A finds only cmd/solmq-conn-util/logs.go:304, on a gdurunner.Runner).
- runner/runnertest.Call.String: A 0, B 2. No explicit .String() call on a Call in A or B (reviewer 0/0 is right for explicit calls), but B invokes it implicitly: B:internal/engine/runner_test.go:74 and :127 format a []runnertest.Call with %+v (t.Errorf("calls = %+v, ...", calls)), and fmt calls the value-receiver String() of each element. So the reviewer's reason 'No tool reaches it' is not accurate; removing it would compile but change those failure messages. Counted here as 2 implicit uses, 0 explicit.
- runner.Runner.Run: A 1, B 7. Reviewer 12/12 counts implementations as uses. Calls on a runner.Runner value only: A 1 (cmd/solmq-conn-util/logs.go:304; the 11 test implementations are confirmed but are not calls). B 7, not 4: internal/engine/runner.go:55, 135, 154, 164 plus internal/engine/runner_test.go:80, 111, 119 (NoEnv(fake).Run and r := NoEnv(fake); r.Run, NoEnv returns runner.Runner per runner.go:47). B implementations taking runner.Cmd are 8 (confirmed), so even on the reviewer's own convention B is 15, not 12.
- runner.Runner.Attach: A 1, B 4. Reviewer 6/10 counts implementations as uses; its own breakdown is confirmed (A 1 call + 5 implementations, B 4 calls + 6 implementations). Calls on a runner.Runner value only: A cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:271; B internal/engine/runner.go:63 and 174, internal/engine/runner_test.go:97 and 122.
- runner.Echo: A 0, B 39. Reviewer's 66 is the raw text count of 'runner.Echo' in B, of which 27 are comment mentions (grep -rnE --include='*.go' '//.*\brunner\.Echo\b' over B = 27 lines, e.g. internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:20, internal/k8s/deploy.go:64 and 70). Code uses are 39: 38 in _test.go files and one in production, internal/engine/runner.go:127 (type assertion).
- runner.Runner: A 99, B 54. Reviewer's B figure 64 is the raw text count of 'runner.Runner'; 10 of those are comment mentions (grep -rnE --include='*.go' '//.*\brunner\.Runner\b' over B = 10), leaving 54 code uses (comment-stripped count: find B -name '*.go' | xargs sed 's://.*$::' | grep -o 'runner\.Runner' = 54). A is 99 code uses of gdurunner.Runner plus 1 comment mention (reviewer 100, within tolerance).
- runner.Resolve: A 0, B 1. One code use in B: internal/cli/context.go:302 (path, err := runner.Resolve(c.Name())). The other three hits are comments: internal/cli/context.go:279, internal/cli/cli_test.go:4166 and 4253. Reviewer's 4 includes them (its reason text says so).
- runner.Exec: A 1, B 7. B code uses are 7: internal/engine/runner.go:26, 28, 31; internal/engine/resolve_test.go:16, 59; internal/engine/runner_test.go:139, 140. The other 3 of the reviewer's 10 are not uses: comments at internal/cli/context.go:249 and internal/engine/resolve_test.go:55, and a string literal at internal/engine/resolve_test.go:60. A has 1 code use (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:189); main.go:142 and main_test.go:361 are comments (reviewer 3).

Rule challenge: 30 names examined, 3 challenges.

- **[high] runner/runnertest.Call.String**: keep, as an owner call: 'No tool reaches it', and removing it is offered as the mechanical reading of the rule -> keep, with no owner call; and change what it prints to the full masked display form (argv, stdin byte count, env keys masked, an attach mark)
  - The premise is wrong, so removal is not the mechanical reading. Call has a value-receiver String (runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:51), so fmt uses it for %v and %+v, also for each element of a []Call. B prints the record that way at internal/engine/runner_test.go:74 and :127 (`calls = %+v`), and M's own tests do at runner/runnertest/runnertest_test.go:67, 70 and 73. A grep for `.String()` cannot see these. Removing the method would turn those failure messages into a raw struct dump that includes the fixture's Stdin bytes and Env values. Side effect to fix in the same step: because of String, those %+v messages print only the argv although they claim to show the record (B's line 74 says 'want the one call with its environment', and no env is printed). That is the same gap as the reviewer's missing item 'one display form of a whole Cmd', so String should be that form.
- **[medium] runner.MaskEnv**: keep, as an owner call: 'can be unexported' behind a display function for a whole Cmd -> keep, with no owner call, also after the Cmd display function lands
  - The unexport alternative does not fit the one real use. B's maskedKeys masks a bare env list for a test failure message (internal/container/manager_test.go:1138-1140); a display function that takes a whole Cmd does not serve it without a made-up Argv. Exec.Announce hands a tool the path and arguments only (runner/runner.go:146), so a tool that wants env keys in its own trail, or in an error about a tool-built Cmd with Env (B's compose deploy, internal/container/manager.go:887 and 917), needs this function. Hiding it would push tools to hand-roll secret masking, which the security rule forbids. The other two B sites (internal/container/preflight_test.go:250, internal/engine/resolve_test.go:100) only re-test M's function and can go, but that does not change the answer.
- **[low] runner.DefaultGrace**: unexport-internal-only (owner call) -> keep, or decide it as one item with Exec.Grace: not the constant alone
  - Three points against unexporting it in isolation. (1) Exec.Grace stays exported and its doc names the constant (runner/runner.go:148-149). Neither tool sets Grace either; only in-package tests do (runner/runner_test.go:986, 1018, 1040, 1073). So the reviewer's test ('neither tool names it') would hide the knob as well, and hiding the default while keeping the knob leaves a third tool unable to write 'the default times N'. (2) M's convention for a zero-means-default knob is an exported default: kube.RolloutTimeout (kube/kube.go:126-127, used at 135), confirm.DefaultNoPromptFlag (confirm/prompter.go:11-12 and 36), watch.DefaultInterval (watch/watch.go:27-28). Unexporting this one is inconsistent unless confirm.DefaultNoPromptFlag, which no tool names either, gets the same treatment. (3) Under the output hook every client call becomes a piped run, so the drain half of the grace (runner/runner.go:137-139 and 232-235) starts to bound calls that hand the terminal's descriptor to the child today (B internal/engine/runner.go:133-136). The value becomes something the guide must state, not less visible. Low confidence because the reviewer already marked it harmless either way.

Additions:

- (signature) The hook of design 3.2 cannot tell a parsed answer from output to show. It passes (stream, chunk) only, and the note says the tool sees every byte. Today neither tool shows a parsed stdout: A returns it to the parser alone (internal/runner/runner.go:86-92, 13 call sites), B captures stdout and streams only stderr (internal/engine/runner.go:142-145, 33 production calls). With B's hook writing at once, every `get -o json`, inspect and can-i answer would land on the terminal. The hook needs a third fact (M is also parsing this chunk, or the operation name), or the helper needs a mode that feeds only stderr to the hook. One case is not optional: the secret compare reads the secret VALUE on stdout (podman/podman.go:85-86 and 93; A internal/runner/runner.go:554, B internal/container/manager.go:550-556), so that stream must never reach the hook. The reviewer's helper 'feeds every chunk to the hook'.
- (signature) A hook writer turns every run into a piped run. os/exec hands an *os.File to the child as it is and copies through a pipe for any other writer (the reason CheckRun exists, runner/runner.go:87-88). B's streamed calls pass os.Stdout and os.Stderr (internal/engine/runner.go:115-120 and 133-136; 26 production sites, compose up at internal/container/manager.go:917), and A's logs does too (cmd/solmq-conn-util/logs.go:304), so the child sees a terminal today. Under the hook, compose and pull progress and any colour fall back to non-terminal output, and the drain wait after exit (runner/runner.go:137-139, 232-235) applies to every client call. The reviewer's reason for Runner.Run ('built on its two writers without changing it') holds for the interface, not for this behaviour. Decide whether a client may be given plain writers instead of a hook (which would also keep runnertest.Call.Stdout meaningful, the reviewer's own open note), and add a smoke row for B's compose output.
- (signature) The hook is called from two goroutines when stdout and stderr are different writers (Runner doc, runner/runner.go:124-126), and order across the two streams is then no longer arrival order. Combined's one-writer merge (runner/runner.go:279-285), which A's 20 sites rely on, cannot be reproduced once chunks are marked stdout or stderr. The helper should serialise hook calls itself, so a tool's hook need not lock (A appends to one buffer), and its doc should say cross-stream order is approximate.
- (signature) Result holds one Argv and one ExitCode (design 3.2, lines 85-89), but one operation may take several commands (3.3 rule 1, lines 105-109: the version read, `secret rm --ignore`, `secret create`). Settle whether a method returns the last command's Result, one per command, or reports each command through a callback. Until then Exec.Announce (runner/runner.go:146 and 215) is the only per-command trail, which is one more reason it stays.
- (signature) Result.Argv 'as run' cannot carry the resolved path: resolution happens inside Exec.Run (runner/runner.go:208), below the Runner seam the helper sits on, and Fake and Echo resolve nothing. Say that Argv[0] is the name as built, and that the resolved path is seen only by Announce or by a tool's own Resolve call (B internal/cli/context.go:302).
- (signature) Exec.Announce receives the path and arguments only (runner/runner.go:146), so B's --verbose trail (internal/engine/runner.go:31-33) cannot show the stdin size or the env keys that Echo prints (runner/echo.go:41-50). If Announce took the Cmd, or the display line the reviewer lists under missing, the trail and Echo would print one form. The review lists no exported struct field as a name: Exec.Grace (runner/runner.go:148-149) is set by neither tool and should be decided with DefaultGrace.
- (signature) Interactive executors need three *os.File. The client of design 3.1 holds Runner, Base and Output only, and shared code should not reach for os.Stdin itself (the reviewer's own point on Echo.W). Both tools pass their process files at one site each (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:271, B internal/engine/runner.go:174). Also, exec runs in three forms today, not one: attached (those two sites), streamed (B internal/k8s/transport.go:63, internal/container/transport.go:82) and captured, with or without stdin (B internal/k8s/transport.go:71 and 80; A internal/runner/runner.go:1207). Catalogue 3.4 lists one Exec per client that 'returns the child's exit code'. If it covers only the attached form, kube.Exec and containercli.Exec must stay exported for the other two, and the coveredBy of Runner.Attach is a third of the picture.
- (signature) Runner.Run's contract says a non-zero exit wraps *exec.ExitError (runner/runner.go:126-127). No fake can build one. The helper that fills Result.ExitCode should read an ExitCode() method, as exitcode.ChildStatus does (exitcode/exitcode.go:98-99, 141-150), and the interface doc should promise 'an error that carries the exit status'. Neither tool's production code inspects *exec.ExitError today.
- (signature) runner.NoEnv, if it moves into M, collides with systemd.Client. Catalogue 3.4 gives that client the session env, which A attaches as Cmd.Env on every systemctl and loginctl call (internal/runner/runner.go:586-599). B sets the same variables process-wide through os.Setenv precisely because of its NoEnv policy (internal/container/rootless.go:139-142, internal/container/manager.go:59-64). Shared code cannot call os.Setenv, so B's 'compose only' row (paritydoc/body.md:225) has to become 'compose and the user session', and NoEnv can only wrap the Runner a tool gives kube.Client.
- (missing) Cmd has no copy-safe way to add environment entries. A wrote one (QuadletScope.WithEnv, internal/runner/runner.go:594-599, 9 call sites) and systemd.Client will need the same inside M. A small Cmd method keeps each client from re-writing the append-with-copy.
- (missing) Design 3.3 rule 6 keeps the preview 'as today', but no tool ships one. B retired --dry-run and installs Echo from tests only (internal/cli/cli_test.go:57-63); A never used it (paritydoc/body.md:224). B's roughly 30 production isEcho branches (for example internal/container/manager.go:551, internal/k8s/preflight.go:62) run only under tests. The owner should say whether the guide and the example tool present Echo as a real preview mode. If yes, runner.IsEcho is needed and the clients need a stated behaviour for a version-dependent operation under Echo (B takes the fallback pair, internal/container/manager.go:596-597). If no, IsEcho would be new API for a test seam. Echo's line also bypasses the hook: it goes to Echo.W (runner/echo.go:26-35 and 49), which B's watch names as one of two outputs that escape its frame (internal/cli/watch.go:32-33).
- (signature) runnertest: Fake.Script receives no context (runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:91) and Fake.Run checks ctx only before it records (118-120), so a scripted run cannot last until it is cancelled. A's log-follow fake needs exactly that (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main_test.go:6255-6285: write, signal, wait on ctx.Done), and the tests of the two Logs executors will too. Pass ctx to the Script, or add a Reply field that blocks until ctx is done.
- (signature) runnertest: Call.Has and FailOn match a substring of any token (runner/runnertest/runnertest.go:41-48 and 166-173). 'rm' is inside '--format', which podman.Version and podman.SecretData both carry (podman/podman.go:28 and 93), so FailOn("rm", err) in a test of the secret fallback of design 3.3 rule 1 would also fail the version read. Both tools match whole tokens for the can-i probe (A internal/runner/runner_test.go:94-101, B internal/cli/cli_test.go:258-264), while B's own failOn is the substring form (internal/container/transport_test.go:116-131). Keep Has exported (it is the documented helper for a hand-written Script, runnertest.go:6-7), and add a whole-token or token-sequence matcher beside it for the reviewer's On or Route.
- (missing) runnertest: a recording hook. Design section 6 tests what each executor 'writes to the client's writers' (docs/design/2026-11-15-shared-deploy-api.md:194-195). Every such test, in the six client packages and in both tools, needs a hook that records (stream, chunk) in order. Ship one beside Fake so it is written once.
- (placement) The can-i default script: M's convention for test helpers is a package of its own (containerart/artifacttest, notice/noticetest, runner/runnertest), not a helper inside a production package, so it should be a kube test package that imports runnertest, not 'a test helper beside kube/rbac'. A's default also answers the namespace read (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main_test.go:347-358: `get namespace <ns>` answers namespace/<ns>), which is kube and not rbac, so the kube level is the right home.
- (placement) Docs that record today's split and must change in the same step as any of the reviewer's proposals. docs/developer.md:135-145 lists NoEnv, IsEcho, Interactive, the verbose announcer and the follow rule under 'Some runner policy stays in the tools'. paritydoc/body.md:205-226 holds the Runner seams text (names Combined, Split, Output) and the policy table (verbose trail, runner.Echo, environment for the child, cancelled log follow); it is canonical in M and checked in both tools by paritydoc.Check. The runner package doc also describes capture and output policy at runner/runner.go:6-9 and 29-31, beyond the table at 11-20 the reviewer names.

### execguard

- **ApproveFlag** (const, constant; A0 B0): keep. CR-0001 decided both tools take the flag name from it; adoption is still open in both. The value carries the dashes while both flag libraries register the bare name (see signatureNotes).
  - In M: execguard/execguard.go:167,170,175,178,358,398,411 (the messages of Approve and Check)
  - Reached: Both tools print it through the Check and Approve messages; neither names the constant. Each holds its own literal: A cmd/solmq-conn-util/commands.go:27 and main.go:1593, B internal/cli/flags.go:239,244,257.
- **UnsafeChars** (const, constant; A2 B7): keep. Another M package (runner) and both tools' host-path, token and timezone gates read it, so it cannot be unexported. Counts are code only: A has 4 more mentions in comments, B 7 in comments and 2 in test strings.
  - In M: execguard/execguard.go:303 (checkToken); runner/echo.go:115 (quoteTok)
- **PathSeparators** (const, constant; A0 B3): keep. B builds its cross-platform BaseName, HasPathSeparator and isPathSep on it (hostpath.go:168,181,188) so a path rule and the command rule share one separator set. Unexporting would make B copy the literal.
  - In M: execguard/execguard.go:173,227,345 (Approve, Rules.wiring, checkBinary)
  - Reached: A reaches it only through Check and Approve.
- **Rules** (type, type; A2 B8): keep. The tool states per command field which binaries may run and under which key name; that is customisation and M must not hold a tool's keys. A sets no Subword (it appends compose itself), B uses it for docker.compose.
  - In M: execguard/execguard.go:133 (Check parameter) and its unexported methods wiring, allowed, list, scope
- **Approvals** (type, type; A40 B3): keep. It is the opaque carrier of the operator's approvals between Approve and Check. A threads it through every wrapper in internal/runner/runner.go (105 to 561); once a client is set up once per run with guarded Base tokens that threading goes, but the type stays.
  - In M: execguard/execguard.go:133 (Check parameter), :160 (Approve result)
- **Check** (func, pure-helper; A7 B8): keep. It is the one command guard and no client replaces it: design 3.1 says Client.Base holds tokens the tool already guarded. Where it runs once clients exist (today: validator plus every executor) is in signatureNotes and missing. Counts are code only; A has 3 comment mentions, B 9 plus 1 test string.
- **Approve** (func, cli-helper; A5 B1): keep. The only way to build a non-empty Approvals, called once per run by both tools. It asks nothing and reads nothing, so it fits the shared-code rules unchanged.
- **IsControl** (func, pure-helper; A2 B3): keep. Both tools build their host-path, timezone, java-option and probe-token gates on it. If M takes over those gates (missing items 1 and 2) the tools' uses shrink, and whether it then stays public or moves to a neutral charset home is in placement.
  - In M: execguard/execguard.go:290 (checkToken)
- **IsInvisible** (func, pure-helper; A2 B3): keep. Same standing as IsControl: both tools use it in every value gate they wrote on top of the charset. containercli/inspect.Printable repeats the test with unicode.Is(unicode.Cf, r) at inspect.go:197 instead of calling it.
  - In M: execguard/execguard.go:298 (checkToken)

#### execguard: missing

- One exported charset check for a single value or token: valid UTF-8, no control, no whitespace, no invisible rune, none of UnsafeChars. M has it only as the unexported checkToken (execguard/execguard.go:282), so each tool rebuilt it from the exported pieces, and B copied the unexported isSpace.
  - A: internal/validate/validate.go:1134 (unsafeRune) and :1871 (SafeToken)
  - B: internal/config/artifactvalues.go:56 (checkTimezone) and internal/config/execguard.go:89 (isSpace copy)
  - Proposal: execguard gains an exported check, for example CheckValue(field, value string) error with the shared messages and without the tilde and index wording; the renderers of section 8 call it on every value written bare. Both tools drop their copies.
- The host-path gate: UnsafeChars minus backslash, plus the rune checks and a refusal of a leading tilde. Both tools derive the same set and apply it to every path that lands in a Volume= line or a compose volumes entry.
  - A: internal/validate/validate.go:1150 (pathShellMeta) and :1164 (safeHostPath)
  - B: internal/config/hostpath.go:47 (hostPathUnsafe) and :73 (CheckHostPath)
  - Proposal: M owns one CheckHostPath(field, path string) error. Charset in execguard; the call sits where the quadlet Unit and compose Service models take a mount source, since M writes that line after section 8. Owner call on the home (execguard, the planned tilde package, or containerart). A returns a bool and B an error with a message: the M version returns the error.
- The binaries that speak each command family. Both tools keep a per-platform allowlist; kubernetes (kubectl, oc) and podman (podman) are identical, docker differs (A: docker; B: docker, docker-compose, nerdctl).
  - A: internal/validate/validate.go:53 (AllowedCommands)
  - B: internal/config/execguard.go:40 (execBinaries)
  - Proposal: Owner call. Either M states, beside each client, the binaries whose command surface that client issues (kube: kubectl, oc; containercli: docker, nerdctl, and docker-compose for the compose tokens; podman: podman) and a tool picks from them for Rules.Allowed, or the lists stay tool customisation. The field and scope names stay with the tool either way.
- Check, then hand the tokens to whatever executes. Each tool wraps Check in an accessor that every executor resolves its base argv through, so no argv is built from unchecked tokens.
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:105 (ParseCommand: strings.Fields, Check, AsUsage)
  - B: internal/config/execguard.go:276, :286, :299 (ClusterCommand, RuntimeCommand, ComposeCommand)
  - Proposal: Owner call. execguard returns a checked token value, for example func (r Rules) Guard(cmd []string, a Approvals) (Tokens, error), and each Client.Base takes that type instead of []string, so a client cannot be set up with unchecked tokens and the check runs once per run at client setup. Splitting a scalar command and the key names stay in the tool.

#### execguard: placement

- UnsafeChars, IsControl, IsInvisible (and the unexported isSpace, invalidUTF8) are a general value charset, not command rules: runner/echo.go:115 and both tools' path, timezone and option gates read them. When the section 8 renderers check the values they write, containerart would import execguard only for these. Owner call: leave them here, or give the charset a neutral package that execguard, runner and containerart import.
- containercli/inspect.Printable (containercli/inspect/inspect.go:197) defines control and invisible with unicode.IsControl and unicode.Cf instead of execguard.IsControl and IsInvisible, while its doc cites execguard. The sets differ on NEL (U+0085). One definition should serve both.
- PathSeparators is used outside M only for host-path questions in B (internal/config/hostpath.go:168,181,188). It should sit with whichever package ends up owning the host-path gate; until then execguard is right, because Check and Approve use it.
- ApproveFlag, Rules, Approvals, Check and Approve are in the right package.

#### execguard: signatures

- ApproveFlag is "--allow-command" with the dashes, but both flag libraries register the bare name: A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1593 (flag.FlagSet.Var) and B internal/cli/flags.go:239,244 (cobra) pass "allow-command". To adopt CR-0001 without a TrimPrefix in each tool, add a bare-name constant (for example ApproveName = "allow-command" and ApproveFlag = "--" + ApproveName).
- Check is a free function with the Rules first, while envref spells the same idea as a method (Ref.Check). Rules.Check(cmd, a) would match, and pairs with a Rules.Guard if the checked-token type of missing item 4 is taken.
- Check and Approve return unmarked errors, and both tools then mark them as usage errors themselves (A internal/runner/runner.go:108 and cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1602; B through internal/cli/platform.go:188). A guard refusal is a usage error by the exitcode contract, so M could return it already marked (execguard importing exitcode) or as a typed refusal like the clients' typed errors. A validates by matching message text today (internal/validate/validate_deploycommand_test.go:58).
- Approve takes the tool name per call while Rules carries none, and the Check messages say "this tool" and "the env file" (execguard/execguard.go:346-358, 408-411). For a third tool the config file may not be an env file: settle whether Rules or Approve carries the tool name and the config noun.
- IsControl and IsInvisible are exported but the third rune predicate, isSpace (execguard/execguard.go:212), is not: B copies it (internal/config/execguard.go:89) and A calls unicode.IsSpace (internal/validate/validate.go:1135). Export it, or export the whole value check (missing item 1) and stop exporting the pieces.
- The package doc (execguard/execguard.go:18-21) and docs/developer.md:96-97 say a tool calls Check in every executor right before argv is built. With one client per run holding Base, that sentence has to become "once, at client setup", or the clients must enforce it (missing item 4).

### exitcode

- **OK** (const, constant; A42 B2): keep. Public exit-status contract. A's handlers return an int and use the constant directly; B reaches it through Code and names it in tests.
  - In M: exitcode/exitcode.go:120 (Code)
  - Reached: B's main reaches it through Code (main.go:25).
- **Failure** (const, constant; A4 B7): keep. Public exit-status contract, used by both tools and by runner. B's 7 uses are all in tests.
  - In M: exitcode/exitcode.go:127 (Code); runner/runner.go:271 (Attach, for a child killed by a signal)
- **Usage** (const, constant; A33 B38): keep. Public exit-status contract. A returns it from int handlers; B's 38 uses are all test assertions against Code.
  - In M: exitcode/exitcode.go:122 (Code)
- **Usagef** (func, cli-helper; A3 B27): keep. The way a tool marks a usage error where it is made. Both tools use it and the classification stays a tool decision.
- **AsUsage** (func, cli-helper; A4 B14): keep. Both tools use it to mark errors made elsewhere, including the execguard refusals. It is also what M would call if guard errors come back already marked (execguard signatureNotes).
- **IsUsage** (func, cli-helper; A7 B4): keep. A branches on it in production code (main.go:1262) and gdu 13 makes watch.Loop a second M consumer in another package. B uses it in tests only.
  - In M: exitcode/exitcode.go:47 (AsUsage), :121 (Code); watch.Loop will call it under gdu 13 (docs/todo.md:708), not built yet
- **ChildExit** (func, cli-helper; A0 B6): keep. It is the opt-in that lets a session's own status pass through Code, which any tool with error-returning handlers needs; one consumer no longer matters under rule 5. B has 4 production and 2 test uses.
  - Reached: A does not reach it: its cli verb returns the code runner.Attach hands back straight from an int handler (cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:271-275).
- **Exited** (func, cli-helper; A0 B3): keep. It turns an exit code into the error ChildStatus reads. With containercli.Client.Exec and kube.Client.Exec returning the child's code in Result (design 3.2), B's engine.Interactive goes and this is what B calls on Result.ExitCode. It takes plain values because exitcode cannot import runner (runner/runner.go:46 imports exitcode).
  - Reached: A does not reach it (it returns the Attach code as an int, cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:275).
- **Code** (func, cli-helper; A2 B32): keep. The single classifier a tool's main calls. Both tools use it in production; 31 of B's 32 uses are tests.
- **ChildStatus** (func, cli-helper; A0 B2): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Only Code reads it in production. B names it in two test assertions that can go through exitcode.Code(exitcode.ChildExit(err)) instead (7, and Failure for the no-status case). Keep it exported only if the guide wants a tool to read a session's status without exiting on it.
  - In M: exitcode/exitcode.go:124 (Code)
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through Code.

#### exitcode: placement

- Nothing is misplaced. Exited serves runner.Attach but must stay here: runner imports exitcode (runner/runner.go:46), so the reverse import is not possible.

#### exitcode: signatures

- The usage side has three helpers (Usagef makes, AsUsage marks, IsUsage reads); the child side has Exited (makes), ChildExit (marks) and ChildStatus (reads), with no IsChildExit. If ChildStatus is unexported the child side reads only through Code, which is consistent with how both tools use it.
- runner.Exec.Attach reports a child killed by a signal as exitcode.Failure (runner/runner.go:267-271), while the planned Result.ExitCode is documented as -1 when the command never started (design 3.2). The breaking tag should say what Result.ExitCode holds for a signalled child, so Exited(name, res.ExitCode) and Code keep agreeing that the result is never negative.
- Exited(name, code) and ChildExit(err) are separate steps by design (a status passes through only where the caller says so). The client Exec methods should return the code in Result and not an Exited error, or B's ChildExit call sites (internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:514,518; internal/cli/ops_container.go:366,367) would wrap an error that M already built.
- The constants are untyped ints, which is what lets A write `return exitcode.Usage` from int handlers and B pass Code's result to os.Exit. No change needed.

### envref

- **Ref** (type, type; A2 B2): keep. Credential resolution stays in the tools (design 3.5) and both build one Ref per secret field with their own key names. Design 9.1 lists env references as a ready safety layer for a third tool.
- **UnsetError** (type, type; A1 B0): keep. A finds it with errors.As to turn an unset variable into a warning at validate; that policy is the tool's and needs the type. Neither tool reads the fields EnvKey, Name or Empty directly.
  - In M: envref/envref.go:136 (returned by Ref.Resolve)
  - Reached: B receives it as the error of Ref.Resolve (internal/config/secretref.go:73) and returns it to main unexamined.
- **UnsetError.Error** (method, other; A0 B0): keep. It is the method that makes *UnsetError an error, so it cannot be unexported.
  - Reached: Called through the error interface: A prints the value with %s (internal/validate/validate.go:427), B prints the error Resolve returned (main.go:23).
- **ValidName** (func, pure-helper; A0 B0): keep, owner call. By the letter it is used inside M only, but the decided work needs this grammar in other packages: gdu 4 makes the secret emitters check the env var (compose.Secret.EnvVar is written bare, containerart/compose/compose.go:84-86) and the Deployment renderer writes env names. B also keeps a regex copy (internal/config/validate.go:398). The other reading is unexport-internal-only.
  - In M: envref/envref.go:105 (Ref.Check)
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through Ref.Check and Ref.Resolve (A internal/validate/validate.go:406, internal/gen/gen.go:805; B internal/config/secretref.go:73).
- **Ref.Check** (method, pure-helper; A1 B0): keep. A's validator needs the rules that require no environment, apart from Resolve, so it can lint without exported credentials.
  - In M: envref/envref.go:124 (Ref.Resolve runs it first)
  - Reached: B reaches it through Ref.Resolve (internal/config/secretref.go:73).
- **Ref.Resolve** (method, pure-helper; A2 B1): keep. Both tools resolve every secret through it, with the lookup seam injected, so it reads no environment itself and fits executor rule 4 by construction.

#### envref: missing

- The environment-variable name grammar. M already owns it as envref.ValidName; B keeps a regex with the same rule for the names Kubernetes projects from a Secret.
  - A: none: A checks names only through Ref.Check (internal/validate/validate.go:406)
  - B: internal/config/validate.go:398 (envVarNameRE), used at :1318
  - Proposal: No new code in envref. B calls envref.ValidName, and M's compose Secret model and Deployment renderer use the same function when they check an env name (gdu 4, section 8), which is the reason to keep it exported.

#### envref: placement

- ValidName is a name grammar, and M's other grammars sit with their command family (containercli.ValidName, kube.ValidLabel, the subdomain grammar gdu 4 adds). It is right in envref as long as env names are checked only for references; if compose, quadlet and the Kubernetes renderer all import envref just for it, a neutral home is the owner's call.
- Ref, UnsetError, Ref.Check and Ref.Resolve are in the right package.

#### envref: signatures

- ValidName shares its bare name with containercli.ValidName (containercli/containercli.go:221), which is a different grammar. The breaking tag adds more grammars (gdu 4), so settle one naming scheme, for example ValidEnvName here, or leave the package qualifier to carry it.
- Ref.Check and Ref.Resolve return unmarked errors, and only the unset case is typed (*UnsetError). The exitcode contract makes all of them usage errors (exitcode/exitcode.go:8-10 names the environment variables the env file names); both tools mark them on their own. Same question as for execguard: mark in M or leave to the tool, one answer for both packages.
- Resolve already returns the literal when Name is empty (envref/envref.go:127-129), yet both tools skip the call in that case (A internal/gen/gen.go:800, B internal/config/secretref.go:70), and A's resolve path sets EnvKey equal to Field with no Literal (internal/gen/gen.go:805). The guide should show the one intended call; no signature change is needed.
- The nil-lookup message ends "run a command that deploys" (envref/envref.go:131-132), which is one tool's wording inside M. A third tool may read the environment in other commands; reword it to state only that this command has no environment access.
- Ref and UnsetError use value and pointer receivers respectively (Ref.Check on a value, *UnsetError as the error). That matches how both tools use them (struct literal, errors.As with a pointer); keep.

### Verifiers for the guards group

Usage re-count: 25 names counted, 0 corrections.


Rule challenge: 25 names examined, 2 challenges.

- **[medium] exitcode.ChildStatus**: unexport-internal-only -> keep
  - Three reasons it should stay exported. (1) A tool test names it: B internal/engine/runner_test.go:340 and :348. The proposed substitute, exitcode.Code(exitcode.ChildExit(err)), cannot carry the second assertion: Code returns Failure both for a marked error with no status behind it and for a session that really exited 1 (exitcode/exitcode.go:117-128), so 'an Attach error carries no exit status' can no longer be told from 'the session exited 1'. (2) It is the only reader of the child mark. childExitError is unexported (exitcode.go:74) and there is no IsChildExit, so without ChildStatus a tool with error-returning handlers cannot tell a passed-through session status from its own failure; Code folds a passed-through 1 or 2 into Failure or Usage (the collision exitcode.go:70-73 itself describes). That matters today: A prints nothing and returns the session's code (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:271-275), while B prints 'Error: kubectl: exit status 7' before exiting 7, because main prints every non-nil error (B main.go:22-25). Closing that difference in B, or giving a third tool A's behaviour from a main that only holds an error, needs ChildStatus in main; Result.ExitCode is gone by then. (3) Consistency with what was decided: CR-0001 can remove confirm.IsNotTerminal because the type behind it, NotTerminalError, is exported (confirm/prompter.go:139,146) and errors.As still reaches it. Here the type is unexported, so removing the reader removes the capability. The usage side keeps its reader (IsUsage) and gdu 13 shows a reader being needed across packages (docs/todo.md:708). Keeping costs nothing: it is documented and tested (docs/test.md:87).
- **[medium] execguard.IsControl**: keep -> change-signature
  - Keep it exported, but not with today's behaviour. Its doc says 'C0, DEL and C1' and the body then excludes U+0085, 'left to the whitespace check, which explains it better' (execguard/execguard.go:190-196). That hole is a message-ordering detail of checkToken, where the control branch (:290) runs before the whitespace branch (:294); it is not a property a public predicate should have. Callers outside checkToken that run no whitespace check after it inherit the hole: B internal/config/validate.go:361 (probe tokens, whitespace deliberately allowed, see the comment above :367) and A internal/validate/validate.go:1509 (java options, spaces allowed). U+0085 passes both gates and reaches the artifact and every log line, which is the terminal-rewrite case the doc of IsControl says it closes. The near-miss already confuses the tools: B chose unicode.IsControl for secret values and its comment describes execguard.IsControl as 'the ASCII set' (B internal/config/validate.go:976-985), which it is not. The reviewer's own placement note 2 wants one definition for containercli/inspect.Printable (containercli/inspect/inspect.go:197) too, but with today's body that would stop Printable stripping NEL from engine text. So 'keep unchanged' cannot give the single definition the reviewer asks for. Proposal: IsControl reports all of C1 including U+0085, and checkToken keeps its better wording by testing whitespace first for that one rune (or by ordering the two branches the other way). TestExportedCharset (docs/test.md:218) and B internal/config/hostpath_test.go:100 pin the current set and change with it.

Additions:

- (signature) execguard signatureNote 2 (Check as a method on Rules) is not more consistent with M. M's free Check functions take the rule or subject first: kube/rbac.Check(p Probe, ...) (kube/rbac/rbac.go:152), paritydoc.Check (paritydoc/paritydoc.go:57), noticetest.Check (notice/noticetest/noticetest.go:40). The two methods, runner.Cmd.Check (runner/runner.go:71) and envref.Ref.Check (envref/envref.go:91), have the checked value as receiver. In execguard the checked value is cmd, not Rules, so the free function matches the house pattern, and a rename churns about 17 call sites in the tools (A 7, B 8 code uses plus tests) for no gain. Only take the method form if the Guard type of missing item 4 is taken.
- (signature) execguard signatureNote 5 offers 'export the whole value check and stop exporting the pieces'. The second half is not available. Two tool gates deliberately admit what a whole-value check refuses and so need the bare predicates: B validateProbeCommand allows whitespace and shell syntax (B internal/config/validate.go:351-371) and A javaOptionsProblem allows spaces (A internal/validate/validate.go:1486-1513). M itself needs them across packages for inspect.Printable and for the gdu 4 free-operand rule. So: export the whole check AND keep IsControl and IsInvisible, and export isSpace (execguard/execguard.go:212) as the third piece.
- (signature) Missing item 1 (CheckValue) must state its tilde rule, because the three existing copies disagree: M's checkToken refuses only a leading tilde (execguard/execguard.go:311-320), A's SafeToken refuses a tilde anywhere (UnsafeChars+"~", A internal/validate/validate.go:1872), and B's checkTimezone has no tilde rule (B internal/config/artifactvalues.go:56-80). 'Without the tilde wording' leaves the behaviour open. It should also say which SafeToken call sites do not move to CheckValue at all because they are names on an argv that gdu 4 gives typed grammars: A cmd/solmq-conn-util/instances.go:115,119,124, status.go:573,706 and shell.go:158.
- (signature) Missing item 4 (Rules.Guard returning a sealed Tokens type that Client.Base requires) has four costs the note does not list. (a) Tools legitimately extend guarded tokens after the guard: gdu 11 puts --context <ctx> into Base, with a value read from kubectl and not from config (design note lines 64 and 165); A appends the word compose to its guarded docker tokens (A internal/runner/runner.go:179) and sets no Subword. A sealed type needs an append or With method, or A must adopt a compose Rules with Subword as B does (B internal/config/execguard.go:71,299-304). (b) Commands M does not offer are still built by the tool on the same tokens (design note line 169; A internal/runner/runner.go:335 and :934 build image inspect), so the type must hand back its []string. (c) Every client package would import execguard, and every M and tool test that fills a client for runnertest.Fake would need Guard or an escape-hatch constructor, which undoes 'cannot be set up with unchecked tokens'. (d) 'Once per run' is really once per client: B guards a fourth command per replication site through SiteCommand with its own Rules (B internal/config/execguard.go:60-62 and 242-258), which the note's whereB list omits. Design 3.1 decided Base as []string on 2026-11-15, so this is a change to a decided shape, not only an owner call on execguard.
- (signature) The bare-name constant for ApproveFlag (execguard signatureNote 1) should be decided together with confirm.DefaultNoPromptFlag = "--no-prompt" (confirm/prompter.go:12), which has the same two-form need: A holds the dashed literal at cmd/solmq-conn-util/commands.go:63 and registers the bare name at main.go:1612; B registers bare at internal/cli/flags.go:52 and :213 and commands.go:717. One scheme for both constants. Note also the asymmetry: confirm lets the tool name its flag (Options.NoPromptFlag, confirm/prompter.go:36-37) while execguard hard-wires ApproveFlag into its messages; that is fine under CR-0001 but the guide for a third tool should say the approval flag's name is fixed by M.
- (signature) exitcode signatureNote 3 says the client Exec methods should return the code in Result and not an error, 'or B's ChildExit call sites would wrap an error that M already built'. Wrapping an M-built error is exactly today's pattern and what ChildExit is for: B wraps engine.Interactive's exitcode.Exited error (B internal/engine/runner.go:173-179; B internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:514,518; ops_container.go:366,367), and ChildStatus reads any error with ExitCode() int (exitcode/exitcode.go:146-151). The other way round is the inconsistent one: design 3.3 rule 2 has every executor return a typed error, and Exec alone would return nil for a non-zero exit. And with a nil error plus Result.ExitCode -1 for 'never started' (design note line 87), a tool that calls Exited(name, res.ExitCode) without checking err first words a start failure as 'exit status -1'. Proposal for the tag: Exec returns Result.ExitCode and a typed error that implements ExitCode() int (built with exitcode.Exited inside M, or equivalent); B keeps ChildExit(err) unchanged, A returns res.ExitCode. That unexported-method interface is then part of the public contract and should be documented as such.
- (signature) The 'who marks a usage error' question (execguard signatureNote 3, envref signatureNote 2) is wider than those two packages and touches two stated contracts. The breaking tag adds many more M errors that are refusals of what the tool was given: every gdu 4 builder grammar error (21 call sites in A, 37 in B, docs/todo.md:297), the secret emitters' name, target and env-var errors (CR-0004), the tilde package's errors and the gdu 6 network-word check. One answer has to cover all of them. And marking inside M reverses two written sentences: 'The caller decides which errors are usage errors by marking them' (exitcode/exitcode.go:11-12) and 'A tool marks usage errors' (docs/developer.md:76). No M package calls Usagef or AsUsage today (usedInM is empty for both), so this would be the first. Marking in M does not block the tools' policies: usageError unwraps (exitcode.go:36), so A's errors.As for *envref.UnsetError (A internal/validate/validate.go:425-426) still works.
- (missing) The command-token tilde expansion. B expands a leading ~ in every command token except argv[0] before the guard runs (B internal/config/execguard.go:199-236, using expandTildeToken at hostpath.go:452); gdu 2 makes A follow and lifts expandTilde and expandTildeToken into the new tilde package (docs/todo.md:183-184). The 'every token but the first' loop is a common operation bound to execguard's leading-tilde rule, and it changes execguard itself: the refusal at execguard/execguard.go:317-319 says 'nothing downstream expands' and 'write the path out in full', and docs/developer.md:101 says expansion stays in each tool. Both become wrong advice once both tools expand through M. Proposal: tilde holds the per-command form (the tool passes the tokens and the home resolver), and the checkToken message is reworded in the same step to name a token that reached the guard unexpanded.
- (missing) Correction to missing item 2 (host-path gate): the two tools do not apply the same rule. B refuses a leading tilde (the last branch of CheckHostPath, B internal/config/hostpath.go:73 onward). A does not: safeHostPath is UTF-8, unsafeRune and pathShellMeta only, and its comment says a tilde is permitted (A internal/validate/validate.go:1152-1166); A has no tilde expansion yet. So an M CheckHostPath with the leading-tilde refusal changes A's behaviour and must land with or after gdu 2's tilde lift in A, not before. A also permits ':' in a host path while gdu 4's Volume= line builder refuses one in the source (docs/todo.md:295-296): the M gate has to say which of the two refuses it.
- (missing) The gdu 4 free-operand rule is a third, weaker value check that the reviewer's missing list does not place: 'a free operand refuses only an empty value, a leading - and control characters' (docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:128-129), used by the targets of kube Get and Logs. With the typed grammars (names) and CheckValue (bare values) that makes three tiers. The builders run it themselves, so it need not be exported, but it needs one home that kube and containercli share (internal/build is the precedent) and it needs the control predicate, which is one more importer of the charset.
- (missing) Hand-rolled copies the counts do not show, because they do not name the exported symbols. Separator set: A internal/libs/libs.go:571 spells "/\\" and :574 uses the OS-dependent filepath.IsAbs for the same name-or-path question B answers with HasPathSeparator and IsAbsHostPath on execguard.PathSeparators (B internal/config/hostpath.go:143,168,181,188); B's own doc at hostpath.go:127-137 says filepath.IsAbs is wrong for an env file that crosses operating systems. So PathSeparators has a second consumer waiting in A (countA 0 hides it), and the three cross-OS helpers are candidates to travel with the tilde or host-path package. Control set, C0 and DEL only, without C1: A cmd/solmq-conn-util/completion.go:184, internal/consolidate/consolidate.go:624, internal/storebuild/storebuild.go:656, internal/libs/libs.go:578; B internal/broker/broker.go:386 and :403, internal/config/load.go:137, internal/convert/convert.go:717, internal/k8s/matechannel.go:174. Each should either adopt the shared predicate or be recorded as deliberate when the charset gets its home.
- (missing) Evidence for missing item 3 (M states the binaries per client) that tips it away from 'tool customisation': B allows docker-compose 'for docker.compose on a host carrying only the standalone v1 binary' (B internal/config/execguard.go:30-31 and :40), but the shared compose secrets block reads values through the environment provider, which needs Docker Compose 2.23.1 or later (containerart/compose/compose.go:72-74). Which binaries can run what M renders and issues is knowledge about M's own artifacts and builders, so M is the place that can keep the list true; A lists only docker (A internal/validate/validate.go:53-57).
- (placement) The case for a neutral charset home is stronger than placement note 1 states, and 'cannot be unexported because runner reads it' (UnsafeChars) is not exact. Importers after the decided work: runner (runner/echo.go:115), containercli/inspect (Printable), the tilde package (B's code to be lifted uses isSpace at hostpath.go:438 and :457 and the separator set at :479), the gdu 4 free-operand check in kube and containercli, and the section 8 renderers. That is five or more packages importing a package whose doc says it 'decides whether a command read from config may run'. M also holds two more private definitions the note does not list: runner's printable uses unicode.IsPrint (runner/echo.go:121-130) and quadlet.Escape uses c < 0x20 || c == 0x7f (containerart/quadlet/quadlet.go:55). A read by another M package forces a shared home, not a public one: if missing items 1 and 2 are taken, UnsafeChars has no tool consumer left (A internal/validate/validate.go:1150,1872 and B internal/config/hostpath.go:52, artifactvalues.go:76 all go), and it could sit in an internal package as internal/build does, with only the predicates and the checks public. The doc at execguard/execguard.go:90-91 invites a tool to derive its own set, so this is the owner's call, but it should be made knowing both options.
- (placement) The host-path gate of missing item 2 does not have one natural home; it has three parts with three owners. The charset part belongs with the charset. The leading-tilde refusal is, in B's own words, the fail-closed half of tilde expansion (comment inside CheckHostPath, B internal/config/hostpath.go:73 onward), so it belongs with the tilde package of gdu 2. The colon and percent rules belong to the Volume= line builder of gdu 4 (docs/todo.md:289-296). Whatever the split, one exported function must remain callable by a tool at load, before any rendering: the tools gate the value as written (B hostpath.go:62-67) and A's validate verb renders nothing, so a check that lives only inside the section 8 models would move the refusal from validate to deploy. execguard itself should not take the path gate: its package doc scopes it to commands (execguard/execguard.go:1-7).

### confirm

- **Line** (func, cli-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only. Neither tool names it; the Prompter is the only caller and the package doc tells a tool to keep one Prompter per run so the shared buffered reader is not lost. A bare Line call invites the per-call reader bug the Prompter exists to prevent.
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:96 (Prompter.ReadLine); confirm/line.go:38 and :52 (YesNo, Yes)
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through Prompter.ReadLine (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1151, B internal/cli/platform.go:276) and through Ask and Gate.
- **YesNo** (func, cli-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only. Used inside M only; the exported form of the same thing is Ask or Gate with Lenient.
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:110 (Prompter.Ask, Lenient)
  - Reached: Reached through Prompter.Ask and Gate with confirm.Lenient (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1623, B internal/cli/confirm.go:80).
- **Yes** (func, cli-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only. Used inside M only; the exported form is Ask or Gate with Strict.
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:108 (Prompter.Ask, Strict)
  - Reached: Reached through Prompter.Gate with confirm.Strict (B internal/cli/confirm.go:59 and :98); A never asks a strict question.
- **DefaultNoPromptFlag** (const, constant; A0 B0): keep, owner call. It is the same case as execguard.ApproveFlag, where CR-0001 decided both tools take the flag name from M: Gate's error names this flag, so the flag each tool registers must be this one. If the owner does not want the tools to read it, the mechanical outcome is unexport-internal-only together with Options.NoPromptFlag, which neither tool sets.
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:84 (Prompter.flag, the fallback Gate names in its error)
  - Reached: Both tools get the text through Gate's error; A re-declares the same string as noPromptFlagName (cmd/solmq-conn-util/commands.go:63) and registers "no-prompt" (main.go:1612), B registers "no-prompt" as a literal (internal/cli/commands.go:717).
- **Form** (type, type; A0 B0): keep. Parameter type of two exported methods both tools call. The decided typed-name question (docs/todo.md:101) is a different shape (it needs the expected name), so it should be a new method rather than a third Form.
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:19 (type of Lenient and Strict), :103 and :123 (parameter of Ask and Gate)
  - Reached: The type of the Lenient and Strict constants both tools pass to Ask and Gate (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1623, B internal/cli/confirm.go:59).
- **Lenient** (const, constant; A4 B5): keep. Both tools pass it at every reversible-action question.
- **Strict** (const, constant; A0 B2): keep. B's irreversible deletions use it and it is plainly generic; A has no strict question today. One user is enough under rule 5.
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:107 (Ask branches on it)
- **Options** (type, type; A3 B2): keep. The Prompter's seams; both tools fill IsTerminal and In (A only in tests, cmd/solmq-conn-util/main_test.go:286-290). Out is set only by A's test helper and NoPromptFlag by nobody: see signatureNotes.
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:43 and :48 (Prompter field and New parameter)
- **Prompter** (type, type; A10 B2): keep. The one prompt object per run in both tools. Design 3.3 rule 4 says an executor never asks, so the question stays a tool call and this is the type it goes through.
- **New** (func, cli-helper; A3 B2): keep. Both tools build their Prompter with it. A constructor is right here although the planned clients are filled struct literals (design 3.1), because the Prompter holds the private buffered reader.
- **Prompter.Terminal** (method, cli-helper; A2 B4): keep. Both tools ask it before a step that must agree with the gate (A before opening a shell, B before the platform menu and the restart question).
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:93 and :104 (ReadLine and Ask)
- **Prompter.ReadLine** (method, cli-helper; A1 B1): keep. Both tools read their platform-menu answer with it. Its one use in each tool is the same numbered menu, which M should own (see missing); the method then still serves the decided typed-name question.
- **Prompter.Ask** (method, cli-helper; A1 B2): keep. Both tools use it for a question --no-prompt must not answer. Two of the three call sites then rebuild Gate's unanswered fold and wrap by hand (see missing and signatureNotes), and its parameter order differs from Gate's.
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:127 (Gate)
- **Prompter.Gate** (method, cli-helper; A3 B5): keep. The destructive-command rule of the parity body, stated once; the most used name of the package in both tools. The breaking tag is the moment to settle its parameter order against Ask (signatureNotes).
- **NotTerminalError** (type, type; A0 B0): keep. Once IsNotTerminal is removed (CR-0001) the exported type is the only way a tool can branch on the cause with errors.As, and design 3.3 rule 2 wants errors a tool can branch on. Whether it becomes a sentinel like the planned kube.ErrForbidden is in signatureNotes.
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:94 and :105 (returned by ReadLine and Ask)
  - Reached: Returned by ReadLine and Ask and wrapped by Gate, so it is in every refusal both tools print; B's tests pin its text (internal/cli/ops_k8s_test.go:70).
- **NotTerminalError.Error** (method, other; A0 B0): keep. The error interface of a kept type; it goes or stays with NotTerminalError.
  - In M: tests only (confirm/prompter_test.go:231)
  - Reached: Its text is the cause inside Gate's error line; B's tests compare it (internal/cli/ops_k8s_test.go:70 and :1465).
- **IsNotTerminal** (func, pure-helper; A0 B0): remove. Decided by CR-0001 (docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:68). The removal also rewrites the doc line at confirm/prompter.go:138, the five test uses to errors.As, and the docs/test.md:182 row.
  - In M: tests only (confirm/prompter_test.go:122, :189, :193, :239, :243)
- **Unanswered** (func, cli-helper; A2 B1): keep, owner call. All three tool uses are the same three lines after Ask or ReadLine (if err == nil && !answered { err = confirm.Unanswered() }). It stays until M owns that fold (see missing); once it does, this becomes internal or a sentinel error.
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:129 (Gate)
- **IsCharDevice** (func, pure-helper; A2 B0): keep, owner call. A uses it to decide whether stderr takes a progress line (cmd/solmq-conn-util/progress.go:100), which is generic. Its home is the question: it is not about confirming, and watch carries a second copy of the test.
  - In M: confirm/terminal.go:14 (StdinIsTerminal)
  - Reached: B reaches the same test through Prompter.Terminal and through watch.CanRedraw, which repeats it inline (watch/watch.go:192-193).
- **StdinIsTerminal** (func, pure-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only. Used inside M only, and the Terminal doc (confirm/prompter.go:50-52) tells a tool to ask the Prompter rather than probe stdin itself, which both tools do.
  - In M: confirm/prompter.go:55 (Prompter.Terminal's default)
  - Reached: The default behind Prompter.Terminal when Options.IsTerminal is nil, which is the production path of both tools (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:191, B internal/cli/confirm.go:36); B names it only in a comment (internal/cli/context.go:46).

#### confirm: missing

- A numbered menu question: list N choices, read a number from 1 to N, return the choice. Both tools write the list, call ReadLine, run strconv.Atoi and range-check by hand, for the platform choice.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1144-1163 (promptPlatformMenu)
  - B: internal/cli/platform.go:268-286 (promptPlatform; it writes the list straight to os.Stderr at :269-271, past the Prompter's Out seam)
  - Proposal: confirm: a Prompter method such as Choose(heading string, choices []string) (index int, err error) that writes through the Prompter's Out, returns *NotTerminalError or the unanswered error, and a distinct error for an out-of-range reply. The tool keeps the wording of its hint and the decision to offer a menu at all.
- A question answered by typing a given name (decided, gdu 1: docs/todo.md:101 and :79-82; a namespace without the tool's label is deleted only after a yes and then the typed namespace name). Not built; neither tool has it, and both ask only a yes/no today.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go:160-163 (confirmNamespaceRemoval, Lenient today)
  - B: internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:812 (confirmDelete on the now-empty namespace)
  - Proposal: confirm: a Prompter method such as AskName(prompt, want string) (matched, answered bool, err error) with exact, case-sensitive comparison, no Form. Whether --no-prompt answers it is the tool's rule (it needs --delete-namespace as well), so it should not go through Gate.
- A question that must be answered but that --no-prompt does not answer: Ask, turn an unanswered prompt into an error, wrap with the alternative the operator should use. Both tools rebuild Gate's body for it because Gate can only name the Prompter-wide flag.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/status.go:1007-1016 (confirmInstall, names --install) and cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1151-1157 (names --platform)
  - B: internal/cli/confirm.go:148-158 (confirmScope, names an env-file key)
  - Proposal: confirm: either Ask and ReadLine return the unanswered error themselves (dropping the answered result), or a method such as Require(prompt string, f Form, doing, instead string) (bool, error) worded like Gate with the caller's alternative. Either removes the three hand-written folds and lets Unanswered go internal.

#### confirm: placement

- IsCharDevice: a general terminal test, used by A for stderr progress (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:192) and repeated inline in watch.CanRedraw (watch/watch.go:192-193). One home for both is an owner call: leave it in confirm and accept the copy, or a small terminal package that confirm and watch both import.
- DefaultNoPromptFlag: correctly placed beside Gate, the rule it serves, the same way ApproveFlag sits in execguard; no move.
- No name in confirm belongs in a client package: design 3.3 rule 4 keeps every question out of the executors.

#### confirm: signatures

- Counts in this package are code references in .go files, tests included; mentions in comments are not counted.
- Parameter order: Ask(prompt, f Form) puts the form last, Gate(noPrompt, f Form, prompt, doing) puts it before the prompt (confirm/prompter.go:103 and :123). Settle one order while both tools touch every call site.
- Three result conventions for the same question: Line, YesNo and Yes return (value, answered); ReadLine and Ask return (value, answered, err); Gate returns (bool, error) with unanswered folded into the error. Three of the five Ask/ReadLine call sites in the tools fold it at once (A main.go:1152-1154, A status.go:1010-1012, B confirm.go:150-152).
- Two error styles for the two causes: not-a-terminal is a type (*NotTerminalError, tested with errors.As once IsNotTerminal is removed), unanswered is a constructor func whose only test is errors.Is(err, io.EOF). The design note's example branches on a sentinel (errors.Is(err, kube.ErrForbidden), section 3.6); pick one style for confirm in the same tag.
- Gate's hint flag is per Prompter (Options.NoPromptFlag, set by neither tool) while the tools need a per-call alternative (--install, --platform, an env key); that is why they bypass Gate at three sites. If the per-call form lands, Options.NoPromptFlag can go.
