- Options.Out is set only by A's test helper (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main_test.go:289); in production both tools take the os.Stderr default, and B writes its menu lines to os.Stderr directly (internal/cli/platform.go:269-271), so B's tests cannot capture that prompt through the seam.
- DefaultNoPromptFlag carries the dashes ("--no-prompt") while both flag libraries register the bare name (A main.go:1612, B commands.go:717); if the tools are to read it, the same question as for execguard.ApproveFlag applies: export the bare name, the dashed form, or both.
- confirm's errors are operator sentences with no "confirm:" prefix, unlike watch ("watch: ..." at watch/watch.go:39 and :132) and the builder packages; deliberate, but worth one line in the guide.

### abbrev

- **Entry** (type, type; A5 B5): keep. The declaration both tools write for every vocabulary. Note is filled only by B (internal/cli/aliases.go:33, internal/config/roles.go:44); A leaves it empty.
  - In M: abbrev/abbrev.go:50 and :73 (Set field and New parameter), :148 (returned by Entries)
- **Set** (type, type; A4 B5): keep. Both tools hold one per vocabulary (commands, targets or roles, platforms). It is CLI vocabulary, outside the clients and renderers, and the design leaves it untouched.
- **New** (func, cli-helper; A3 B3): keep. Both tools build every set with it and rely on its panic as the validation test (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main_test.go:3904). It is the one constructor in M that panics instead of returning an error; documented, and worth a line in the guide.
- **Set.Kind** (method, cli-helper; A0 B0): remove, owner call. Nothing reaches it: both tools word their unknown-value errors and headings themselves (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1119, B internal/config/roles.go:66, B internal/cli/abbrevdoc_test.go:158 takes the heading as a parameter). Keep it only if M gains the error helper or the reference table listed under missing, which would be its first reader.
  - In M: tests only (abbrev/abbrev_test.go:135); New's panics read the field, not the method
- **Set.Expand** (method, cli-helper; A6 B3): keep. The lookup both tools run at every input site.
- **Set.Short** (method, cli-helper; A0 B4): keep. B applies the command set to its command tree with it (production, internal/cli/aliases.go:118). A does not need it because its sets are built from a model that already holds the aliases.
- **Set.Entries** (method, cli-helper; A0 B2): keep. B's abbreviation reference and its start/stop test read the notes through it (tests only). It is the only accessor that returns Note, so the docs-generator use the package doc names depends on it.
- **Set.Names** (method, cli-helper; A0 B6): keep. B's completion list and role error come from it (production). A keeps a parallel hand-written list (platformNames, cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:68) that this would replace.
- **Set.Shorts** (method, cli-helper; A1 B2): keep. Used by both: B's role error in production, A in a test that pins the alias count.
- **Set.List** (method, cli-helper; A0 B2): keep. B's --platform error is rendered from it. A builds the same sentence by hand from two parallel lists (platformSpellings, cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:109-115, and wantList, main.go:386-397), which is the drift List exists to prevent; adopting it changes A's error text.

#### abbrev: missing

- The platform vocabulary itself. Both tools declare the same three canonical words and the same three short forms (kubernetes/kube, docker/dk, podman/pm) and build the same Set from them; a third tool on the three platforms would declare it a third time.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:68 (platformNames), :82-86 (platformAliasList), :93-105 (newPlatformAbbrev)
  - B: internal/config/platform.go:15 (Platforms), :22-26 (platformShort), :33-46 (newPlatforms)
  - Proposal: Owner call. A small M package (for example platform, importing abbrev) that exports the three names and the ready Set, under rule 5. Against it: the abbrev package doc says it owns the rules, not the vocabulary (abbrev/abbrev.go:9-13), and in both tools the canonical word doubles as an env-file section key, which the module is meant never to hard-code; todo item 19 (one shape for the platform sections) is the place this would be decided.
- The unknown-word error rendered from a set. Each tool writes it per vocabulary, three different shapes for the same fact (an Oxford-comma list, canonical (short) pairs, two pipe-joined lists).
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1117-1119 with platformSpellings at :109-115 and wantList at :386-397
  - B: internal/config/platform.go:64-66 and internal/config/roles.go:64-67
  - Proposal: Owner call, small. abbrev: a method such as Resolve(word string) (string, error) returning 'invalid <kind> "<word>": expected <List()>', which would also give Set.Kind its first reader. Each tool's wording is otherwise customisation and may stay; then Kind should go.
- The abbreviation reference table: a Set rendered as a Markdown table (word, short forms, notes column only when a note exists). The abbrev package doc names a docs generator as a caller, and only B has one.
  - A: none: A's command reference is generated from its own cliVerbs model, and its platform short forms are written out in prose
  - B: internal/cli/abbrevdoc_test.go:158-190 (writeAbbrevTable, in a test that generates docs/abbreviation.md)
  - Proposal: Owner call, low priority. abbrev: a pure renderer such as (s *Set) Markdown(label string) string, typed value in and text out like the section 8 renderers. One tool only, so it is worth doing only if the guide tells a third tool to publish the same reference.

#### abbrev: placement

- No name in abbrev sits in the wrong package.
- If the platform vocabulary moves into M (missing, first item), it belongs in its own package beside abbrev, not inside it: abbrev stays the rules.

#### abbrev: signatures

- Counts in this package are code references in .go files, tests included; mentions in comments are not counted. B's internal/cli/examples_test.go:35 calls examples.Names(), another package, and is not counted.
- New panics on a bad declaration where every other M constructor or checker returns an error (for example watch.CheckInterval, execguard.Approve). The reason is stated at abbrev/abbrev.go:59-61 and both tools rely on it; no change proposed, but the guide should say it.
- Three names one letter apart with three shapes: the field Entry.Short ([]string), the method Set.Short(canonical) ([]string for one word) and the method Set.Shorts() (all, flattened). If anything is renamed in the breaking tag, Set.Short is the one (for example ShortsOf).
- Set.Short returns nil both for an unknown word and for a declared word with no short form, while Set.Expand returns (string, bool). B's start/stop test has to read Entries to tell the two apart (internal/cli/aliases_test.go:183-196).
- kind is a required argument of New (it panics when empty, abbrev/abbrev.go:74-76) yet is read only by New's own panics; no exported method that a tool calls uses it. Either Kind gains a reader (missing, second item) or the argument is justified by the panic text alone.
- A wraps Expand in the same expand-or-return-unchanged helper three times (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:120-125, :252-257, commands.go:905-912); B always turns a miss into an error. No M change needed, but it shows the two tools use the (string, bool) result in two fixed ways.

### watch

- **ClearScreen** (const, constant; A3 B3): keep. Both tools' tests count and split frames on it; without the export each would hard-code the escape bytes. Neither tool writes it in production, which is the point: the loop is the only writer.
  - In M: watch/watch.go:158 (Options.head)
- **DefaultInterval** (const, constant; A2 B2): keep. Both tools take their flag default from it, so the two tools refresh at the same pace by construction.
  - In M: tests only (watch/watch_test.go:409)
- **MinInterval** (const, constant; A1 B1): keep. Both tools check their flag against it with their own wording, because the flag spelling differs per tool by decision (docs/developer.md:307).
  - In M: watch/watch.go:38 and :40 (CheckInterval)
- **MaxInterval** (const, constant; A1 B1): keep. Same as MinInterval: the shared bound each tool's flag check reads.
  - In M: watch/watch.go:38 and :40 (CheckInterval)
- **CheckInterval** (func, pure-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Used inside M only: neither tool can use it at flag time, because its text names no flag and it is not a usage error, so both re-check with MinInterval and MaxInterval. The alternative is to keep it and make it return an exitcode usage error both tools wrap, which gdu 13 makes natural (the loop will import exitcode anyway).
  - In M: watch/watch.go:128 (loop, behind Loop and LoopFrames)
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through LoopFrames, which refuses an out-of-range Options.Every before the first pass; by then each tool has already refused the value itself (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/status.go:123-125, B internal/cli/flags.go:186-193).
- **Options** (type, type; A1 B1): keep. Both tools fill the same six fields (Every, Title, Redraw, Out, Warn, Sleep); Now is set by neither and stays as M's own test seam.
  - In M: watch/watch.go:83, :106 and :127 (parameter of Loop, LoopFrames and loop)
- **Loop** (func, cli-helper; A0 B0): keep. Decided by CR-0001: watch.Loop stays (docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:68). Both tools moved to LoopFrames, so docs/developer.md:246 and :307, which still say status --watch runs watch.Loop, are stale, and the guide should say when a tool picks which.
  - In M: tests only (watch/watch_test.go:49, :137, :165, :190)
- **LoopFrames** (func, cli-helper; A1 B1): keep. The loop both tools run behind status --watch. It is the one place M owns a sleep and a retry, which the executor rules forbid elsewhere; that is consistent because it runs the tool's refresh, not a command, and gdu 13 already decides which errors end it.
- **Sleep** (func, pure-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Used inside M only; the tools reach it as the nil default and name it only in a comment (A status.go:1061). The owner's call is the other direction: B carries its own copy of the same context-aware pause (internal/broker/broker.go:94-106), so a kept, exported Sleep would have a second user, but then it does not belong in watch.
  - In M: watch/watch.go:139 (loop's default when Options.Sleep is nil)
  - Reached: The production pause of both tools: each passes a nil Options.Sleep outside tests (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/status.go:381 builds watchIO without one, B internal/cli/watch.go:80 passes the nil App.Sleep).
- **CanRedraw** (func, cli-helper; A1 B1): keep. Both tools pass CanRedraw(os.Stdout) as Options.Redraw; it also switches on Windows escape processing, which neither tool should reimplement.
  - In M: tests only (watch/watch_test.go:431, watch/vt_windows_test.go:41)

#### watch: missing

- A frame writer that is safe for concurrent writes. LoopFrames hands the refresh a plain bytes.Buffer (watch/watch.go:107-110); a refresh that runs commands gets stdout and stderr copied by separate goroutines, so B wraps the frame in its own mutex writer. The planned Output hook (design 3.2) delivers stdout and stderr chunks as they arrive, so every tool that points a client's hook at a frame meets the same race.
  - A: none: A collects first and renders the frame from one goroutine (cmd/solmq-conn-util/status.go:1110-1117)
  - B: internal/cli/watch.go:82 and :100-111 (lockedWriter)
  - Proposal: watch: LoopFrames passes the refresh a writer that serialises Write itself, and its doc says so. One tool today, plainly generic, and it removes a type from B.
- The interval range refusal at flag time. Both tools repeat the MinInterval/MaxInterval comparison with their own wording because CheckInterval's error names no flag and is not a usage error.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/status.go:119-125 (watchFlag.Set, whole seconds)
  - B: internal/cli/flags.go:182-193 (watchInterval, a duration, each refusal an exitcode.Usagef)
  - Proposal: Owner call between two ends: unexport CheckInterval and leave the wording to each tool (the flag spellings differ by decision), or keep it, return a usage error that carries the range, and have both tools wrap it with their flag name. Parsing the flag value stays in each tool either way.

#### watch: placement

- Sleep: a general context-aware pause, not a watch concept. If it stays exported it should live where a non-watch caller would look for it (B's poll loop at internal/broker/broker.go:94-106 is the second user); if it is unexported, no move.
- CanRedraw: repeats confirm.IsCharDevice's test inline (watch/watch.go:192-193) because watch does not import confirm. Same owner call as the confirm placement line: accept the copy, or one small terminal package for both.
- ClearScreen, the interval constants, Options, Loop and LoopFrames are in the right package.

#### watch: signatures

- Counts in this package are code references in .go files, tests included, read through the gduwatch alias in A; mentions in comments are not counted.
- Loop and LoopFrames agree with each other: (ctx, Options, refresh), refresh takes the pass time first, both return error. No change needed between the two.
- Two hook shapes in M after the breaking tag: Options.Warn is func(msg string), the planned client hook is func(runner.Stream, []byte) (design 3.2). They carry different things, so this is acceptable, but the guide should name both.
- Options.Sleep (field) and Sleep (function) share a name, so the field's doc has to say 'Sleep (the function) when nil' (watch/watch.go:69-70). Unexporting the function removes the clash.
- CheckInterval returns a plain error with a 'watch:' prefix, while gdu 13 (docs/todo.md:703-709) makes the loop sort refresh errors by exitcode.IsUsage and B classifies every interval refusal as a usage error (internal/cli/flags.go:180-192). If CheckInterval stays exported it should be a usage error too.
- Options.Redraw and Options.Out are two fields for one fact: both tools pass CanRedraw(os.Stdout) and os.Stdout side by side (A status.go:381, B watch.go:77-78 and :131). Keep both, since tests need to force redraw into a buffer, but the doc at watch/watch.go:54-56 should say Redraw must describe Out.
- Pending behaviour change, not a signature: gdu 13 makes Loop and LoopFrames return a refresh error that is a usage error instead of retrying it; the doc comments at watch/watch.go:74-82 and :102-105 change in the same tag.
- Docs drift to fix with the tag: docs/developer.md:246 and :307 say status --watch runs watch.Loop; both tools run watch.LoopFrames (A status.go:1103, B watch.go:74).

### Verifiers for the cli group

Usage re-count: 40 names counted, 0 corrections.


Rule challenge: 40 names examined, 4 challenges.

- **[medium] confirm.Prompter.Gate**: keep -> change-signature
  - Gate as it stands cannot word the decided namespace question, so the breaking tag has to change it, not only reorder it. gdu 1 (M docs/todo.md:79-82, docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:108-110 and :183-185): --no-prompt alone never deletes a namespace; only --no-prompt with --delete-namespace does. Both tools send that question through Gate today: A cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go:161, B internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:812 through confirm.go:68-70 and :80 (B's comment at ops_k8s.go:783 still says --no-prompt approves it). After gdu 1: (a) B must not hand a.noPrompt to Gate, because Gate returns true on it without asking (M confirm/prompter.go:124-126); (b) the refusal must name both flags, but the hint is fixed per Prompter (prompter.go:82-87, :132) and one Prompter per run is the rule (confirm/doc.go:16-17), so a scripted `remove --no-prompt` with no terminal would be told to pass --no-prompt. A already passes a constant false at two of its three Gate calls (namespace.go:161, main.go:1643) and tests the flag itself (main.go:596, namespace.go:129), and three more sites rebuild Gate by hand for the same reason (A status.go:1007-1016, A main.go:1151-1157, B confirm.go:148-158). Proposed shape: the bool becomes 'approved', computed by the tool, and a per-call alternative text replaces Options.NoPromptFlag (empty means the default flag). The reviewer's Require idea is this same change; I would make it the recommendation for Gate, not a note.
- **[low] confirm.NotTerminalError**: keep -> change-signature
  - It is the only exported error type in M with no fields. rbac.RefusedError (M kube/rbac/rbac.go:131-134) and envref.UnsetError (envref/envref.go:52-58) carry data a tool reads, which is what errors.As is for. NotTerminalError is struct{} with a pointer receiver (confirm/prompter.go:139-141): once IsNotTerminal goes (CR-0001) the only test left is errors.As with a pointer-to-pointer target, and errors.Is(err, &confirm.NotTerminalError{}) compares pointers to zero-size values, which the Go spec leaves unspecified. The design's own example branches on a sentinel (design note line 148, errors.Is(err, kube.ErrForbidden)). Neither tool names the type; both ask Prompter.Terminal first (A shell.go:168, B platform.go:242 and confirm.go:125). So the breaking tag can make it var ErrNotTerminal with the same text (B's pinned wording at internal/cli/ops_k8s_test.go:67-72 still holds) and put ErrUnanswered beside it in place of the Unanswered() constructor, at no call-site cost. The reviewer left this as a pick-one note; I would recommend the sentinel pair.
- **[low] confirm.DefaultNoPromptFlag**: keep -> rename
  - If it is kept on the CR-0001 analogy it should look like its analogue. execguard.ApproveFlag (M execguard/execguard.go:69) has no Default prefix and no override. The prefix exists only because of Options.NoPromptFlag (confirm/prompter.go:36-37), which neither tool sets and which the parity body rules out for these two tools (paritydoc/body.md:146 and :166 name --no-prompt as the shared rule). Keeping both leaves two answers to who owns the flag name. Rename to NoPromptFlag and drop the Options field; a per-call alternative on Gate (see the Gate challenge) covers a tool that needs other wording. Both spellings are needed by the tools: A uses the dashed form in its command model (cmd/solmq-conn-util/commands.go:63, :286, :321-339) and the bare form at registration (main.go:1612); B registers the bare form at three sites, not one (internal/cli/commands.go:717, flags.go:52, :213) and names it again at commands.go:146 and :543.
- **[low] confirm.IsCharDevice**: keep -> move-package
  - The case for one terminal home is stronger than the two copies the reviewer counted. M holds three terminal facts in two packages: confirm.IsCharDevice (M confirm/terminal.go:7-10), its inline copy in watch.CanRedraw (watch/watch.go:192-193), and the Windows escape switch enableVT, unexported inside watch (watch/vt_windows.go:16-29). A carries its own copy of that switch (cmd/solmq-conn-util/vt_windows.go:25-41) for its attached shell (shell.go:265); B has none, so its attached session on a plain Windows console is not covered. The planned interactive executors (design note lines 100-101; Exec in containercli.Client and kube.Client, section 3.4) are where that switch belongs, and they cannot reach it inside watch. So the tag needs a shared terminal helper in any case, at least an internal one. A's use of IsCharDevice is for stderr progress (main.go:192, progress.go:100) and has nothing to do with confirming. One small package (the char-device test, the escape switch, CanRedraw built on both) is where a third tool would look. Still the owner's call, but with this evidence I would not default to keep in confirm.

Additions:

- (missing) confirm, namespace questions under gdu 1: the reviewer's third missing item lists three sites that rebuild Gate. Add two more that the decided flow forces: A cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go:160-163 and B internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:812 (through confirm.go:68-70, :80). Both the yes question and the typed-name question are answered only by --no-prompt together with --delete-namespace (M docs/todo.md:79-82), so both need a caller-computed approval and a caller-supplied alternative text; the reviewer applied that only to AskName.
- (missing) Windows escape processing for an attached session. A switches it on itself before its shell (cmd/solmq-conn-util/vt_windows.go:25-41, called at shell.go:265), duplicating M's unexported watch.enableVT (M watch/vt_windows.go:16-29). B has no such call (no SetConsoleMode or enableVT anywhere in B). The planned interactive Exec methods (design note lines 100-101 and section 3.4) should switch it on, or M should export the switch from the terminal home; either removes A's copy and closes B's gap.
- (missing) watch test helper. The same Sleep fake (record each pause, stop after n) is written three times: M watch/watch_test.go:28-38, A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main_test.go:4333-4345, B internal/cli/watch_test.go:20-30. B also pins the section-line shape with its own regexp (watch_test.go:33), which follows M's unexported timeLayout and sectionWidth (watch/watch.go:32-33). A watch/watchtest package (StopAfter, a section-line matcher) fits beside runnertest, noticetest and artifacttest, which the design lists as the tests a tool runs (design note lines 334-337).
- (missing) confirm, the reply hint. Every lenient call site appends the same ' [y/N] ' by hand (A main.go:1624, :1644, namespace.go:162, status.go:1009; B confirm.go:80, :113, :131, :140, :149), and the text must agree with the Form passed beside it; a '[y/N]' on a Strict question would make 'y' decline silently. M knows the Form, so Ask and Gate could append the hint. Low priority, and only for Lenient: B words its strict hint two ways on purpose (confirm.go:59-61, :98-100).
- (placement) The concurrency fix the reviewer puts in watch belongs first in the clients' Output hook. M's runner passes the two writers straight to os/exec (M runner/runner.go:226), and os/exec serialises writes when stdout and stderr are the same comparable writer, which is what B passes (internal/cli/watch.go:82 and :95: one *lockedWriter for both). So a race on the frame is not established for the plain Run path today. It becomes real with the planned hook: marking each chunk stdout or stderr (design note lines 80-96) needs two distinct writers, so os/exec uses two goroutines and the hook is called concurrently. Design 3.2 should state that a client never overlaps hook calls (one lock per call inside the client). A serialising frame writer in LoopFrames then only matters for a refresh that runs commands in parallel (A has 4 status workers, main.go:194, but renders from one goroutine).
- (placement) Platform vocabulary: a new platform package would overlap a type M already exports. containercli.Engine with Docker = "docker" and Podman = "podman" (M containercli/containercli.go:26-32) holds two of the three words. If the vocabulary moves into M it must reuse or replace Engine, not add a second typed spelling.
- (placement) Terminal helpers: if the owner keeps IsCharDevice in confirm, the Windows escape switch still has to leave watch (an internal package at least), because the interactive executors in containercli and kube need it and should not import watch. See the IsCharDevice challenge.
- (signature) Unexporting confirm.Yes cannot be a plain lower-casing. Ask declares a named result yes and calls Yes in its body (M confirm/prompter.go:103, :108); a package function named yes would be shadowed there and the call would not compile. Line lower-cased compiles but is shadowed by the local line in YesNo and Yes (confirm/line.go:38, :52). Pick other names (for example readLine, lenientYes, strictYes) or fold the two forms into Ask. All confirm, abbrev and watch tests are in-package (package confirm, package abbrev, package watch), so the lifted test names TestPromptYesNo and TestPromptYes (docs/test.md:167-168) survive the unexport unchanged.
- (signature) Gate's first parameter is named noPrompt (M confirm/prompter.go:123), which invites passing the flag verbatim. Under gdu 1 that is wrong for the namespace question (the approval is --no-prompt AND --delete-namespace). Name it for what it means (approved) whatever else changes.
- (signature) A nil *Prompter has the per-call reader bug that is the reviewer's reason for unexporting Line: in() returns a fresh bufio.NewReader(os.Stdin) on every call (M confirm/prompter.go:61-64), and B builds a new Prompter per call when its App is nil (internal/cli/confirm.go:32-34). Decide in the breaking tag whether nil stays safe; if it does, the type doc (prompter.go:40-41) should say a second question may lose its answer.
- (signature) Ask treats every Form other than Strict as Lenient (M confirm/prompter.go:107-111), so an out-of-range value falls to the weaker form. With new question shapes arriving, an unknown Form should fail closed (treat as Strict, or return an error).
- (signature) 'Unanswered' will mean two things in M after the tag: a prompt that hit end of input (confirm.Unanswered, M confirm/prompter.go:151-153) and an executor read that returned nothing (design note lines 110-111; B already uses 'answered' for that at internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:796-803). When the error style for confirm is fixed, pick names that cannot be confused with the clients' typed error.
- (signature) Prompter.ReadLine: the reviewer's reason for keep contradicts its own proposals. If Choose and AskName both land in M as Prompter methods, neither tool calls ReadLine any more (its only uses are the two menus: A main.go:1151, B platform.go:276). Keep it deliberately as the free-text primitive for a third tool and say so in the guide, or unexport it with Line.
- (signature) watch.Options.Now is exported, set by neither tool, and not needed exported for M's own tests, which are in-package (M watch/watch_test.go:55 sets it from package watch). Either the tools plumb it to pin the header time (both can only match a header prefix today: A main_test.go:4362, B watch_test.go:169) or it becomes an unexported field.
- (signature) gdu 13 premise is stale for A. M docs/todo.md:708-710 says solmq-conn-util's refresh returns no errors, so nothing changes for it. Since A moved to LoopFrames its refresh returns the collection error to the loop (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/status.go:1110-1115), so the usage-error rule does apply to A. Also, the loop's own refusal of Options.Every (M watch/watch.go:128-130) should be a usage error even if CheckInterval is unexported: it is the only refusal a tool that does not pre-check gets, and it would otherwise exit 1.
- (signature) Docs corrections to the reviewer's notes. (1) M docs/developer.md:246-250 is not stale: it describes Loop, and :251-257 describes LoopFrames and says when Loop stays. Only the S8 row at :307 says status --watch runs watch.Loop, and that row sits in the Adoption order table (:290-298), a record of the step as built; LoopFrames came in v0.1.2 (docs/consuming.md:68-72). Mark it superseded or leave it; do not treat it as drift in the rules text. (2) Unexporting Sleep leaves three mentions to fix: the same S8 row ('give way to watch.CanRedraw and watch.Sleep'), the Options.Sleep field doc (watch/watch.go:69-70) and A's comment at status.go:1061. (3) Removing Set.Kind also removes the lifted test TestKind (abbrev/abbrev_test.go:134-138, source B per docs/test.md:103) and that row; the reviewer listed such follow-ups for IsNotTerminal only.
- (signature) Package docs still name exactly two tools, against the third-tool aim (design note section 9.2): confirm/doc.go:3 ('the one decided for both tools'), watch/watch.go:1-2 ('in solmq-conn-util and solace-util') and the Options.Title example (watch.go:50-51). abbrev/abbrev_test.go:339-376 copies A's command, platform and target words as literals. Reword in the same tag as the guide.
- (signature) abbrev drift is not only in A. B also hand-writes the platform spellings in one message, 'kubernetes|docker|podman (kube|dk|pm)' (B internal/cli/platform.go:243-244), beside the List() rendering it uses at config/platform.go:66. So both tools carry a pipe-joined rendering next to List (A pipeList and wantList at main.go:380-397, B roles.go:66-67); if abbrev gains any renderer, the pipe form is the one both would use.

### containercli

- **Engine** (type, type; A3 B2): keep. The Info error's hint and NoPrevious are keyed on it, and it becomes a field of containercli.Client set once per run. Both tools only ever convert their platform word to it.
  - In M: same package: parameter of InfoError (containercli.go:54), EngineHint (:67), NoPrevious (:301); receiver of the unexported title (:35)
- **Docker** (const, constant; A0 B0): keep. It is the documented value of Engine that a Client, the guide and the example tool are set up with. The tools' casts should become these constants in the breaking tag so the match stops depending on spelling.
  - In M: same package: title (containercli.go:37), EngineHint (:69)
  - Reached: Neither tool names it: each casts its own platform word, containercli.Engine(sess.platform) (A logs.go:211) and containercli.Engine(m.P) (B preflight.go:47), which equals this constant only because the spellings coincide.
- **Podman** (const, constant; A0 B0): keep. Same as Docker: the value a Client is set up with, needed by the guide and example tool.
  - In M: same package: title (containercli.go:39), EngineHint (:73, :80)
  - Reached: Same as Docker: reached through the cast of the tool's platform word (A internal/runner/runner.go:1133, B internal/container/manager.go:1399).
- **Info** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:containercli.Client.Info. Client.Info runs it and returns the hinted error. Both call sites are a plain run-then-wrap (A runner.go:391-398, B preflight.go:33-47); B's preview branch works through runner.Echo.
- **InfoError** (func, other; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:containercli.Client.Info (catalogue: "error carries the platform hint"). It is the error Client.Info returns, so no tool builds it once Info is an executor. Its engine and rootless arguments become Client fields and its out argument should go (see signatureNotes).
- **EngineHint** (func, pure-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only; by:containercli.Client.Info (the hint inside its error). Only InfoError calls it; no tool names it in code. If the Info error becomes a typed error, a Hint field there gives a tool the text without this export.
  - In M: same package: InfoError (containercli.go:60)
  - Reached: Both tools get its text inside InfoError's message (A runner.go:398, B preflight.go:47); A names it only in a comment (runner.go:375).
- **ComposeUp** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:containercli.Client.ComposeUp. Both tools build it and run it at once; the compare and the question happen before the call and stay in the tool. The signature changes anyway for -p (gdu 9).
- **ComposeDown** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:containercli.Client.ComposeDown (with -p, gdu 9). Same as ComposeUp: one build, one run, in both tools.
- **ComposeDigest** (func, pure-helper; A2 B2): keep. A pure function no client covers; each tool computes it before the compare-then-ask that stays in the tool. Its home is a placement question once gdu 10 adds a quadlet digest (see placement).
- **Start** (func, operation-builder; A1 B2): unexport-covered-by-client; by:containercli.Client.Start. Built and run in one step in both tools. B's test use (runtime_test.go:70) only needs any builder to feed runCmd, which goes away with the client.
- **Stop** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:containercli.Client.Stop. Built and run in one step in both tools.
- **Restart** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:containercli.Client.Restart. Built and run in one step in both tools.
- **Remove** (func, operation-builder; A0 B0): remove. Already decided in CR-0001. Nothing in either tool or in M outside its own test reaches it, and the catalogue has no Remove method.
- **List** (func, operation-builder; A1 B3): unexport-covered-by-client; by:containercli.Client.List. Every production use runs it and parses the answer at once. B's test helper listArgs (manager_test.go:1967) derives the expected argv from it and would spell the argv or assert through runnertest.Fake.
- **Row** (type, type; A0 B3): keep. It is what Client.List returns and what HasName and ByImage take.
  - In M: same package: ParseList (containercli.go:177), HasName (:198), ByImage (:208)
  - Reached: A never names it but ranges over ParseList's result and reads row.Name (cmd/solmq-conn-util/instances.go:255-256).
- **ParseList** (func, parser; A2 B3): unexport-covered-by-client; by:containercli.Client.List. Every production call parses a listing the tool has just run, which Client.List returns as rows. The one standalone use is a B test building rows from text (secrets_test.go:910), which can use Row literals.
- **HasName** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. A pure exact-name match over the rows Client.List returns; the tool applies it in its own compare (is it running before a redeploy, is it gone after a remove under gdu 9).
- **ByImage** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. A pure filter both tools use for --all discovery, each with its own image marker, so the marker stays in the tool and the filter in M.
- **ValidName** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. The typed grammar gdu 4 makes every bare-name builder check, and both tools call it on names read back from the engine. B still holds a regexp copy for its env file (internal/config/validate.go:404) that should call this instead.
- **ExecOptions** (type, type; A1 B2): keep. It is the argument of Client.Exec. Whether it also carries the run mode (attached, captured, stdin bytes) is part of the Exec decision below.
  - In M: same package: Exec (containercli.go:239), Upload (:268)
- **Exec** (func, operation-builder; A1 B2): unexport-covered-by-client, owner call; by:containercli.Client.Exec (returns the child's exit code). Covered only if Client.Exec offers all four ways the tools run it: streamed (B transport.go:82), stdout captured and returned (transport.go:90), stdin bytes plus capture (transport.go:98, which sets Cmd.Stdin after building), and an attached terminal (B manager.go:1477, A cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:273). If the client covers fewer, B's broker.Transport still needs the builder alone.
  - In M: same package: Upload (containercli.go:268)
- **Upload** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:containercli.Client.Upload. Both tools build it and run it at once, with no step between.
- **LogOptions** (type, type; A1 B8): keep. It is the argument of Client.Logs, and B passes it through its own Manager.Logs from the CLI layer. The type of Tail is open under gdu 14 (see signatureNotes).
  - In M: same package: Logs (containercli.go:282)
- **Logs** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:containercli.Client.Logs. A takes only .Argv and runs it with a cancellable context and its own stdout and stderr (cmd/solmq-conn-util/logs.go:285-303); the context and the stream-marked hook of Client.Logs give it the same. B builds and runs at once.
- **NoPrevious** (func, cli-helper; A2 B1): keep, owner call. Both tools use it for one shared refusal and no client covers it. Its text hard-codes the flag word --previous (containercli.go:302), which fits the execguard.ApproveFlag precedent for the two tools but a third tool with another flag name would want the name passed in.

#### containercli: missing

- Copy a file into or out of a container: `<engine> cp <local> <name>:<dest>` and the reverse. Plainly generic and absent from the 3.4 catalogue.
  - A: none (A sends files on stdin through containercli.Upload, internal/runner/runner.go:1170)
  - B: internal/container/transport.go:125 and :135 (kubectl twin at internal/k8s/transport.go:106 and :116)
  - Proposal: containercli: an unexported cp builder with Client.CopyTo and Client.CopyFrom, name checked with ValidName; the kube twin belongs to kube.Client.
- Registry login with the password on stdin: `<engine> login --username U --password-stdin [registry]`.
  - A: none
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:465-474
  - Proposal: containercli: Client.Login(ctx, registry, user string, password []byte), the password only on Cmd.Stdin, the error never carrying it.
- A check that the compose command itself works (`<compose> version`), separate from the engine preflight. B also probes `<engine> version` beside `info`.
  - A: none (A only runs `info`, internal/runner/runner.go:391, and appends "compose" to the engine argv at :179)
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:329 (engine) and :337 (compose)
  - Proposal: containercli: Client.ComposeVersion (or Info checking the compose tokens too), with a hint of the same kind as InfoError's. The engine `version` probe duplicates Info and can go.
- Split a listing into names safe to hand back to the engine and rows skipped, with the skip said aloud. Both tools write the same loop over ByImage's rows with ValidName.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/instances.go:255-261
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:1283-1290
  - Proposal: containercli: one pure function, for example SafeNames(rows []Row) (names []string, skipped []Row); each tool keeps its own wording of the note.
- An error-returning container-name check that words the rule, as gdu 4 asks of builders. Each tool words the grammar itself, and B checks it with a private regexp copy.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/instances.go:260 (rule spelled in the note); cmd/solmq-conn-util/lifecycle.go:24 and :65 (private DNS-label rule for lifecycle names, already decided to go)
  - B: internal/config/validate.go:404 (regexp copy) and :1613-1616 (message)
  - Proposal: containercli: CheckName(name string) error beside ValidName, used by the builders and by both tools at their boundary, so the rule text exists once.
- Engine text made safe for a terminal in more than one field: whole command output line by line with the line breaks kept, and all three fields of a listing row.
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:49-55 (safeOut, used on every engine error text)
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:1232-1234 (printableRow)
  - Proposal: Wherever Printable lands (see placement): a multi-line form that keeps newlines. Client errors that embed engine output should apply it themselves so a tool cannot forget.
- The engine's display name. Engine.title is unexported, so B keeps its own copy for its messages.
  - A: none
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:1528-1533 (platformTitle)
  - Proposal: containercli: export it as Engine.Title().
- "Is this container running" as one read: `ps --filter status=running` matched by exact name, with unanswered told apart from not running. Both tools chain List, ParseList and HasName for it, and gdu 9 adds the same read after a remove.
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:206-212
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:984-993
  - Proposal: containercli: optional Client.Running(ctx, name) (bool, Result, error), one command; an unanswered listing is the typed error. The decision taken on the answer stays in the tool.
- Image commands A builds by hand: `image inspect` (two shapes) and `image pull`. Generic, but design note section 4 names image inspect as a command the tool keeps building.
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:335, :356 and :934
  - B: none
  - Proposal: No change unless the owner reverses section 4; under rule 5 they would be containercli.Client.ImageInspect and ImagePull. Listed so the exception is a recorded choice.
- A one-shot resource sample: `stats --no-stream --format <template> <names...>`, with a template chosen because the two engines' JSON differs.
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:948-957
  - B: none
  - Proposal: containercli: Client.Stats plus a row parser, if a status report is meant to be a common operation; otherwise it stays A's, like image inspect.

#### containercli: placement

- inspect.Containers and inspect.Decode: Client.Inspect is planned on containercli.Client, and an unexported name in package inspect cannot be called from package containercli. They move into containercli (or to internal/), or they stay exported.
- inspect.Container, State, Health, Mount: follow the builder and decoder into containercli if the packages merge; otherwise containercli imports inspect for the types alone.
- inspect.Printable: not an inspect concern. B applies it to containercli.Row fields (manager.go:1233) and A to whole command output (runner.go:52). It belongs beside the output hook, in runner (next to Quote, which already escapes unprintable tokens) or in containercli.
- ComposeDigest: gdu 10 plans "a digest function beside ComposeDigest for a quadlet unit". A podman-unit digest does not belong in containercli, so either both digests share one home (containerart, next to the renderers that write the label) or the quadlet one goes to containerart/quadlet and this one stays.
- NoPrevious: correctly here; it is the engine half of kube.LogOptions.Previous.

#### containercli: signatures

- Error results differ: Exec and Upload return (runner.Cmd, error); Info, ComposeUp, ComposeDown, Start, Stop, Restart, List and Logs return runner.Cmd alone. gdu 4 gives every name-taking builder an error, and every Client method returns (Result, error).
- Upload returns a non-zero Cmd beside an error: containercli.go:268-270 sets c.Stdin after Exec and returns c, err. Unreachable today because Upload checks the name first, but once Exec checks ValidName (gdu 4) a refused name returns a Cmd holding the body. kube.Upload returns runner.Cmd{} on error.
- Upload's shape differs from kube.Upload: (base, name, dest, body, mkdir) here, (base, pod, o ExecOptions, dest, body, mkdir) in kube; the empty-argument messages differ too. The two Client.Upload methods should read alike.
- Positional bools: ComposeUp(..., recreate), List(base, running), Upload(..., mkdir), InfoError(..., rootless, ...). ComposeUp and ComposeDown gain the project for -p (gdu 9): take an options struct (Project, ProjectDir, Env, Recreate) instead of a sixth positional.
- What the Client must hold: section 3.1 shows Runner, Base and Output. This package also needs the compose tokens (B resolves them separately, manager.go:179; A appends "compose", runner.go:179), the Engine, and whether podman is rootless (for the Info hint), all passed per call today.
- InfoError's out argument: A passes its captured, cleaned output (runner.go:398), B passes "" because the output already reached the terminal (preflight.go:47). With the hook delivering every byte, the Info error should not embed output, or A prints it twice.
- The hook on reads: Client.List and Client.Inspect would hand stdout to the tool's hook. B captures these silently today (manager.go:988, inspect.go:67) and its hook writes to the terminal at once; the raw inspect JSON also carries the Config.Env that Decode never reads on purpose (inspect.go:6-7). The hook needs to know the operation, or reads must not deliver stdout.
- Client.Exec run modes (owner call under Exec): streamed, captured stdout returned to the caller, stdin bytes, and attached terminal are all in use; ExecOptions has only Stdin and TTY.
- LogOptions.Tail is a string passed as given (containercli.go:273-278). gdu 14 decided count-only and left open whether Logs refuses a bad value: settle the type and the check here and in kube.LogOptions together.
- Parsers take string while B's capture returns []byte, converted at each site (manager.go:992, :1226). Moot once the clients return parsed values.
- Count method for countA and countB: references on code lines in .go files, tests included; lines that are only a comment are not counted. No file in either tool imports these packages under an alias.

### containercli/inspect

- **Containers** (func, operation-builder; A1 B3): unexport-covered-by-client, owner call; by:containercli.Client.Inspect (decoded). Client.Inspect covers the three decode uses. Two points need the owner: it must move into containercli to be unexported and still reachable by the client, and B's Describe prints the raw answer (manager.go:1379-1382), which only works through the client if the hook receives the raw JSON.
- **Container** (type, type; A2 B10): move-package, owner call. It is what Client.Inspect returns, so it stays exported; it moves to containercli if the builder and decoder do. The alternative is to leave the types here and have containercli import inspect.
  - In M: same package: Decode (inspect.go:128, :140); named in a comment at containercli/containercli.go:126
- **State** (type, type; A0 B0): move-package, owner call. Goes wherever Container goes. Restarting and Paused are decoded and read by neither tool.
  - In M: same package: field of Container (inspect.go:50), built in Decode (:149)
  - Reached: Field type of Container.State: A reads ec.State.Error (internal/statusreport/parse.go:601), B reads s.State.Status and s.State.Running (internal/container/inspect.go:121, :135).
- **Health** (type, type; A0 B0): move-package, owner call. Goes wherever Container goes. It doubles as the JSON wire shape (inspect.go:101), so a field rename changes the decode.
  - In M: same package: State.Health (inspect.go:77), the wire shape (:101), health (:180)
  - Reached: Field type of State.Health: A reads ec.State.Health.Status (internal/statusreport/parse.go:603), B reads Status and FailingStreak (internal/container/inspect.go:45-50).
- **Mount** (type, type; A0 B0): move-package, owner call. Goes wherever Container goes. It also doubles as the JSON wire shape (inspect.go:114).
  - In M: same package: Container.Mounts (inspect.go:59), the wire shape (:114)
  - Reached: Element type of Container.Mounts: A ranges over ec.Mounts (internal/statusreport/parse.go:631-640), B over s.Mounts (internal/container/inspect.go:167-172).
- **Decode** (func, parser; A2 B3): unexport-covered-by-client, owner call; by:containercli.Client.Inspect (decoded). Client.Inspect returns the decoded containers, so the run-then-decode sites go. Two uses decode a document with no runner in reach: A's statusreport.ParseInspect (parse.go:552-553, six calls in parse_test.go) and B's test helper decodeOne (inspect_test.go:55-57); they change to take []Container, or this pure parser stays exported.
- **Printable** (func, pure-helper; A12 B11): move-package, owner call. Stays exported: no client covers it and both tools call it on every engine string they print. It is used well beyond inspect answers (listing rows in B, whole command output in A), so it belongs in runner beside Quote and the output hook, or in containercli.

#### containercli/inspect: missing

- "No such container" as an answer. Decode returns an empty slice for `[]`, and each tool makes its own judgement of it.
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:217-226 (loops for a name match, reads no match as digest unknown)
  - B: internal/container/inspect.go:75-77 (own error); internal/container/manager.go:936-938 (reads it as changed)
  - Proposal: containercli: Client.Inspect returns the typed not-found error of design 3.3 rule 2 when a named container is not in the answer, and the tool decides what that means.
- The ID of each secret a podman container was created with. Decided in gdu 10 and not built; Container.Secrets holds names only.
  - A: internal/statusreport/parse.go:645-646 (reads the names)
  - B: none yet
  - Proposal: containercli (or inspect): Container.Secrets becomes []Secret{Name, ID}, decoded from Config.Secrets; the compare with the store's IDs is the pure function gdu 10 names, in podman.

#### containercli/inspect: placement

- Containers and Decode sit in a package the planned containercli.Client cannot reach once they are unexported; move them into containercli (design 3.1: the method sits beside the builder it runs) or into internal/.
- Container, State, Health, Mount: with the builder and decoder gone, this package would hold four types and Printable. Fold it into containercli, or keep it as the types-only package containercli imports.
- Printable: move to runner (or containercli). It is terminal hygiene for any command's text, built on the same character classes as execguard.IsControl and execguard.IsInvisible.

#### containercli/inspect: signatures

- Containers returns (c, ok bool) for an empty name list, where Exec and Upload return (runner.Cmd, error); B discards ok at all three sites (inspect.go:66, manager.go:931, :1380) and A turns it into its own error (runner.go:916-919). Under gdu 4 it returns an error and checks each name with ValidName.
- Containers builds its argv by hand (inspect.go:30-33) instead of through internal/build.Cmd, unlike every containercli builder.
- Container.Secrets changes from []string to a name-and-ID shape (gdu 10); A's parse.go:645-646 follows in the same tag.
- Health and Mount are both the public type and the JSON wire shape (inspect.go:101, :114). If the public types move or gain fields, give the wire struct its own private shapes.
- State.Restarting and State.Paused are read by neither tool; decide whether the breaking tag keeps them.
- Decode takes a string while B's capture is []byte (converted at inspect.go:71, manager.go:936). Moot if Decode is unexported behind Client.Inspect.
- Error prefixes are the package name ("inspect: ...", inspect.go:131, :136); they change to "containercli: ..." if the package is folded in, which both tools' message tests would see.
- Count method for countA and countB: references on code lines in .go files, tests included, in files that import this package; lines that are only a comment are not counted. No alias is used for it in either tool.

### Verifiers for the containercli group

Usage re-count: 32 names counted, 0 corrections.


Rule challenge: 32 names examined, 7 challenges.

- **[medium] containercli/inspect.Decode**: unexport-covered-by-client -> keep
  - Rule 3 (design note section 7 point 6, lines 209-211) hides builders. Section 1 lists parsers as "Pure, as today" (lines 16-17) and section 4 keeps "builders, parsers and one pure compare function, which each tool runs" for gdu 16/18 (line 167). A pure parser is not a second way to run an operation, which is what the rule removes. Unlike ParseList, which reads M's private ps template (containercli/containercli.go:157) and has no use apart from List, Decode reads the engines' own JSON and is needed where no runner is in reach: A's statusreport.ParseInspect(doc, now, match) (internal/statusreport/parse.go:552-553) sits in a runner-free package whose six tests feed engine fixtures (parse_test.go:496, 592, 615, 622, 625, 678), and B's decodeOne (internal/container/inspect_test.go:55-57) carries its health-spelling and partial-answer tests. Unexporting makes both hand-build Container literals, which drops the engine-shaped fixtures, or stand up a Client and a fake only to decode. Nothing is gained in safety: Decode is where "the environment is never decoded" is enforced (containercli/inspect/inspect.go:6-7), and Client.Inspect calls it either way. A third tool's report tests need the same path from a fixture to a Container.
- **[medium] containercli/inspect.Containers**: unexport-covered-by-client -> keep as a named rule-3 exception (moved beside Client.Inspect), unless section 3.4 gains a raw inspect method; only then unexport
  - The catalogue holds "Inspect (decoded)" only (design note line 125). B's Describe (internal/container/manager.go:1372-1383, builder at :1380) runs the same command to print the raw answer and decodes nothing; that step stays in the tool. The reviewer's way to cover it, the hook receiving the raw JSON, is what this package forbids: the raw answer carries Config.Env (containercli/inspect/inspect.go:6-7; M's TestDecodeNeverReadsEnv, inspect_test.go:178-193; B's TestReportStateNeverPrintsTheEnvironment, internal/container/inspect_test.go:274), and under design 3.2 (lines 91-99) it would reach B's terminal on every status and deploy and A's end-of-run buffer. Routing Describe through the decoded Inspect also makes describe fail on a decode error ("not a list of containers", inspect.go:136), although describe is the verb used when the answer looks odd. B building `container inspect` by hand instead would drop the name check gdu 4 puts in the builder. So the default should be: add Client.Describe (raw, streamed, nothing decoded) to 3.4 and unexport, or keep the builder exported. Unexporting with neither leaves B without the command.
- **[medium] containercli/inspect.Container**: move-package -> keep (stays in package inspect; only the builder moves into containercli)
  - Design 3.1 asks only that the method sit beside the builder it runs (line 74). If Decode stays exported, package inspect becomes the pure decode package: four types and Decode, with no runner import (inspect.go:18 imports runner only for Containers), and containercli imports it one way, so no cycle. A's statusreport (parse.go:12, :567, :629) and B's inspect.go keep their import; A names inspect.Container twice and B ten times, and none of those lines changes. Folded into containercli the names lose their qualifier and the builder Containers would sit beside the type Container, so the move forces renames as well. M already keeps kube/rbac and kube/nsgate as packages beside kube.Client (3.4, lines 129-130), so a sub-package beside a client is the house shape. The "inspect: " error prefix (inspect.go:131, :136) then stays true and no message test in either tool moves.
- **[medium] containercli/inspect.State**: move-package -> keep (stays in package inspect with Container)
  - Follows Container. In containercli the bare name State says nothing (state of the engine, the client, the container?) and would need a rename; M already has systemd.State as a function and kube.NamespaceState as a type, so a third unqualified State adds confusion. inspect.State reads correctly and no tool line changes (A parse.go:601, B inspect.go:121, :135 reach it as a field).
- **[medium] containercli/inspect.Health**: move-package -> keep (stays in package inspect with Container)
  - Follows Container. containercli.Health would read as the engine's health, not a healthcheck verdict, so the move needs a rename. It is also the JSON wire shape (inspect.go:101); staying put keeps the decoder and its wire type in one package.
- **[low] containercli/inspect.Mount**: move-package -> keep (stays in package inspect with Container)
  - Follows Container. It is the JSON wire shape (inspect.go:114) and is reached only as Container.Mounts (A parse.go:631-640, B inspect.go:167-172). Rendering moves into M (rule 4), so a compose or quadlet model will have its own mount type; a containercli.Mount beside those would be ambiguous, inspect.Mount is not.
- **[medium] containercli/inspect.Printable**: move-package (runner or containercli) -> move-package to runner only, under a name that does not collide with runner's unexported printable
  - The containercli alternative is inconsistent with the other packages: kubectl text has the same exposure and neither tool filters it today (A copies kubelet Reason strings unfiltered at internal/statusreport/parse.go:252, :263, :270), so the helper must be reachable from kube and its client too. runner is the one package containercli, kube, podman and systemd all import, it is where the output hook's Stream type lives (design 3.2, line 92), and it already imports execguard (runner/echo.go:13), which owns the character classes. Two things the reviewer's note misses: runner already has an unexported printable(s string) bool (runner/echo.go:121) with different semantics (it keeps tab and newline and tests IsPrint), so one of the two must be renamed; and Printable is not built on execguard's classes exactly: it uses unicode.IsControl (inspect.go:197), which drops NEL, tab and newline, while execguard.IsControl leaves NEL out (execguard/execguard.go:194-196). Settle one definition when it moves.

Additions:

- (missing) Raw describe: `container inspect <name>` printed unparsed. B manager.go:1372-1383. Not in 3.4, and Client.Inspect (decoded) does not cover it without sending Config.Env through the hook. Proposal: containercli.Client.Describe(ctx, names...) that streams to the hook and decodes nothing, or the builder stays exported (see the Containers challenge).
- (missing) Selection by exact name that returns the item, and the image filter over decoded containers. HasName returns only a bool and ByImage takes only []Row, so each tool loops by hand: B Status over rows (manager.go:1338-1342), A DockerDeployDigest over inspect results (internal/runner/runner.go:221-225), B deployedDigest takes cs[0] without a name match (manager.go:936-940), and A filters inspect results by image substring itself (parse.go:559). Proposal: pure selectors for []Row and []inspect.Container (find by exact name, filter by image).
- (missing) The `--since` check. Both tools parse with time.ParseDuration and pass the canonical string, with different bounds: A refuses <= 0 (cmd/solmq-conn-util/logs.go:63-72), B refuses only < 0 (internal/config/duration.go:32-45). docs/todo.md item 14 leaves "Whether Since gets the same shared check" open (lines 753-757). Decide it with Tail; LogOptions.Since as a time.Duration rendered by M removes the question for both containercli and kube.
- (missing) Typed errors for this client. containercli has none today (fmt.Errorf and errors.New only: containercli.go:59, :242-246, :265, :302), while design 3.3 rule 2 asks for refused, not found and unanswered. Needed: engine unreachable (Info, carrying the hint), unanswered (List), not found (Inspect). Not found for Start, Stop and Restart needs matching engine text that differs between docker and podman; decide whether the client returns it at all.
- (missing) Correction to the reviewer's "no such container" item: the case does not arrive as exit 0 with `[]`. Both tools return the run error before Decode sees anything (A runner.go:920-923, B internal/container/inspect.go:67-70; B deployedDigest manager.go:932-935), so the len(cs)==0 branches (B inspect.go:75-77, manager.go:937) only see a fake. Client.Inspect must classify a failed run whose stdout holds fewer containers than were asked for, and with several names in one call (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/status.go:616) decide whether it returns the found ones beside a typed error naming the missing ones; today one missing name fails A's whole status. The engines' exit code and stdout for a missing name need a row in docs/smoke.md.
- (missing) A labelled container listing. 3.4 gives kube.Client.ListLabelled and podman.Client.SecretLabels, and gdu 1 puts the labels on the compose service and the quadlet unit, but List's template carries no label (containercli.go:157) and there is no `ps --filter label=k=v`. Both tools still discover their containers by image substring (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/instances.go:255, B manager.go:1251). Not decided anywhere; record it as a choice (Row gains Labels, or a label filter on Client.List).
- (placement) A third option for inspect that the reviewer's two do not list: package inspect stays as the pure decode package (Container, State, Health, Mount, Decode; no runner import) and only the builder moves into containercli, unexported, beside Client.Inspect. Dependency runs containercli -> inspect only. A variant that mirrors rbac.Run and nsgate.Read (3.4 lines 129-130) is inspect.Read(ctx, client, names...), but that takes Inspect off containercli.Client and changes the catalogue.
- (placement) Result, runner.Stream and the typed-error sentinels are shared by the containercli, podman, systemd and kube clients. They belong in runner, the only package all four import, so a tool can errors.Is one not-found or unanswered value across clients instead of one per package (design 3.6 shows kube.ErrForbidden only).
- (placement) The quadlet digest of gdu 10 needs no exported twin "beside ComposeDigest". With rendering in M (rule 4) the unit renderer itself renders without the label line, hashes and renders again; the tool passes its label key and reads the digest from the render result. That settles the reviewer's open placement: the quadlet digest lives in containerart/quadlet, and ComposeDigest stays in containercli, because its second input is the compose child's environment, a command-time value.
- (placement) Name grammars: the reviewer's CheckName(name) error for containercli should be named and shaped together with the grammars gdu 4 adds in kube (label, subdomain) and with envref.ValidName, so M does not end with ValidName in two packages, ValidLabel in a third and Check* forms that differ per package.
- (signature) A stream-marked hook cannot keep A's merged output order. A runs compose, the lifecycle verbs, the preflight, the upload and the status script through runner.Combined (A internal/runner/runner.go:189, :300, :395, :1182, :1207), which hands one writer to both streams so they merge in arrival order (M runner/runner.go:123-128, :279-285). A hook that marks each chunk stdout or stderr (design 3.2) needs two writers, so the order between the streams is no longer the child's. Say so in 3.2, or let a client be told to merge.
- (signature) One hook per client against per-call capture. A's RunStatusScript parses the merged output of one exec for a marker line (runner.go:1207-1212) and B's transport returns one exec's stdout to its caller (internal/container/transport.go:85-99). Either Client.Exec returns the captured bytes, or Client is documented as a plain value a tool may copy with another Output hook for one call. The same applies to Logs: A's run-wide hook buffers, but its logs verb must write through at once (cmd/solmq-conn-util/logs.go:304).
- (signature) Attach is not the same as TTY. A attaches the three files with TTY off for a one-shot command, with -i only when stdin is piped (cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:244-256, :271). Client.Exec's attached mode must be its own switch, not derived from ExecOptions.TTY; this is a fifth combination beside the reviewer's four run modes.
- (signature) The hook on reads also covers Info: both tools discard `info` stdout on success (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:628-632, B internal/container/preflight.go:42-47). Evidence the reviewer's note can cite: design 3.2 says "the tool still sees every byte" (lines 98-99), while inspect.go:6-7, M's TestDecodeNeverReadsEnv (inspect_test.go:178-193) and B's TestReportStateNeverPrintsTheEnvironment (internal/container/inspect_test.go:274) all pin that the container environment never reaches output. A is exposed too: it reads the listing and the inspect silently (runner.go:920, :974) and prints its buffer at the end.
- (signature) Engine has no validity check. Any string converts (A logs.go:211, runner.go:398, :1133; B preflight.go:47, manager.go:1399), title() falls through to the raw word (containercli.go:44) and EngineHint to the generic hint (:85). As a Client field set once per run, a value other than Docker or Podman should be refused at setup or first use, not surface as a vaguer hint.
- (signature) Client.Base is a bare []string, so nothing shows it passed execguard. B re-runs the guard on every call as its second enforcement point (manager.go:138-143, transport.go:50-56) and tests that every path refuses an unapproved runtime (internal/container/runtime_test.go:54-80). With a client set up once, decide where that check lives: the client checks Base itself, takes a type only execguard can produce, or B builds the client from runtime() per operation.
- (signature) NoPrevious hard-codes `--previous` (containercli.go:302) while each tool spells the flag itself (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/commands.go:120 and logs.go:118, B internal/cli/commands.go:271). M has two precedents and this follows neither: a constant both tools take their flag from (execguard.ApproveFlag, execguard/execguard.go:69, CR-0001) or an option with a default (confirm.DefaultNoPromptFlag, confirm/prompter.go:12, :36). Also give containercli.LogOptions a Previous field like kube.LogOptions (kube/kube.go:230-234) that Client.Logs refuses with this error, so the second guard each tool keeps (A runner.go:1129-1134, B manager.go:1397-1400) exists once and B's separate `previous bool` argument goes.
- (signature) The catalogue marks only Inspect as "(decoded)" (design note line 125). ParseList's unexport rests on Client.List returning []Row; state List's return type in 3.4, with the running filter in an options struct.
- (signature) Twins in other packages that should change in the same way: inspect.Containers' (Cmd, ok bool) result is also podman.RemoveSecrets' (podman/podman.go:107); InfoError's shape (an error constructor with a next step) is also kube.MissingNamespace's (kube/kube.go:102); and the owner call on Exec must be taken once for containercli.Exec and kube.Exec, since A's ExecArgv, LogsArgv and InstallScript (runner.go:1057, :1119, :1165) and B's two Transport implementations switch between the pair. If the two clients' Exec, Logs and Upload methods share one shape, a tool can hold them behind one interface and drop the switch.
- (signature) If the owner does fold inspect into containercli, State, Health and Mount need new names, the builder Containers must not sit beside the type Container, and the builder's name must not collide with the Client.Inspect method's unexported builder.
- (signature) Both tools repeat `digest := ComposeDigest(file, env)` then `env = append(env, VAR+"="+digest)` (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:782-786, B manager.go:891-892). Once the compose model in M knows the digest variable (rule 4), one helper can return the digest and the env entry together so the variable name cannot drift between the rendered file and the child's environment.

### containerart/compose

- **Escape** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Both tool calls sit on lines the compose File/Service renderer of design 8.2 will write itself (A's inline config content, B's image line), so afterwards only Quote and the renderer call it. It must stay exported only if the model's extra-settings slot takes raw text from the tool instead of typed values.
  - In M: compose.Quote (containerart/compose/compose.go:44); containerart/compose/compose_test.go:34
- **Quote** (func, pure-helper; A18 B9): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Design 8.2 gives the quoting rule to the module's writer and every one of the 27 tool calls is a scalar the File/Service renderer writes; the plain YAML half (strconv.Quote, no '$' doubling) should be exported from the new writer package instead, since both tools hand-roll it for Kubernetes YAML (see missing). B's cpuset line (render.go:1113) is an extra setting, so this holds only if the extras slot is typed.
  - In M: compose.FlowList (compose.go:52), compose.ServiceSecrets (compose.go:99), compose.TopSecrets (compose.go:114); containerart/compose/compose_test.go:20
- **FlowList** (func, renderer-piece; A4 B1): unexport-internal-only. Its only uses are the healthcheck test:, entrypoint: and command: lines, all fields of the Service model that the module writes after 8.2. A's test use (dockergen_test.go:416) rebuilds an expected line and falls to a golden.
  - In M: containerart/compose/compose_test.go:99 only
- **Posture** (const, constant; A1 B1): keep, owner call. The Service renderer writes the block after 8.2 so no tool needs it, but artifacttest reads it from another package and 8.4 keeps artifacttest, so it cannot be unexported without moving it to an internal/ package. Owner chooses between keeping it as the published statement of the posture and hiding it so no tool can hand-write a service.
  - In M: artifacttest.CheckComposePosture (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:104, :108); containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:26; containerart/compose/compose_test.go:107
- **Secret** (type, type; A4 B2): keep. It is already the value a tool supplies (name, absolute target, env var) and becomes the secret entry of the Service model. CR-0004's checks on the three fields move into the renderer's error.
  - In M: parameter of ServiceSecrets (compose.go:92) and TopSecrets (compose.go:107); containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:16
- **ServiceSecrets** (func, renderer-piece; A1 B1): unexport-internal-only. Secret mounts are named in the decided design as something the module writes, so the File/Service renderer emits this block from Service.Secrets. The error return and quoted target decided in CR-0004/gdu 4 then land on the renderer, not on a public emitter.
  - In M: containerart/compose/compose_test.go:131; containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:29
- **TopSecrets** (func, renderer-piece; A1 B1): unexport-internal-only. The top-level secrets: map is derived from the same Service.Secrets list, so the File renderer writes it and no tool step needs it alone. Same CR-0004 consequence as ServiceSecrets.
  - In M: containerart/compose/compose_test.go:147; containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:40

#### containerart/compose: missing

- The compose File/Service model and its renderer (decided, design 8.2 layer 3): name, image, container_name, labels, restart, user, posture, ports, environment, secrets, healthcheck, volumes. A also needs entrypoint/command, service-level configs with targets and tmpfs; B needs hostname and the extras (cpuset, mem_limit, shm_size, ulimits).
  - A: internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:97 (Render), :150 (renderService)
  - B: internal/render/render.go:1043 (Compose)
  - Proposal: containerart/compose: File and Service types with Render returning bytes and an error; a typed extras slot (key plus scalar, list or map, each marked quoted or bare) so the module still does the quoting.
- Line writer with indentation, raw blocks and block-scalar line splitting (design 8.2 layer 1).
  - A: internal/yamlwriter/yamlwriter.go:14 (Writer), :35 (SplitLines)
  - B: internal/render/render.go:1048 (strings.Builder plus fmt.Fprintf throughout Compose)
  - Proposal: A new writer package (top level, since kube documents use it too), holding Line, Raw and the block-scalar helper; compose, quadlet and the kube documents render through it.
- Plain YAML double-quoted scalar without '$' doubling, hand-applied in both tools for Kubernetes YAML. compose.Quote cannot serve there because it doubles '$'.
  - A: internal/deploy/deploy.go:385 (strconv.Quote)
  - B: internal/render/render.go:87 (%q)
  - Proposal: The writer package exports the plain quote (the rule compose.Quote already holds); compose keeps an unexported wrapper that adds Escape. A tool writing a Custom resource body uses the writer's quote.
- healthcheck: block (test as an exec-form flow list, interval, timeout, retries, start_period).
  - A: internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:287-293
  - B: internal/render/render.go:1127-1136
  - Proposal: containerart/compose: a Healthcheck value on Service, shared in shape with the quadlet Unit's health fields so one tool value feeds both sinks.
- The deploy-digest label left for compose to interpolate from the environment, written as ${VAR:-} and deliberately not escaped. A writes it bare, B quoted.
  - A: internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:182 (and :178 for the stores digest)
  - B: internal/render/render.go:1175
  - Proposal: containerart/compose: a label value kind 'interpolated from VAR' on Service.Labels, the counterpart of containercli.ComposeDigest (containercli/containercli.go:129); the caller still passes its own key and variable name.
- Top-level configs: entries with inline content: | where every line is '$'-escaped and blank lines stay empty. One tool only, but a plain compose feature the File model needs or A cannot render through the module.
  - A: internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:306-315 (renderContentConfig), :236-247 (service-level configs)
  - B: none (B inlines no file)
  - Proposal: containerart/compose: File.Configs with Name, Content and the service-side Target; the renderer escapes each line.
- Compose project name: the grammar and the name: line. A validates with a DNS label and omits the line when empty; B folds a container name into compose's grammar and always writes it.
  - A: internal/validate/validate.go:1814 (kube.ValidLabel), internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:102-103
  - B: internal/render/render.go:1030 (ComposeProject), :1063
  - Proposal: containerart/compose: one project-name grammar (ValidProject) used by File.Render and by containercli.Client's -p (gdu 9); whether a fold helper like B's is offered is an owner call.
- The network line and the accepted network words per engine (gdu 6, decided: the lists live in this module beside the compose and quadlet packages). Today only B writes a mode, and only bridge or host.
  - A: none: renderService (internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:150-270) writes no network key
  - B: internal/render/render.go:1141-1142; internal/config/validate.go:1705-1707
  - Proposal: containerart/compose: docker's words (bridge, host, none), a predicate for the tool's load check, and the renderer states the project network with driver: bridge when no word is given.
- Sink grammars for values written bare into the artifact: image reference, publish port entry, health duration, restart policy. Each tool holds its own at load.
  - A: internal/validate/validate.go:1521 (checkImage), :1637 (checkPorts), :1680 (checkRestart)
  - B: internal/config/artifactvalues.go:86 (validateImage), :132 (validContainerPort), :187 (validHealthDuration); restart is the constant at internal/render/render.go:1076
  - Proposal: containerart (a small shared package beside compose and quadlet, or compose with quadlet reusing it): predicates in the style of kube.ValidLabel that the tools call at load and the renderers call again before writing; the tool keeps its field names and error wording.

#### containerart/compose: placement

- compose.Quote: its YAML-scalar half belongs in the writer package of design 8.2 layer 1, shared with the kube documents; only the '$' doubling is compose's own.
- compose.Posture and compose.ServiceSecrets carry the service indent (4 spaces) in their bytes; indentation belongs to the writer once the File model exists.
- compose.Secret: quadlet needs the same name-and-target pair for its Unit model; a shared mount type in a containerart parent package (compose adding EnvVar) is the alternative to two parallel types. Owner call.

#### containerart/compose: signatures

- Counts are code references only; comment mentions are left out (A: compose.Quote at dockergen.go:95, compose.Escape at dockergen.go:302; B: compose.Quote at render.go:1130, config/hostpath.go:77 and config/hostpath_test.go:127, compose.Posture at render.go:1078).
- Treating a piece the File/Service renderer writes as no longer exported is the rendering analogue of rule 3; the design states it for builders and clients only, so the owner should confirm it once for Quote, Escape, FlowList, ServiceSecrets and TopSecrets.
- ServiceSecrets and TopSecrets return a string and no error, while CR-0004 and gdu 4 decided they check name, target and env var and return an error.
- Name goes through Quote (compose.go:99, :114) but Target and EnvVar are written bare (compose.go:100, :115); CR-0004 decided the target is quoted.
- Three output conventions in one package: blocks at indent 4 ending in a newline (Posture, ServiceSecrets), a block at indent 0 (TopSecrets), and scalars with no newline (Quote, FlowList).
- The tools disagree on lines the renderer must settle: image: bare in A (dockergen.go:153) and through Escape in B (render.go:1072); label values bare in A (dockergen.go:170, :182) and quoted in B (render.go:1175); name: only when set in A (dockergen.go:102) and always in B (render.go:1063).
- Quote's doc leaves invalid UTF-8 to each tool's load check (compose.go:38-43); a renderer that returns an error can refuse it itself.
- compose.Secret is a type while quadlet.Secret is a function: one name, two kinds, across sibling packages.

### containerart/quadlet

- **EscapePercent** (func, pure-helper; A0 B4): unexport-internal-only. todo.md item 4 already calls it the stopgap until the line builder exists, and B's four calls are Health* lines the Unit renderer writes. The decided line builder doubles '$' as well as '%', so this percent-only form should not outlive it as public API.
  - In M: quadlet.Secret (containerart/quadlet/quadlet.go:79); containerart/quadlet/quadlet_test.go:56
- **Escape** (func, pure-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only. No tool names it; it is the escaping inside Environment and, after 8.2, inside the Unit renderer's quoted values.
  - In M: quadlet.Environment (containerart/quadlet/quadlet.go:68); containerart/quadlet/quadlet_test.go:40
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through quadlet.Environment (A internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:169, B internal/render/render.go:987).
- **Environment** (func, renderer-piece; A6 B1): unexport-internal-only. Environment is one of the values the tool supplies in 8.2, and the Unit renderer writes one line per pair. No step that stays in a tool needs a single line.
  - In M: containerart/quadlet/quadlet_test.go:24; containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:58
- **Secret** (func, renderer-piece; A1 B1): unexport-internal-only. Secret mounts at absolute targets are written by the module in the decided design, so the Unit renderer emits these lines and carries CR-0004's checks (comma in the name, relative target) in its error. Unexporting also frees the name for the Unit model's secret type.
  - In M: containerart/quadlet/quadlet_test.go:108; containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:59, :84, :318
- **Posture** (const, constant; A1 B1): keep, owner call. Same position as compose.Posture: the Unit renderer writes it, but artifacttest reads it across packages, so unexporting needs an internal/ home. Owner decides whether the posture stays a published constant.
  - In M: artifacttest.CheckQuadletPosture (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:124); containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:55, :80; containerart/quadlet/quadlet_test.go:115

#### containerart/quadlet: missing

- The quadlet Unit model and its renderer (decided, design 8.2 layer 3): [Unit], [Container] with image, name, labels, user and group, posture, ports, environment, secrets, volumes, health keys, then [Service] and [Install]. B needs HostName and the extras (PodmanArgs, ShmSize, Ulimit, [Service] Limit* lines).
  - A: internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:135 (RenderQuadlet)
  - B: internal/render/render.go:888 (Quadlet)
  - Proposal: containerart/quadlet: Unit type with Render returning the filename, the bytes and an error; extras as key and value pairs per section so the module escapes them.
- The line builder of gdu 4 for Image=, ContainerName=, HostName=, Label=, PublishPort= and Volume=, doubling '%' and '$' and refusing ':' in a Volume source. Both tools write these lines with no escaping.
  - A: internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:148-151, :166, :200
  - B: internal/render/render.go:901-903, :982, :985
  - Proposal: containerart/quadlet: one unexported line writer used by every key of the Unit renderer, replacing EscapePercent's partial coverage.
- Health* keys from an argv and timings. A joins the argv with spaces and escapes nothing; B escapes '%' only.
  - A: internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:214-219
  - B: internal/render/render.go:961-974
  - Proposal: containerart/quadlet: a health value on Unit shared in shape with compose's healthcheck; the renderer joins the argv, escapes it, and refuses a token holding a space.
