- User= and Group= from the run-as identity, including the 'leave the image's user' case. A passes ints, B splits a uid:gid string.
  - A: internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:160-163
  - B: internal/render/render.go:892, :904-907 (splitUser at :1282)
  - Proposal: containerart/quadlet and compose: one user value (uid, gid, or image default) on both models, written as user: "uid:gid" in compose and User=/Group= in the unit.
- The unit skeleton both tools repeat: [Unit] Description with Wants/After network-online.target, [Service] Restart=, [Install] WantedBy=.
  - A: internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:141-144, :223-230
  - B: internal/render/render.go:895-898, :1000-1013
  - Proposal: containerart/quadlet: fixed layout in the Unit renderer; Description, Restart and WantedBy are values, the network-online lines are the module's.
- The unit file name <name>.container, built by hand in three places.
  - A: internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:232
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:792, :1053
  - Proposal: containerart/quadlet: Unit.Filename() (or a FileName(name) function) so the write, the old-unit read and the removal agree.
- Network= line and podman's accepted words (gdu 6): private as the stated default, host only when asked.
  - A: none: RenderQuadlet (internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:147-221) writes no Network= key
  - B: internal/render/render.go:978-979; internal/config/validate.go:1705-1707
  - Proposal: containerart/quadlet: the word list (private, bridge, pasta, slirp4netns, host, none), a predicate for the load check, and the renderer always writes the line.
- Restart words per sink: compose's restart policy against systemd's Restart= vocabulary. A checks both at load; B writes the constant always on both.
  - A: internal/validate/validate.go:1680 (checkRestart)
  - B: internal/render/render.go:1010 (Restart=always), :1076 (restart: always)
  - Proposal: containerart/quadlet and compose: each package lists its sink's restart words and offers the predicate, as gdu 6 does for the network words.
- The unit deploy digest of gdu 10: render without the label line, hash, render again with it. Not built in either tool yet; decided for the breaking tag.
  - A: none yet (A writes only its two leader labels, internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:150-152)
  - B: none yet (no Label= line in internal/render/render.go:888-1015)
  - Proposal: containerart/quadlet: a method on Unit that returns the digest and renders with the caller's label key, so no tool repeats the two-pass render.

#### containerart/quadlet: placement

- The quadlet key release table (quadletKeySince, containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:186) is knowledge about quadlet keys; with the Unit renderer here it belongs in quadlet, read by artifacttest, so a new key has one place to be registered.
- artifacttest.parseRelease (artifacttest.go:208) and podman.SupportsReplace (podman/podman.go:35) each parse a podman release; one parser in podman should serve the key floor and podman.Client.Version.
- quadlet.Secret (function) blocks the name the Unit model wants for its secret type, mirroring compose.Secret.

#### containerart/quadlet: signatures

- Counts are code references only; comment mentions are left out (A: quadlet.Secret at podmangen.go:104; B: quadlet.Posture at render.go:909, quadlet.Escape at render/servercert_test.go:253).
- Secret escapes '%' only (quadlet.go:79) while Escape doubles '$' too (quadlet.go:47-48) and gdu 4 decided the line builder doubles both; a '$' in a secret name or target is written as it is today.
- No function returns an error; CR-0004 and gdu 4 decided quadlet.Secret checks name and target and returns one, and the doc already says a comma in the name is unsupported (quadlet.go:73-74) without refusing it.
- Environment escapes the key together with the value (quadlet.go:68) and checks neither; a key is an identifier and should be held to a grammar, not escaped.
- Lines carry no newline (Environment, Secret) while Posture is a block ending in one (quadlet.go:5-6); callers must remember which.
- Escape is for a double-quoted value and EscapePercent for an unquoted one, but neither name says so, and compose.Escape means a third thing ('$' doubling only).
- A never calls EscapePercent and writes HealthCmd= raw (podmangen.go:215); B escapes Health* but not Image=, ContainerName=, HostName= or Volume= (render.go:901-903, :985). The Unit renderer should leave no per-line choice.

### containerart/artifacttest

- **Widening** (func, test-helper; A1 B1): keep. Both tools pass it to AssertAbsent over every rendered shape, and design 8.4 keeps artifacttest as the check on what a tool ships. It still leaves out Kubernetes spellings (see missing).
  - In M: containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:108, :120, :168
- **HostNetwork** (func, test-helper; A1 B3): change-signature, owner call. B picks the compose and quadlet spellings by slice index (render_test.go:1276, :1278), so the order is API. Proposal: per-sink accessors (compose key, quadlet key, podman flags, and hostNetwork: true for manifests) or fold the opt-in rule into gdu 6's stated-network check so no caller indexes a list.
  - In M: containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:114, :129, :137
- **CheckAbsent** (func, test-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only, owner call. No tool calls a Check function; the package doc exports them so the rules are testable without a fake testing.T, and the module's own tests sit in the same package and reach an unexported one. Keep only if a tool should run the checks outside a test, which nothing decided asks for.
  - In M: AssertAbsent (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:356); containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:110, :168
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through AssertAbsent (A internal/gen/gen_runas_test.go:139, B internal/render/render_test.go:1262).
- **CheckComposePosture** (func, test-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Used inside the module only, by its Assert wrapper and the in-package tests. Same owner question as CheckAbsent.
  - In M: AssertComposePosture (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:363); containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:194, :205
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through AssertComposePosture (A internal/gen/gen_runas_test.go:102, B internal/render/render_test.go:1239).
- **CheckQuadletPosture** (func, test-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Used inside the module only, by its Assert wrapper and the in-package tests. Same owner question as CheckAbsent.
  - In M: AssertQuadletPosture (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:370); containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:216, :227
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through AssertQuadletPosture (A internal/gen/gen_runas_test.go:103, B internal/render/render_test.go:1242).
- **CheckQuadletKeys** (func, test-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Used inside the module only. If the release table moves to quadlet (placement), a floor check on a Unit there could replace it; until that is decided it follows the other Check functions.
  - In M: AssertQuadletKeys (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:377); containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:238, :253, :262
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through AssertQuadletKeys (A internal/gen/gen_runas_test.go:158, B internal/render/render_test.go:1664).
- **CheckSecretTargets** (func, test-helper; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Used inside the module only, by its Assert wrapper and the in-package tests. Same owner question as CheckAbsent.
  - In M: AssertSecretTargets (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:384); containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:277, :303, :314
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through AssertSecretTargets (A internal/gen/gen_runas_test.go:168, B internal/render/render_test.go:1537). compose.Secret's doc also names it (compose.go:81).
- **AssertAbsent** (func, test-helper; A3 B2): keep. Both tools use it, A also with its own Kubernetes tokens appended, and 8.4 keeps artifacttest as the check on what a tool ships.
  - In M: containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:351, :368
- **AssertComposePosture** (func, test-helper; A1 B1): keep. Both tools use it and 8.4 keeps the check even though the renderer makes the posture right by construction; the module's own renderer tests and the example tool can call it too.
  - In M: containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:352, :369
- **AssertQuadletPosture** (func, test-helper; A1 B1): keep. Both tools use it and 8.4 keeps the check; it is the positional one (the [Container] section) that a plain substring test cannot replace.
  - In M: containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:353, :371
- **AssertQuadletKeys** (func, test-helper; A1 B1): keep. Both tools hold their units to their own podman floor with it (A 4.5, B 4.7), and that floor stays a tool's choice after rendering moves. The extras slot makes it more needed, since a tool can still add a key.
  - In M: containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:354, :372
- **AssertSecretTargets** (func, test-helper; A2 B2): keep. Both tools use it on compose files and units, and 8.4 keeps artifacttest as the check on what a tool ships.
  - In M: containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:355, :370, :373

#### containerart/artifacttest: missing

- Kubernetes posture and widening tokens. The module will write the Deployment's security context (design 8.2 layer 2), but the check and the forbidden spellings exist only as local strings in A; Widening's doc excludes Kubernetes spellings.
  - A: internal/gen/gen_runas_test.go:81-82 (posture strings), :136 (allowPrivilegeEscalation: true, hostNetwork, hostPID, hostIPC)
  - B: none (B renders only the operator's custom resource; no such token in its tests)
  - Proposal: containerart/artifacttest, or a sibling test package under kube: a manifest posture check and a Kubernetes widening list. Which package is an owner call, since artifacttest sits under containerart.
- A check that every rendered object carries the ownership labels (gdu 1, todo.md item 1 scope names artifacttest for it). Decided, not built.
  - A: none yet (A's only labels are the leader labels, internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:150-152 and internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:169-173)
  - B: none yet (B writes one compose label, internal/render/render.go:1174-1175, and no quadlet label)
  - Proposal: containerart/artifacttest: AssertLabels(t, name, artifact, tool, instance) for compose, quadlet and manifests, reading the label vocabulary the module defines.
- A check that the artifact states its network mode, with host networking allowed only when asked (gdu 6 scope). B hand-rolls the opt-in half with a count over an indexed HostNetwork entry; A forbids host networking through the token list.
  - A: internal/gen/gen_runas_test.go:135
  - B: internal/render/render_test.go:1273-1288
  - Proposal: containerart/artifacttest: AssertNetwork(t, name, artifact, want) taking the expected word, replacing the index picks and the separate HostNetwork list for the common case.
- The golden diff helper of gdu 5, repeated five times across the tools and once unexported here.
  - A: internal/deploy/deploy_test.go:362, internal/gen/golden_test.go:578, internal/render/render_test.go:275 (lineDiff)
  - B: internal/examples/examples_test.go:53, internal/cli/commanddoc_test.go:52 (firstDiff)
  - Proposal: A new test-support package beside artifacttest and noticetest (todo.md item 5), not artifacttest itself; paritydoc.firstDifference (paritydoc/paritydoc.go:102) can use it.
- Listing the [Container] keys a unit carries. B re-scans each unit by hand after AssertQuadletKeys to prove its fixtures reached the keys under test.
  - A: none
  - B: internal/render/render_test.go:1665-1674
  - Proposal: containerart/quadlet beside the release table (or artifacttest): a function returning the [Container] keys of a unit, built on the existing unitLines scan.

#### containerart/artifacttest: placement

- quadletKeySince and parseRelease (artifacttest.go:186, :208) hold quadlet and podman release facts; they belong in quadlet and podman, with artifacttest only applying them.
- The package sits under containerart, but gdu 1's label check and a manifest posture check cover Kubernetes output too; either it moves up a level or a sibling is added under kube. Owner call.
- Widening and HostNetwork mix compose keys, quadlet keys and podman flag spellings in one list; with gdu 6 placing the network words beside compose and quadlet, the host spellings could be derived from those lists instead of restated here.

#### containerart/artifacttest: signatures

- Counts are code references only; comment mentions are left out (B: artifacttest.Widening at render_test.go:1250).
- CheckSecretTargets and AssertSecretTargets read either sink in one function, while the posture checks are one per sink and CheckAbsent takes any text; pick one convention when the label and network checks are added.
- The artifact parameter is named artifact in five functions and unit in the four quadlet ones; the Assert order (t, name, artifact, extra) is consistent and should be kept for new checks.
- HostNetwork's slice order is a documented contract (artifacttest.go:49-50) and Widening's is not; a list whose positions are API should be separate accessors.
- CheckQuadletKeys takes the floor as MAJOR.MINOR text and returns a bad floor through the same error as its findings (artifacttest.go:154-157); A passes the literal "4.5" (gen_runas_test.go:158) and B a constant (render_test.go:1632). A typed release shared with podman's version reading would remove the parse.
- CheckComposePosture and composeSecretItems assume one service per file at indent 4 and secret items at indent 6 (artifacttest.go:97-99, :262-266); once the module's writer fixes the layout, the doc should say the checks read the module's own layout.
- The error texts tell the reader to write compose.Posture or quadlet.Posture (artifacttest.go:107-112, :137); they need rewording to point at the File and Unit renderers if those constants stop being what a tool writes.

### Verifiers for the containerart group

Usage re-count: 24 names counted, 0 corrections.


Rule challenge: 24 names examined, 4 challenges.

- **[medium] containerart/compose.Posture**: keep -> unexport-internal-only (the constant moves to an internal/ package that compose and artifacttest both read)
  - The only reason given for keep is Go visibility: artifacttest reads it across packages (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:104, :108). The module already solves that with internal/: internal/build exists because 'it is internal, so neither tool can import it' (internal/build/build.go:1-4). After 8.2 the Service renderer writes the block, so both tool uses go (A internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:194, B internal/render/render.go:1090), and no tool test names the constant: both use AssertComposePosture (A internal/gen/gen_runas_test.go:102, B internal/render/render_test.go:1239). The reviewer applies the rendering analogue of rule 3 to Quote, Escape, FlowList, ServiceSecrets and TopSecrets; Posture is the same case and keep makes it the one exception, a public constant whose only purpose is a hand-written service, which 8.4 ('right by construction') no longer has. Two more reasons. (1) Posture is the only thing artifacttest's non-test code takes from compose (artifacttest.go:20, :104-112), so moving it removes the artifacttest -> compose import; compose's in-package tests (compose_test.go is package compose) can then call artifacttest.Assert* on File.Render output without an import cycle, which the reviewer's own reason on AssertComposePosture assumes. (2) The reviewer's placement note says the indent baked into the bytes belongs to the writer, so the exported value would change shape in the same tag anyway. TestPostureBytes (containerart/compose/compose_test.go:105-111) moves with it.
- **[medium] containerart/quadlet.Posture**: keep -> unexport-internal-only (the constant moves to an internal/ package that quadlet and artifacttest both read)
  - Same case as compose.Posture. After 8.2 the Unit renderer writes the two lines, so both tool uses go (A internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:164, B internal/render/render.go:924) and no tool test names the constant: both use AssertQuadletPosture (A internal/gen/gen_runas_test.go:103, B internal/render/render_test.go:1242). The one remaining reader is artifacttest.CheckQuadletPosture (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:124), which is a visibility problem the module already solves with internal/ (internal/build/build.go:1-4). It is the only thing artifacttest's non-test code takes from quadlet (artifacttest.go:21, :124), so an internal home also removes the artifacttest -> quadlet import and lets quadlet's in-package tests (quadlet_test.go is package quadlet) hold Unit.Render output to artifacttest.Assert* without an import cycle. Keeping it exported is inconsistent with unexporting Environment, Secret, Escape and EscapePercent on the ground that the Unit renderer writes them. TestPostureBytes (containerart/quadlet/quadlet_test.go:114-118) moves with it.
- **[medium] containerart/compose.Escape**: unexport-internal-only -> move-package (the '$' doubling goes to the shared writer of 8.2 layer 1, or an internal/ package, as an option of its quote; compose keeps no wrapper)
  - The reason says that afterwards only Quote and the compose renderer call it, and the placement note says only the '$' doubling is compose's own. That is wrong: the Kubernetes sink doubles '$' too. A's Deployment writes container env values through envValueQuote, which doubles every '$' because Kubernetes expands $(VAR) in env values and reads $$ back as one '$' (A internal/deploy/deploy.go:287-294, used at :628 and :632). After 8.2 layer 2 the module's Deployment renderer writes those lines (environment is in its container spec), from another package, so an unexported compose function cannot serve it and the kube side would re-implement the rule. todo.md item 2 also asks for the '$' rule to be stated per sink, the manifest included (docs/todo.md:185-186). So the writer needs both quotes: plain (Secret stringData, A deploy.go:385; a Custom body) and '$'-doubled (every compose scalar; Deployment env, command and args), and compose.Quote and compose.Escape both dissolve into it instead of compose keeping 'an unexported wrapper that adds Escape'. Whether tools can reach the doubled form then follows the writer, which 8.2 says is usable on its own. No tool step needs it today: B's custom resource writes no env value (B internal/render/render.go:132, :188), and A's inline config content (dockergen.go:313) is covered by the proposed File.Configs.
- **[low] containerart/artifacttest.HostNetwork**: change-signature -> remove (the opt-in rule moves into gdu 6's network check; the four podman flag spellings join Widening)
  - Per-sink accessors add names in the same tag that removes their callers. gdu 6 is decided and ships in the breaking tag (docs/todo.md:28-29): both tools take the platform's own network word, every artifact states its mode, and host is written only when the env file asks (docs/todo.md:362-365, :371-378). That gives A the opt-in rule B has, so A's 'forbid everywhere' use (A internal/gen/gen_runas_test.go:135) and B's index picks (B internal/render/render_test.go:1276, :1278, :1288) both become 'this artifact states word X', which is the AssertNetwork(t, name, artifact, want) the reviewer already lists as missing. What is left of the list is the four podman flag spellings (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:55). Neither tool ever writes them legitimately: B opts in through the key (render.go:979, :1142). They belong in Widening(), which already holds the other podman flags (artifacttest.go:35, :43-44). Today B's host shape is not checked for them at all, since that branch only counts the key (render_test.go:1281-1287). The doc sentence 'solmq-conn-util forbids host networking in every artifact' (artifacttest.go:51) is stale after gdu 6 either way. The reviewer names this as its second option; under gdu 6 it is the only one that leaves no unused API. Accessors are right only if a tool may forbid host networking without stating a mode, which gdu 6 rules out.

Additions:

- (placement) '$' doubling is not compose-only. A's Kubernetes Deployment doubles '$' in container env values (envValueQuote, A internal/deploy/deploy.go:287-294, used at :628, :632), and todo.md item 2 wants the '$' rule stated per sink, manifest included (docs/todo.md:185-186). The writer package should hold two quotes, plain and '$'-doubled; the compose placement note 'only the $ doubling is compose's own' and the missing entry 'compose keeps an unexported wrapper that adds Escape' need correcting.
- (placement) compose.FlowList's YAML half (a one-line flow sequence of quoted items) is a writer concern too: A hand-writes one for the Kubernetes init container command (A internal/deploy/deploy.go:601). If the Deployment renderer keeps that layout, the flow sequence belongs in the writer with the quote chosen per sink, not in compose.
- (placement) Nearly every field is common to the Service and the Unit: image, name, hostname, user, environment, secrets, mounts, ports, network word, health, labels, restart, resources. The reviewer shares three of them piecemeal (secret pair, healthcheck, user). One container description in a containerart parent package, consumed by compose.File and quadlet.Unit, lets a tool describe its container once; A builds two parallel inputs today (A internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:67-84, internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:82-91) and B feeds one config to two renderers (B internal/render/render.go:888, :1043). Sink-only parts stay on the sink type: EnvVar and inline configs on compose, Description and WantedBy on the unit. Owner call.
- (placement) The label vocabulary of gdu 1 (key names, value grammar, a per-sink renderer; docs/todo.md:89-90, per-sink value check at :108-110) serves compose, quadlet, podman secrets and manifests, so it cannot live under containerart. The artifacttest missing entry AssertLabels says it reads 'the label vocabulary the module defines' without placing it: a top-level package beside the writer, with Service.Labels and the Unit's Label= lines taking its output.
- (missing) Bind mount value with options, shared by both sinks. A writes ':ro' (A internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:264, :267; internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:200), B writes ':Z' (B internal/render/render.go:985, :1150). Proposal: Mount{Source, Target, ReadOnly, Relabel} on both models; the renderer writes the suffix. The ':' refusal in a source that gdu 4 decided for Volume= applies to compose's short volume syntax as well, which also splits on ':'.
- (missing) Read-only root and its tmpfs scratch. gdu 6 decided solmq-conn-util writes a read-only root into every artifact (docs/todo.md:357-358); neither tool writes one today. The Service and Unit models need the field and tmpfs entries (A has one compose tmpfs already, dockergen.go:252-257), and the release table has neither ReadOnly nor Tmpfs (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:192-198), so AssertQuadletKeys would report both as unknown keys.
- (missing) Resources as typed fields, not extras. Design 8.2 lists resources among the values the tool supplies (docs/design/2026-11-15-shared-deploy-api.md:254-256) and reserves the extras slot for settings like ulimits (:273-274). The reviewer files B's memory and cpuset caps under extras; they are compose cpuset:/mem_limit: (B internal/render/render.go:1113, :1116) and quadlet PodmanArgs=--cpuset-cpus=/--memory= (render.go:950, :953). As typed fields the module owns the per-sink spelling and the reason Memory= is avoided (podman 5.5, artifacttest.go:183-185, :198), and the Unit's extras slot need not offer PodmanArgs at all. Those two lines are the only PodmanArgs either tool writes, and PodmanArgs is the one key through which the podman flags in Widening() and HostNetwork() (artifacttest.go:43-44, :55) can enter a unit.
- (missing) A guard on the extras slot. A typed extra named privileged, cap_drop, security_opt, user, network_mode or secrets (compose), or NoNewPrivileges, DropCapability, Network or Secret (quadlet), repeats or undoes a key the model writes, and a 'bare' extra value holding a newline adds keys. Render should refuse a key the model owns and a control character in any extra. It cannot call artifacttest for this: artifacttest imports testing and both renderer packages (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:18, :20-21). So the owned-key lists live in compose and quadlet, and artifacttest reads them.
- (missing) Ports under host networking. B drops the ports on both sinks when the mode is host (B internal/render/render.go:978-984, :1141-1148). With the network word in the model the renderers need the rule (omit or refuse ports when the word is host or none), or each tool keeps the branch.
- (missing) Labels on the compose project network. gdu 1's scope labels only the service because 'neither tool renders a top-level network' (docs/todo.md:104-105). gdu 6's default now writes the project network with driver: bridge (docs/todo.md:377-378), which is an engine object. The File renderer and AssertLabels need a decision on whether that network carries the ownership labels.
- (missing) A leading comment line in the unit. A writes '# Generated by solmq-conn-util ...' above [Unit] (A internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:139) and artifacttest's unitA fixture models that shape (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:69-72); B writes none. The Unit model needs an optional header comment, or A's line is dropped on purpose.
- (signature) Escaping in a unit depends on the section. The line builder's rule (double '%' and '$') is right for [Container] values, which quadlet copies into ExecStart. Description= (A podmangen.go:142, B render.go:896, both built from an operator name and unescaped today) and WantedBy= (B render.go:1013, from config) are read by systemd itself, which expands '%' specifiers there but not '$': they need '%' doubled and '$' left alone. The reviewer's line-builder list omits both keys, and the percent-only form survives inside the renderer for [Unit], [Service] and [Install].
- (signature) The env-var name grammar already exists: envref.ValidName (envref/envref.go:69-71). The renderers should reuse it for compose.Secret.EnvVar ('must be a plain variable name', containerart/compose/compose.go:84-86), compose environment keys (written bare, B render.go:1159) and the quadlet Environment key (containerart/quadlet/quadlet.go:67-68), rather than add a new predicate to the sink grammars.
- (signature) CheckComposePosture demands the block exactly once per file (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:97-107). A File model holding services fails that for a third tool with two services; the count must become once per service.
- (signature) Test fallout of the unexports inside the module: artifacttest_test.go builds its fixtures from compose.ServiceSecrets, compose.TopSecrets, quadlet.Environment and quadlet.Secret (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest_test.go:29, :40, :58, :59, :84, :318), so they move to File.Render and Unit.Render output or to literals. The reviewer's remark that the module's own renderer tests can call AssertComposePosture holds only from an external test package while artifacttest imports compose and quadlet (artifacttest.go:20-21).
- (signature) Docs that name the names to be unexported and must change in the same step: this module's docs/developer.md:146-173 (pieces not renderers, the quoting policy, Posture, the secret emitters, CheckQuadletKeys) and docs/consuming.md:82; A's docs/DESIGN.md:48-50 and :429 and docs/test.md:664, :677, :707. B's docs name only kept names (docs/container-security.md:87, :285).
- (signature) The Check/Assert question is not artifacttest's alone. noticetest exports the same pair, Check and CheckDocs behind Assert and AssertDocs (notice/noticetest/noticetest.go:40, :117, :165, :174), and no tool calls its Check half (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/support_test.go:45-59, B internal/cli/commanddoc_test.go:98), while paritydoc.Check is called directly by both (A support_test.go:102, B commanddoc_test.go:112). The plan review listed the Check functions as reached indirectly and staying (docs/review/2026-11-13-plan-review.md:383-384). The owner's answer for the five artifacttest Check functions should be given once for noticetest too. artifacttest's tests are in-package (artifacttest_test.go:1), so unexporting costs the module nothing.
- (signature) File.Render always writing the secrets: blocks makes the Docker Compose 2.23.1 floor of the environment provider the module's to state. It lives only in comments today (containerart/compose/compose.go:72-74, A internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:121-122) and nothing checks it, unlike the quadlet key floor.

### podman

- **SystemQuadletDir** (const, constant; A0 B1): keep. No client covers it and solace-util needs the rootful default at config load, where it cannot take QuadletDir's error path. solmq-conn-util reaches it only through QuadletDir.
  - In M: podman/podman.go:142 (QuadletDir returns it for rootful)
- **Version** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:podman.Client.Version. Catalogue 3.4 lists Version (read once per run); both tools only run it to feed SupportsReplace, which the client now does itself (3.3 rule 1).
  - In M: podman/podman_test.go only
- **SupportsReplace** (func, pure-helper; A3 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:podman.Client.CreateSecret (picks replace or the fallback) and podman.Client.SecretMatches (gates the 4.7-only read). 3.3 rule 1 moves the version decision into the client: both tools use the bool only to pick create --replace and to skip the stored-value read below 4.7. Client.SecretMatches must then report below-4.7 as not matching (or a typed unsupported) so the tools keep 'every secret counts as changed'.
  - In M: podman/podman_test.go only
- **CreateSecret** (func, operation-builder; A2 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:podman.Client.CreateSecret. Named in the catalogue with labels and the replace fallback; neither tool has a step that needs the bare builder. Neither tool passes labels today, so the client signature must make them mandatory input (gdu 16, P10).
  - In M: podman/podman_test.go only
- **SecretData** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:podman.Client.SecretMatches. Catalogue: SecretMatches (the value never printed). Keeping the builder exported would let a tool route the secret value through its own output path; the client keeps it inside M.
  - In M: podman/podman_test.go only
- **SecretMatches** (func, parser; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:podman.Client.SecretMatches. It is the parser of SecretData's answer and both tools call it only straight after that read; the client method of the same name does both.
  - In M: podman/podman_test.go only
- **RemoveSecrets** (func, operation-builder; A2 B2): unexport-covered-by-client; by:podman.Client.RemoveSecrets, and the below-4.7 half of podman.Client.CreateSecret. Catalogue method; one use in each tool is the rm half of the replace fallback, which moves into Client.CreateSecret, the other is the teardown delete the tool will call through the client.
  - In M: podman/podman_test.go only
- **IDMap** (func, operation-builder; A2 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:podman.Client.IDMap. Catalogue method. solmq-conn-util's test compares against the builder's Argv (runner_test.go:522); that assertion moves to a literal argv or to M's own test.
  - In M: podman/podman_test.go; named in the doc of rootless.CheckIDMap (rootless/rootless.go:224)
- **QuadletDir** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. A pure function with seams that no client covers and both tools call; where the unit is written stays a tool step (the file write is the tool's, gdu 7). Whether it sits better beside the quadlet Unit model is a placement question, listed below.
  - In M: podman/podman_test.go only

#### podman: missing

- The operations the catalogue already assigns to podman.Client, which each tool still chains by hand: version read plus the 4.7 gate, create --replace or rm-then-create, and read-back-and-compare of a stored value.
  - A: A:internal/runner/runner.go:475-485 (version), :497-518 (create or fallback), :546-556 (matches); gate at A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:956-957
  - B: B:internal/container/manager.go:623-632 (version), :602-617 (create or fallback), :550-563 (matches)
  - Proposal: podman.Client.Version, CreateSecret and SecretMatches as decided (3.4). Two points the client must settle: the fallback's rm error and create error need to be told apart in the typed error (both tools word them differently today), and under runner.Echo the version reads as unknown so the preview shows the rm-then-create pair, which is what solace-util does now.
- The uid map and the gid map are always read as a pair, two `podman info` calls in a loop over (subuid, false, uid) and (subgid, true, gid).
  - A: A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/rootless.go:72-88
  - B: B:internal/container/rootless.go:208-238
  - Proposal: podman.Client.IDMap returns both maps from one call (one --format template with both ranges) as a typed value, for example IDMaps{UID, GID}; rootless.CheckIDMap takes that value. Owner: podman (read) and rootless (check).
- The unit file name and the systemd service name derived from the container name: <name>.container and <name>.service, and the unit path dir/<name>.container.
  - A: A:internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:232 and A:internal/gen/gen.go:1048
  - B: B:internal/container/manager.go:792-793 (again at :1026, :1053, :1176, :1328)
  - Proposal: containerart/quadlet owns both names once it renders the unit (design 8.2 layer 3): the Unit model reports its file name and the service quadlet generates from it, so the tool passes one container name and never appends a suffix. Owner: containerart/quadlet.
- `podman unshare <command>`: run a command inside the rootless user namespace (chmod, chown, find, test -w on the data directory).
  - A: none
  - B: B:internal/container/manager.go:432, :438, :1153 and B:internal/container/rootless.go:439
  - Proposal: One tool only, but the command is generic to rootless podman and not workload-specific: an exported builder podman.Unshare(base, argv...) (no client method, since what runs inside is the tool's), with the free-operand checks of gdu 4. Owner judgement whether one consumer is enough; the alternative is the design's rule that the tool builds it itself. Owner: podman.
- Registry login with the password on stdin: `<engine> login --username <u> --password-stdin [registry]`.
  - A: none
  - B: B:internal/container/manager.go:465-475
  - Proposal: Engine-agnostic (docker and podman), so it belongs to containercli, not podman: a builder or containercli.Client.Login with Stdin set as CreateSecret does. Listed here only because it was found in the podman deploy path; for the containercli review.

#### podman: placement

- QuadletDir and SystemQuadletDir describe where the quadlet artifact goes, not a podman command; once containerart/quadlet owns the Unit model (design 8.2) they could sit beside it. Staying in podman is fine if podman remains the home for podman host facts; owner call.
- The builder IDMap is in podman and the reader of its answer (CheckIDMap, covers) is in rootless: after podman.Client.IDMap the tool still chains two packages. Acceptable, since rootless stays pure; the alternative is a typed map value in podman with a Covers(id) method that rootless.CheckIDMap takes.
- SupportsReplace, SecretMatches and the 4.7 knowledge become private to the client; nothing moves package.

#### podman: signatures

- Counting note for countA/countB in all three packages: code references only (tests included); mentions inside comments and string literals are not counted. No file in either tool imports podman, systemd or rootless under an alias.
- Three return shapes for five command builders: Version, CreateSecret and IDMap return runner.Cmd; SecretData returns (runner.Cmd, error); RemoveSecrets returns (runner.Cmd, ok bool). gdu 4 makes every name-taking builder return an error; RemoveSecrets' ok should become the same error or a client no-op for zero names (solmq-conn-util discards ok at runner.go:508 and :531).
- Name checks differ: SecretData refuses only an empty name, CreateSecret and RemoveSecrets check nothing (podman.go:63-64 says so). gdu 4 decided a typed secret-name grammar for all three.
- CreateSecret takes labels as variadic key=value strings written as given. Every create must pass the ownership labels (gdu 16, P10), and a label rides argv, so the client should take the typed label set the label helper of gdu 1 produces and hold each entry to the label grammar, not free strings.
- CreateSecret's replace bool disappears from the public signature: the client decides (3.3 rule 1). Client.CreateSecret(ctx, name, value, labels).
- value is []byte in CreateSecret and SecretMatches while both tools hold strings and convert at every call (runner.go:502, manager.go:612); out is a string in SecretMatches while runner.Output gives bytes (manager.go:562 converts). Pick one type per side for the client.
- IDMap selects uid or gid with a bool, and rootless.CheckIDMap selects the same thing with the strings "subuid"/"subgid": two spellings of one choice. One typed kind, or one call that returns both maps.
- SupportsReplace is named for `create --replace` but also gates `secret inspect --showsecret` (podman.go:79-81). When it goes private, name it for the version floor (4.7), not for one of the two features.
- podman.Client is set up with Runner, Base and Output only (3.1). solmq-conn-util attaches the repaired user session to systemctl and loginctl commands alone (runner.go:594-599), while solace-util exports it process-wide so podman commands inherit it too (rootless.go:175-181). Decide whether podman.Client also carries the session env, so rootless podman under sudo/su/cron behaves the same in both tools.
- Catalogue methods with no builder or parser in the package today: SecretLabels and SecretIDs (gdu 10, 16). They are new code, not unexported existing code.

### systemd

- **Systemctl** (func, operation-builder; A0 B4): keep, owner call. No catalogue method covers what solace-util builds with it: `status --no-pager`, `cat`, `show --property=Version`, and a hand-fix command it prints (manager.go:1049). The alternative is to add Status, Cat and a manager probe to systemd.Client and unexport this; with the session env held by the client, a tool-built systemctl command otherwise has to attach that env itself.
  - In M: systemd/systemd.go:29, :33, :36, :39, :45, :79 (every systemctl builder is built on it)
- **DaemonReload** (func, operation-builder; A2 B2): unexport-covered-by-client; by:systemd.Client.DaemonReload. Catalogue method; when to reload stays in each tool, the command does not. It has no test of its own in systemd_test.go today.
- **Start** (func, operation-builder; A2 B3): unexport-covered-by-client; by:systemd.Client.Start. Catalogue method. solmq-conn-util passes the builder as a function value to pick start or restart (runner.go:765-771), which becomes a method value; solace-util's test use (rootless_test.go:400) tests a wrapper the client replaces.
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go only
- **Stop** (func, operation-builder; A2 B2): unexport-covered-by-client; by:systemd.Client.Stop. Catalogue method; the stop, confirm, remove order stays in each tool (gdu 9). The client's typed error should tell 'unit not loaded' (exit 5) from a real failure, which neither tool can do today.
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go only
- **Restart** (func, operation-builder; A2 B3): unexport-covered-by-client; by:systemd.Client.Restart. Catalogue method; restart versus start is the tool's choice from Active, the command is the client's.
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go only
- **IsActive** (func, operation-builder; A1 B2): unexport-covered-by-client; by:systemd.Client.State. Catalogue: State (is-active). The read-stdout-alone, ignore-the-exit-error rule both tools repeat by hand becomes the client's.
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go only
- **State** (func, parser; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:systemd.Client.State. The parser of IsActive's answer, called in both tools only on that answer. answered=false maps onto the typed 'unanswered' error of 3.3 rule 2.
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go only
- **Active** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. A pure judgement on the word Client.State returns; restart-or-start is a decision that stays in the tool (3.5), so the tool needs it alone.
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go only
- **Stopped** (func, pure-helper; A0 B1): keep. The teardown rule of gdu 9 (secrets go only after the unit is confirmed gone) is chained in each tool and needs this judgement; solmq-conn-util does not call it yet because its remove returns on any stop error (runner.go:793-797).
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go only
- **NRestarts** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:systemd.Client.NRestarts. Catalogue method; both tools run it and parse it in one step and fall back to the engine's count on error, which stays their choice.
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go; named in a comment at containercli/inspect/inspect.go:53
- **ParseNRestarts** (func, parser; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:systemd.Client.NRestarts. Parser of NRestarts' answer, never used apart from it. The empty answer for an unknown unit should surface as the typed not-found error.
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go only
- **ShowLinger** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:systemd.Client.Linger. Catalogue method. The client needs the uid, per call or at set-up; solmq-conn-util carries it in its scope (runner.go:701), solace-util passes it per call.
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go only
- **EnableLinger** (func, operation-builder; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:systemd.Client.EnableLinger. Catalogue method. It names no user on purpose (least privilege); the client keeps that, returns a typed refusal, and the tool decides what a refusal means.
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go only
- **ParseLinger** (func, parser; A1 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:systemd.Client.Linger. Parser of ShowLinger's answer, used only on it. rootless.CheckLinger's doc must then name Client.Linger as the source of its state argument.
  - In M: systemd/systemd_test.go; named in the doc of rootless.CheckLinger (rootless/rootless.go:139)

#### systemd: missing

- The read chains the catalogue already assigns to systemd.Client: linger state, unit state from is-active, restart count.
  - A: A:internal/runner/runner.go:700-709 (linger), :738-740 (state), :992-1002 (NRestarts)
  - B: B:internal/container/rootless.go:340-349 (linger), B:internal/container/manager.go:967-970 (state), B:internal/container/inspect.go:99-109 (NRestarts)
  - Proposal: systemd.Client.Linger, State and NRestarts as decided (3.4), each returning the typed unanswered or not-found error in place of today's three shapes (string, string plus bool, int plus error).
- Attaching the repaired user session to every `systemctl --user` and loginctl command.
  - A: A:internal/runner/runner.go:594-599 (QuadletScope.WithEnv, on Cmd.Env)
  - B: B:internal/container/manager.go:1489-1509 with B:internal/container/rootless.go:175-181 (os.Setenv, memoised)
  - Proposal: Decided: systemd.Client holds user mode and session env (3.4) and sets Cmd.Env itself. solace-util chose os.Setenv over Cmd.Env on purpose (rootless.go:139-142, to keep Cmd.Env for compose secrets), so its adoption must accept Cmd.Env for these two non-secret variables. Owner: systemd.
- `systemctl show <unit> -p <prop>...` with a KEY=value reader, of which NRestarts and the planned LoadState (gdu 9) are single-property cases.
  - A: none
  - B: B:internal/container/limits.go:187-189 (the command, against the system manager) and :400-410 (parseUnitProps)
  - Proposal: One tool, plainly generic: a builder systemd.Show(user, unit, props...) and a property parser, or Client.Show returning a map; NRestarts and LoadState become callers of it. Note the caller asks the system manager about user@<uid>.service from a rootless run, so user mode must be selectable per call, not only per client. Owner: systemd.
- Unit views for an operator: `systemctl status <unit> --no-pager` and `systemctl cat <unit>`.
  - A: none
  - B: B:internal/container/manager.go:1329 and :1375
  - Proposal: Either systemd.Client.Status and Client.Cat (output to the hook, exit code in the Result, since status exits non-zero for an inactive unit), or leave them to the tool through the exported Systemctl. This is the same decision as Systemctl's recommendation. Owner: systemd.
- A probe that the (user) manager answers at all, run before a unit is written: `systemctl [--user] show --property=Version`.
  - A: none
  - B: B:internal/container/rootless.go:382-395
  - Proposal: One tool, plainly generic: systemd.Client.ManagerVersion (or Ping) returning the version or the typed unanswered error. solmq-conn-util has no such preflight and would gain it. Owner: systemd.

#### systemd: placement

- ShowLinger, EnableLinger and ParseLinger are loginctl commands in a package documented as systemctl plus loginctl; the catalogue keeps Linger and EnableLinger on systemd.Client, so they stay. The rule that reads their answer (CheckLinger) is in rootless, joined only by the string "yes".
- Active and Stopped are judgements on a state word and stay beside Client.State; no move.
- Nothing in systemd belongs in another package. The session env the client is set up with comes from rootless.UserSession; pass it in as []string so systemd does not import rootless.

#### systemd: signatures

- Every systemctl builder takes user bool first; with the client that becomes a field set once (3.1). solace-util also asks the system manager from a rootless run (limits.go:185-189), so the client needs a way to address the system manager for one call, or the tool keeps a second client.
- No builder returns an error today. gdu 4 decided that a builder taking a unit name checks a typed grammar and returns an error: Start, Stop, Restart, IsActive, NRestarts (and the planned LoadState) all gain one; Systemctl's free args get the free-operand rule (no empty value, no leading dash, no control characters).
- Unlike podman, kube and containercli, nothing here takes base tokens: the program names systemctl and loginctl are fixed in the package (systemd.go:20, :100, :106). State that systemd.Client has no Base (the catalogue gives it user mode and session env only), or add one for symmetry with execguard.
- Three shapes for reading an answer: State returns (string, answered bool), ParseNRestarts returns (int, error), ParseLinger returns a bare string with no way to say unanswered. The client methods should all use the typed unanswered and not-found errors of 3.3 rule 2.
- Parser naming is uneven: State (for IsActive) against ParseNRestarts and ParseLinger. Moot once they are private, but the client method names should follow the catalogue (State, NRestarts, Linger) and the private pairs one pattern.
- Two spellings of the same systemd option: NRestarts uses `-p NRestarts --value` (systemd.go:79), ShowLinger uses `--property=Linger` and ParseLinger strips the Linger= prefix (systemd.go:100, :111). Pick one form for every show-style read.
- Active and Stopped take a raw string. A named type for the unit state returned by Client.State, with Active and Stopped as methods, would stop a tool passing an untrimmed or unrelated string; small, but every caller changes in this tag anyway.
- ShowLinger takes the uid and EnableLinger takes nothing by design (least privilege, systemd.go:103-105). Keep that asymmetry in the client and say so in the method docs.
- Catalogue method with no builder today: LoadState (gdu 9), needed to tell 'not loaded' from 'inactive' before a stop. The exit codes recorded in todo item 9 (stop exits 5, is-active exits 3 or 4) can feed the typed not-found error.

### rootless

- **Decide** (func, pure-helper; A2 B1): keep. The shared rootless rule, pure, used by both tools, and it takes the tool's key name as an argument, so it hard-codes no tool config. No client covers a rule.
  - In M: rootless/rootless_test.go only
- **Session** (type, type; A0 B0): keep. It is the value that sets up systemd.Client's session env (catalogue: user mode and session env), so it must stay nameable. The four exported fields are unread by both tools; keeping them is harmless and lets a tool report them.
  - In M: rootless/rootless.go:84 (UserSession's result), :114 and :127 (method receivers); rootless/rootless_test.go
  - Reached: Neither tool writes the type name: both receive it from UserSession and call its methods (A internal/runner/runner.go:662-666, B internal/container/rootless.go:170-182). Neither reads its four fields directly.
- **UserSession** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. Pure with seams (an executor may not read the environment, 3.3 rule 4), used by both tools, and it is the set-up step for systemd.Client. No client covers it.
  - In M: rootless/rootless_test.go only
- **Session.Env** (method, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. It is what a tool hands to systemd.Client as the session env; both tools call it today, one for Cmd.Env and one for os.Setenv.
  - In M: rootless/rootless_test.go only
- **Session.String** (method, pure-helper; A0 B1): keep. solace-util prints it as its 'user session' report row; it is a generic description any tool can show, and the two-consumer rule is discarded.
  - In M: rootless/rootless_test.go only
- **CheckLinger** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. A pure refusal on the state systemd.Client.Linger will return; whether to refuse or to enable is each tool's policy (validate refuses, deploy enables), so the tool needs it alone.
  - In M: rootless/rootless_test.go only
- **Unbounded** (const, constant; A0 B4): keep. The value ParseLimit returns for infinity/unlimited; a caller of ParseLimit or CheckCore must be able to name it. Used by solace-util only, which the discarded two-consumer rule no longer counts against it.
  - In M: rootless/rootless.go:162 (ParseLimit), :174 (LimitText)
- **ParseLimit** (func, parser; A0 B2): keep. A generic reader of one limit value; solace-util applies it to `systemctl show` and `ulimit` answers it reads itself, steps that stay in the tool. It follows the CheckCore family decision if that family moves.
  - In M: rootless/rootless.go:203 (HardLimit)
- **LimitText** (func, pure-helper; A0 B10): keep. The inverse of ParseLimit for messages, used ten times in solace-util's report rows; it must stay exported as long as Unbounded is. It follows the CheckCore family decision if that family moves.
  - In M: rootless/rootless.go:220 (CheckCore's message)
- **ProcLimits** (func, operation-builder; A0 B1): keep, owner call. A builder no client covers (the catalogue gives rootless no client), so rule 3 keeps it exported. The judgement is whether the session core-limit check is common or solace-util's own: only its unit sets a core ulimit, which design 8.2 calls a workload extra.
  - In M: rootless/rootless_test.go only
- **HardLimit** (func, parser; A0 B1): keep, owner call. Parser of ProcLimits' answer with no client to absorb it; generic over any /proc/self/limits line (solace-util could read 'Max open files' with it in place of its own `ulimit -Hn` probe at limits.go:301). Same family decision as ProcLimits and CheckCore.
  - In M: rootless/rootless_test.go only
- **CheckCore** (func, pure-helper; A0 B1): keep, owner call. docs/developer.md:212 calls it generic, and it is true of any rootless container that sets a core ulimit; but solmq-conn-util has no such limit and never calls the family (ProcLimits, HardLimit, CheckCore). Under rule 5 the owner should confirm it is a common check and not solace-util customisation (then move-to-tool: solace-util).
  - In M: rootless/rootless_test.go only
- **CheckIDMap** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): change-signature, owner call. Stays exported (the tool chains it after podman.Client.IDMap), but should return a typed error carrying file, id and ranges: solace-util re-derives the cause from the raw output to word its report row after the check fails (rootless.go:230-235). Proposal: a *IDMapError the tool can branch on, and a typed kind in place of the "subuid"/"subgid" string.
  - In M: rootless/rootless_test.go; named in the doc of podman.IDMap (podman/podman.go:117)
- **NextFreeRange** (func, pure-helper; A1 B2): keep. Pure, used by both tools to build the same refusal. If M takes over the refusal assembly (see missing) it could become private, but that is a later step, not a consequence of the clients.
  - In M: rootless/rootless_test.go only
- **SubIDFix** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. The shared fix text both tools append in the same way; each then adds its own alternative, which is the customisation that stays in the tool.
  - In M: rootless/rootless_test.go only

#### rootless: missing

- The administrator's form of the linger fix, printed when `loginctl enable-linger` is refused: 'Ask an administrator to run: sudo loginctl enable-linger <name>'.
  - A: A:internal/runner/runner.go:688-692
  - B: B:internal/container/rootless.go:369-372
  - Proposal: rootless.LingerFix(user string) string beside SubIDFix, appended by the tool to the typed refusal systemd.Client.EnableLinger returns. Both tools write the same two lines today. Owner: rootless.
- Assembling the sub-id refusal: the CheckIDMap error, a period, a newline, then SubIDFix fed from NextFreeRange with need = id+1.
  - A: A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/rootless.go:96-111
  - B: B:internal/container/rootless.go:253-259
  - Proposal: One pure function in rootless that takes the cause, the user, the files' text, whether they were read, and the ids, and returns the full refusal; the tool appends only its own alternative. It also settles a difference: solmq-conn-util sizes the range from max(uid, gid)+1 (rootless.go:108), solace-util from the failing id+1 (rootless.go:254). Owner: rootless.
- Reading /etc/subuid and /etc/subgid for NextFreeRange, and carrying the 'computed' flag when a read fails.
  - A: A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/rootless.go:97-107 (os.ReadFile per file)
  - B: B:internal/container/rootless.go:274-283 (`sh -c cat /etc/subuid /etc/subgid` through the runner)
  - Proposal: A builder like ProcLimits (rootless.SubIDFiles() runner.Cmd) so both tools ask the host the runner reaches, a fake sees the probe and a preview skips it. docs/developer.md:183-184 currently leaves this read to each tool; rule 5 reopens it. Owner: rootless.
- Resolving the account name that the fix lines name literally, with the numeric uid as fallback.
  - A: A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/runas.go:251-257 (os/user LookupId)
  - B: B:internal/container/rootless.go:289-295 (`id -un` through the runner)
  - Proposal: A builder for `id -un` plus the fallback rule in rootless. The two tools differ in substance: solace-util's comment says os/user misses directory (LDAP/AD) accounts, which is exactly the population that lacks sub-id ranges. Owner judgement, since developer.md leaves the account name to each tool. Owner: rootless.
- The existence probe UserSession needs for the runtime directory.
  - A: A:internal/runner/runner.go:626-635 (os.Stat)
  - B: B:internal/container/rootless.go:159-169 (`sh -c 'test -d "$0"' <dir>` through the runner, exit 1 read as absent)
  - Proposal: A builder and its exit-code reading in rootless (DirProbe), so the isDir seam has one shared implementation that goes through the runner. Lower priority: the seam already lets each tool choose. Owner: rootless.
- The two settings that follow from the rootless decision: `systemctl --user` or not, and the unit's WantedBy target.
  - A: A:internal/runner/runner.go:660 (UserMode) and A:internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:230 (WantedBy=default.target for every unit)
  - B: B:internal/config/load.go:278-284 (--user and default.target when rootless, multi-user.target when rootful)
  - Proposal: The quadlet Unit model takes the rootless bool and writes WantedBy (design 8.2 layer 3), and systemd.Client's user mode is set from the same bool. The tools disagree today for a rootful unit (default.target against multi-user.target), which the shared renderer settles. Owner: containerart/quadlet and systemd.
- Resolving the whole podman scope in one step: Decide, then the quadlet directory, then the user session.
  - A: A:internal/runner/runner.go:651-669 (ResolveQuadletScope)
  - B: B:internal/container/euid.go:41, B:internal/config/hostpath.go:404 and B:internal/container/rootless.go:170 (three separate moments)
  - Proposal: Owner judgement. A pure rootless.Resolve returning {Rootless, QuadletDir, Session} would give a third tool one call to set up podman.Client and systemd.Client, and fits the guide and example tool. Against it: solace-util lets the operator set podman.quadletDir and resolves the session lazily through its runner, so it would not use the composite as is.

#### rootless: placement

- Session and UserSession describe the systemd user session and feed systemd.Client; they stay in rootless (the rule is about rootless podman under sudo, su and cron), with the client taking the entries as []string so systemd imports nothing from rootless.
- ProcLimits is the only command builder in a package of rules and checks, and rootless has no client in the catalogue. It stays exported under rule 3; say in the package doc that rootless builders are run by the tool directly and so bypass the Output hook and Result of 3.2.
- CheckLinger judges a logind answer that systemd reads. It stays in rootless as the rootless rule; the alternative home is systemd beside Client.Linger. No strong case to move.
- ParseLimit, LimitText and Unbounded are generic limit helpers that solace-util also applies to `systemctl show` output (limits.go:217). They stay with HardLimit and CheckCore; if the core-limit family moves to solace-util, all six go together.

#### rootless: signatures

- Error prefixes are uneven: the UserSession probe error and both HardLimit errors start with "rootless: " (rootless.go:94, :200, :205), while Decide, the missing-directory refusal, CheckLinger, ParseLimit, CheckCore and CheckIDMap have none. Settle one rule (operator-facing refusals unprefixed, probe failures prefixed, or all one way).
- No check returns a typed error, while the design gives executors typed errors to branch on. solace-util works around it twice: it re-parses the id map output after CheckIDMap fails (rootless.go:230-235) and tests the linger state itself before calling CheckLinger (rootless.go:350-359). Typed refusals for CheckIDMap, CheckLinger and CheckCore would remove both.
- CheckIDMap names the kind with file string ("subuid"/"subgid") while podman.IDMap names it with gid bool; the tools carry both in one struct per row (A rootless.go:72-76, B rootless.go:208-215). One typed kind across the two packages.
- CheckLinger takes the raw word and compares it with "yes" (rootless.go:144); solmq-conn-util repeats the same comparison in its own code (runner.go:684). A bool or typed linger state from systemd.Client.Linger removes the magic string.
- SubIDFix returns a string to append and its doc fixes the joining format in prose (rootless.go:302); both tools then write the same `%w.\n%s`. Either M joins it (see missing) or the format stays a documented convention, but not both.
- NextFreeRange returns (start, end) and the tool carries computed separately into SubIDFix; a small result struct {Start, End, Computed} would keep the three together.
- HardLimit takes the line name as a free string and solace-util passes the literal "Max core file size" (limits.go:380). Export a constant for the core line, or a CoreHardLimit wrapper, so the name lives beside CheckCore.
- The euid convention (-1 where there is none) is shared by Decide and UserSession and should be stated once in the package doc; CheckLinger takes uid with no such case.
- Decide returns a bare bool whose meaning (true = rootless) is only in the doc, and both tools immediately rename it (rootlessMode at runner.go:652, discarded at euid.go:41). A named result or a small Mode type would read better at call sites; low priority.
- Session.Env returns only the derived entries by design (rootless.go:112-113). When systemd.Client takes them, keep that: passing inherited values too would overwrite nothing but would put them on every Result.Argv env listing.

### Verifiers for the podman-host group

Usage re-count: 38 names counted, 0 corrections.


Rule challenge: 38 names examined, 3 challenges.

- **[medium] systemd.Systemctl**: keep -> unexport-internal-only, with Status, Cat and a manager probe (or one Show) added to systemd.Client
  - 1. All four solace-util uses are three generic read-only views plus one printed string: status --no-pager (B:internal/container/manager.go:1329), cat (manager.go:1375), show --property=Version (B:internal/container/rootless.go:384), and a hand command inside an error that is never run (manager.go:1049). None is workload customisation, so rule 5 puts them in M, and the reviewer's own missing list already proposes Show, Status/Cat and ManagerVersion. If those are accepted, nothing in a tool reaches Systemctl. 2. An exported Systemctl cannot meet gdu 4. The reviewer's signature note gives its args the free-operand rule (docs/todo.md:276-277: no leading dash), but every real call passes a flag through args: --no-pager, --property=Version, and M's own NRestarts passes -p and --value (systemd/systemd.go:79). Either the rule is not applied, and Systemctl is an unchecked route to `start <unit>` around the client and the unit grammar, or solace-util's three calls are refused. 3. It takes user per call and carries no session env, while the catalogue puts both on the client (design 3.4). Keeping it makes solace-util keep its own session attach (manager.go:1489-1509) for these calls alone, and they bypass the Output hook and the Result. 4. The escape hatch is not needed: for a command M does not offer the tool writes the argv itself (rule 3), which solace-util already does for the system-manager read (B:internal/container/limits.go:187-189) instead of calling Systemctl(false, ...). 5. M choosing the command also closes a gap: `systemctl cat` is built without --no-pager today (manager.go:1375). Against: rule 3 read literally keeps a builder no client covers, and a third tool may want a verb M lacks; it can build that argv itself.
- **[low] rootless.NextFreeRange**: keep -> unexport-internal-only, behind one exported function that assembles the sub-id refusal, decided for this tag
  - The reviewer keeps it and defers the refusal assembly to 'a later step'. The plan has none: the next tag ships everything (design section 7 point 11), so deferring means a second break of the same call sites. Both tools call it only to feed SubIDFix inside the same refusal (A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/rootless.go:108-110, B:internal/container/rootless.go:254-258, :278-281). That assembly is a common operation under rule 5: same inputs, same `%w.\n%s` join, a format M's doc already prescribes (rootless/rootless.go:302). Only the alternative each tool appends is customisation. The two tools also size the range differently (max(uid,gid)+1 against id+1), which an exported NextFreeRange leaves unsettled. Against: it is pure and harmless to keep, and v0.x allows a later break.
- **[low] rootless.SubIDFix**: keep -> unexport-internal-only, behind the same exported refusal-assembly function as NextFreeRange
  - Same case as NextFreeRange. Its only callers are the two hand-written joins (A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/rootless.go:109-110, B:internal/container/rootless.go:258), and its doc states the joining format in prose (rootless/rootless.go:302) because M does not do the join. With the reviewer's proposed assembly function the tool passes the cause, the account name and the files' text, then appends its own alternative; nothing in a tool needs the bare fix text. Decide with the missing item, in this tag.

Additions:

- (signature) systemd.Client.State and design 3.3 rule 6 collide. Rule 6 says a read that answers nothing makes 'its parser report unknown' (docs/design/2026-11-15-shared-deploy-api.md:116-117), but "unknown" is a real state word that Stopped accepts as confirmed down (systemd/systemd.go:69). A client that returns the word for no answer, for example under runner.Echo, lets a teardown proceed, where solace-util refuses on no answer today (B:internal/container/manager.go:1041). State must return the typed unanswered error and an empty or zero state, never the string. kube.NamespaceState is the precedent: a typed state whose zero value is unknown (kube/kube.go:63-65). This is the concrete reason to make Active and Stopped methods on a typed state, not functions on a raw string.
- (signature) The Output hook and the read methods. Design 3.2 hands every chunk to the hook unmodified, but both tools capture a probe's stdout silently today (A: runParsed and Split, A:internal/runner/runner.go:86-92; B: engine.Output, B:internal/engine/runner.go:142-145). Taken literally, Version, IDMap, State, LoadState, NRestarts and Linger would put '5.8.2', 'inactive', 'Linger=no' into A's buffered report and onto B's terminal. Decide per method whether stdout reaches the hook, or mark the chunk so the hook can tell a parsed read from an action. podman.Client.SecretMatches is a hard exception: its stdout is the secret value, so it must never reach the hook (catalogue: 'the value never printed'), and the method doc must say so.
- (signature) Result for an operation that takes several commands. Result holds one Argv, one ExitCode and one Duration (design 3.2), but podman.Client.CreateSecret below 4.7 runs rm then create, plus the version read on the first call, and SecretLabels needs `secret ls` then `secret inspect` (docs/todo.md:864-866). State which command the Result describes, or return one per command. solmq-conn-util concatenates the output of both halves today (A:internal/runner/runner.go:508-514).
- (signature) podman.Client keeps the version for the run, so it holds state: pointer receivers, or a pointer field, where design 3.1 shows a plain struct literal (kube.Client{...}). State what a failed version read caches; both tools treat it as 'below 4.7' and go on (A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:956-957, B:internal/container/manager.go:627-631). Once the 4.7 gate is inside the client, no tool calls Client.Version, the catalogue method. Either it serves reports and returns a parsed version with a comparator (the same floor a tool passes to artifacttest.CheckQuadletKeys, docs/developer.md:171-172), or it is private too.
- (signature) Base as a plain []string drops a rule both tools follow: the command guard runs in every executor right before argv is built (docs/developer.md:96-98; A calls ParseCommand in each wrapper, runner.go:476, :497, :527, :546, :562; B guards in m.runtime() on every call, B:internal/container/manager.go:140-143). A client set up once guards once. Either podman.Client takes a type only execguard can produce, or developer.md restates the rule as 'once, at client set-up'.
- (signature) systemd.Client.Linger takes a uid, and the package convention is euid -1 where there is none (rootless/rootless.go:38). ShowLinger(-1) builds `loginctl show-user -1 --property=Linger` (systemd/systemd.go:99-101), a dash-led token. Both tools guard before the call today; the client method should refuse uid < 0 itself, in the spirit of gdu 4.
- (signature) The session env on systemd.Client must be optional and attached only in user mode. solmq-conn-util attaches it only when UserMode is set and Env is non-empty (A:internal/runner/runner.go:594-599). solace-util resolves the session lazily at the first systemctl call and reports a failure as a report row (B:internal/container/rootless.go:146-184, :91-97), so it needs either a client built after that probe or one with no Env while it keeps os.Setenv.
- (signature) With podman.Client.IDMap returning both maps, rootless.CheckIDMap can take uid and gid together and report the first failing kind in its typed error; both tools stop at the first failure (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/rootless.go:85-87, B internal/container/rootless.go:229-236). The kind then appears in no signature, and the loop both tools write goes. Note solmq-conn-util skips the probe itself for id 0 (rootless.go:77-79); with one call it can skip only when both ids are 0.
- (signature) systemd.Client.EnableLinger need not classify a refusal. Both tools treat every failure of the enable alike and print the administrator form (A:internal/runner/runner.go:688-692, B:internal/container/rootless.go:364-372). Telling a polkit refusal from other failures would mean reading loginctl's stderr text; a plain wrapped error plus the proposed LingerFix is enough.
- (signature) Correction to the reviewer's record for DaemonReload: usedInM is not empty and it does have a test. TestUserFlagOnEveryBuilder covers it at systemd/systemd_test.go:21. The recommendation is unaffected.
- (missing) The 'needs logind' hint on a linger read that cannot run. Both tools write it by hand: A:internal/runner/runner.go:704-706 and B:internal/container/rootless.go:343-345. Proposal: the typed unanswered error from systemd.Client.Linger carries the hint, as containercli.Client.Info's error carries the platform hint (catalogue 3.4). Owner: systemd.
- (missing) The test for 'this host has a user session to repair and rootless host checks to run' differs. solmq-conn-util uses a Linux flag (runner.go:661, cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:898); solace-util uses euid >= 0 and not Echo (internal/container/rootless.go:56-61, :151-157). On a non-Linux POSIX host the two tools behave differently. Proposal: one predicate in rootless, stated beside the euid -1 convention. Owner: rootless.
- (missing) The run-as value that feeds both the unit's user line and CheckIDMap is parsed differently. solmq-conn-util: a bare uid means gid 0, and 'image' keeps the image's user (A:internal/spec/runas.go:29-32, :42-43). solace-util: a bare uid checks gid = uid, and names are skipped (B:internal/container/rootless.go:300-314). With rendering in M the Unit and Service models take the user (design 8.2), so one typed run-as (numeric uid:gid, the image's own, or names) can serve the renderers and CheckIDMap. Owner: containerart for the type, rootless for the check.
- (missing) The store name of a podman secret is the container name plus a suffix in both tools: A:internal/gen/gen.go:1054-1056 and B:internal/render/render.go:714-719. M will write the Secret= lines (design 8.2) and create, list and remove the same names through podman.Client, so one helper can own the derivation and the secret-name grammar of gdu 4. Owner: containerart/quadlet or podman.
- (missing) The pure halves of gdu 16 and gdu 10 have no named home. The catalogue lists only the reads (SecretLabels, SecretIDs). Still to place: the delta 'labelled names minus the names the config renders' (docs/todo.md:867-868), the compare of the IDs a container recorded against the store's (docs/todo.md:558-559), and the rule that a partial `secret inspect` answer is a failed listing (docs/todo.md:876-878), which Client.SecretLabels must return as a typed error. Owner: podman.
- (missing) The quadlet unit file's mode differs: solmq-conn-util writes 0644 (A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:993), solace-util 0600 (B:internal/container/manager.go:740); both create the directory 0755. docs/todo.md:545 already notes the world-readable unit. The mode is a property of the artifact, so it belongs with the Unit model or the atomic-write package of gdu 7, beside QuadletDir. Owner: containerart/quadlet or the gdu 7 package.
- (missing) For the containercli review: solace-util probes `<engine> version` and `<compose> version` by hand as a reachability check (B:internal/container/manager.go:329, :337). On podman this is a second version call beside podman.Client.Version. solmq-conn-util has no such probe.
- (placement) podman.Client repeats containercli.Client's set-up for the same engine: solace-util builds one base for both families (B:internal/container/manager.go:155-161). The kube family avoids that by handing the kube.Client to rbac.Run and nsgate.Read (catalogue 3.4). Either podman.Client is built from a containercli.Client, or the guide says to fill both from one Base, Runner and hook.
- (placement) Evidence for the CheckCore, ProcLimits and HardLimit owner call that the reviewer did not cite: the canonical parity body lists the session core check under Inherent differences as matching the broker's needs and staying in solace-util, and says solmq-conn-util's unit asks for no core limit (paritydoc/body.md:202-203). If the family stays in M, that line needs rewording, since half the check is shared; if it moves, Unbounded, ParseLimit and LimitText go with it as the reviewer says.
- (placement) If the reviewer's rootless proposals are accepted (SubIDFiles, `id -un`, DirProbe beside ProcLimits), rootless holds four command builders and no client. Each tool then still wraps run, capture and parse by hand for four probes, which is the duplication design section 2 sets out to remove. Decide a small client for these host probes, or state that they stay bare builders outside the hook and Result.
- (placement) The parse of podman's 'containerID:size' pairs lives in rootless (covers, rootless/rootless.go:244-265, whose comment says podman owns the format). When podman.Client.IDMap returns decoded data, as Inspect does (catalogue), the parse moves to podman. Pick the import direction: rootless.CheckIDMap takes a plain range type, so that neither package imports the other, as the reviewer chose for systemd and the session env.
- (placement) Text that names the builders and wrappers and must change in the same step. paritydoc/body.md:33-48: rules 4 and 5 name PodmanDeploy, PodmanSecretCreate and createPodmanSecrets; the last no longer exists in solace-util except in comments (manager.go:1071, :1096; the function is writePodmanSecrets, manager.go:602). docs/developer.md:179-213 and :226-231. The package docs that say nothing executes (podman/podman.go:5-8, systemd/systemd.go:4-6). The comment at containercli/inspect/inspect.go:53. The rows of docs/test.md:372-396 keep their lifted test names although the functions they name become private.

### kube

- **Apply** (func, operation-builder; A2 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:kube.Client.Apply. Each tool calls it once, only to run it at once (A through gdurunner.Combined, B through runKube); no step that stays in a tool needs the Cmd alone. Code refs A/B: 1/1, the rest are comments.
- **Delete** (func, operation-builder; A3 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:kube.Client.Delete (timeout, gdu 12). One call per tool, run at once; the private builder gains --timeout (gdu 12) in the same step. Code refs A/B: 1/1.
- **NamespaceManifest** (func, renderer-piece; A8 B5): change-signature, owner call. Design 8.2 and gdu 1 make the Namespace a rendered document with Meta (labels, lib and version annotations), which a bare ns argument cannot carry; replace it with the Namespace document type, for example Namespace{Meta}.Render(), in the package that holds the Kubernetes documents. Real calls: A namespace.go:65, deploy.go:348, deploy_test.go:895; B prep.go:108, prep.go:403. Which package holds the documents is the owner's call.
- **NamespaceState** (type, type; A2 B1): keep; by:kube.Client.NamespaceState (returned value). It is the value the planned Client.NamespaceState returns (design 3.1), and both tools branch on it in their own create-namespace sequence, which stays in the tool.
  - In M: kube/kube.go:89 (ParseNamespace result) and the three constants
- **NamespaceUnknown** (const, constant; A4 B1): keep; by:kube.Client.NamespaceState (returned value). A value of the kept state type; the tools' own wrappers that return it today (A runner.go:271, B prep.go:54) go away with the client, but the branch on it stays. See signatureNotes for unknown state versus typed error.
  - In M: kube/kube.go:96
  - Reached: B reaches it through the default branch of its switch (internal/k8s/prep.go:96)
- **NamespaceAbsent** (const, constant; A2 B2): keep; by:kube.Client.NamespaceState (returned value). Both tools branch on it to decide between a hard stop and a warning, which is tool policy (design 3.5).
  - In M: kube/kube.go:94
- **NamespaceExists** (const, constant; A2 B2): keep; by:kube.Client.NamespaceState (returned value). Both tools branch on it to skip the create probe, which is tool sequence.
  - In M: kube/kube.go:92
- **GetNamespace** (func, operation-builder; A2 B3): unexport-covered-by-client; by:kube.Client.NamespaceState. Both tools wrap it in the same run-then-parse helper that the client method replaces (design section 2 table). B's test at prep_test.go:27 builds the argv with it and moves to the fake runner's recorded argv. Code refs A/B: 1/2.
- **ParseNamespace** (func, parser; A1 B2): unexport-covered-by-client; by:kube.Client.NamespaceState. The parser of GetNamespace's answer, called only right after the run in both tools; the client keeps its own copy of the output and parses it. Code refs A/B: 1/1.
- **MissingNamespace** (func, pure-helper; A2 B2): keep, owner call. A pure error wording both tools use for the same branch of a sequence that stays in the tool (absent namespace, refused create probe), so no client method covers it. Owner's call whether shared wording for a tool-side branch stays a free function taking base, becomes a method on Client (which holds Base, pinned context included), or moves to the tools. Code refs A/B: 1/1.
- **DeleteNamespace** (func, operation-builder; A3 B3): unexport-covered-by-client; by:kube.Client.DeleteNamespace (timeout, gdu 12). One call per tool, run at once as the last gate step; the private builder gains --timeout (gdu 12) and the dash-led name refusal (gdu 4). Code refs A/B: 1/1.
  - In M: named in the nsgate package doc only (kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:15)
- **Scale** (func, operation-builder; A3 B3): unexport-covered-by-client; by:kube.Client.Scale. Every call site runs the Cmd at once; the order around it (scale, then wait) stays in each tool. Code refs A/B: 2/3.
- **RolloutRestart** (func, operation-builder; A2 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:kube.Client.RolloutRestart. One call per tool, run at once. Code refs A/B: 1/1.
- **RolloutTimeout** (const, constant; A2 B4): keep; by:argument of kube.Client.RolloutStatus (design 3.1 example). The design's own example passes it to kc.RolloutStatus, and B prints it in its timeout messages; gdu 12 adds a delete default beside it. Its type (string against time.Duration) is in signatureNotes. Code refs A/B: 0/3.
  - In M: kube/kube.go:135 (RolloutStatus default)
  - Reached: A names it in comments only and reaches it by passing "" to RolloutStatus (internal/runner/runner.go:262)
- **RolloutStatus** (func, operation-builder; A4 B1): unexport-covered-by-client; by:kube.Client.RolloutStatus. The design's worked example (3.6): both tools run it and classify a refusal, which the client does and returns as kube.ErrForbidden. Code refs A/B: 1/1.
- **Forbidden** (func, parser; A3 B16): keep, owner call; by:the kube.ErrForbidden typed error every client method returns (design 3.3 rule 2, 3.6).
