All four live call sites (A lifecycle.go:136; B ops.go:159, adminsecret.go:162, deploy.go:76) follow a RolloutStatus or Get the client will cover, so mechanically it could go private. It classifies any kubectl output, though, and tools keep building commands M does not offer (A top pod at runner.go:887; B describe, set env, cp, named deletes), so keeping it lets those refusals be judged the same way; A already hand-matches Forbidden at secretprobe.go:41. Code refs A/B: 1/3.
- **GetOptions** (type, type; A3 B21): keep; by:argument of kube.Client.Get and ListLabelled. The options the planned Client.Get takes; B stores it in its own getRead struct (inspect.go:45), so it must stay nameable.
  - In M: kube/kube.go:83 (GetNamespace), kube/kube.go:159 (Get)
- **Get** (func, operation-builder; A2 B15): unexport-covered-by-client, owner call; by:kube.Client.Get, kube.Client.ListLabelled. Every call site runs the Cmd at once, so the rule unexports it, but only if Client.Get has a capture-only form that keeps the answer from the output hook: A reads a whole Secret as JSON (secretprobe.go:36 via runner.go:859) and B reads one Secret key (matechannel.go:152), and design 3.2 hands every chunk to the hook, which in A prints at the end. If the owner does not want that form, Get stays exported for those reads. Code refs A/B: 2/14.
  - In M: kube/kube.go:83 (GetNamespace)
- **ExecOptions** (type, type; A2 B4): keep; by:argument of kube.Client.Exec and kube.Client.Upload. The options the planned Client.Exec and Client.Upload take. Upload overwrites its Stdin and TTY fields (kube.go:219), noted in signatureNotes.
  - In M: kube/kube.go:188 (Exec), kube/kube.go:215 (Upload)
- **Exec** (func, operation-builder; A3 B4): unexport-covered-by-client, owner call; by:kube.Client.Exec. Covered, provided Client.Exec offers the three ways the tools run it today: an attached terminal session returning the child's exit code (A shell.go:273, B ops.go:57), captured output (A runner.go:1207, B matechannel.go:98), and stdin bytes set on the Cmd after building (B transport.go:79). If the client offers only one form, the builder has to stay exported. Code refs A/B: 1/4.
  - In M: kube/kube.go:220 (Upload)
- **Upload** (func, operation-builder; A2 B4): unexport-covered-by-client; by:kube.Client.Upload. All three call sites run the Cmd at once; B's mate channel uses a second set of tokens, which is a second Client (gdu 11 scope). Code refs A/B: 1/2.
- **LogOptions** (type, type; A1 B10): keep; by:argument of kube.Client.Logs. The options the planned Client.Logs takes; B's CLI layer builds the value from its flags (commands.go:839) and passes it down.
  - In M: kube/kube.go:238 (Logs)
- **Logs** (func, operation-builder; A1 B2): unexport-covered-by-client; by:kube.Client.Logs. A takes only .Argv and streams it under a cancellable context (cmd/solmq-conn-util/logs.go:303), B streams through runKube; a client method taking ctx and the output hook covers both. Code refs A/B: 1/2.
- **ValidLabel** (func, pure-helper; A7 B0): keep. A grammar a tool checks at its own boundary (gdu 4), and the builders will call it too. B duplicates it and should adopt it; A also uses it for container and compose project names (validate.go:1732, 1814), see placement.
  - In M: kube/kube.go:56 (NamespaceManifest)
  - Reached: B reaches it only through NamespaceManifest's refusal; it keeps its own copy of the rule (internal/config/validate.go:443)

#### kube: missing

- Delete one object by kind and name: delete <kind> <name> -n <ns> --ignore-not-found --timeout=<t>. The catalogue has Delete (manifest) and DeletePod only, while gdu 18 has both tools delete leftovers by name.
  - A: none today: A deletes by manifest only (internal/runner/runner.go:146)
  - B: internal/k8s/prep.go:283 (secret), internal/k8s/deploy.go:215 (pvc), internal/k8s/ops.go:138 (pod)
  - Proposal: kube: one Client method for a named delete (kind, name, ns, timeout) with a private builder; DeletePod becomes a call of it. Names checked per gdu 4, bound per gdu 12.
- Read the current context, and the helpers that find a --context in the tokens and pin one (gdu 11). CurrentContext and WhoAmI are in the catalogue but no builder exists.
  - A: none: A never reads or pins a context (docs/todo.md item 11)
  - B: internal/cli/context.go:261
  - Proposal: kube: Client.CurrentContext and Client.WhoAmI with private builders, plus pure helpers for the tokens (find --context X or --context=X; add --context=<name>).
- Typed outcome errors (refused, not found, unanswered) and a not-found classifier. kube.ErrForbidden appears in the design's example (3.6) and does not exist; today only Forbidden(out) does.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/secretprobe.go:39-41 (its own string match for NotFound and Forbidden)
  - B: internal/k8s/ops.go:159, internal/k8s/adminsecret.go:162, internal/k8s/deploy.go:76 (kube.Forbidden on captured stderr; no not-found path, it relies on --ignore-not-found)
  - Proposal: kube: ErrForbidden, ErrNotFound and an unanswered sentinel returned by every client method, built on Forbidden and a new NotFound classifier beside it.
- Reading `get -o json` whether kubectl answered a List or a single object.
  - A: internal/statusreport/parse.go:150 (kubeItems)
  - B: internal/k8s/inspect.go:98 (normalizeToList)
  - Proposal: kube: one pure parser, for example Items(out) ([]json.RawMessage, error), beside Client.Get.
- The DNS-1123 subdomain grammar (decided in gdu 4), and B's private copy of the label grammar.
  - A: internal/validate/validate.go:1314 (dnsSubdomainRE, used at :1440)
  - B: internal/config/validate.go:474 (subdomain), internal/config/validate.go:443 (label; B never calls kube.ValidLabel)
  - Proposal: kube: ValidSubdomain beside ValidLabel; B drops both private rules.
- The Kubernetes documents of design 8.2: the metadata block, Secret (Opaque, TLS, image pull with the registry payload) and the --- join. Decided, but no package is named.
  - A: internal/deploy/deploy.go:374-418 (Secrets inline), internal/gen/gen.go:507 (dockerConfigJSON), internal/deploy/deploy.go:331 (separator)
  - B: internal/k8s/secrets.go:35 (secretManifest.render), internal/k8s/secrets.go:249 (dockerRegistrySecret), internal/k8s/prep.go:418 (joinManifests)
  - Proposal: A documents package under kube (for example kube/manifest) so kube itself stays commands and their answers; NamespaceManifest moves there as the Namespace document. Owner names the package.
- A cluster reachability check with the login hint, the counterpart of containercli.Client.Info.
  - A: none: A's first can-i probe doubles as it (internal/runner/runner.go:384-388)
  - B: internal/k8s/check.go:40 (version -o json)
  - Proposal: kube: a Client method (Version or Reachable) whose unanswered error carries the hint now worded only inside rbac.Check (kube/rbac/rbac.go:165-167). One tool only; owner's call.
- Generic reads and copies one tool builds by hand: describe, top pod, cp.
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:887 (top pod)
  - B: internal/k8s/ops.go:31 and :36, internal/k8s/operator.go:652 (describe); internal/k8s/transport.go:106 and :116 (cp)
  - Proposal: kube: Client.Describe, Client.Top and Client.Copy if the owner wants them offered; otherwise they stay tool-built under rule 3 and Forbidden stays exported for them. One tool each; owner's call.

#### kube: placement

- NamespaceManifest: a rendered document, not a command; it belongs with the Kubernetes documents of design 8.2, whichever package the owner names for them.
- ValidLabel: a plain DNS-1123 grammar that A also applies to docker and podman names and the compose project name (A internal/validate/validate.go:1732, :1814); with the subdomain grammar of gdu 4 and containercli.ValidName it could sit in one names package. Staying in kube is defensible; owner's call.
- Forbidden: stays in kube, as the source of the planned kube.ErrForbidden; rbac.RefusedError is the second spelling of a refusal and should relate to it (rbac signatureNotes).

#### kube: signatures

- Counts: countA and countB are every mention in .go files, comments included; each reason gives the code references where they differ.
- Namespace position: Scale, RolloutRestart and RolloutStatus take kind, name, ns as positional strings; Get, Exec, Upload and Logs carry the namespace in an options struct; GetNamespace and DeleteNamespace take it as the object. Pick one convention for the client methods.
- Options position: Get(base, o, what...) puts options before the operands; Exec(base, pod, o, argv...), Upload(base, pod, o, ...) and Logs(base, target, o) put them after the target.
- Target spelling: Scale and the rollouts take kind and name apart and join them; Logs takes a joined target (B passes "pod/"+name at internal/k8s/ops.go:43); Get takes free operands; Exec takes a bare pod. gdu 4 already splits bare names from free operands; the method signatures should show which is which.
- Errors: only NamespaceManifest, Exec and Upload return an error today. gdu 4 gives every name-taking builder one; with the builders private, the client method returns it and no method should be left without an error path.
- Timeouts: RolloutStatus takes a string and reads "" as RolloutTimeout; gdu 12 adds timeouts to Delete and DeleteNamespace and leaves parameter against options struct open; Result.Duration is a time.Duration. Settle one type and one meaning of zero, and name the delete default to match RolloutTimeout.
- Upload ends in a positional mkdir bool and silently overwrites o.Stdin and o.TTY (kube/kube.go:219); a narrower options struct (Namespace, Container, Mkdir) says what it really takes.
- Exec has no place for stdin bytes: B sets cmd.Stdin after building (internal/k8s/transport.go:79). Client.Exec needs an input for it, and a stated attached form (design 3.2 last sentence).
- Output hook against captured reads: design 3.2 hands every chunk to the hook, but Get -o json, the namespace read and can-i are answers the tools capture and do not show today (B internal/k8s/inspect.go:67, A internal/runner/runner.go:859), and two of them carry Secret data. The breaking tag should say which methods bypass the hook.
- NamespaceState: a failed read is NamespaceUnknown with no error today; design 3.1 has the method return state, result and error. Settle whether a refused read is the Unknown state, a typed error, or both.
- Three spellings of a refusal: kube.Forbidden(out), the planned kube.ErrForbidden, and *rbac.RefusedError. The tag should leave one way to ask (errors.Is) plus the classifier for tool-built commands.
- MissingNamespace takes base only to print the command; once a tool holds a Client it is the one helper still needing the raw tokens.
- LogOptions.Tail and Since are passed as given; gdu 14 leaves open whether Logs refuses a bad value now that it can return an error.

### kube/rbac

- **Probe** (type, type; A14 B91): keep; by:argument of rbac.Run. The question both tools build lists of (which is tool content: what a write needs) and the argument of the planned rbac.Run.
  - In M: kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:61 (DeleteProbe); throughout kube/rbac/rbac.go
- **Probe.String** (method, pure-helper; A0 B0): keep. The Stringer is how a probe appears in M's own errors and in B's skip row; removing it would change that text to a struct dump.
  - In M: kube/rbac/rbac.go:138 (RefusedError.Error), kube/rbac/rbac.go:165 and :170 (Check), through %s
  - Reached: B formats a Probe with %s in a report row (internal/k8s/checkreport.go:666); both tools print it inside every refusal message
- **ClusterScoped** (func, pure-helper; A0 B3): keep. B needs it alone in a step that stays in the tool: putting its own namespace on a namespaced probe before asking (preflight.go:81-86). The fixed seven-resource list is a limit for a third tool, which can set AllNamespaces instead.
  - In M: kube/rbac/rbac.go:44 (Probe.clusterWide)
  - Reached: A reaches it through CanI for its persistentvolumes probe (internal/runner/runner_test.go:1330)
- **CanI** (func, operation-builder; A4 B2): unexport-covered-by-client; by:rbac.Run. Both tools build it only to run it and read the verdict (A CheckProbe, B preflightOne and canI), which is exactly rbac.Run. A's test at runner_test.go:1330 and B's preview echo move to the runner (design 3.3 rule 6). Code refs A/B: 2/1.
- **Answer** (func, parser; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only; by:rbac.Run. Neither tool names it; only Verdict and Check call it. The value a tool may want is already RefusedError.Answer.
  - In M: kube/rbac/rbac.go:119 (Verdict), kube/rbac/rbac.go:153 (Check)
  - Reached: both tools reach it through Check, and B also through Verdict
- **Refused** (func, parser; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only; by:rbac.Run. Neither tool names it; a tool tells a refusal by errors.As on *RefusedError.
  - In M: kube/rbac/rbac.go:123 (Verdict), kube/rbac/rbac.go:157 (Check)
  - Reached: both tools reach it through Check, and B also through Verdict
- **Verdict** (func, parser; A0 B2): unexport-covered-by-client; by:rbac.Run. B's one caller (canI) runs the probe and reads it without failing; rbac.Run's result carries the same two bits (nil is allowed, *RefusedError is answered and refused, anything else is unanswered), so B derives them with errors.As. Once private nothing in M calls it, so it is deleted with TestVerdict rather than kept. Code refs A/B: 0/1.
- **RefusedError** (type, type; A6 B8): keep; by:returned by rbac.Run (catalogue 3.4). The catalogue names it as what rbac.Run returns, and both tools branch on it with errors.As to choose between a stop and a warning. A's test reads its Probe field (runner_test.go:1224). Code refs A/B: 3/6.
  - In M: kube/rbac/rbac.go:158 (Check)
- **RefusedError.Error** (method, other; A0 B0): keep. Makes the kept type an error; its wording (what to ask a cluster admin for) is the shared part both tools print unchanged.
  - Reached: through the error interface: A prints it in its namespace warnings (cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go:79), B in its kept-namespace warning (internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:809)
- **Check** (func, parser; A5 B3): unexport-covered-by-client; by:rbac.Run. The parser of CanI's answer, called in both tools on the line after the run; rbac.Run does both. B's test at prep_test.go:28 uses it to make a refusal and can construct &rbac.RefusedError directly. Code refs A/B: 1/2.
  - In M: named in the nsgate package doc only (kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:13)

#### kube/rbac: missing

- The table from an object's kind to the plural resource (and its scope) a probe asks about, and the probe list derived from the objects a tool writes.
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:414 (kindResources), internal/runner/runner.go:437 (KubernetesProbes)
  - B: none as a table: resource names are written by hand per probe (internal/k8s/deploy.go:136-158, internal/k8s/operator.go:312-326)
  - Proposal: Once M renders the documents (8.2) each kind knows its resource and scope; rbac (or the documents package) offers the probes for a verb over a set of documents or objects, and ClusterScoped reads the same table. One tool only, plainly generic; owner's call.
- A typed 'unanswered' result. Both tools find it only as 'an error that is not *RefusedError', and B special-cases the preview runner around the probe.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go:72
  - B: internal/k8s/prep.go:64-66, internal/k8s/preflight.go:62-70 (isEcho branch)
  - Proposal: rbac.Run returns nil, *RefusedError, or an error matching one unanswered sentinel (design 3.3 rule 2), also under runner.Echo (rule 6), so a tool words a preview without knowing the runner.

#### kube/rbac: placement

- ClusterScoped: knowledge about resource scope; if the document types of 8.2 carry kind, resource and scope, the table belongs with them and rbac reads it.
- The login hint inside Check (kube/rbac/rbac.go:165-167) is generic 'no working context' wording that every kube.Client method's unanswered error needs too; it belongs in kube, with rbac wrapping it.
- CanI and Check: fold into rbac.Run in this package, as the catalogue says; nothing moves out.

#### kube/rbac: signatures

- Check takes four positional values (p, stdout, stderr, runErr) and the tools disagree on stderr: A passes what it captured (internal/runner/runner.go:409), B passes "" because stderr already streamed (internal/k8s/preflight.go:74). rbac.Run should own the capture so the message is the same in both.
- Check's two unanswered errors are untyped fmt.Errorf values (kube/rbac/rbac.go:165, :170); design 3.3 rule 2 asks for a typed unanswered.
- Under runner.Echo, exit 0 with an empty answer lands in Check's 'wrapper changed the output' branch (kube/rbac/rbac.go:170), which is the wrong message for a preview; rule 6 wants 'unknown'.
- Two reading styles: Verdict returns two bools, Check returns an error. With Run there is one, the error.
- *RefusedError against kube.ErrForbidden: decide whether errors.Is(err, kube.ErrForbidden) is true for a *RefusedError, so a tool asks 'refused?' one way for a probe and for a refused command.
- CanI returns no error and places Verb, Resource, Name and Namespace as given (gdu 4 lists it); Run should check them and return the error.
- CanI builds its argv by hand (make and append, kube/rbac/rbac.go:81-92) while kube uses internal/build.Cmd; use the one helper when it goes private.
- An empty Probe.Namespace means the context's namespace and the refusal then says 'the current namespace'; B fills the namespace in first to avoid that (internal/k8s/preflight.go:81-86). Decide whether Run takes a default namespace or the tools keep doing it.

### kube/nsgate

- **Object** (type, type; A27 B3): move-package, owner call. A uses it as its general kind-and-name reference outside the gate: what a deploy wrote (deploy.go:806), the input to its probe list (runner.go:437), its generate result (internal/gen/gen.go:216). The rendered documents (8.2) and the gdu 18 compare of listed against rendered names need the same type, so it belongs in kube (or the documents package), with nsgate taking it from there.
  - In M: kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:93 (Occupants parameter), :136 and :159 (discounted, ownedBy)
- **Protected** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. A pure judgement both tools call as the first gate step; the gate's order stays in the tools, so no executor covers it.
- **List** (func, operation-builder; A5 B4): unexport-covered-by-client; by:nsgate.Read. Each tool calls it once, runs it and hands the answer to Occupants, which is nsgate.Read. gdu 1 widens the listing from kubectl api-resources, so one listing becomes more than one command, which only an executor can hide (3.3 rule 1). Code refs A/B: 1/1.
- **DeleteProbe** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. The one probe the gate needs, which each tool passes to its own probe step before asking; that step stays in the tool and becomes rbac.Run(DeleteProbe()).
- **Occupants** (func, parser; A3 B3): change-signature; by:reads the answer of nsgate.Read; stays pure (catalogue 3.4). The catalogue keeps it exported and pure, but gdu 1 adds a label discount for this tool and this instance, which (out, owned) cannot express. Proposal: Occupants(listing, Owned{Objects []Object; Product, Instance string}), returning []Object rather than joined strings. Code refs A/B: 1/1.

#### kube/nsgate: missing

- Running the listing and wording its failure: the planned nsgate.Read. Both tools wrap List themselves, and only B tells 'no answer' (a preview) from an unreadable listing.
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:871 (KubernetesListJSON)
  - B: internal/k8s/namespace.go:57-66
  - Proposal: nsgate.Read through a kube.Client, as catalogued, returning the listing for Occupants and a typed unanswered error when the runner answered nothing, so B's empty-output branch (namespace.go:63) and A's missing one become the same behaviour.

#### kube/nsgate: placement

- Object: belongs in kube (or the documents package), see its entry; nsgate would import it.
- List: becomes the private builder or builders behind nsgate.Read; the widened listing of gdu 1 (api-resources, then get) stays inside this package.
- DeleteProbe: fine here; it is the gate's own fixed probe and already ties nsgate to rbac.

#### kube/nsgate: signatures

- The package doc's step list (kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:10-15) names List, rbac.Check and kube.DeleteNamespace, all three of which go private; rewrite it to Read, rbac.Run and Client.DeleteNamespace.
- List returns no error and places ns unchecked (gdu 4 lists it); Read checks the name and returns the error.
- List builds its argv by hand (kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:53-56), like rbac.CanI; use internal/build.Cmd when it goes private.
- Occupants takes the listing as a string while B holds bytes and converts (internal/k8s/namespace.go:67); kube.ParseNamespace takes a string too. Pick one type for captured answers across the three packages.
- Occupants treats an empty listing as an error ('it is kept'), while kube.ParseNamespace treats an empty answer as Absent; under runner.Echo (design 3.3 rule 6) the empty case should surface from Read as unanswered, not as Occupants' error.
- Occupants takes []Object and returns []string in lower-case kind/name form; returning []Object keeps one type through the gate and the gdu 18 compare, and leaves the wording to the tool.
- A nil owned means 'count everything' (kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:90); with a struct parameter for the label discount, say the same for an empty product or instance.

### Verifiers for the kube group

Usage re-count: 39 names counted, 1 corrections.

- kube.RolloutTimeout: A 0, B 3. Only class difference found, and only under a code-use reading. The reviewer's 2/4 is exact as a mention count (comments included, as its signatureNotes state) and its own reason already says code refs 0/3. A has no code use: its two hits are comments at A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/lifecycle.go:121 and internal/runner/runner.go:253; A reaches the value by passing "" to kube.RolloutStatus at internal/runner/runner.go:262. B code uses: B:internal/k8s/ops.go:144, ops.go:187, operator_test.go:1746; the fourth B hit is a comment at ops.go:149. Search: grep -rnP '(?<![A-Za-z0-9_])kube\.RolloutTimeout(?![A-Za-z0-9_])' --include=*.go over A and B.

Rule challenge: 39 names examined, 6 challenges.

- **[medium] kube.Get**: unexport-covered-by-client, with the fallback that Get stays exported for captured reads if the owner does not want a capture-only form -> unexport-covered-by-client with no fallback: Client.Get returns the captured stdout as its data and keeps it from the hook; a shown table is a second method or a Client value with a showing hook
  - The fallback is not a workable option under rules 1 and 3, so it should not be offered to the owner. Captured reads are most of the use: both of A's (internal/runner/runner.go:841, :859, through runParsed) and every parsed read in B (internal/k8s/inspect.go:66 is the funnel for all getJSON callers; prep.go:353, :378; adminsecret.go:78; deploy.go:74; matechannel.go:152). Only B's shown tables stream (internal/k8s/ops.go:20-26, operator.go:633-638) plus its preview echoes (statusreport.go:378, :432, :452). If Get stayed exported for the captured ones, both tools keep their run-capture-classify wrappers (A runParsed, B outKube, outTeeKube, getJSONTee) for the majority of reads, and those reads miss the pinned Base (gdu 11), the operand check gdu 4 puts in the method, and ErrForbidden and ErrNotFound, which is exactly where A string-matches NotFound and Forbidden (cmd/solmq-conn-util/secretprobe.go:39-41) and B tests Forbidden (internal/k8s/adminsecret.go:162, deploy.go:76). Result in design 3.2 has no output field, so a Get that returns only Result hands a tool no data at all: returning stdout follows from 3.3 rule 2 (returns data), it is not an extra form. Client is a plain struct the tool fills in (3.1), so the shown case needs no special form either.
- **[medium] kube.Exec**: unexport-covered-by-client, provided Client.Exec offers three ways to run; otherwise the builder stays exported -> unexport-covered-by-client with the forms as part of the tag, and four of them: streamed, captured, stdin bytes, and attached with Stdin and TTY taken separately
  - Same objection to the fallback: an exported builder would leave B's whole broker transport (internal/k8s/transport.go:50-82: Run, Output, OutputInput), the mate channel (internal/k8s/matechannel.go:88, :98) and A's status script (internal/runner/runner.go:1203-1207) on tool-held tokens outside the client. The mate channel is the case gdu 11 names for a second pin, and its pod names are the ones that reach kube.Exec checked only for being non-empty (docs/todo.md:1101-1103), so it is the call that most needs the method's own name check. The reviewer's list of ways is also one short: A's one-shot `cli -- <command>` attaches with no terminal, with -i only when stdin is piped, and hands over the process's own stdin stream, not bytes (cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:252, :256, :271). An attached form described as a terminal session would not cover it.
- **[low] kube.Forbidden**: keep (owner's call), as the classifier for tool-built commands -> unexport-covered-by-client, if kube.Client gets an exported way to run a tool-built argv; keep only if that run logic goes into an internal package
  - rbac.Run and nsgate.Read live in other packages and run 'through a kube.Client' (design 3.4). They cannot reach unexported run logic in kube, so kube either exports a generic run (Base plus arguments, hook, Result, typed errors) or the logic moves to an internal package. If it is exported, the commands that are the whole reason to keep Forbidden (A top pod, internal/runner/runner.go:887-899; B describe internal/k8s/ops.go:31, :36, set env watch.go:456, cp transport.go:106, :116, named deletes prep.go:283, deploy.go:215) run through it and return ErrForbidden, and the reviewer's own note 'leave one way to ask' is met with one way instead of two. This fact changes the owner's call and is not in the reason given.
- **[low] kube.ValidLabel**: keep -> rename (for example ValidDNSLabel, with the new subdomain grammar as ValidDNSSubdomain)
  - gdu 1 brings Kubernetes metadata labels into M: one label vocabulary with key names and a value grammar (docs/todo.md:89-90, :108-110). Beside that, kube.ValidLabel reads as 'a valid label value' when it is the DNS-1123 name grammar, and the two grammars differ (a label value allows upper case, '_' and '.': A internal/validate/validate.go:1313). A already holds both side by side (kube.ValidLabel at validate.go:904, its private label rule at :1330). The breaking tag changes every builder call site anyway; this name has 7 call sites in A and none in B, so the rename is cheapest now.
- **[low] kube/nsgate.Protected**: keep, in nsgate -> move-package to kube (nsgate keeps calling it), and Client.DeleteNamespace refuses a protected name itself
  - kube.DeleteNamespace's doc says 'Run it only through the gate in kube/nsgate' (kube/kube.go:107-108) and nothing enforces it. Once it is an exported client method offered to a third tool through the guide, refusing default, kube-system, kube-public and kube-node-lease inside the executor is the same kind of backstop as gdu 4's name refusal (a tool that forgets its own check still cannot, docs/todo.md:247-249). It is not orchestration: it asks nothing and chains nothing. kube cannot import nsgate, because nsgate will import kube for Read, so the predicate has to sit in kube. The tools' first gate step stays for its wording (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go:98, B internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:792).
- **[low] kube/nsgate.DeleteProbe**: keep -> change-signature: DeleteProbe(ns string), asking about the one namespace
  - DeleteProbe() is delete on namespaces with no name (kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:61), and CanI turns every namespaces question into --all-namespaces (kube/rbac/rbac.go:44, :62-66, :86-88). So to delete one namespace the gate asks for delete on every namespace, and a refusal tells the operator to get a cluster role binding (rbac.go:51-52). A request about one namespace is evaluated in that namespace too, which is the reason both tools read it by name (B internal/k8s/prep.go:35-40), so an identity granted delete on its own namespace alone is refused by the probe although the delete would pass. The package's own rule is to ask only what the write needs (rbac.go:6-8). The fix needs the name on the probe and CanI not forcing --all-namespaces when a namespaces probe names its object. The error is in the safe direction, and it wants one hand check on a cluster, but the signature can only change in the breaking tag.

Additions:

- (missing) kube: a generic run for a command M does not offer. rbac.Run and nsgate.Read sit in other packages and must run through a kube.Client (design 3.4), so kube needs an exported method that runs Base plus given arguments with the hook, Result and typed errors, or an internal package holding that logic. Exported, it also carries the remaining tool-built commands on the pinned Base (gdu 11): B describe (internal/k8s/ops.go:31, :36, operator.go:652), set env (watch.go:456), cp (transport.go:106, :116), get sc (check.go:82, :100); A top pod (internal/runner/runner.go:887-899). B's kubectl and kubectlOutput (internal/k8s/cluster.go:126-132, :200-206) then go. Without it each tool keeps the run-capture-classify wrapper that design section 2 sets out to remove. Owner's call.
- (missing) kube and nsgate: the namespace's own label (gdu 1, docs/todo.md:79-82, :93-95). The gate chooses one question or two from it. The gate listing does not return the Namespace object, and the namespace read asks -o name (kube/kube.go:83), so a read is missing (a richer NamespaceState, or a labels read) and so is the pure judgement 'labelled as created by this tool'. Neither tool has either today.
- (missing) nsgate: the question decision as a pure function. Design section 1 counts the namespace gate's judgement among the pure functions, and gdu 1 fixes the table: labelled, one yes; unlabelled, a yes and then the typed name; --no-prompt deletes only with --delete-namespace; no terminal keeps (docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:108-110, :183-185). Both tools code this step themselves (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go:129-130, B internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:812), and the package doc's step 4 still says --no-prompt approves (kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:14). It decides and does not ask, so it fits M. Owner's call.
- (missing) kube: the parser and the compare of gdu 18 (docs/todo.md:1009, :1018-1026): ListLabelled's answer as []Object, and a pure function for listed minus rendered. Two traps for it: `-o name` prints a lower-case resource with its group for non-core kinds (deployment.apps/x), which Object.matches' fold on the kind (kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:33-35) does not equate with Deployment; and a partial listing exits 1 after printing what it could list (docs/todo.md:1021-1022), which must be an error with no names. The method fixes its output to names, never -o json (decisions.md:203).
- (missing) kube: the label pieces the renderer and the listing need (gdu 1, docs/todo.md:89-90, :108-110): the product-and-instance selector, and the Kubernetes label key and value grammar (63 characters, alphanumeric ends), which is not ValidLabel's grammar. A keeps a private copy (internal/validate/validate.go:1313, used at :1330, :1346, :1445). The reviewer lists the subdomain grammar only.
- (missing) kube: a typed timeout. gdu 12 words an expired delete as 'requested and still in progress' and has the gate keep the namespace with that warning (docs/todo.md:675-677), and RolloutStatus calls a timeout a real result (kube/kube.go:131-132). Design 3.3 rule 2 names refused, not found and unanswered only. Neither tool can tell a timeout from a failed rollout today: B says 'did not become ready within 300s' for any failure (internal/k8s/ops.go:144, :187), A 'did not finish rolling out' (cmd/solmq-conn-util/lifecycle.go:140). Add the error, and the shared delete wording, for RolloutStatus, Delete, DeleteNamespace and the named delete.
- (missing) kube: kubectl wait (gdu 17). The item was left to solace-util because it is 'the only tool that manages a StatefulSet' (docs/todo.md:917-918), which is the two-consumer reasoning rule 5 discards. `wait --for=condition=Ready pod/<pod> -n <ns> --timeout=<t>` is a common operation, so if the owner's cluster check answers OnDelete it is a client method, not a tool-built command. Owner's call.
- (missing) rbac: the kind-to-resource table is not 'one tool only'. gdu 18 has both tools probe delete for each kind that has a leftover (docs/todo.md:1029-1031) and the PV cluster-wide (:1002-1004), starting from listed kind and name pairs. Both then need the table and its scope, which only A has (internal/runner/runner.go:414-420). A decided item requires it.
- (missing) kube: a Secret's key names without its values. A reads a whole Secret as JSON only to check that keys exist (cmd/solmq-conn-util/secretprobe.go:36, read by internal/statusreport/parse.go:478), which brings every value into the process. B reads a name and one annotation by jsonpath to keep the private key out (internal/k8s/prep.go:350-353). One read that returns key names only would be the secret-safe shared form. One tool today; owner's call.
- (placement) Import order. nsgate already imports rbac (kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:25), and rbac.Run and nsgate.Read will import kube. So kube must import none of rbac, nsgate or the documents package, and two of the reviewer's proposals cannot both hold: the scope table 'with the document types, and rbac reads it' makes rbac import the documents package, and 'rbac (or the documents package) offers the probes' makes the documents package import rbac if that side is chosen. Put Object and the kind, resource and scope table in kube, the probes-from-objects helper in rbac, and let the documents package import kube.
- (placement) Object: kube, not the documents package. Client.ListLabelled (gdu 18) returns kind and name pairs, and kube should not import the rendering package for its own return type.
- (placement) Result, the hook type and the unanswered error are shared by the four clients, rbac.Run and nsgate.Read. One definition, in runner (design 3.2 already writes runner.Stream; runner has no such type today), not an unanswered sentinel in each of kube, rbac and nsgate as the three missing lists read.
- (placement) The label vocabulary of gdu 1 is not a kube matter alone: compose services, quadlet units and podman secrets carry the same keys (docs/todo.md:104-107). Keys and values in one small package that kube, the documents package, compose, quadlet and podman all import; only the selector and the Kubernetes value check sit with kube.
- (placement) The tail-count check of gdu 14 (docs/todo.md:750) serves kube.LogOptions and containercli.LogOptions alike, so it belongs in neither package alone.
- (signature) The hook of design 3.2 is func(stream, chunk): it does not say which operation a chunk belongs to. The tools capture and do not show can-i (A internal/runner/runner.go:407-409, B internal/k8s/preflight.go:73), the namespace read (A runner.go:269, B internal/k8s/prep.go:52) and parsed gets (A runner.go:841-859, B internal/k8s/inspect.go:66-67). A tool cannot keep those out of its output per operation, only per Client value. The tag should say that parsed reads withhold stdout from the hook, or the guide should tell a tool to hold a second Client with a quiet hook.
- (signature) nsgate.Read's answer holds every Secret and ConfigMap of the namespace in full: the listing asks -o json (kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:55-56), while Occupants reads only kind, type and metadata (nsgate.go:63-74). Read must never pass that stdout to the hook or into an error (3.3 rule 5). A's wrapper today appends the captured output to the listing error and prints it (internal/runner/runner.go:86-90, :871-873; cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go:106), and a partial listing prints what it could list before it exits 1 (docs/todo.md:1021-1022). The widening of gdu 1 is the moment to ask for metadata only, as decided for the label listing (decisions.md:203).
- (signature) ErrForbidden must be classified from stderr alone. Forbidden(out) matches ' is forbidden: ' anywhere (kube/kube.go:144-146), and for Logs, Exec and Get the stdout is workload or object text that can hold those words, which would turn a real failure into a refusal and so into a warning. A passes merged output today (cmd/solmq-conn-util/lifecycle.go:136, from Combined at internal/runner/runner.go:262); B passes stderr only (internal/k8s/ops.go:158-159).
- (signature) Rule 6 says a read under runner.Echo reports unknown. ParseNamespace reads an empty answer as Absent (kube/kube.go:93-94), because of --ignore-not-found, and B relies on it: 'a preview takes the first-deploy path' (internal/k8s/prep.go:44-45). Client.NamespaceState cannot tell an Echo from an absent namespace by the output. State the exception to rule 6, or say how the client knows. The same holds for any Get with IgnoreNotFound.
- (signature) rbac.Run under Echo: B passes a probe in a preview (internal/k8s/preflight.go:62-70 returns nil; :113-118 answers yes) and stops on an expired login. If Run returns one unanswered error for both, B still has to ask engine.IsEcho (internal/k8s/check.go:26-28), so the proposal's 'a tool words a preview without knowing the runner' holds only if 'exit 0 with no answer' and 'failed with no verdict' stay distinguishable, as Check separates them today (kube/rbac/rbac.go:159, :170).
- (signature) Cancel: A ends a followed log cleanly when its own context is cancelled, by errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) (cmd/solmq-conn-util/logs.go:304-306). Client.Logs, and every method, must keep the runner's cause in the chain (3.3 rule 5) and must not turn a cancel into the unanswered error.
- (signature) ExecOptions after the split: Stdin and TTY mean something only for the attached form. Without a terminal, -i follows from stdin bytes being given (B sets the flag and the bytes in two places, internal/k8s/transport.go:55, :79), as Upload already forces them (kube/kube.go:219). The attached form takes them apart: A attaches with no terminal and with -i only when stdin is piped (cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:252, :271).
- (signature) Parallel shapes with containercli.Client. Both tools switch platform over exec, logs and upload in one place: A internal/runner/runner.go:1067 and :1074, :1127 and :1139, :1170 and :1175; B one broker.Transport interface with a Kubernetes and a container implementation (internal/k8s/transport.go:58-99, internal/container/transport.go:77-118). kube.Client.Exec, Logs and Upload and containercli.Client's should take and return the same shapes. The builders differ today: kube.Upload(base, pod, o, dest, body, mkdir) against containercli.Upload(cmd, target, path, body, mkdir).
- (signature) GetOptions has no --no-headers, so B builds `get sc <name> -o custom-columns=... --no-headers` and `get sc -o jsonpath=...` by hand, outside kube.Get (internal/k8s/check.go:82, :100), and then asks can-i to learn whether the failed read was a refusal (internal/k8s/checkreport.go:665). With Get private, add the field so these reads go through Client.Get and its ErrForbidden.
- (signature) Error style: M's typed errors are struct types found with errors.As (kube/rbac/rbac.go:131 RefusedError, envref/envref.go:52 UnsetError, confirm/prompter.go:139 NotTerminalError), and CR-0001 removes the one predicate helper, confirm.IsNotTerminal. The design's example is a sentinel with errors.Is (3.6). Pick one style across the four clients. A struct that carries the operation and kubectl's words (3.3 rule 5) can still answer errors.Is for a sentinel.
- (signature) Result on failure: tools word errors from the argv. B strips the base to print 'run `kubectl ...` yourself' (internal/k8s/inspect.go:78) and names the program in a session's exit status (internal/engine/runner.go:178). So each method returns a filled Result beside a non-nil error, and Result.Argv keeps Base as its prefix.
- (signature) Occupants: if it returns []Object, Object needs a String (lower-case kind/name). A prints each occupant as one string (cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go:117) and B cuts the string apart again (internal/k8s/namespace.go:82). Take product and instance as the label type that Meta and ListLabelled take, not two bare strings, so the gate, the selector and the renderer cannot drift.
- (signature) Two ways to learn that an object is absent: A reads kubectl's NotFound from a get without the flag (cmd/solmq-conn-util/secretprobe.go:39), B passes IgnoreNotFound and reads an empty answer (internal/k8s/prep.go:353, :378; statusreport.go:91), which under Echo looks the same as absent. With ErrNotFound planned, say which one Client.Get offers, or that ErrNotFound arises only without the option.
- (signature) Client form: design 3.1 writes kube.Client as a value, while podman.Client keeps state (the version read once per run, 3.3 rule 1). Choose one form for all four clients, and have rbac.Run and nsgate.Read take kube.Client in that form.

### notice

- **Width** (const, constant; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Neither tool names it and only wrap and the in-package tests read it, so it is the same class as the unused exports CR-0001 ruled on name by name. docs/developer.md:86 already states the 80-column wrap in prose; a tool that wanted to assert widths would need it exported, and none does.
  - In M: notice/notice.go:106 (wrap); notice/notice_test.go:92 and :100 (same package)
  - Reached: Tools see it only as the line length of what Lines, Markdown and Short return; no tool reads the number.
- **Notice** (type, type; A1 B2): keep. Both tools hold a value of it and noticetest takes it as a parameter. Nothing in the client or rendering design touches it.
  - In M: notice/noticetest/noticetest.go:40, :117, :165, :174 (parameter type); docs_test.go:14 (through New)
- **New** (func, pure-helper; A1 B2): keep. The only constructor, used by both tools and by M's own doc gate. Every caller wraps it in a hand-written must function (see missing).
  - In M: docs_test.go:14; paritydoc/paritydoc_test.go:14; notice/noticetest/noticetest_test.go:17
- **Notice.Lines** (method, pure-helper; A3 B0): keep. A's two doc generators emit it line by line and A's test feeds it to AssertASCII (support_test.go:45). It is also the body of Markdown.
  - In M: notice/notice.go:132 (Markdown); notice/notice_test.go:70, :87, :170
  - Reached: B reaches it through Notice.Markdown (notice/notice.go:132).
- **Notice.Markdown** (method, pure-helper; A0 B3): keep. B's three doc generators write it (third site: internal/cli/abbrevdoc_test.go:102) and noticetest.Check is built on it.
  - In M: notice/noticetest/noticetest.go:41 (Check); paritydoc/paritydoc_test.go:18; notice/noticetest/noticetest_test.go:25
  - Reached: A reaches it through noticetest.Assert and AssertDocs, whose Check calls it (notice/noticetest/noticetest.go:41).
- **Notice.Plain** (method, pure-helper; A1 B0): keep. One consumer (A's HTML generator page check), which rule 5 no longer disqualifies. It is generic to any non-Markdown copy of the notice, and a tool cannot rebuild it because the sentence (text) is unexported.
  - In M: notice/notice_test.go:174, :191, :233 (tests only)
- **Short** (func, cli-helper; A3 B0): keep. A's version command prints it and two A tests pin it (third site: support_test.go:46). B's version command (internal/cli/version.go:39-42) prints the same first line without it, so the gap is in B, not in M (see missing).
  - In M: notice/notice_test.go:176, :203, :217 (tests only)

#### notice: missing

- A must constructor for the notice. Every consumer wraps notice.New in its own panic-on-error function because the arguments are constants: one copy in A, two identical copies in B, and M's own doc gate does the same with t.Fatal (docs_test.go:14-17).
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/support.go:32-38 (mustNotice)
  - B: internal/cli/commanddoc_test.go:78-84 and internal/broker/importdoc_test.go:88-94 (mustNotice, duplicated)
  - Proposal: notice.Must(name, kind, covers string) Notice, panicking with the New error, in the style of regexp.MustCompile. Owner call: shared code may not call os.Exit, and a panic on constant arguments is a new shape for M; the alternative is to leave the three wrappers where they are.
- The version command's output. Both tools print the same first line (name, version, Go version, os/arch). A then prints notice.Short under it; B prints nothing more, so B's binary carries no support statement at all.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1190-1196 (actVersion)
  - B: internal/cli/version.go:39-42 (newVersionCmd RunE, no notice.Short)
  - Proposal: At the least B adopts notice.Short in its version command (a tool change, and a parity row). If the owner wants the whole block common, package notice gains one function that returns the version line plus the short notice from name and version; the Go and platform words come from runtime, which a pure function may read. Owner call.

#### notice: placement

- No name in notice sits in the wrong package.
- Short is a package function while every other rendering is a Notice method; that is right, because the short form names no tool (notice/notice.go:149).

#### notice: signatures

- Counting note for all three packages: countA and countB are code references in .go files, tests included; mentions in comments (for example A cmd/solmq-conn-util/support.go:12, :20, :21) are not counted. Neither tool imports these packages under an alias.
- coveredBy is empty for every name in the three packages: none is a command builder or the parser of a command's answer, so no client method of design note 3.4 applies and rule 3 (unexport a covered builder) decides nothing here.
- New is the only function that returns an error; Lines, Markdown, Plain and Short cannot fail. Consistent, no change needed.
- A writes the notice with Lines() in a loop (abbreviation.go:78, commands.go:619); B writes Markdown() + "\n" (commanddoc_test.go:124). Both forms are needed, but the caller owns the blank line either side in both cases; a single doc line on Markdown already says so (notice.go:129-130).
- The guide and the example tool (design note 9.3 step 3) should show New, Markdown and Short: a third tool passes its own name and covers clause, but the wording stays "Solace Professional Services" (notice/notice.go:90-94, :150-151), which fits only Solace PS tools. That matches the Solace-values gap in design note 9.2 and is an owner matter, not a signature change.

### notice/noticetest

- **Options** (type, type; A2 B1): keep. Both tools pass it. Field use: Strict B only (commanddoc_test.go:98) plus M (docs_test.go:18); Files A only (support_test.go:47); Exclude has no consumer in A, B or M's doc gate, only noticetest_test.go:208-234 (see signatureNotes).
  - In M: docs_test.go:18; parameter of Check, CheckDocs, Assert, AssertDocs (noticetest.go:40, :117, :165, :174)
- **Check** (func, test-helper; A0 B0): keep, owner call. It is the pure rule behind Assert, kept exported so the rule is testable without a fake testing.T and usable by a tool that wants the error rather than a test failure. By the letter of the rules it is internal-only (the tests are in-package and would still compile), so the plan review's question 6.2.1 on the Check functions behind the Assert ones is the owner's to close.
  - In M: notice/noticetest/noticetest.go:154 (CheckDocs) and :167 (Assert); noticetest_test.go (same package)
  - Reached: A reaches it through Assert (support_test.go:59) and AssertDocs; B through AssertDocs (commanddoc_test.go:98).
- **CheckDocs** (func, test-helper; A0 B0): keep, owner call. Same case as Check: the error-returning form behind AssertDocs, named in docs/consuming.md:91. If it stays exported, its nothing-checked error (noDocsError, noticetest.go:102) should be detectable by a caller; today only AssertDocs can tell it apart.
  - In M: notice/noticetest/noticetest.go:176 (AssertDocs); noticetest_test.go (same package)
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through AssertDocs (A support_test.go:47, B commanddoc_test.go:98).
- **Assert** (func, test-helper; A1 B0): keep. A checks its two generated docs from the renderer's bytes, before they are written. One consumer is enough under rule 5, and the guide lists noticetest among the tests a new tool runs (design note 9.3 step 3).
  - In M: notice/noticetest/noticetest_test.go:315, :316 (tests only)
- **AssertDocs** (func, test-helper; A1 B1): keep. The doc gate of both tools and of M itself. Its argument order (n before root) is the reverse of CheckDocs (root before n); see signatureNotes for the optional fix.
  - In M: docs_test.go:18; notice/noticetest/noticetest_test.go:290-309
- **AssertASCII** (func, test-helper; A2 B0): keep, owner call. A uses it on both notice forms; lifted from A (docs/test.md:144). It knows nothing about notices, so the owner may prefer it in the test-support package that todo item 5 plans (docs/todo.md:330); until that package exists it stays here.
  - In M: notice/noticetest/noticetest_test.go:322, :332 (tests only)

#### notice/noticetest: missing

- A plain-ASCII gate over a whole rendered output. AssertASCII takes lines without newlines and rejects every control byte, so A hand-rolls the byte loop for whole outputs twice. B has no such gate.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/completion_test.go:427-428 and internal/statusscript/statusscript_test.go:67-68
  - B: none found (grep for ascii over B's test files shows no gate)
  - Proposal: One tool only, but plainly generic and M's own rule (plain ASCII everywhere). A second assert that takes []byte and allows LF, beside AssertASCII, in noticetest or in the test-support package of todo item 5. Owner call.
- The drift gate for a generated doc: render, compare with the committed file, and rewrite it under an update switch. Both tools write it per doc, with a package-level -update flag each time.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/commands_doc_test.go:25-43 (TestCommandsDocInSync, flag at :15)
  - B: internal/cli/commanddoc_test.go:31-48 (TestCommandDocs, flag at :23) and internal/broker/importdoc_test.go:29-44
  - Proposal: A helper in the test-support package of todo item 5 that takes the path, the rendered bytes and the update switch as a parameter (docs/developer.md:32-33: never a package flag), normalises CRLF and reports through the shared diff function. The flag stays in each tool. Owner call: this widens item 5 beyond its one diff function.

#### notice/noticetest: placement

- AssertASCII is not about notices; it fits the test-support package todo item 5 plans beside artifacttest and noticetest (docs/todo.md:330). Owner call, and only once that package exists.
- Check, CheckDocs, Assert, AssertDocs and Options sit in the right package.

#### notice/noticetest: signatures

- Argument order differs across the four checks: Check(doc, n, o), CheckDocs(root, n, o), Assert(t, name, doc, n, o), but AssertDocs(t, n, root, o). The breaking tag can make it AssertDocs(t, root, n, o); cost is one call site in A (support_test.go:47), one in B (commanddoc_test.go:98) and one in M (docs_test.go:18). The types differ, so the compiler catches a missed site.
- Options.Exclude has no consumer: A lists Files, B and M use the default glob, and only noticetest_test.go:208-234 sets it (docs/consuming.md:91 documents its v0.1.3 behaviour). Owner call: remove the field in the breaking tag or keep it for a third tool.
- Options mixes two scopes: Check reads only Strict and silently ignores Files and Exclude; CheckDocs reads all three. A doc line on Check would do, or Check could take the strict flag alone.
- The zero Options is the weaker, non-strict mode. A runs every doc non-strict (support_test.go:47, :59) because its generated docs carry a comment between the H1 and the notice; B and M run strict. The parity body could state which mode a tool's hand-written docs must pass.
- noDocsError (noticetest.go:102) is unexported, so a direct caller of CheckDocs cannot detect the nothing-checked case with errors.As; only AssertDocs can (noticetest.go:180-183). Export a sentinel or type if CheckDocs stays exported.
- Errors from Check carry no package prefix and do not name the doc (Assert and CheckDocs add the name), while notice.New and paritydoc.Check prefix theirs with the package name. Harmless, but the three packages are otherwise read together.

### paritydoc

- **Title** (const, constant; A0 B0): unexport-internal-only, owner call. Once Compose is removed (CR-0001) nothing outside the package can use the title: tools only call Check, and the pin script keeps each tool's own first lines (scripts/dev-all.sh:90). CR-0001 did not list it, so the owner confirms; it also names exactly two tools, the gap design note 9.2 records.
  - In M: paritydoc/paritydoc.go:50 (Compose) and :63-64 (Check); paritydoc_test.go:84, :100-103, :117-123 (same package)
  - Reached: Both tools reach it through paritydoc.Check, which compares line 1 of their docs/parity.md with it (paritydoc.go:63).
- **Body** (func, pure-helper; A0 B0): remove. Decided: CR-0001 removes it in the breaking tag (docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:68). The package doc (paritydoc.go:6), the comment on the embedded variable (:21-22) and Check's doc (:55) still name Body and are reworded in the same change; the in-package tests switch to the variable.
  - In M: paritydoc/paritydoc_test.go:43, :64, :66, :84, :113 (tests only; Check reads the unexported var body directly)
- **Compose** (func, pure-helper; A0 B0): remove. Decided: CR-0001 removes it in the breaking tag. Four user-facing messages tell the reader to regenerate the page with paritydoc.Compose(notice.Markdown()); they must be reworded in the same change (the pin task of dev-all is what composes the page), and the tests that build a page with it need a local helper.
  - In M: paritydoc/paritydoc_test.go:32, :75, :200, :213 (tests only). Named in remedy text at paritydoc/paritydoc.go:60 and :71, scripts/dev-all.sh:114 and scripts/dev-all.ps1:134.
- **Check** (func, pure-helper; A1 B1): keep. Both tools hold their docs/parity.md to the shared body with it, and CLAUDE.md names it as the check. The design changes the body's text (new rows and rules), not this function.
  - In M: paritydoc/paritydoc_test.go:79-204 (tests only); named in docs/consuming.md:24

#### paritydoc: missing

- One diff function for a golden or doc mismatch (todo item 5, scope decided, shape undecided). A has three copies of lineDiff, B two of firstDiff, and M a third wording in the unexported firstDifference (paritydoc/paritydoc.go:102).
  - A: internal/deploy/deploy_test.go:362, internal/gen/golden_test.go:578, internal/render/render_test.go:275 (lineDiff)

Rendering moving into M (design note section 8) changes every golden in both tools once, so this is the tag to land it in.
- The test that reads docs/parity.md and fails on a paritydoc.Check error. Both tools write the same eight lines; noticetest gives an Assert wrapper for the notice, paritydoc gives none for the body.
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/support_test.go:96-105
  - B: internal/cli/commanddoc_test.go:107-115

#### paritydoc: placement

- firstDifference and clip (paritydoc/paritydoc.go:102, :122, unexported) are the module's only line-diff code and belong in the test-support package of todo item 5 once it exists, with paritydoc calling it.
- paritydoc as a whole is the contract of exactly two tools (Title, and the body's own text); design note 9.2 lists it as a gap for a third tool and 9.3 does not resolve it. Owner call: state in the new-tool guide that a third tool does not run paritydoc.Check, or split the body into a generic contract and the pair's differences.
- Check and the embedded body sit in the right package.

#### paritydoc: signatures

- Removing Compose leaves stale remedies: paritydoc/paritydoc.go:39, :43, :47 go with the function, but :60 and :71 (inside Check), scripts/dev-all.sh:114 and scripts/dev-all.ps1:134 stay and must name what replaces it (scripts/dev-all.sh pin, or pasting the notice under the title).
- Check hard-codes the path "docs/parity.md" and the prefix "paritydoc:" in every message. A prints the error bare (support_test.go:103); B prefixes its own path first (commanddoc_test.go:113), so B's failure names the file twice. Either Check drops the path and callers add it, or a CheckFile owns it (see missing).
- Check takes only the doc, while noticetest.Check takes (doc, n, o): paritydoc judges the notice's position and shape but not its wording (paritydoc.go:56). That split is deliberate and both tools run both checks on docs/parity.md (A lists it in supportNoticeDocs, support_test.go:27; B's strict glob covers docs/*.md).
- After the two removals the exported surface is Check alone (plus Title if the owner keeps it). The package doc's first paragraph (paritydoc.go:5-7) describes the page as Title, notice, then Body, and needs the same rewording.
- Design note section 5 says a contract rule may name the module's executor instead of each tool's wrapper, so body.md changes in the breaking tag; Check's byte-for-byte rule means both tools must take the new body through the pin step in the same adoption round.

### Verifiers for the docs group

Usage re-count: 17 names counted, 0 corrections.


Rule challenge: 17 names examined, 2 challenges.

- **[medium] notice/noticetest.AssertASCII**: keep -> move-package
  - The reviewer keeps it in noticetest 'until that package exists'. That premise does not hold: docs/todo.md:28-29 puts items 4 to 14 in the one breaking tag, so the test-support package of item 5 (docs/todo.md:330) and of item 8 (docs/todo.md:455) is created in that same tag, and the reviewer's own paritydoc entry says the diff function lands in this tag. Deferring the move means a second break after the tag that is meant to carry every break. The function knows nothing about notices (notice/noticetest/noticetest.go:197-206), and its place already costs M two copies of the loop: package notice cannot import noticetest (noticetest.go:18 imports notice, a cycle), so notice/notice_test.go:157-167 hand-rolls it, and paritydoc/paritydoc_test.go:47-51 hand-rolls it again with LF allowed. Rendering in M (design note 8.2) adds compose, quadlet and manifest renderer tests that want the same gate, and importing notice/noticetest from those packages is a wrong-way dependency. Move it in the breaking tag, together with the []byte form that allows LF (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/completion_test.go:427-428, A internal/statusscript/statusscript_test.go:66-68). Cost: A cmd/solmq-conn-util/support_test.go:45-46 and the row at M docs/test.md:144. The destination package name stays the owner's call; the timing does not.
- **[low] notice/noticetest.Options**: keep -> change-signature
  - The type stays, but its zero value is the weak mode and it already fails open in the one tool that relies on it. A cmd/solmq-conn-util/support_test.go:47 passes Options{Files: ...} without Strict over eight hand-written docs; the first lines of each are H1, blank line, alert, so strict would pass there. The reviewer's note that A runs non-strict 'because its generated docs carry a comment' is true only for :59 (A docs/commands.md:1-8 shows the GENERATED comment between H1 and notice); at :47 the weak mode is in force by omission. M's own rule is 'directly under the H1' (project CLAUDE.md), and a third tool that follows the guide gets the weak check from Options{}. In the one breaking tag either invert the field (zero value strict, an explicit opt-out for a generated-file comment) or drop the mode by moving A's comment below the notice (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/abbreviation.go:70-80, commands.go:612-621). Call sites: A support_test.go:47 and :59, B internal/cli/commanddoc_test.go:98, M docs_test.go:18. On the reviewer's 'remove Exclude' option: keep it. A's allow-list (support_test.go:19-28) leaves docs/test.md unchecked, and that file carries no notice (A docs/test.md:1-3, no match for the notice sentence in the file); the default glob with a deny-list would have caught it.

Additions:

- (signature) notice.Must: the reviewer calls a panic on constant arguments 'a new shape for M'. It is not. abbrev.New panics on a bad declaration and its doc gives the regexp.MustCompile reason (abbrev/abbrev.go:57-75). M today has two conventions for a declaration in source: abbrev.New panics, notice.New returns an error that all six callers treat as fatal (A support.go:32-38, B commanddoc_test.go:78-84, B importdoc_test.go:88-94, M docs_test.go:14-17, M paritydoc/paritydoc_test.go:12-19, M notice/noticetest/noticetest_test.go:15-22). Adding Must beside New follows the existing precedent; the owner call is only which of the two shapes is the module's convention.
- (signature) Unexporting notice.Width leaves four comments naming an identifier a reader can no longer see: notice/notice.go:16, :97, :120 and :154 (the exported docs of Lines and Short say 'wrapped at Width'). Reword them to '80 columns' in the same change; docs/test.md:118 already says 80 columns.
- (signature) Unexporting paritydoc.Title together with removing Compose leaves no API and no remedy that gives a tool the title text when it starts a page. The empty-page error (paritydoc/paritydoc.go:60) and the line-3 error (:69-71) do not print it; only the line-1 error does (:64). pin cannot be the remedy the reviewer suggests for the script's own messages: pin dies on exactly that state (scripts/dev-all.sh:111-114, scripts/dev-all.ps1:132-134) and its sync keeps the tool's own head lines (dev-all.sh:90), so it never writes or corrects line 1. The reworded remedies must spell out the page head: the title text, a blank line, notice.Markdown(), a blank line, then run pin.
- (signature) Removing Body and Compose also removes two tests and their catalogue rows, which the reviewer does not list: TestBodyIsACopy (paritydoc/paritydoc_test.go:63-69) and TestComposeRoundTrip (:199-217), rows docs/test.md:151 and :158, and the package count at docs/test.md:41. The page helper (:30-37) and TestCheckAcceptsEitherToolsNotice (:75) need a local composition. TestComposeRoundTrip was the only Go test tying composition to Check; after the removal the composition lives only in the two scripts (dev-all.sh:86-100, dev-all.ps1:102-117), checked only when both tools' tests run after pin.
- (signature) The shared diff function (todo item 5) has to pick an argument order and type; the copies disagree: A lineDiff(want, got string) string (A internal/gen/golden_test.go:578), B firstDiff(got, want []byte) string (B internal/cli/commanddoc_test.go:52), M firstDifference(got, want []string, offset int) error (paritydoc/paritydoc.go:102). M's Check functions put the subject first, so (got, want) matches the module.
- (signature) Content type across the test-support packages: noticetest and paritydoc take []byte (noticetest.go:40, :165; paritydoc.go:57), artifacttest takes string (containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:354-382). Design note 8.2 (line 251) makes a renderer 'a typed value in and bytes out', so every artifacttest call would convert. Settle one type in the breaking tag, with the diff function and the ASCII assert.
- (signature) noticetest.CheckDocs with default Options fails a repo that has README.md but no docs/*.md: the empty glob returns noDocsError (noticetest.go:119-127) before README.md is appended (:136). The example tool of design note 9.3 step 3 is likely README-only, so it must pass Options.Files or the default must accept README.md alone. Decide which before the guide is written.
- (signature) notice.New's kind check accepts any lower-case word, but its error says pass "tool" or "module" (notice/notice.go:58-66). Either the set is closed (two constants) or the message should say 'one lower-case word such as'. Minor, but the guide will show this call.
- (signature) Counting cross-check: the plan review's inventory marks notice.Notice and notice.New as (B) only (docs/review/2026-11-13-plan-review.md:463, :465). A names both (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/support.go:32-33), so the reviewer's counts are right and the inventory is stale on these two.
- (placement) Items 5 and 8 each say 'a test-support package' (docs/todo.md:330, :455) without saying whether it is one package or whether it imports testing. That decides the reviewer's placement note for firstDifference: if paritydoc calls the shared diff function (docs/todo.md:336) and that package imports testing for Assert-style helpers, paritydoc, which imports only embed, errors, fmt and strings today (paritydoc/paritydoc.go:10-15), pulls testing into every importer. The pure diff function needs a home free of testing, or paritydoc keeps its own.
- (placement) M has a second first-difference reporter the reviewer does not list: noticetest.describe (notice/noticetest/noticetest.go:70-97) walks the notice line by line and prints got and want, in a third wording beside paritydoc.firstDifference. If item 5 gives M one diff function, describe is a candidate caller too.
- (placement) noticetest.Check and CheckDocs are the same case as artifacttest's five Check functions, with the same sentence in both package docs (noticetest.go:3-4, containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:6-7). The owner call the reviewer raises is one decision for all seven names, not one per package. The reviewer's second reason for keeping them, a tool that wants the error outside a test, is weak because noticetest imports testing (noticetest.go:16); symmetry with artifacttest and with paritydoc.Check is the real reason.
- (missing) A's docs/test.md carries no support notice and no gate looks at it: it is outside supportNoticeDocs (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/support_test.go:19-28) and opens with H1, blank line, prose (A docs/test.md:1-3). B's and M's docs/test.md carry it. A tool-side gap for the adoption round, and the evidence behind keeping Options.Exclude.
- (missing) The parity body holds no rule on the notice or on version output: a search of paritydoc/body.md finds no line with 'notice', 'version' or 'support', and its sections are Contract, Divergences, Decisions, Inherent differences and Runner seams (body.md:11, :141, :160, :192, :205). The 'parity row' the reviewer proposes for B's version command would be the first such rule, so it should state all three points at once: which docs carry the notice, that it sits directly under the H1, and that version prints notice.Short under its first line.
- (missing) The drift-gate helper is wider than the reviewer's three doc tests. Package-level update flags: B has six (internal/cli/commanddoc_test.go:23, internal/broker/importdoc_test.go:17, internal/convert/convert_test.go:17, internal/examples/examples_test.go:18, internal/k8s/secrets_test.go:20, internal/render/render_test.go:22), A three (cmd/solmq-conn-util/commands_doc_test.go:15, internal/gen/htmlgolden_test.go:23, internal/gen/golden_test.go:35). Two of the doc gates print no diff at all (A commands_doc_test.go:39-42, B importdoc_test.go:41-43). With rendering in M the artifact goldens move into M's own tests, which must take the update switch as a parameter (docs/developer.md:32-33), so M is a third consumer of the helper and the widening of item 5 is forced by section 8, not optional.
- (missing) The []byte ASCII assert has consumers inside M, which the reviewer's entry (A twice, B none) omits: notice/notice_test.go:157-167 and paritydoc/paritydoc_test.go:47-51 each hand-roll it with LF allowed, and M's future renderer tests need it for every artifact.
- (missing) If the version block becomes a function in M, it can also print the module version: the module will read its own version for the lib annotation (docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:158-160), and neither tool's version output names the module it was built with. It must go under the first line, which a script reads and A pins (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1190-1193). Owner call.
- (missing) notice.Must does not remove B's real duplication: the covers clause is declared twice, in two test packages, because a test cannot import another package's test (B internal/cli/commanddoc_test.go:73-74, B internal/broker/importdoc_test.go:80-84). B's notice lives only in _test files, so B's binary could not print the long form at all. The fix is in B: one non-test declaration, as A has (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/support.go:28).

## 2. Operations

A runs 48 distinct external command shapes, B runs 87.

### 2.1 Client methods

- **containercli.Client.Info** (used by both)
  - argv: `<engine> info`
  - Returns: Result. Nothing is parsed; both tools drop the engine's stdout.
  - Typed errors: One class, unreachable: wraps the run error and carries containercli.EngineHint(engine, rootless). Neither tool tells refused or not found apart here.
  - Notes: The client's set-up must hold the engine name and the rootless flag for the hint (A internal/runner/runner.go:391-399; B internal/container/preflight.go:28-48). A puts the captured output into the error, B lets stderr stream and passes out as "". Under runner.Echo it passes and B prints its own skipped row. First call of every engine verb in both tools. No stdin, env or terminal.
- **containercli.Client.List** (used by both)
  - argv: `<engine> ps --all --no-trunc --format '{{.Names}}\t{{.Image}}\t{{.Status}}'`
  - argv: `<engine> ps --all --no-trunc --filter status=running --format '{{.Names}}\t{{.Image}}\t{{.Status}}'`
  - Returns: []containercli.Row (ParseList inside) and Result. HasName, ByImage and ValidName stay exported pure functions.
  - Typed errors: Unanswered: the listing could not run. B aborts a docker deploy on it (container/manager.go:984-992); A errors (runner.go:206-209). Under Echo: no rows and no error.
  - Notes: Names are matched in Go, never with --filter name=. The post-remove check of gdu 9 (is the container gone) is List plus HasName in the tool. Rows are engine text, so the tool passes them through inspect.Printable before showing them.
- **containercli.Client.Inspect** (used by both)
  - argv: `<engine> container inspect <names...>`
  - Returns: []inspect.Container decoded, in engine order, and Result. gdu 10 adds the ID of each Config.Secrets entry. B's status --detail prints the raw JSON (container/manager.go:1379-1382): return the raw bytes as well, or B runs that one through Run.
  - Typed errors: Not found: a name the engine does not know (non-zero exit, or an empty array; B builds this error itself in container/inspect.go inspectContainer). Unanswered or undecodable: A reads it as digest "" = changed (runner.go:213-219), B as a warning in status.
  - Notes: The raw answer holds the container's environment, so its stdout must not go to the hook (owner question 1). With no names nothing runs.
- **containercli.Client.ComposeUp** (used by both)
  - argv: `<compose> -f - -p <project> --project-directory <dir> up -d [--force-recreate]`
  - Returns: Result, with stdin as a byte count and every env value masked.
  - Typed errors: None: failed or ok. Neither tool classifies.
  - Notes: Needs compose tokens beside the engine tokens: A derives them as docker.command plus 'compose' (runner.go:179); B has its own guarded docker.compose (container/manager.go:878). stdin = the compose file. Cmd.Env = the secret values and the deploy-digest variable; B's engine.NoEnv guard must not wrap this client. -p is new (gdu 9, 19). --force-recreate: B only (--restart); A always passes false. Can pull images, so it runs long and its output is progress for the hook. ComposeDigest stays a pure exported function.
- **containercli.Client.ComposeDown** (used by both)
  - argv: `<compose> -f - -p <project> --project-directory <dir> down`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: None. It exits 0 for a project it cannot find, so 'gone' is the tool's own check with List afterwards (gdu 9).
  - Notes: stdin = the compose file rendered afresh; Cmd.Env = a placeholder for every secret, in both tools. Neither tool passes -p today (A runner.go:185; B container/manager.go:1135).
- **containercli.Client.Start, Stop, Restart** (used by both)
  - argv: `<engine> start <name>`
  - argv: `<engine> stop <name>`
  - argv: `<engine> restart <name>`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: None used by either tool.
  - Notes: Docker only in both tools; on podman both go through systemd.Client (A runner.go:284-321; B container/manager.go:1170-1203). The name grammar of gdu 4 (ValidName) belongs inside.
- **containercli.Client.Logs** (used by both)
  - argv: `<engine> logs [-f] [--timestamps] [--tail <n>] [--since <t>] <name>`
  - Returns: Result when the stream ends.
  - Typed errors: None. A cancelled context must stay reachable with errors.Is(err, context.Canceled): A turns it into exit 0 under --follow (cmd/solmq-conn-util/logs.go:304-309). --previous is refused before any run with containercli.NoPrevious.
  - Notes: Unbuffered, stdout and stderr kept apart (A: logs > file). The executor must keep no copy of the stream, which is unbounded under -f. A needs a pass-through hook for this call, not its buffer hook (owner question 2). B installs no signal handler for it. The tail check of gdu 14 stays a shared pure function.
- **containercli.Client.Exec (and ExecOutput)** (used by both)
  - argv: `<engine> exec <name> <argv...>`
  - argv: `<engine> exec -i <name> <argv...>`
  - Returns: Result with the child's exit code. Two forms are needed: output to the hook (B containerTransport.Run), and stdout returned to the caller (B Output and OutputInput, container/transport.go:77-99; A captures both streams merged, runner.go:1207).
  - Typed errors: None at this layer. A non-zero child exit is an error that carries the code: B's shell-script verb reports the process status, A reads any non-zero as 'the exec failed'.
  - Notes: No '--' on an engine. Optional stdin bytes add -i: B feeds a CLI script body or a curl config holding a credential, so stdin shows as a byte count only. A runs up to 4 at once (cmd/solmq-conn-util/status.go:920-923), so the hook is per call or safe for concurrent use. No terminal. What runs inside the container is the tool's.
- **containercli.Client.Attach** (used by both)
  - argv: `<engine> exec -i -t <name> <argv...>`
  - argv: `<engine> exec [-i] <name> <argv...>`
  - Returns: Result with the child's exit code, and a nil error once the session ran.
  - Typed errors: Only 'the session never ran'. The child's code is the tool's to pass on (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:271-275; B exitcode.Exited then exitcode.ChildExit, internal/cli/ops_container.go:366-367).
  - Notes: Catalogue correction: the catalogue's single Exec covers this and the non-attached form. Hands over the three terminal files; no context, no hook. A's one-shot form has no -t and sets -i only when stdin is not a terminal (shell.go:245-254), so both flags stay options. SIGINT handling and the Windows VT mode stay in the tool. Under Echo the code is 0.
- **containercli.Client.Upload** (used by both)
  - argv: `<engine> exec -i <name> sh -c "[mkdir -p '<dir>' && ]cat > '<dest>'"`
  - Returns: Result, stdin as a byte count.
  - Typed errors: None.
  - Notes: stdin = the file body: A's status script; B's certificate bundle with a private key, CLI scripts, a whole configuration. mkdir: A true (runner.go:1170), B false (container/transport.go:111).
- **containercli.Client.ComposeVersion (add)** (used by B)
  - argv: `<compose> version`
  - Returns: Result. The version text is not read today.
  - Typed errors: One class, compose unavailable. The error should carry the hint B words today: install the compose plugin, or point the compose command at docker-compose (container/manager.go:333-340).
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue. B validate only. A has no compose probe: a missing plugin shows only at compose up, after the redeploy question (runner.go:179-189).
- **containercli.Client.Login (add)** (used by B)
  - argv: `<engine> login --username <user> --password-stdin [<registry>]`
  - Returns: Result, stdin as a byte count.
  - Typed errors: None: failed or ok.
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue. stdin = the registry password, never argv (B container/manager.go:455-479). It changes the engine's credential store on the host, so a tool runs it after its questions (gdu 3). A relies on credentials the engine already holds.
- **containercli.Client.CopyTo, CopyFrom (add)** (used by B)
  - argv: `<engine> cp <local> <name>:<dest>`
  - argv: `<engine> cp <name>:<remote> <local>`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: None; B counts the failures per file.
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue (B container/transport.go:119-136). The local path is a free operand: refuse empty, a leading '-' and control characters (gdu 4). The container name takes the name grammar.
- **containercli.Client.InspectImage (add)** (used by A)
  - argv: `<engine> image inspect <ref>`
  - Returns: A decoded image: RepoDigests, Config.Entrypoint, Config.Cmd; and Result.
  - Typed errors: Not found: the engine does not have the image, so A pulls and asks again (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:831-846; today every failure reads as not found). Undecodable.
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue, and design section 4 names it as tool-built (owner question 7). One decoded read replaces A's two shapes: the --format template at runner.go:335 (deploy with created stores) and the plain inspect at runner.go:934 (status --details). The ref of the second comes back from the engine, so the builder checks it.
- **containercli.Client.Stats (add)** (used by A)
  - argv: `<engine> stats --no-stream --format '{{.Name}}\t{{.CPUPerc}}\t{{.MemUsage}}\t{{.MemPerc}}' <names...>`
  - Returns: Rows (name, CPU percent, memory use, memory percent) and Result.
  - Typed errors: None: any failure is a note in A's report.
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue (A runner.go:942-963, status --details). A template is used because docker and podman disagree on the JSON shape; that is engine knowledge, not connector knowledge. It samples for an interval, so it is slower than a metadata read.
- **containercli.Client.Run, Output (add, generic)** (used by both)
  - argv: `<engine> <args the tool built>`
  - argv: `A: <docker> image pull <image>`
  - argv: `B: <engine> version`
  - Returns: Run: Result, output to the hook. Output: stdout bytes and Result.
  - Typed errors: Never started, and the child's exit code. No text classification.
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue. It carries the commands the module does not offer (decision 3) through the same base tokens, hook, Result and masking (A runner.go:356; B container/manager.go:329). Without it each tool keeps its own capture wrapper beside the client.
- **podman.Client.Version** (used by both)
  - argv: `<podman> version --format '{{.Client.Version}}'`
  - Returns: The client version, read once and kept for the run.
  - Typed errors: Unanswered. Both tools then take the remove-then-create path and count every secret as changed; it is never an error (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:956-957; B container/manager.go:622-632).
  - Notes: stdout alone: podman can warn on stderr first. The kept value needs state behind a pointer, so a copied client shares it. Once CreateSecret and SecretMatches gate inside the client, no tool calls it (see unusedMethods). Under Echo it reads nothing, so a preview shows the older pair, which B does on purpose today (container/manager.go:590-592).
- **podman.Client.CreateSecret** (used by both)
  - argv: `podman 4.7 and later: <podman> secret create --replace [--label k=v]... <name> -`
  - argv: `below 4.7 or version unread: <podman> secret rm --ignore <name>, then <podman> secret create [--label k=v]... <name> -`
  - Returns: Result of the last command run.
