  - Typed errors: No class beyond failed, but the error must say which step failed, and that a create failing after the rm leaves the secret gone until the next deploy. Both tools word the two steps apart today (A runner.go:508-517; B container/manager.go:602-616).
  - Notes: stdin = the value (a credential, a config file, a store, a private key). Neither tool passes a label today; gdu 1 and 16 make every create pass them, because --replace sets exactly the labels passed. The fallback choice moves in from both tools (design 3.3 rule 1).
- **podman.Client.RemoveSecrets** (used by both)
  - argv: `<podman> secret rm --ignore <names...>`
  - Returns: Result. With no names nothing runs.
  - Typed errors: None: --ignore absorbs 'already gone', so any failure is real. The policy stays in the tools: B fails a remove only for the secret that holds the private key, after trying the rest (container/manager.go:1080-1118); A reports the error only when the unit removal succeeded (main.go:1050-1054).
  - Notes: A passes many names in one call; B one name per call, so a failure belongs to a name. Keep it variadic. gdu 9: only after the unit is confirmed gone.
- **podman.Client.SecretMatches** (used by both)
  - argv: `<podman> secret inspect --showsecret --format '{{.SecretData}}' <name> (podman 4.7 and later only)`
  - Returns: matches bool, true only when the stored value is exactly the new one; and Result.
  - Typed errors: None needed. Absent, unreadable, different, podman below 4.7 and Echo all read as 'changed' in both tools (A runner.go:545-556; B container/manager.go:550-563). A not-found split would be new behaviour.
  - Notes: The stored value arrives on stdout and must reach neither the hook, the Result nor an error. Below 4.7 the method runs nothing. It takes the new value and compares with podman.SecretMatches inside.
- **podman.Client.SecretLabels** (used by neither)
  - argv: `<podman> secret ls --format <names>`
  - argv: `<podman> secret inspect --format <name and Spec.Labels> <names...>`
  - Returns: name -> labels for the secrets in the store; the tool filters on product and instance.
  - Typed errors: Partial or failed listing (inspect exits non-zero when a name is gone): the tool warns, deletes nothing and continues (gdu 16).
  - Notes: No caller today; gdu 16. secret ls cannot filter by label, so the operation is two commands and the catalogue names no listing: fold both into this method, or add a names method. The exact templates are for the step's plan.
- **podman.Client.SecretIDs** (used by neither)
  - argv: `<podman> secret inspect --format <name and ID> <names...>`
  - Returns: name -> the store's current ID.
  - Typed errors: Not found or partial (a named secret is gone): counts as changed.
  - Notes: No caller today; gdu 10. Compared by a pure function with the IDs Inspect decodes from Config.Secrets. Whether the IDs behave as podman's source says is still an owner check (todo item 10).
- **podman.Client.IDMap** (used by both)
  - argv: `<podman> info --format '{{range .Host.IDMappings.UIDMap}}{{.ContainerID}}:{{.Size}} {{end}}'`
  - argv: `the same with GIDMap`
  - Returns: The mapping text for rootless.CheckIDMap (pure), and Result.
  - Typed errors: Unanswered (the read failed): both tools stop with 'cannot read the range'. An empty answer is an answer: no allocation at all (B container/rootless.go:216-236).
  - Notes: Rootless and a numeric run-as only. Both tools also run it in validate; A passes no approvals there (cmd/solmq-conn-util/rootless.go rootlessHostErrors).
- **podman.Client.Unshare (add)** (used by B)
  - argv: `<podman> unshare <argv...>`
  - argv: `B: chmod 775 <dataDir> ; chown <runUser> <dataDir> ; find <dataDir> -mindepth 1 -delete ; sh -c 'test -w "$0"' <dataDir>`
  - Returns: Result with the child's exit code.
  - Typed errors: None; B reads the test as a bool from the exit code (container/rootless.go namespaceWritable).
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue (B container/manager.go:432, 438, 1153; container/rootless.go:439). The wrapper is engine-generic; what runs inside the user namespace is B's. The chown, chmod and find change the host, so they follow the questions (gdu 3).
- **systemd.Client.DaemonReload** (used by both)
  - argv: `systemctl [--user] daemon-reload`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: None.
  - Notes: Like every method here it carries the client's user mode and session env. Both tools run it after a write and after the unit file is removed (A runner.go:758, 804; B container/manager.go:839, 1061). gdu 10: a digest mismatch forces it before the restart.
- **systemd.Client.Start, Stop, Restart** (used by both)
  - argv: `systemctl [--user] start <unit>`
  - argv: `systemctl [--user] stop <unit>`
  - argv: `systemctl [--user] restart <unit>`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: None used today. Stop on a unit that is not loaded exits 5 (todo item 9): expose it through Result.ExitCode or as not found, so a repeat remove can be idempotent. B follows a failed stop with State and goes on only when systemd.Stopped holds (container/manager.go:1026-1051); A returns (runner.go:793-797).
  - Notes: Deploy: start when not running, restart when running and changed. Lifecycle verbs on podman use these, not the engine.
- **systemd.Client.State** (used by both)
  - argv: `systemctl [--user] is-active <unit>`
  - Returns: (state word, answered bool) and Result. systemd.Active and systemd.Stopped stay pure.
  - Typed errors: Unanswered = nothing on stdout. The exit code is ignored on purpose, since it is non-zero for every state but active; a stopped unit is never an error.
  - Notes: stdout alone; stderr and the run error are dropped (A runner.go:738-740; B container/manager.go:967-970). A deploy reads unanswered as not running; B's remove refuses on it.
- **systemd.Client.LoadState** (used by neither)
  - argv: `systemctl [--user] show <unit> -p LoadState --value`
  - Returns: The load state word (loaded, not-found, masked and so on).
  - Typed errors: Unanswered.
  - Notes: No caller today; gdu 9. The same command as NRestarts with another property, so a typed wrapper over Show.
- **systemd.Client.NRestarts** (used by both)
  - argv: `systemctl [--user] show <unit> -p NRestarts --value`
  - Returns: (count, known). An empty or non-numeric answer is unknown.
  - Typed errors: None: both tools fall back, A to the container's own count, B to an 'unknown' row (A runner.go:992-1002; B container/inspect.go:95-110).
  - Notes: Status path. A asks once per instance on every run and tick; B only for the container its env file names.
- **systemd.Client.Linger** (used by both)
  - argv: `loginctl show-user <euid> --property=Linger`
  - Returns: yes, no, or whatever logind said; and Result.
  - Typed errors: Unanswered (logind did not answer, or the user has no session): both tools stop a deploy and fail validate.
  - Notes: loginctl takes no --user but needs the session env. A builds its error from the command line and both streams (runner.go:700-709), so Result must come back on failure too.
- **systemd.Client.EnableLinger** (used by both)
  - argv: `loginctl enable-linger`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: None beyond failed: both tools then name 'sudo loginctl enable-linger <user>' for an administrator and stop (A runner.go:687-693; B container/rootless.go:364-374).
  - Notes: Names no user on purpose (least privilege). Deploy only, never validate. A host change, so it belongs after the questions (gdu 3).
- **systemd.Client.Show (add)** (used by B)
  - argv: `systemctl [--user] show --property=Version`
  - argv: `systemctl show user@<euid>.service -p LimitNOFILE -p LimitNOFILESoft -p LimitMEMLOCK -p LimitCORE -p DelegateControllers`
  - Returns: property -> value, and Result.
  - Typed errors: Unanswered (a run error, or nothing printed; B skips the row on an empty answer, container/limits.go:182-198).
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue. NRestarts and LoadState are the same command with one property. The second shape asks the SYSTEM manager about a system unit and must not carry --user, so a rootless run needs a second client value in system mode. Which properties, and what they must be, is B's.
- **systemd.Client.Run (add, generic)** (used by B)
  - argv: `systemctl [--user] status <unit> --no-pager`
  - argv: `systemctl [--user] cat <unit>`
  - Returns: Result; output to the hook.
  - Typed errors: None: a non-zero exit is a warning in B.
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue. B builds these with the generic systemd.Systemctl (container/manager.go:1329, 1375). They must go through the client to get the user mode and the session env. B also builds a status argv only to print it in an error (container/manager.go:1049): Result.Argv or a builder that stays exported covers that.
- **kube.Client.CurrentContext** (used by B)
  - argv: `<kubectl> config current-context`
  - Returns: The context name, checked before it goes onto an argv.
  - Typed errors: No context set (kubectl in a pod on its service account): the tool then calls WhoAmI. Unanswered.
  - Notes: Reads the kubeconfig and contacts nothing. B only displays it today (internal/cli/context.go:254-264); gdu 11 makes both tools resolve it once and pin --context=<name> into the base tokens. It resolves with the tokens it is given, so B's mate channel sets up a second client for the other site's command. Finding --context in the tokens and adding the pin are pure helpers.
- **kube.Client.WhoAmI** (used by neither)
  - argv: `<kubectl> auth whoami`
  - Returns: The identity the run acts as.
  - Typed errors: Refused; unsupported (a cluster older than 1.28); unanswered. All three warn and continue (decided 2026-11-15).
  - Notes: No caller today; gdu 11.
- **kube.Client.Apply** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> apply -f -`
  - Returns: Result, stdin as a byte count.
  - Typed errors: None used: any failure stops the deploy.
  - Notes: stdin = the manifest (Secret values, private keys), never argv or disk. A scans this call's own output for 'persistentvolume/<x> configured' (main.go:1807-1818), so it needs the output of this one call apart from the rest (owner question 2). B applies the Namespace, the Secrets and the custom resource in separate calls (internal/k8s/cluster.go:189-191).
- **kube.Client.Delete** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> delete -f - --ignore-not-found --timeout=<t>`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: Timed out: the delete was accepted and continues in the cluster; the tool says so and the gate keeps the namespace (gdu 12). Neither tool classifies today, and neither passes a timeout.
  - Notes: stdin = the teardown manifest. A default constant beside RolloutTimeout; the owner times one real broker remove first (gdu 12).
- **kube.Client.NamespaceState** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> get namespace <ns> --ignore-not-found -o name`
  - Returns: kube.NamespaceState (exists, absent, unknown) and Result.
  - Typed errors: None surfaced: a failed or refused read is Unknown and the create probe decides (A runner.go:268-274; B internal/k8s/prep.go:51-57).
  - Notes: stdout alone. gdu 1 also needs the namespace's own labels, to choose one question or two (owner question 12).
- **kube.Client.DeleteNamespace** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> delete namespace <ns> --ignore-not-found --timeout=<t>`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: Timed out (gdu 12). Every failure is a warning in both tools and the namespace is kept (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go removeNamespace; B internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:823-825).
  - Notes: Only after the gate, in nsgate's order. ns is a bare operand, so the grammar check of gdu 4 belongs inside.
- **kube.Client.Scale** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> scale <kind>/<name> [-n <ns>] --replicas=<n>`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: None.
  - Notes: A: deployment, 0 to stop and the env.yaml count to start (runner.go:241-243). B: statefulset 1 and 0, and the operator deployment (internal/k8s/ops.go:178-204, operator.go:608-618).
- **kube.Client.RolloutRestart** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> rollout restart <kind>/<name> [-n <ns>]`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: None.
  - Notes: A: its deployment (runner.go:245). B: the operator deployment only; B restarts broker pods by deleting them.
- **kube.Client.RolloutStatus** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> rollout status <kind>/<name> [-n <ns>] --timeout=<t>`
  - Returns: Result; progress to the hook.
  - Typed errors: Refused: a warning and a success in both tools (parity rule 16; A cmd/solmq-conn-util/lifecycle.go:131-140 on merged output, B internal/k8s/ops.go:157-165 on stderr). Timed out or a failed rollout: an error in both; a separate timed-out class lets the wording be exact.
  - Notes: An empty timeout means kube.RolloutTimeout (300s). It blocks for up to the timeout. gdu 17: kubectl refuses it for an OnDelete StatefulSet; B's replacement wait is its own, run through Run.
- **kube.Client.DeleteNamed (replaces DeletePod)** (used by B)
  - argv: `<kubectl> delete <kind> <name...> [-n <ns>] --ignore-not-found --timeout=<t>`
  - argv: `B today: delete pod -n <ns> <pod> ; delete secret <name> -n <ns> ; delete pvc <claim> -n <ns>, each with --ignore-not-found`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: Timed out; refused. The policy stays in B: the first Secret failure stops its loop (internal/k8s/prep.go:281-285); each PVC failure is warned, collected and joined (internal/k8s/deploy.go:213-221).
  - Notes: Catalogue correction. B builds all three by hand with no timeout (ops.go:138, prep.go:283, deploy.go:215). gdu 18 has both tools delete labelled leftovers by name: Secrets, A's Service, its libs PVC, and the PV cluster-wide.
- **kube.Client.Get** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> get <what...> [--all-namespaces | -n <ns>] [-l <selector>] [--ignore-not-found] [-o <output>]`
  - argv: `A: get pods [<names...>] [--all-namespaces | -n <ns>] [-l app=<name>] -o json ; get <secret|deployment|service|configmap|persistentvolumeclaim> <name> [-n <ns>] -o json`
  - argv: `B: get <kind> [<name>...] (-n <ns> | --all-namespaces) [--ignore-not-found] -o json ; get secret <name> -n <ns> --ignore-not-found -o jsonpath=<name and one annotation> ; get pod <pod> -n <ns> --ignore-not-found -o name ; get sc -o jsonpath=<default class> ; get sc <name> -o custom-columns=V:<field> --no-headers`
  - Returns: stdout bytes for the tool's own decoder, and Result.
  - Typed errors: Refused: B warns on it at deploy.go:76 and adminsecret.go:162, A in its Secret probe. Not found, when --ignore-not-found is not set: A stops the deploy on it (cmd/solmq-conn-util/secretprobe.go:38-41, matched today on the loose text 'NotFound' or 'not found'). Unanswered: exit 0 with nothing on stdout where a document was asked for, which is B's preview case (deploy.go ConfirmBrokerApplied).
  - Notes: With --ignore-not-found an empty answer means absent, so empty cannot be an error there. GetOptions has no --no-headers: B's custom-columns read (internal/k8s/check.go:100) moves to jsonpath, or the option is added. A reports any failed component read as MISSING, refused included (status.go:582); the typed errors let it say which. A's get secret -o json puts every value on stdout: use SecretKeys.
- **kube.Client.ListLabelled** (used by neither)
  - argv: `<kubectl> get <kind,kind,...> -n <ns> -l <product label>=<tool>,<instance label>=<instance> -o name`
  - Returns: kind/name lines.
  - Typed errors: Refused or partial (kubectl prints the kinds it could list and exits 1): a failed listing; warn, delete nothing, continue (gdu 18).
  - Notes: No caller today; gdu 18. Names only, never the content of a Secret. The -l selector has one live user today (A status, runner.go:841).
- **kube.Client.Logs** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> logs <target> [-n <ns>] [-c <container>] [-p] [-f] [--timestamps] [--tail <n>] [--since <t>]`
  - Returns: Result when the stream ends.
  - Typed errors: None; a cancelled context stays reachable with errors.Is, as for the engine's Logs.
  - Notes: A: a pod with -c connector (runner.go:1122-1127). B: pod/<pod> and deployment/<operator> (internal/k8s/ops.go:41-44, operator.go:644-647), so the target is a free operand (gdu 4). Streamed, never copied.
- **kube.Client.Exec (and ExecOutput)** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> exec [-i] <pod> [-n <ns>] [-c <container>] -- <argv...>`
  - Returns: Result with the child's exit code; ExecOutput also returns stdout.
  - Typed errors: None at this layer; a non-zero exit carries the code. kubectl gives 'pod not reachable' and 'the command exited 1' the same status, so no not-found class is promised.
  - Notes: The same three uses as the engine's Exec: stream (B kubectlTransport.Run), capture stdout (B Output), stdin bytes plus capture (B OutputInput; internal/k8s/transport.go:59-82). A names the container on every call (-c connector); B never does. B's mate channel runs it against another cluster's client.
- **kube.Client.Attach** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> exec -i -t <pod> [-n <ns>] [-c <container>] -- <argv...>`
  - argv: `<kubectl> exec [-i] <pod> [-n <ns>] [-c <container>] -- <argv...>`
  - Returns: Result with the child's exit code.
  - Typed errors: Only 'the session never ran'.
  - Notes: As the engine's Attach (A shell.go:256-271; B internal/k8s/ops.go:50-58, marked ChildExit at internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:513-519).
- **kube.Client.Upload** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> exec -i <pod> [-n <ns>] [-c <container>] -- sh -c "[mkdir -p '<dir>' && ]cat > '<dest>'"`
  - Returns: Result, stdin as a byte count.
  - Typed errors: None.
  - Notes: stdin = the file body. A: mkdir true and -c connector (runner.go:1175-1177). B: mkdir false (internal/k8s/transport.go:92), also against the mate site's client (internal/k8s/matechannel.go:69).
- **kube.Client.SecretKeys (add)** (used by A)
  - argv: `<kubectl> get secret <name> [-n <ns>] -o <a template that prints the data key names only>`
  - Returns: The key names.
  - Typed errors: Not found (A stops the deploy); refused (A warns); anything else (A warns with the first line).
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue. A's probe of operator-managed Secrets reads -o json and so captures every value to learn the key names (secretprobe.go:29-66). A names-only read keeps the values out of the process and the hook.
- **kube.Client.SecretValue (add)** (used by B)
  - argv: `<kubectl> get secret <name> -n <ns> -o jsonpath={.data.<key>}`
  - Returns: The decoded value of one key.
  - Typed errors: Not found; refused; no such key (an empty answer). B words them as 'read secret' and 'has no key' today and tells no refusal apart (internal/k8s/matechannel.go:139-160).
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue. The value is a password: never to the hook, the Result or an error. A dot in the key is escaped for jsonpath. B runs it with its own base or another site's command.
- **kube.Client.CopyTo, CopyFrom (add)** (used by B)
  - argv: `<kubectl> cp -n <ns> <local> <pod>:<dest>`
  - argv: `<kubectl> cp -n <ns> <pod>:<remote> <local>`
  - Returns: Result.
  - Typed errors: None; B counts the failures per file.
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue (B internal/k8s/transport.go:100-117). Same operand rules as the engine's copy.
- **kube.Client.ServerVersion (add)** (used by B)
  - argv: `<kubectl> version -o json`
  - Returns: The client and server versions.
  - Typed errors: Unanswered: the API server cannot be reached. B stops validate on it (internal/k8s/check.go:39-44).
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue. B discards the answer today. The server version also settles the 'cluster older than 1.28' wording of gdu 11.
- **kube.Client.Top (add)** (used by A)
  - argv: `<kubectl> top pod [<names...>] [--all-namespaces | -n <ns>] [-l <selector>] --containers --no-headers`
  - Returns: Rows per pod and container, and Result.
  - Typed errors: None: any failure is a note in A's report (no metrics API).
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue (A runner.go:886-904, status --details).
- **kube.Client.Run, Output (add, generic)** (used by B)
  - argv: `<kubectl> <args the tool built>`
  - argv: `B: describe pod -n <ns> <pod> ; describe service/<lb> -n <ns> ; describe deployment/<operator> -n <opNS> ; set env deployment/<operator> -n <opNS> WATCH_NAMESPACE=<ns,ns>`
  - Returns: Run: Result, output to the hook. Output: stdout bytes and Result.
  - Typed errors: Never started, and the exit code.
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue. These go through B's own Cluster.kubectl today (internal/k8s/ops.go:31, 36; operator.go:652; watch.go:456). Through the client they get the pinned context of gdu 11, which a hand-built argv would miss.
- **kube/rbac.Run** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> auth can-i <verb> <resource>[/<name>] [--subresource <s>] (-n <ns> | --all-namespaces | nothing)`
  - Returns: nil when allowed, and Result.
  - Typed errors: *rbac.RefusedError (the answer no, in kubectl's three spellings); unanswered (a run error with no verdict; it carries the login hint); unreadable (exit 0 without yes). Both tools branch on refused with errors.As (A namespace.go ensureNamespace; B internal/k8s/prep.go:60-65, 96-104).
  - Notes: stdout alone is the verdict. A passes the captured stderr into the unanswered error (runner.go:407-410); B streams it and passes "" (internal/k8s/preflight.go:71-75). B's non-failing form (allowed, answered) is the same call read differently (preflight.go:105-122). Under Echo it is unanswered, and B then passes by its own choice. The set of probes and the loop over them stay in each tool.
- **kube/nsgate.Read** (used by both)
  - argv: `<kubectl> get all,persistentvolumeclaims,secrets,configmaps -n <ns> -o json`
  - argv: `gdu 1 widens the type list from: <kubectl> api-resources --verbs=list --namespaced -o name`
  - Returns: The listing document for nsgate.Occupants (pure).
  - Typed errors: Refused or failed: the namespace is kept with a warning and exit 0, in both tools. Unanswered (nothing on stdout): B skips quietly as a preview (internal/k8s/namespace.go:55-68).
  - Notes: stdout alone. After gdu 1 it is two commands and also needs the namespace's own labels (owner questions 11 and 12). Protected and DeleteProbe stay pure.
- **runner (the primitive under every client).Do (add)** (used by B)
  - argv: `any runner.Cmd the tool built`
  - argv: `B host commands: mkdir -p <dir> ; chown <user> <dir> ; find <dir> -mindepth 1 -delete ; sysctl -w fs.nr_open=<n> ; id -un ; sh -c <probe> <arg> ; cat /proc/self/limits`
  - Returns: Result, with stdout returned on request.
  - Typed errors: Never started, and the child's exit code (B reads exit 1 of test -d as 'no such directory', container/rootless.go:159-169).
  - Notes: Not in the catalogue. B's host commands have no client and need none: the data directory and the limits are the broker's. They still need the hook, the Result and Echo, which B gets today from its own engine.Run and engine.Output (internal/engine/runner.go:133-145). A starts no host program.

### 2.2 Gaps: common operations no catalogue method covers

- Delete objects by kind and name, with a bound
  - A: No call today. gdu 18 adds it: labelled leftovers (Secrets, the Service, the libs PVC, the PV by its derived name).
  - B: internal/k8s/ops.go:138 (pod), internal/k8s/prep.go:283 (secret), internal/k8s/deploy.go:215 (pvc), each built by hand with --ignore-not-found and no --timeout.
  - Proposal: kube.Client.DeleteNamed(kind, names, namespace, timeout). It replaces the catalogue's DeletePod and takes the gdu 12 timeout and its timed-out error.
- Exec that returns stdout, with optional stdin bytes, as a different call from an attached session
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:1201-1214 (captured, merged); cmd/solmq-conn-util/shell.go:256-271 (attached).
  - B: internal/container/transport.go:77-99 and internal/k8s/transport.go:59-82 (Run, Output, OutputInput); container/manager.go:1464-1478 and k8s/ops.go:50-58 (attached).
  - Proposal: On containercli.Client and kube.Client: Exec (output to the hook), ExecOutput (stdout returned, optional stdin), Attach (terminal, child's exit code). The catalogue's one Exec 'returns the child's exit code' describes only Attach.
- Run a command the tool built itself through the client (decision 3 leaves the building to the tool, not the running)
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:356 (image pull); also :335, :887-899, :934, :957 if those reads stay tool-built.
  - B: internal/k8s/cluster.go kubectl and kubectlOutput; container/manager.go:147-153 (Manager.run); systemd.Systemctl at container/manager.go:1329, 1375 and container/rootless.go:384; every host command.
  - Proposal: Run and Output on each of the four clients, over one exported primitive (runner.Do) that host commands use directly. They add the base tokens, the pinned context, the session env, the hook, the Result and the masking, and decide nothing.
- Read a Secret without its values reaching the tool's output
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/secretprobe.go:36 (get secret -o json: the whole Secret is captured to read key names).
  - B: internal/k8s/matechannel.go:150-152 (jsonpath of one key: a password on stdout).
  - Proposal: kube.Client.SecretKeys (names only) and kube.Client.SecretValue (one key, decoded, never to the hook, the Result or an error), each with not found and refused told apart.
- Read properties of a unit or of the manager, also from the system manager
  - A: None.
  - B: internal/container/rootless.go:384 (show --property=Version, user manager); internal/container/limits.go:182-190 (show user@<euid>.service, system manager, built as a literal argv).
  - Proposal: systemd.Client.Show(unit, properties). NRestarts and LoadState become wrappers over it. The tool sets up a second client in system mode for the system unit.
- Check that the compose command exists before a deploy
  - A: None: the first compose call is 'up', after the redeploy question (internal/runner/runner.go:179-189).
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:333-340.
  - Proposal: containercli.Client.ComposeVersion, whose error carries a hint as Info's does.
- Log the engine in to a registry with the password on stdin
  - A: None.
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:455-479.
  - Proposal: containercli.Client.Login(registry, user, password).
- Copy a file into or out of a container or pod
  - A: None (A uploads from memory with Upload).
  - B: internal/container/transport.go:119-136; internal/k8s/transport.go:100-117.
  - Proposal: CopyTo and CopyFrom on containercli.Client and kube.Client.
- Run a command inside rootless podman's user namespace
  - A: None.
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:432, 438, 1153; internal/container/rootless.go:439.
  - Proposal: podman.Client.Unshare(argv...), returning the child's exit code. The commands run inside stay B's.
- Check that the API server answers
  - A: None: an unreachable cluster shows as an unanswered can-i.
  - B: internal/k8s/check.go:39-44.
  - Proposal: kube.Client.ServerVersion.
- Inspect an image, decoded
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:335 (entrypoint and command, deploy) and :934 (RepoDigests, status --details).
  - B: None.
  - Proposal: containercli.Client.InspectImage with a not-found error; a builder and a decoder beside inspect.Containers and inspect.Decode. Design section 4 names this command as tool-built, so it needs the owner's word (question 7).
- Sample resource usage
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:942-963 (stats) and :886-904 (top pod).
  - B: None.
  - Proposal: containercli.Client.Stats and kube.Client.Top, each with its parser lifted from A's statusreport.
- Tell a timed-out delete or wait from a failed one
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/lifecycle.go:139 (one error for both).
  - B: internal/k8s/ops.go:157-165 (one error for both); no delete has a bound.
  - Proposal: A timed-out error class beside refused, not found and unanswered, returned by Delete, DeleteNamespace, DeleteNamed and RolloutStatus (gdu 12 needs it for its wording).

### 2.3 Catalogue methods neither tool needs

- podman.Client.Version as an exported call: both tools read the version only to choose replace or fallback and to gate the secret read-back (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:956-957; B internal/container/manager.go:622-632). Once CreateSecret and SecretMatches gate inside the client, no tool calls it. Keep the read internal.
- kube.Client.DeletePod as its own method: only B deletes a pod (internal/k8s/ops.go:138). DeleteNamed covers it together with B's Secret and PVC deletes and the leftovers of gdu 18.
- No caller in either tool today, kept only because a decided item needs it: systemd.Client.LoadState (gdu 9), podman.Client.SecretIDs (gdu 10), podman.Client.SecretLabels (gdu 16), kube.Client.WhoAmI (gdu 11), kube.Client.ListLabelled (gdu 18). Each needs its callers in both tools in the same tag.
- Every other catalogue method has a live call in both tools, except kube.Client.CurrentContext, which only B calls today (gdu 11 adds A).

### 2.4 Operations both tools build locally

- No external command is built by hand in both tools: A's five local shapes and B's local shapes do not overlap. What both build locally are the seams and host facts below.
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:335, 356, 886-904, 934, 957
  - B: See oneToolOnly.
- The capture wrapper around every builder call (stdout apart for parsing, stderr kept for the error or the refusal test)
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:86-92 (runParsed), plus direct gdurunner.Combined and gdurunner.Split calls
  - B: internal/engine/runner.go:133-166 (Run, Output, RunTee, OutputTee)
- The platform switch for exec, attach, upload and logs: one call site, the kube or the engine builder
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:1057-1082 (ExecArgv), 1119-1143 (LogsArgv), 1165-1183 (InstallScript)
  - B: internal/container/transport.go:59-116 and internal/k8s/transport.go:50-97, behind the interface at internal/broker/transport.go:36-49
- Handing the rootless session environment to systemctl and loginctl children
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:594-599 (Cmd.Env on each command)
  - B: internal/container/rootless.go:146-184 (written into this process with Setenv, once per run)
- The runtime-directory test handed to rootless.UserSession
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:625-634 (os.Stat)
  - B: internal/container/rootless.go:159-169 (sh -c 'test -d "$0"', exit code 1 = absent)
- Reading /etc/subuid and /etc/subgid for rootless.NextFreeRange
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/rootless.go:96-111 (os.ReadFile through hostFacts)
  - B: internal/container/rootless.go suggestSubIDRange (sh -c 'cat /etc/subuid /etc/subgid 2>/dev/null')
- Resolving the account name for the administrator's remedy line
  - A: cmd/solmq-conn-util/runas.go:251-257 (os/user.LookupId)
  - B: internal/container/rootless.go hostUserName (id -un)
- The quadlet unit file: write, compare with the installed text, remove (gdu 7 covers the write only)
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:116-126 and 799-803; cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:958-960
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:827-831 (writeArtifact) and 1054-1060 (os.Remove)
- Command text for a remedy or an error, joined from argv tokens
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:704-706
  - B: internal/container/manager.go:1047-1049 and 1106-1110
- Re-checking the command tokens with execguard on every call
  - A: internal/runner/runner.go:105-111 (ParseCommand, called inside each helper)
  - B: internal/k8s/cluster.go Cluster.cmd and internal/container/manager.go:139-143 (the config accessors run execguard.Check each time)

### 2.5 Operations only one tool runs

- **A, common**: docker image inspect --format (entrypoint and command), before a deploy with created stores. The read is engine-generic and needs a not-found answer to decide on a pull: containercli.Client.InspectImage. The comparison with the store script's entrypoint stays A's. Design section 4 names it tool-built, so owner question 7 decides.
- **A, common**: <engine> image inspect <ref> (RepoDigests), status --details. The same decoded InspectImage; nothing in it is the connector's.
- **A, customisation**: docker image pull <image>. Run only inside A's entrypoint check. A plain argv with nothing to parse or classify: A builds it and runs it through containercli.Client.Run.
- **A, common**: <engine> stats --no-stream --format .... Engine-generic, and the template exists because docker and podman disagree on the JSON shape, which is the module's kind of knowledge: containercli.Client.Stats.
- **A, common**: kubectl top pod ... --containers --no-headers. The Kubernetes half of the same report: kube.Client.Top. Could wait for a later tag with Stats if the owner wants a smaller one.
- **A, common**: kubectl get secret <name> -o json to check an operator-managed Secret's keys. Any tool whose config names an existing Secret needs it, and a names-only read keeps the values out of the process: kube.Client.SecretKeys.
- **A, customisation**: The in-container status script: sh -c payload with the absent marker, and its install. The connector's own step. It runs through ExecOutput and Upload.
- **A, customisation**: One-shot cli session without a terminal, -i only when stdin is not a terminal. A's choice of flags. Attach keeps TTY and Stdin as options, so nothing else is needed.
- **B, customisation**: <engine> version as a reachability row. Info already answers this in both tools and runs right after it in B's own check (container/manager.go:216-230). Drop the row, or keep it over Run.
- **B, common**: <compose> version. Every docker deploy of both tools goes through compose, and A would gain the probe: containercli.Client.ComposeVersion.
- **B, common**: <engine> login --username <u> --password-stdin [<registry>]. A credential on stdin is the module's to handle and mask: containercli.Client.Login.
- **B, common**: podman unshare <argv> (chmod 775, chown, find -delete, test -w on the data directory). The wrapper is engine-generic: podman.Client.Unshare. The four commands run inside are B's customisation and stay in B.
- **B, common**: <engine> cp and kubectl cp, both directions. Generic verbs with operands a builder should check (gdu 4), and the last hand-built argvs in B's two transports: CopyTo and CopyFrom.
- **B, customisation**: systemctl [--user] status <unit> --no-pager, and systemctl [--user] cat <unit>. Display only; nothing to parse or classify. B builds the arguments and runs them through systemd.Client.Run, so they carry the user mode and the session env.
- **B, common**: systemctl --user show --property=Version (does the user manager answer). A generic check before a rootless unit is written, which A lacks: systemd.Client.Show.
- **B, customisation**: systemctl show user@<euid>.service -p LimitNOFILE ... (system manager). The properties and their thresholds are the broker's. The read itself is the common Show, on a client in system mode.
- **B, common**: kubectl config current-context. Already in the catalogue; gdu 11 makes both tools resolve and pin it.
- **B, common**: kubectl version -o json. A generic reachability check with a typed unanswered error: kube.Client.ServerVersion.
- **B, customisation**: kubectl get sc (default class by jsonpath; one field by custom-columns). What is asked and what counts as suitable is the broker's storage rule. The read is kube.Client.Get once the custom-columns form moves to jsonpath.
- **B, common**: kubectl delete secret | pvc | pod <name> --ignore-not-found. gdu 12 bounds every delete and gdu 18 has both tools delete by name: kube.Client.DeleteNamed.
- **B, customisation**: kubectl set env deployment/<operator> WATCH_NAMESPACE=.... The operator's own variable and B's operator lifecycle. Through kube.Client.Run.
- **B, customisation**: kubectl describe pod | service | deployment. Display only. Through kube.Client.Run, so it gets the pinned context.
- **B, common**: kubectl get secret <name> -o jsonpath={.data.<key>} (a password). A secret on stdout is the module's to keep from the hook: kube.Client.SecretValue.
- **B, customisation**: Reads of the broker custom resource, its pods, services, StatefulSets, PVCs and the TLS Secret's annotation. The objects and what is read from them are the operator's. All are kube.Client.Get.
- **B, customisation**: The mate channel on a second cluster (upload, cli, rm through another site's command). Replication is the broker's. It needs a second kube.Client with its own base tokens and its own pinned context (gdu 11).
- **B, customisation**: In-broker commands: cli -Apes, the CLI skeleton, curl -is -K -, rm, mkdir, bash, sh, hostname. The workload's own steps (design 3.5). They run through Exec, ExecOutput and Upload.
- **B, customisation**: Host: mkdir -p, chown and find -delete on the data directory. The broker's persistent directory. Over runner.Do.
- **B, customisation**: Host: sysctl -w fs.nr_open, and the probes for nr_open, ulimit -Hn, the nearest writable directory and /proc/self/limits. The broker's host limits. rootless.ProcLimits is a builder no client covers, so it stays exported under decision 3; whether B's limit helpers stay in the module is for the surface review.
- **B, common**: compose up --force-recreate. Already a parameter of ComposeUp; A always passes false.
- **B, customisation**: Cluster.Status and Cluster.OperatorStatus (get ... -o wide, the only B use of a selector). No live caller (internal/k8s/ops.go:17-27; internal/k8s/operator.go:630-640). Delete in B's adoption, do not port.

### 2.6 Owner questions

1. Does stdout that a method parses or returns still go to the Output hook? Design 3.2 says every byte does. Recommended: no. A method that returns an answer keeps its stdout and sends only stderr to the hook; a method that returns nothing sends both. Both tools work this way today (A internal/runner/runner.go:86-92; B internal/engine/runner.go:142-145). With every byte, B's terminal hook would print each JSON read and A's buffer hook would print them at the end. Three reads carry secrets: podman secret inspect --showsecret, B's get secret -o jsonpath={.data.<key>} (internal/k8s/matechannel.go:150-152) and A's get secret -o json (cmd/solmq-conn-util/secretprobe.go:36). The hook cannot tell which call a chunk came from.
2. May a tool use a different hook for one call? Recommended: yes, through a copy of the client with another hook, and the hook contract says it can be called from two goroutines at once. A needs it three times: status runs up to 4 execs at once and parses each one's own output (status.go:920-923), logs must pass stdout and stderr straight through while its other commands buffer (logs.go:304), and it scans the apply's own output (main.go:1807-1818). B needs it for --watch, which points the children at a frame per refresh (internal/cli/watch.go:88-97).
3. Should each client carry a generic Run and Output for a command the tool built itself, over one exported primitive? Recommended: yes. Decision 3 leaves such commands to the tool, but without this they bypass the base tokens, the pinned context, the session env, the hook and the Result, and each tool keeps a second capture path (B's Cluster.kubectl, Manager.run and engine.Run; A's runParsed). The module still decides nothing for them.
4. Which errors does the module tell apart? Recommended: one set shared by every family: refused, not found and unanswered (design 3.3), plus timed out (gdu 12 needs it for the deletes), unsupported (whoami before 1.28), no context set, and partial listing (gdu 16, 18). The error exposes the command's stderr as a field and does not print it, because the hook already delivered it; it keeps the cause chain, so errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) still works for A's log follow; and the Result comes back on failure too. A's Secret probe matches 'not found' loosely today (secretprobe.go:38-41); the typed error would match kubectl's own NotFound line only.
5. Is Exec one method? Recommended: three per client: Exec (output to the hook), ExecOutput (stdout returned, optional stdin bytes) and Attach (terminal, the child's exit code, no context). Also one small interface that a bound pod and a bound container both satisfy for Exec, ExecOutput, Attach, Upload, CopyTo, CopyFrom and Logs. Both tools hold that switch themselves today: A's ExecArgv, LogsArgv and InstallScript (internal/runner/runner.go:1057, 1119, 1165), B's two transports.
6. DeletePod, or a general delete by name? Recommended: kube.Client.DeleteNamed and no DeletePod. B deletes a pod, Secrets and PVCs by name with three hand-built argvs and no timeout, and gdu 18 has both tools delete leftovers by name.
7. Design section 4 names A's image inspect as tool-built. Under rule 5, do image inspect, stats and top pod move into the module? Recommended: yes for the three reads that have a parser: InspectImage (decoded, with the not-found answer A needs before a pull), Stats, and Top. image pull stays A's, over Run. If the tag must stay small, leave all four with A over Run; nothing else depends on them.
8. Which of B's own engine and kubectl verbs are common? Recommended: into the module where it adds secret handling, a typed answer or a check: Login, SecretValue, ComposeVersion, ServerVersion, Unshare, CopyTo and CopyFrom, and systemd Show. Left to B over Run: describe, set env WATCH_NAMESPACE, systemctl status and cat, the StorageClass reads (through Get) and every in-broker command. B's '<engine> version' row can go, since Info answers it.
9. How does the session environment reach systemctl and loginctl? Recommended: the systemd.Client holds it and sets it on each command, as A does (internal/runner/runner.go:594-599). B stops writing it into its own process (internal/container/rootless.go:171-177) and does not wrap that client in engine.NoEnv; the module documents that only ComposeUp, ComposeDown and the systemd client ever set Env. B's read of user@<uid>.service needs a second client value in system mode.
10. Who checks the base tokens? Recommended: a constructor per client that takes the tool's execguard.Rules, the tokens and the approvals, runs execguard.Check once and fails at set-up. Design 3.1 shows a struct literal that trusts its tokens. Both tools re-check on every call today, and B states that an executor must not assume its caller checked (internal/k8s/cluster.go Cluster.cmd). Still to do under CR-0001: neither tool takes the flag name from execguard.ApproveFlag yet (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/commands.go:27; B internal/cli/flags.go:239).
11. What does an operation that runs two commands return? Recommended: the Result of the last command run, which is the failing one on an error, and an error that names the step. Three operations are affected: CreateSecret below podman 4.7 (rm, then create; a failed create leaves the secret gone), SecretLabels (ls, then inspect) and the widened nsgate.Read (api-resources, then get).
12. Two decided items need reads the catalogue does not name; how are they added? Recommended: nsgate.Read (or NamespaceState) also returns the namespace's own labels, which the gate needs to choose one question or two (gdu 1); SecretLabels lists the store itself before it inspects, since podman cannot filter by label (gdu 16); and podman.Client.Version stays internal.
13. Do the host facts both tools read differently move into the module? Recommended: no. A reads the subid files, the runtime directory and the account name in Go; B runs sh, test and id through the runner. The pure functions in rootless already take the values, and executor rule 4 keeps file and environment reads out of the module. One difference to settle between the tools: A resolves the name with os/user (runas.go:251-257), B with 'id -un' because os/user can miss directory accounts.

### 2.7 Census notes

- A: Scope and method. A = A. Caller files given by basename (main.go, lifecycle.go, namespace.go, rootless.go, secretprobe.go, runas.go, status.go, logs.go, shell.go, instances.go) are in A/cmd/solmq-conn-util/; "runner.go" is A/internal/runner/runner.go. Three sweeps merged: (1) every gdurunner use, (2) every M builder call, (3) os/exec. Read-only; nothing was run. A's go.work replaces M with the local checkout (go.mod pins v0.1.3), so builder bodies were read from M. 1. One exec seam. A has no os/exec in non-test code. Every process goes through gdurunner.Exec{} (main.go:189) by four routes only: gdurunner.Combined (buffered, merged), gdurunner.Split (always through local runParsed, runner.go:86, plus four direct calls at runner.go:408, 554, 702, 738), Runner.Run straight to the terminal (logs.go:304, the only streaming call), Runner.Attach (shell.go:271). gdurunner.Output and runner.Echo are never used: A has no preview mode. 2. Commands that start a process: deploy, remove, start, stop, restart, status, logs, cli, and validate (validate only for a rootless podman section on Linux: loginctl show-user and podman info id map). Commands that start none: generate, examples, download, auto-complete, version, help. internal/gen is pure rendering; internal/libs reaches the network through net/http only (libs.go:86), never a child process. 3. Shapes A builds itself ("local"), five in all, all in runner.go: docker image inspect --format (335), docker image pull (356), engine image inspect <ref> (934), engine stats (957), kubectl top pod (887). None is in the design note's catalogue (3.4); section 4 already names the image inspect as tool-built. The other four need the same decision in the surface review: stay local, or gain a builder or a client method. 4. Wrappers that only choose a builder: runner.ExecArgv (1057) and runner.LogsArgv (1119) switch between kube.Exec/containercli.Exec and kube.Logs/containercli.Logs and return only .Argv; the cli attach and logs stream then run that argv from cmd/ (shell.go:271, logs.go:304). A client's Exec and Logs must therefore cover: an attached terminal session returning the child's code, and an unbuffered stream with a cancellable context. 5. Chains that live in A's internal/runner today and are orchestration under decision 2: PodmanDeploy (733: is-active, ask, write, daemon-reload, start or restart), PodmanRemove (790: stop, os.Remove of the unit, daemon-reload), DockerDeployDigest (201: ps then inspect), EnsureLinger (679: show-user then enable-linger), checkImageCommand (main.go:831: inspect, pull, inspect), ensureNamespace and removeNamespace (namespace.go:56, 92), Preflight (376: a loop of can-i probes, or info). The one chain decision 1 moves into M is PodmanSecretCreate's fallback (496-518: secret rm --ignore then secret create below 4.7). 6. Per-call guard today. The command string is tokenised and checked (runner.ParseCommand -> execguard.Check, runner.go:105) inside almost every helper, so one deploy re-parses the same string many times; deploy/remove on kubernetes also parse once into base (main.go:672) and the read verbs once into sess.cmdArgv (instances.go:130). A client with Base set once replaces all of it. systemctl and loginctl take argv[0] as a literal from M and never pass execguard; their only per-run context is the user-mode flag and the session env (QuadletScope, runner.go:578-599), which matches the proposed systemd.Client set-up. 7. Classification today is by text, not exit code: kube.Forbidden(output) for the rollout wait (lifecycle.go:136); a local substring match on NotFound/not found and Forbidden/forbidden for the Secret probe (secretprobe.go:39-41), which is a different test from kube.Forbidden; rbac.Check for can-i; kube.ParseNamespace; the ScriptAbsentMarker line; systemd.State. Everything else is failed/ok. The only exit code A reads is the attached child's (shell.go:275). "Not found" is distinguished in one place only (the Secret probe); status reports any failed component read as MISSING, Forbidden included (status.go:583-584). 8. Output model. Mutating commands are buffered with Combined and printed once at the end by report (main.go:1783); status draws a progress line and prints nothing from the children; logs streams; cli attaches. So A's Output hook would append to a buffer, as design 3.2 says. 9. Contexts. Every call uses context.Background() (bg(), main.go:210) except logs --follow (logs.go:290-304). status --watch does not hand its signal context to the commands of a tick. 10. Secret material on stdin or env: kubectl apply (manifest), compose up/down (file on stdin, credentials and stores in the child env), podman secret create (value on stdin). Secret material in captured stdout: podman secret inspect --showsecret (compared, never printed) and kubectl get secret -o json in the deploy probe (only key names are read, but the whole document is captured). 11. Catalogue methods A has no call for today (they arrive with the gdu items, not from current behaviour): compose down -p, systemd LoadState, podman secret labels / SecretLabels / SecretIDs, kube CurrentContext / WhoAmI, DeletePod, ListLabelled, delete timeouts, containercli.Remove (already removed by CR-0001; A does not call it). A also never passes --force-recreate (ComposeUp recreate=false) and never labels a podman secret. 12. In-container programs, run by the engine and not by A: sh, mkdir, cat (the upload), the status script (sh, wget, sed, tr, base64, head, cut, sort) and the store entrypoint script (sh, base64, mv). sudo, usermod, chmod, chgrp and "docker compose -p <project> down" appear only in message text. 13. Decision 6 check: A's approval flag name is a local constant (allowCommandFlagName = "--allow-command", A/cmd/solmq-conn-util/commands.go:27), not yet execguard.ApproveFlag (M/execguard/execguard.go:69); the values are equal.
- B: All site paths are relative to B: (tool B). Read-only census; nothing was run or edited. How B reaches a process - There is no os/exec, exec.Command or syscall exec anywhere in B's non-test Go (grep over the tree; the only hits are comments in internal/config/execguard.go and validate.go). Every external command goes through M's runner.Runner. - B never calls Runner.Run / Runner.Attach from feature code. It goes through five wrappers in B:internal/engine/runner.go: engine.Run (:133, both streams to the terminal or a Redirect), engine.Output (:142, calls runner.Output :144, stdout captured, stderr streamed), engine.RunTee (:151, stderr teed into a buffer), engine.OutputTee (:161, stdout captured plus stderr teed), engine.Interactive (:173, Runner.Attach :174 -> exitcode.Exited). runner.Combined and runner.Split are not used by B at all; RunTee/OutputTee are B's own equivalents and exist only to feed kube.Forbidden. - Other engine pieces a Client design has to absorb: engine.NewExec (:26, runner.Exec with an Announce hook under --verbose, wired at internal/cli/context.go:174), engine.NoEnv (:47, refuses any Cmd.Env; wraps k8s.Cluster cluster.go:73, both transports, the mate channel), engine.Redirect (:90, used by --watch at internal/cli/watch.go:95 to collect child output into a frame), engine.IsEcho (:125, about 20 call sites skip assertions, file writes and host probes under runner.Echo). - runner.Resolve is called at internal/cli/context.go:302 to print the resolved binary path; it runs nothing. - No import aliases for M packages in B (checked every import line); the package inspect is containercli/inspect. Base tokens - Engine base = cfg.RuntimeCommand(platform) (docker.command / podman.command), compose base = cfg.ComposeCommand() (docker.compose or derived '<docker> compose'), kubectl base = cfg.ClusterCommand() (kubernetes.command, may carry --kubeconfig/--context), mate base = cfg.SiteCommand(site). Each accessor re-runs execguard.Check on EVERY call (internal/config/execguard.go:242-305), so today the base is rebuilt per command, not once per run. A Client set up once per run changes that enforcement point. M builders B calls (all in live code unless noted) - containercli: Info, InfoError, Engine, ComposeUp, ComposeDown, ComposeDigest, Start, Stop, Restart, List, ParseList, HasName, ByImage, ValidName, Exec, Upload, Logs, LogOptions, NoPrevious. containercli.Remove is not called. - containercli/inspect: Containers, Decode, Printable. - podman: Version, SupportsReplace, SecretData, SecretMatches, RemoveSecrets, CreateSecret, IDMap (QuadletDir and SystemQuadletDir are used by internal/config, not for commands). - systemd: Systemctl (generic, 4 shapes: status --no-pager, cat, show --property=Version, and a status argv built only for an error message at manager.go:1049), DaemonReload, Start, Stop, Restart, IsActive, State, Active, Stopped, NRestarts, ParseNRestarts, ShowLinger, ParseLinger, EnableLinger. - rootless: Decide, UserSession (+ Session.Env/String), CheckIDMap, SubIDFix, NextFreeRange, CheckLinger, ProcLimits, HardLimit, CheckCore, ParseLimit, LimitText, Unbounded. - kube: Apply, Delete, NamespaceManifest, GetNamespace, ParseNamespace, MissingNamespace, DeleteNamespace, Scale, RolloutRestart, RolloutStatus, RolloutTimeout, Forbidden, Get/GetOptions, Exec/ExecOptions, Upload, Logs/LogOptions. - kube/rbac: Probe, CanI, Check, Verdict, RefusedError, ClusterScoped. kube/nsgate: List, Occupants, Object, Protected, DeleteProbe. Commands B builds itself (builder = local), i.e. shapes the section 3.4 catalogue does not name - engine: version; login --username U --password-stdin [registry]; cp (both directions); podman unshare chmod / chown / find / sh -c test -w. - compose: version. - systemctl: show user@<uid>.service -p ... (system manager, 5 properties). - kubectl: config current-context; version -o json; get sc (jsonpath) and get sc <name> (custom-columns); delete secret / pvc / pod by name with --ignore-not-found; set env deployment/<op> WATCH_NAMESPACE=...; describe pod / service / deployment; cp (both directions). - host: mkdir -p, chown, find -mindepth 1 -delete, sysctl -w, id -un, and five sh -c probes (test -d, cat /etc/subuid /etc/subgid, nearest-writable walk, cat /proc/sys/fs/nr_open, ulimit -Hn). rootless.ProcLimits (cat /proc/self/limits) is the one host command with an M builder. Failure classification as it stands today (what a typed error would replace) - Forbidden: kube.Forbidden over teed stderr at exactly three sites: ops.go:159 (rollout wait -> warning), deploy.go:76 (CR read-back -> warning), adminsecret.go:162 (data-claim read -> warning). Everywhere else a refused read is detected by a second command, auth can-i via Cluster.canI (statusreport.go:138, checkreport.go:665). - Refused (can-i 'no'): *rbac.RefusedError from rbac.Check, branched with errors.As at prep.go:64, prep.go:98, operator.go:351. - Not found: mostly avoided with --ignore-not-found plus an empty-stdout test (kube.ParseNamespace, LiveAdminSecret, ServerCertChanged, BrokerPodsExist, getJSON); inspectContainer turns an empty inspect array into a local error; podman secret rm uses --ignore. - Unanswered: containerRunningKnown (manager.go:984-992, answered=false aborts the docker deploy), systemd.State (empty reply), rbac.Verdict (answered=false), NamespaceOccupants (empty stdout), ConfirmBrokerApplied (empty stdout). The 'empty stdout means nobody answered' test doubles as the Echo/preview path. - Exit codes: only two sites read one. rootless.go:164-165 (test -d: exit 1 = absent, via an ExitCode() interface on the error) and the interactive sessions (exitcode.Exited -> exitcode.ChildExit). systemctl is-active's exit code is deliberately ignored. Points that bear on the decided items - Podman secret replace fallback (design 3.3 rule 1): today the tool owns the version read (manager.go:627), the replace decision and the rm-then-create pair (manager.go:602-616); Echo forces the older pair. Secrets are created with no labels; compare is by reading the value back (SecretData/SecretMatches), so SecretLabels/SecretIDs (gdu 10, 16) have no caller yet. - ComposeDown carries no -p (gdu 9) and has no post-check; there is no systemd LoadState use. - kubectl: no --context is ever added (gdu 11); the context is only read once for display (context.go:261). No WhoAmI call exists. - Deletes carry no --timeout (gdu 12): kube.Delete, kube.DeleteNamespace and the three local delete-by-name shapes. - No labelled listing (gdu 18) in live code; GetOptions.Selector is used only by the uncalled Cluster.OperatorStatus. - Cmd.Env is used by exactly one command (compose up/down). The rootless session variables are NOT passed as Cmd.Env: ensureUserSession (rootless.go:146-184) writes them into this process with os.Setenv so children inherit them, which matters for a systemd.Client that is meant to carry 'user mode and session env'. - Output today: most commands stream straight to the terminal; captures keep stdout only. A hook that receives every chunk would change what the operator sees for the ~25 capture sites whose stdout is currently hidden (for example the podman secret value read at manager.go:558 and the base64 password read at matechannel.go:151 must not reach a hook that prints). - Every k8s/container entry type (Cluster, Manager, both transports, the mate channel) is rebuilt per CLI call from App (internal/cli/ops_k8s.go:26-39, ops_container.go:33-58); nothing holds a per-run client today. Not external commands (checked so they are not mistaken for one): DNS via net.LookupHost (manager.go:106), os.Hostname and net.InterfaceAddrs for role detection (internal/broker/verify_local.go), os.ReadFile/WriteFile/Remove for the quadlet unit, sysctl drop-in, legacy cert bundle and .broker.yaml, render.Compose / render.Quadlet / render.BrokerCR (pure). The generate commands, convert, examples, auto-complete and version run no external command (generate skips even the preamble probes: internal/cli/helpers.go:59, platform.go:200). Not read line by line: internal/broker files other than the Transport call sites listed (the parsers and script builders), internal/render, internal/config beyond the command accessors, and the full text of docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md and docs/todo.md in M (the gdu numbers above come from the design note's sections 3.4 and 4).

### 2.8 Verifier

- **map.methods containercli.Client.ComposeDown, notes (and B census row 'compose ... down', input)**
  - Was: Map: 'Cmd.Env = a placeholder for every secret, in both tools.' B census: 'child Env = each secret defined as a placeholder.'
  - Should be: Neither tool passes placeholders only. A resolves the real credentials on remove too and passes them to 'down' beside store placeholders; an unset credential stops remove (exit 2) before any command runs, which is against gdu 3 'Remove resolves no credential'. B passes the real configured value of every credential secret and substitutes a placeholder only for the file-backed server-certificate bundle. ComposeDown's env is therefore secret-bearing in both tools and needs the same masking as ComposeUp.
  - Evidence: A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:734-737 (gen.ResolveCredentials runs for both actions), :756 (placeholders are for stores only), :773, :791. B:internal/container/secrets.go:38-47 (only entries with SourceFiles get previewValue; 'Every credential secret carries its own value already'), B:internal/container/manager.go:199-209 and :1130-1135.
- **map.methods containercli.Client.Info, notes**
  - Was: 'First call of every engine verb in both tools.'
  - Should be: True for A's deploy, remove, start, stop, restart, status, logs and cli on docker and podman (not validate, not generate). Not true for B: Info runs only in validate (after '<engine> version' and '<compose> version'), broker deploy (twice), remove, start, stop, restart and configure server-certs on podman. B's status, status --all, logs, cli, shell, copy and every configure/perform exec run no Info first.
  - Evidence: B callers of Manager.Preflight: B:internal/container/manager.go:229, :402, :494, :653, :1000, :1172, :1184, :1196 only; Reachable precedes it at :221; Status (:1329-1333), Logs (:1401), session (:1468-1477) and B:internal/container/transport.go:59-136 call none. A: A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:627-644.
- **map.oneToolOnly 'The in-container status script ...' (reason) and map.methods Exec (and ExecOutput) on both clients**
  - Was: 'It runs through ExecOutput and Upload.' ExecOutput is defined as 'stdout returned to the caller'.
  - Should be: A's status exec needs BOTH streams merged in arrival order and returned for that one call: the script writes its 'status:' notes to stderr and ParseApplication collects them from the merged text. Through ExecOutput (stdout only, stderr to the hook) those notes leave the report. A needs Exec with a per-call hook that buffers both streams, or an ExecCombined form; only B's three uses are stdout-only.
  - Evidence: A:internal/runner/runner.go:1207 (gdurunner.Combined) and :1191-1193; A:internal/statusreport/parse.go:757-764 ('stdout and stderr arrive on one stream ... its "status:" notes are interleaved with the report and are collected as notes here').
- **map.gaps 'Read a Secret without its values reaching the tool's output' (whereA) and map.methods kube.Client.SecretKeys**
  - Was: whereA names one site: secretprobe.go:36.
  - Should be: A has a second site: status --details reads 'get secret <name> -o json' once per Secret a pod references, only to learn that it exists (ObjectExists reads kind and status.phase). SecretKeys is the wrong shape there; an existence read ('-o name', or a Get that returns no body) is what it needs. Both sites must change or the whole Secret still lands in the process on every status --details.
  - Evidence: A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/status.go:582-588; A:internal/runner/runner.go:858-864; A:internal/statusreport/parse.go:459-473.
- **map.methods kube/nsgate Read (notes, typedErrors) and map.ownerQuestions[0]**
  - Was: Read: 'stdout alone.' Question 1: 'Three reads carry secrets: podman secret inspect --showsecret, B's get secret -o jsonpath, A's get secret -o json.'
  - Should be: The namespace listing is a fourth secret-bearing read, in both tools: 'get all,persistentvolumeclaims,secrets,configmaps -n <ns> -o json' returns every Secret's data and every ConfigMap of the namespace, while Occupants reads only kind, type and metadata. In A a failed listing also folds the captured stdout into the error text that removeNamespace prints (a partial listing would print Secret documents; todo item 18 states kubectl prints what it could list and exits 1 -- not run here). Read must keep stdout from the hook, the Result and the error, and should ask for metadata only (decisions P10 already says so for label listings). Also secret-bearing and missing from question 1: A's status --details Secret read (status.go:582) and B's exec transcripts (import driver, RunCLI, cli-script, SEMP curl output).
  - Evidence: M:kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:52-56 (List) and :64-73 (fields Occupants reads); A:internal/runner/runner.go:86-91 and :870-875; A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go:103-108; B:internal/k8s/namespace.go:57; B:internal/broker/importops.go:856 and :873, B:internal/broker/config_ops.go:265 and :277, B:internal/broker/broker.go:160.
- **map.ownerQuestions[0], 'Both tools work this way today' and the recommended rule**
  - Was: 'A method that returns an answer keeps its stdout and sends only stderr to the hook; a method that returns nothing sends both. Both tools work this way today (A runner.go:86-92; B engine/runner.go:142-145).'
  - Should be: Only the stdout half holds for both. A drops the stderr of a successful parsed read (Split, errOut discarded); B streams it. And the rule as worded changes behaviour for the probe methods that 'return nothing': Info, ComposeVersion, ServerVersion and B's '<engine> version' have their stdout discarded today in both tools (A drops Info's merged output on success), so 'sends both' would print the whole 'docker info' on every deploy, remove, start and stop. The rule needs a third class: probes whose stdout is dropped.
  - Evidence: A:internal/runner/runner.go:86-92 (stderr returned only on failure), :395-400 and A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:627-633 (Info output dropped on success); B:internal/container/preflight.go:37-47, B:internal/container/manager.go:329 and :337, B:internal/k8s/check.go:40 (stdout captured by engine.Output and discarded); B:internal/engine/runner.go:142-145.
- **map.ownerQuestions[1], 'A needs it three times'**
  - Was: A needs a per-call hook three times: the parallel status execs, logs, and the apply output scan.
  - Should be: A needs call-scoped output on most calls, not three. Besides those: Info output is dropped on success and put into the error on failure (runner.go:395-398); the image pull output goes only into its error (main.go:837-838); EnsureLinger and ProbeLinger build their errors from the call's own output (runner.go:688-692, :702-706); the namespace delete prints its own output and then a warning, out of band from the final report (namespace.go:142-148); the namespace apply reports at once (namespace.go:69-70); PodmanDeploy discards everything gathered when the question is declined (runner.go:748-750); and every status read prints nothing from the child. One buffer hook per run cannot reproduce 'drop on success, show on failure'.
  - Evidence: Lines as cited, all under A (internal/runner/runner.go and cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go, namespace.go).
- **map.localInBoth 'Handing the rootless session environment ...' and map.ownerQuestions[8]**
  - Was: Mechanism only: A sets Cmd.Env on each systemctl/loginctl command, B writes the process environment once. Recommended: 'B stops writing it into its own process.'
  - Should be: The reach differs too, and the recommendation changes it. In B every child started after ensureUserSession inherits the derived XDG_RUNTIME_DIR and DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS: 'podman info --format' (id map), 'podman unshare', 'podman login', 'podman version', 'podman secret ...', compose, and the host probes. In A only systemctl and loginctl get them; A's podman children never do. If B moves to the systemd.Client carrying the env, its podman children lose the repaired session under sudo/su/cron unless podman.Client carries it as well (and then the 'only ComposeUp, ComposeDown and the systemd client set Env' rule is false). Whether rootless podman needs it there was not run; the owner should decide it as part of question 9.
  - Evidence: B:internal/container/rootless.go:146-184 (m.Setenv at :177, os.Setenv per B:internal/container/manager.go:109); order in PrepHost B:internal/container/manager.go:402-448 and rootlessRows rootless.go:86-110. A: A:internal/runner/runner.go:594-599 (WithEnv used only at :320, :687, :701, :738, :758, :774, :793, :804, :993).
- **map.methods systemd.Client.State, notes**
  - Was: 'stdout alone; stderr and the run error are dropped (A runner.go:738-740; B container/manager.go:967-970).'
  - Should be: A drops both. B drops the run error but streams systemctl's stderr to the terminal (or the watch frame), since systemctlOutput is engine.Output. B also returns 'unanswered' without running anything when ensureUserSession fails.
  - Evidence: B:internal/container/manager.go:967-970 and :1502-1508; B:internal/engine/runner.go:142-145.
- **map.methods systemd.Client.Show (add), typedErrors**
  - Was: 'Unanswered (a run error, or nothing printed; B skips the row on an empty answer ...).'
  - Should be: B tells the two apart and the method must too: a run error is a FAIL row and an error ('cannot read user@<uid>.service limits'); exit 0 with nothing printed is a SKIP. Folding both into one 'unanswered' class loses that. For 'show --property=Version' any error is a failure.
  - Evidence: B:internal/container/limits.go:187-198; B:internal/container/rootless.go:382-394.
- **map.methods podman.Client.Unshare (add), typedErrors**
  - Was: 'B reads the test as a bool from the exit code (container/rootless.go namespaceWritable).'
  - Should be: B reads 'any error' as not writable (m.run(...) == nil); it does not look at the exit code, so 'podman could not start' and 'test -w failed' are the same answer today. The only exit code B reads on a probe is test -d (exit 1) in ensureUserSession.
  - Evidence: B:internal/container/rootless.go:435-439 and :159-169.
- **map.ownerQuestions[9], 'Both tools re-check on every call today'**
  - Was: Both tools re-check the command tokens with execguard on every call.
  - Should be: B does. A does not on its read verbs or its namespace steps: status, logs and cli parse once into sess.cmdArgv and every helper they call takes that slice unchecked; deploy/remove on kubernetes parse once into base for the namespace state, list, probes and delete. Only A's helpers that take the command string re-parse. A also marks a guard refusal as a usage error (exit 2), a class the map's error set does not name; a client constructor must return the execguard error so A can keep that mark.
  - Evidence: A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/instances.go:130; A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:672; helpers taking a pre-parsed argv at A:internal/runner/runner.go:268, :278, :407, :837, :858, :870, :886, :915, :933, :956, :973, :1057, :1119, :1165, :1201; usage mark at runner.go:103-110.
- **map.methods kube.Client.WhoAmI, typedErrors**
  - Was: 'Refused; unsupported (a cluster older than 1.28); unanswered. All three warn and continue (decided 2026-11-15).'
  - Should be: The decision names two cases: a refused whoami, or a cluster older than 1.28, warns and continues. 'Unanswered' (for example an unreachable server) is not decided.
  - Evidence: M:docs/todo.md item 11, Decided (lines 606-619); M:docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md, 'Decided 2026-11-15', gdu 11.
- **map.gaps 'Check that the API server answers' (whereA)**

Notes: Method. Read-only; nothing was run. Read design sections 1, 3, 4, 7, 8, docs/todo.md items 1, 3, 4, 9-12, 16-19 and the decisions file. A: read A:internal/runner/runner.go in full and every caller site in cmd/solmq-conn-util (main.go 615-1070, namespace.go, lifecycle.go, secretprobe.go, rootless.go, logs.go, shell.go, status.go and instances.go call sites). B: read internal/engine/runner.go, internal/k8s (cluster, preflight, check, prep, ops, deploy, namespace, transport, matechannel, adminsecret, inspect and the command sites of operator, watch, statusreport, checkreport), internal/container (manager, preflight, euid, inspect, rootless, limits, transport, secrets), internal/cli/context.go, watch.go and the op wiring, and the Transport call sites of internal/broker. Greps: os/exec, exec.Command, exec.LookPath, syscall exec, StartProcess; every gdurunner/runner/engine call; every M builder call; every runner.Cmd literal and .Argv( call. Sweep 1 (missed commands): none found. Neither tool imports os/exec in non-test code. A has exactly the gdurunner sites the census lists (Combined and Split in runner.go, r.Run at logs.go:304, Attach at shell.go:271). B has 75 engine.* call sites plus the Transport calls in internal/broker; each one maps to a census row, including the wrappers' callers (Manager.run, runCmd, systemctl, systemctlOutput; Cluster.kubectl, kubectlOutput, runKube, outKube, teeKube, outTeeKube, getJSON/getJSONTee). B's production runner is always runner.Exec (context.go:174); runner.Echo arrives only through the test seam NewRunner. The 'missed' list therefore holds facts the censuses left out, not commands. Sweep 2. unusedMethods: all four entries confirmed. podman version is read only at A main.go:956-957 and B manager.go:623-632; only B deletes a pod (ops.go:138); no LoadState, whoami, 'secret ls', api-resources, 'kubectl wait' or delete --timeout exists in either tool; current-context is B only (context.go:261). Gaps: every whereA/whereB cite opens on the stated code except the points in corrections; no further gap found (each census row has a method in the map). oneToolOnly: every tool attribution holds, including the dead Cluster.Status and Cluster.OperatorStatus (callers only in ops_test.go and operator_test.go) and the non-overlap of the two tools' hand-built shapes. Two verdicts depart from text on record and need the owner's word, as the map already says for the first: image inspect (design section 4 names it tool-built) and DeleteNamed (todo item 12 says B's own deletes are bounded 'in the tool', the catalogue says DeletePod). Sweep 3 confirmed. Forbidden is told apart at exactly A lifecycle.go:136 (merged output, only after an error) and B ops.go:159, deploy.go:76, adminsecret.go:162 (teed stderr); A's Secret probe uses its own loose substring test (secretprobe.go:38-41). Cmd.Env is set only for compose up/down in both tools and, in A, for systemctl and loginctl through QuadletScope.WithEnv. rbac errors.As sites: A namespace.go:72; B prep.go:64, :98, operator.go:351. CR-0001: neither tool uses execguard.ApproveFlag yet (A commands.go:27, B flags.go:239). Smaller points for the plan. (a) Owner question 11 counts three two-command operations; a lazily read podman version makes the first SecretMatches or CreateSecret of a run a two-command call as well, and under Echo it adds an echoed 'podman version' line B does not print today (manager.go:623-626). (b) Moving B's StorageClass column read from custom-columns to jsonpath changes its report text: B keeps kubectl's '<none>' on purpose (check.go:96-105). (c) B's lockedWriter (watch.go:99-109) is evidence for the hook contract: one command's stdout and stderr already arrive on two goroutines. (d) todo 'Inputs' item 3 already records that B changes the host (sysctl, linger, data dir owner, registry login) before the redeploy question; ops_container.go:81-84 still shows PrepHost before Deploy. Not confirmable without running a command: that kubectl prints a partial JSON list on stdout and exits 1 when one kind is refused (taken from todo item 18's own wording); whether rootless podman children depend on the repaired XDG_RUNTIME_DIR; the systemctl exit codes quoted from todo item 9.

## 3. Renderers

### 3.1 compose

A writes 59 fields or lines, B writes 71.

#### compose: model

- **compose.File.Project** `string` (in both, required): Roots for every citation: A = A, B = B, M = M. Entry point: func (f File) Render() ([]byte, error), pure; it checks every field and returns one error naming field, value and rule (the CR-0004 direction, M:docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:71). Project is the first line, `name: "<project>"`, through Quote, always written. Grammar is compose's own: ^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$. Empty or off-grammar is refused, never folded and never omitted. Today A omits the line when empty (A:internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:102-104) and B folds the container name and can write name: "" (B:internal/render/render.go:1030-1041,1063). gdu 19 makes it an env-file entry in both tools and gdu 9 passes the same value as -p (M:docs/todo.md:496-497,1065-1069), so the tool hands File.Project to containercli.Client.ComposeUp and ComposeDown too. M writes no version: key, no ---, no comment line; the file ends in one newline.
- **compose.File.Owner** `labels.Owner (one identity type for compose, quadlet and kube Meta; package name fixed in the plan)` (in neither, required): The gdu 1 identity. On compose M writes five labels first in the service's labels block, in this order: app.kubernetes.io/managed-by=<Tool>, services.solace.com/product=<Tool>, services.solace.com/solution=<Solution>, services.solace.com/developed-by=<DevelopedBy>, deploy.solace.com/instance=<Instance> (M:docs/todo.md:64-66). Neither tool writes any of them today. The version and lib annotations are NOT written to a compose file (M:docs/todo.md:67-70), so a tool or module upgrade alone changes no byte and recreates nothing. Nothing is written on a top-level secret or config: a label there reaches no engine object (M:docs/todo.md:104-105). artifacttest gains the check that a rendered service carries them (M:docs/todo.md:111).
- **compose.File.Service** `compose.Service` (in both, required): One service per file, as both tools render (A:internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:105-106; B:internal/render/render.go:1070-1071) and as artifacttest.CheckComposePosture assumes (M:containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:97-100). M writes `services:` at indent 0, the service key at 2, its fields at 4, list items and map entries at 6, a list item's second key at 8: the indents artifacttest.CheckSecretTargets reads (artifacttest.go:262-266). A block with nothing in it is omitted whole; M never writes a key that holds null or an empty string.
- **compose.File.(top-level networks block)** `none: M writes it from Service.Network` (in neither, optional): Written only when Service.Network is empty: `networks:` / ` default:` / ` driver: bridge`, after services and before configs (gdu 6, M:docs/todo.md:377-378). Bare: M's own constants. Not written for bridge, host or none, where the service carries network_mode instead. No labels on it (owner question 5). New line in both tools' files.
- **compose.File.(top-level configs map)** `none: derived from Service.Configs` (in A, optional): Same entries and order as the service's configs list, from the one list: `configs:` then per entry ` "<Name>":` through Quote and ` content: |` with the body at indent 6. Omitted when there are none. A writes it always, with two to four entries (A:internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:108-116); B never.
- **compose.File.(top-level secrets map)** `none: derived from Service.Secrets` (in both, optional): Same entries and order as the service's secrets list, from the one list so the two cannot drift (A:internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:135-144; B:internal/render/render.go:1181-1186): `secrets:` then ` "<Name>":` and ` environment: "<EnvVar>"`. Last block of the file. Omitted when there are none. Replaces compose.TopSecrets (M:containerart/compose/compose.go:107-118), which the File renderer covers and so becomes unexported.
- **compose.Service.Name** `string` (in both, required): Written twice: the service key ` "<Name>":` and `container_name: "<Name>"`, both through Quote. One field because both tools write the same value in both places (A:internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:152,154; B:internal/render/render.go:1071,1073) and the engine verbs, inspect and the redeploy check address the container by it. Grammar containercli.ValidName, [A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_.-]* (M:containercli/containercli.go:218-230), which sits inside compose's service-name grammar. A's narrower DNS-1123 rule (A:internal/validate/validate.go:1732) and B's length cap stay tool checks.
- **compose.Service.Image** `string` (in both, required): `image: "<ref>"` through Quote, first field of the service. Bare in both today: A unescaped (A:internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:153), B through Escape only (B:internal/render/render.go:1072). M holds it to one reference grammar shared by the compose, quadlet and kube models: [registry[:port]/]path[:tag][@sha256:<64 hex>], which is B's grammar (B:internal/config/artifactvalues.go:24-42) plus the digest form A's census reports for A's Ref().
- **compose.Service.Hostname** `string` (in B, optional): `hostname: "<h>"` through Quote, after container_name; omitted when empty. B writes it always, from the node identity (B:internal/render/render.go:1074). Grammar: letters, digits, '.', '_', '-', alphanumeric first. A named field, not the slot: a generic platform setting with the same meaning on quadlet (HostName=).
- **compose.Service.User** `string` (in both, optional): `user: "<v>"` through Quote, after hostname. Form uid[:gid] or name[:group]; grammar B's wider one with an alphanumeric or '_' first character (B:internal/config/validate.go:415), of which A's digits-only uid:gid (A:internal/spec/runas.go:42-63) is a subset. Required unless UserFromImage is true: an empty User without that flag is refused. Today A omits the line for run-as: image (A:internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:191-193) and B writes it with no guard, even empty (B:internal/render/render.go:1075).
- **compose.Service.UserFromImage** `bool` (in A, optional): true: no user: line, the image's own user runs; User must then be empty.
