Note gdu 7's scope names quadlet units only (todo.md:426-428), so generate output is a new consumer of that helper. Highest-impact corrections, in order: Extra's deny list (opens a key set that is closed today, and artifacttest does not know the compose spellings); B's word bridge silently becoming docker's shared default bridge; Mount.ReadOnly's unhardened zero value; the missing nesting rule for targets; the block-scalar line breaks; read_only always written (conflicts with a decision and with B's pinned rule 6). Unverified, flagged as such in the corrections: (a) that compose copies environment secrets and inline configs into the container and that a read-only root refuses the copy - from memory, no compose or engine source on this machine; needs a host check. (b) That compose's loader uses the yaml.v3 family: the line-break rule itself is read from the copy in this machine's module cache. (c) Compose's pass-through of a key-only environment entry. (d) Network isolation between a project network and docker's default bridge is standard engine behaviour, not run here. Smaller points. The model's citations I spot-checked all resolve (artifacttest.go:262-266 and 321, compose.go:66-118, todo.md line ranges, B goldens) except the User grammar attribution noted in the corrections. Where M's grammar is narrower than a tool's load gate, File.Validate at load (owner question 18) is what keeps 'questions first, actions after' true. gdu 19's shared project means one compose project and default network per tool on a host; with one service per file, 'down' for one instance also addresses the shared network - orchestration, outside this task, but it meets owner question 5.

### 3.2 quadlet

A writes 40 fields or lines, B writes 71.

#### quadlet: model

- **quadlet.Unit (whole file).Unit.Render** `func (u Unit) Render() (File, error)` (in both, required): Pure: typed value in, bytes out (design note 8.2, docs/design/2026-11-15-shared-deploy-api.md:250-252). Replaces A's podmangen.RenderQuadlet (A internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:135-233) and B's render.Quadlet (B internal/render/render.go:888-1015); neither returns an error today. M holds every value to the unit's grammar and returns one error listing every problem (field, value, rule), as artifacttest's Check functions do. It never writes an empty assignment and never drops a bad value silently. Rules for every value written unquoted: no control character or newline, no leading or trailing space, no trailing backslash (systemd reads it as a line continuation; from systemd.syntax, not run). Escaping by section: in [Container] every value has '%' and '$' doubled, because quadlet copies them into ExecStart where systemd expands both (docs/todo.md:290-297); in [Unit], [Service] and [Install] only '%' is doubled. The model has no field that can hold a secret value.
- **quadlet.File (Render result).File.Name, File.Service** `string, string` (in both, optional): Derived by M from Unit.Name: "<Name>.container" and "<Name>.service". Same as today in both tools (A podmangen.go:232; B internal/container/manager.go:792). The tool joins the directory from podman.QuadletDir (M podman/podman.go:140-162); the directory is not part of the model.
- **quadlet.File (Render result).File.Content, File.Digest** `[]byte, string` (in both, optional): Content is the unit text, LF only, ending in one newline. Digest is the hex SHA-256 of the unit rendered without the digest label line (todo item 10, docs/todo.md:567-568); empty when Unit.DigestLabel is empty. The tool compares Digest with the running container's label (inspect.Container.Labels); the compare and the question stay in the tool.
- **quadlet.Unit (whole file).(layout, no field)** `none (written by M)` (in both, optional): Sections in the order [Unit], [Container], [Service], [Install]; one blank line between sections, none after [Install]. Both tools already write this. [Service] is always written (today A drops it when restart is empty, podmangen.go:223-227). In each section M's own keys come first in a fixed order, then the generic slot's lines in the order the tool gave them.
- **quadlet.Unit (whole file).(header comment, no field)** `none (derived from Identity.Tool)` (in A, optional): M writes one first line: "# Generated by <tool>. Do not edit: a deploy overwrites this file." with no blank line before [Unit]. A writes a product-specific comment today (podmangen.go:139), B none. No tool version and no module version: neither may reach a unit, so a tool upgrade alone restarts nothing (docs/todo.md:67-70).
- **quadlet (package constant).FileMode** `const FileMode os.FileMode = 0o600` (in both, optional): The one mode both tools pass to the atomic write helper of todo item 7 for the installed unit and for generate's output file. Today A installs at 0o644 in place (A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:993, internal/runner/runner.go:118-122) and B at 0o600, applied only when the file is created (B internal/container/manager.go:740). Parent directory 0o755 in both. See ownerQuestions.
- **quadlet.Unit (whole file).Unit.Identity** `Identity (shared with the compose and Kubernetes models): struct{ Tool, Instance, Solution, DevelopedBy string }` (in neither, required): Tool-supplied, written by neither tool today; decided in todo item 1 (docs/todo.md:64-70, 106-107). M derives from it the five ownership Label= lines and the header comment. The same value gives the labels for every podman secret create, so the unit and its secrets cannot disagree. The tool's version is not used here: the version and lib annotations are Kubernetes only. Value grammar is per sink: the unit does not apply the 63-character Kubernetes bound (docs/todo.md:108-110), only non-empty, no control character, valid UTF-8.
- **quadlet.Unit [Unit].Unit.Description** `string` (in both, required): First key of [Unit]. The tool supplies the whole text (A: product name and instance, podmangen.go:142; B: hostname and node type, render.go:896). M writes it unquoted with '%' doubled and refuses a control character, newline or trailing backslash. Both tools write it raw today; B's hostname can come from the OS hostname (B census note 5).
- **quadlet.Unit [Unit].(Wants= and After=, no field)** `none (written by M)` (in both, optional): M writes "Wants=network-online.target" then "After=network-online.target" in every unit. Both tools write the same two constants, in opposite orders (A podmangen.go:143-144; B render.go:897-898). Further ordering keys of a third tool (After=, Requires= on another unit) go through Unit.Extra with SectionUnit, written after these two.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.Image** `string` (in both, required): First key of [Container]: "Image=<ref>". One reference string; the tool builds it (A repo/name:tag or @sha256 digest; B registry/repo:tag). M holds it to the image reference grammar with a tag or a digest, so nothing is left to escape; today A's SafeToken admits '%' (execguard.UnsafeChars has none, M execguard/execguard.go:92) and the value is written raw (podmangen.go:148; render.go:901). M does not require a registry host.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.Name** `string` (in both, required): "ContainerName=<name>", second key. Also the stem of File.Name and File.Service. M checks containercli.ValidName (M containercli/containercli.go:221) and that "<name>.container" fits 255 bytes. A holds it to a DNS-1123 label today (kube.ValidLabel, M kube/kube.go:263), B to the engine grammar and 100 characters (B internal/config/validate.go:404, 410); each tool may keep its narrower rule at its own boundary.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.HostName** `string` (in B, optional): "HostName=<name>" after ContainerName when set; omitted when empty. M checks an RFC 1123 host name. Needs podman 4.6 (M containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:196), so a tool on a 4.5 floor must leave it empty. B writes it raw today (render.go:903). Named, not a slot entry, because a container host name is a generic platform notion with a grammar M can check.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].(ownership labels, no field)** `none (written by M from Identity)` (in neither, optional): Five Label= lines after ContainerName/HostName, in the decided order: app.kubernetes.io/managed-by=<tool>, services.solace.com/product=<tool>, services.solace.com/solution=<solution>, services.solace.com/developed-by=<developed-by>, deploy.solace.com/instance=<instance> (docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:100-102). Each is written like an Environment line, the whole assignment quoted and escaped: Label="key=value". No annotation is written to a unit (decisions.md:103-105).
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.Labels** `[]Label` (in A, optional): The tool's own labels, after M's five, in the order given. A passes solace-connector/le-mode and, when it applies, solace-connector/role (podmangen.go:120-128, 150-152); B passes none. M refuses a duplicate key and a key M owns (app.kubernetes.io/managed-by, anything under services.solace.com/ or deploy.solace.com/).
- **quadlet.Label.Label.Key, Label.Value** `string, string` (in A, required): Key: the Kubernetes qualified-name grammar (optional DNS prefix, '/', name of up to 63 characters), which fits A's solace-connector/... keys and B's solace-util.deploy-sha256. Value: valid UTF-8 with no control character; written inside the quoted assignment through quadlet.Escape (M containerart/quadlet/quadlet.go:37-62). A writes labels raw and unquoted today.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.DigestLabel** `string` (in neither, optional): The tool's deploy-digest label key (solace-connector/deploy-sha256, solace-util.deploy-sha256; docs/todo.md:562-563). When set, M renders the unit without the line, hashes it, and writes Label="<key>=<hex>" as the last Label line; File.Digest returns the same value. Empty writes no line. Decided in todo item 10 for both tools; neither writes it to a unit today.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.User** `RunAs` (in both, required): After the labels: "User=<uid>" then "Group=<gid>", both or neither. The zero value is an error, so a forgotten field cannot mean root (A's own comment: the zero value is root, podmangen.go:50-52). Quadlet.Posture leaves these two lines to the tool today (M quadlet.go:89-90).
- **quadlet.RunAs.IDs(uid, gid int) RunAs; ImageUser() RunAs** `struct with unexported fields, built by the two constructors` (in both, required): IDs: decimal uid and gid, each 0 to 2147483647 (A's bound, internal/spec/runas.go). M always writes both lines. ImageUser (A only, run-as: image, podmangen.go:160-163) writes neither line. B today writes User always, Group only when the value carries one, and admits names (B validate.go:415, render.go:904-907). Uid 0 is allowed when asked for; whether it is acceptable is the tool's rule.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].(privilege posture, no field)** `none (written by M)` (in both, optional): "NoNewPrivileges=true" and "DropCapability=all" after User/Group, in [Container], always, with no way to turn them off (quadlet.Posture, M quadlet.go:91-92; A podmangen.go:164; B render.go:924). artifacttest.CheckQuadletPosture stays the tool-side check (M artifacttest.go:121-145).
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.ReadOnlyRoot** `bool` (in neither, optional): Not written today. Decided for A in todo item 6 (docs/todo.md:357-358); B refuses a read-only root. true writes "ReadOnly=true" after the posture lines; false writes nothing. The key is not in artifacttest's release table (M artifacttest.go:192-198) and must be added with the podman release read from source.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.Network** `Network (string type; words private, bridge, pasta, slirp4netns, host, none)` (in B, optional): M always writes "Network=<word>" after the posture lines; the zero value writes Network=private (docs/todo.md:372-378; decisions.md:187-189). Any other word, a container:, ns: form or a network name is an error. Today A writes no line and B writes only Network=host when asked (render.go:978-979; B accepts bridge or host, validate.go:1705). Whether quadlet takes Network=private on the oldest podman is still an owner check (decisions.md:226).
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.Ports** `[]Port` (in both, optional): One "PublishPort=" line per entry after Network, in the order given; none when empty. Neither tool defaults a port. M returns an error when ports are given with Network host or none; B drops them silently under host today (render.go:978-984).
- **quadlet.Port.Port.IP, Port.Host, Port.Container, Port.Protocol; ParsePort(string) (Port, error)** `string (address, optional), string (port or lo-hi), string (port or lo-hi), string ("", tcp or udp)` (in both, required): Written as [ip:]host:container[/proto], an IPv6 address in brackets. The grammar is B's validContainerPort (B internal/config/artifactvalues.go), which contains A's host:container integers (A internal/spec/targets.go:185-187). IP, ranges and Protocol are B only. ParsePort is the one parser both tools call on their config entries.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.Resources** `Resources: struct{ CPUSet, Memory string }` (in B, optional): After the ports: "PodmanArgs=--cpuset-cpus=<list>" then "PodmanArgs=--memory=<n[b|k|m|g]>", each only when set (B render.go:945-954). M owns the spelling: Memory= exists only from podman 5.5 (M artifacttest.go:182-185, 198) and there is no cpuset key (B render.go:925-930). M checks the cpu list/range grammar and the memory grammar. Which role gets which value (B's monitor node) stays in B.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.Timezone** `string` (in both, optional): When set, the first Environment line: Environment="TZ=<zone>" (A podmangen.go:168-170; B render.go:784-786). M checks a zone-name charset (letters, digits, '_', '+', '-', '/') and refuses an Env entry named TZ beside it.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.Env** `[]EnvVar` (in both, optional): One line per entry in the order given, each as quadlet.Environment writes it today: Environment="NAME=value" with the whole assignment quoted (M quadlet.go:67-69). A slice, not a map, so the order is the tool's. M refuses a duplicate name, since quadlet keeps only the last one.
- **quadlet.EnvVar.EnvVar.Name, EnvVar.Value** `string, string` (in both, required): Name: a letter or '_', then letters, digits, '_', '.' and '-'. It must admit B's keys built from node and user names, such as redundancy_group_node_solace-primary_connectvia (B identRE, validate.go:384), so it is wider than a shell variable name. Value: escaped by quadlet.Escape (backslash, quote, '%', '$', control characters); M refuses only NUL, which podman would drop the line for (M quadlet.go:34-36). The value may be empty.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.Secrets** `[]Secret` (in both, optional): One "Secret=<name>,type=mount,target=<path>" line per entry after the Environment lines, in the order given (M quadlet.go:78-80). Always type=mount; no mode=, uid= or gid= (podman's default, 0444 owned by root, M quadlet.go:76-77). The tool creates the secrets in the same order through podman.Client.CreateSecret.
- **quadlet.Secret.Secret.Name, Secret.Target** `string, string` (in both, required): Name: podman's secret name grammar (a letter or digit, then letters, digits, '_', '.', '-'); an empty name is an error (A skips it today, podmangen.go:105-108). Target: an absolute path with no comma, whitespace or control character, '%' and '$' doubled; a duplicate target is an error. This is the check todo item 4 moves into the emitter (docs/todo.md:278-280). No field for the value.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.Mounts** `[]Mount` (in both, optional): One "Volume=<source>:<target>[:options]" line per entry after the Secret lines, in the order given. A mounts its libs directory read-only (podmangen.go:199-201); B its data directory (render.go:985). Both write the source raw today.
- **quadlet.Mount.Mount.Source, Mount.Target, Mount.ReadOnly, Mount.Relabel** `string, string, bool, Relabel (RelabelNone, RelabelShared = z, RelabelPrivate = Z)` (in both, required): Source: an absolute host path (a bare name would be read as a named volume, which M does not manage); M refuses ':' , whitespace and control characters and doubles '%' and '$' (docs/todo.md:290-297). M checks the path it is handed, so the resolved prefix A leaves unchecked today is covered. Target: an absolute container path without ':'. Options are built from the typed fields only, comma-joined, ro first then the relabel letter: "ro", "Z", "ro,z". ReadOnly is A only today, Relabel B only.
- **quadlet.Unit [Container].Unit.Health** `*Health` (in both, optional): nil writes no health key. Otherwise five lines after the Volume lines: HealthCmd, HealthInterval, HealthTimeout, HealthRetries, HealthStartPeriod (the order both tools use). When to probe and the timings are the tool's (A: fixed, only with the status script, podmangen.go:214-220; B: healthCheck.enabled with configurable values, render.go:961-975). HealthOnFailure is never written.
- **quadlet.Health.Health.Cmd** `[]string` (in both, required): The probe argv. M joins the tokens with single spaces, doubles '%' and '$', and writes it unquoted. It refuses an empty argv, an empty token, a control character, and a token that holds whitespace, which the joined line cannot carry (B's own comment, render.go:962-969). B escapes only '%' today; A writes a constant raw.
- **quadlet.Health.Health.Interval, Health.Timeout, Health.StartPeriod, Health.Retries** `time.Duration, time.Duration, time.Duration, int` (in both, required): Durations are written as whole seconds, "<n>s" (30s, 90s); a value that is not a whole number of seconds is an error. Interval and Timeout must be above zero, StartPeriod zero or more, Retries 1 or more (B's rules, validHealthDuration). A builds the text from integers, B writes the operator's Go duration string.
- **quadlet.Unit (generic slot).Unit.Extra** `[]Setting` (in B, optional): The slot of design note 8.2 for a workload's own settings (shared-deploy-api.md:273-274). Written last in its section, in the order given. B passes ShmSize, three Ulimit lines and three [Service] Limit lines; A passes nothing.
- **quadlet.Setting.Setting.Section, Setting.Key, Setting.Value** `Section (SectionUnit, SectionContainer, SectionService), string, string` (in B, required): Key: allowed only from a list M holds per section. [Container]: a key of the podman release table that M does not write itself and that is not a widening key (so ShmSize and Ulimit pass; Image, Label, User, Network, Secret, Environment, AddCapability, AddDevice, PodmanArgs and the like are refused). [Service]: the Limit* keys and the timeout and restart-delay keys; never Exec*, Environment*, User or NoNewPrivileges, which would bind or redirect the podman process. [Unit]: ordering and dependency keys. Value: non-empty, escaped by the section rule, and refused when it holds a spelling of artifacttest.Widening() or HostNetwork() (M artifacttest.go:33-56). A key repeats when given more than once.
- **quadlet.Unit [Service].Unit.Restart** `Restart (string type; no, always, on-success, on-failure, on-abnormal, on-abort, on-watchdog)` (in both, optional): First key of [Service]: "Restart=<word>", always written; the zero value writes Restart=always. The words are systemd's, the list A checks today (A internal/validate/validate.go:1670-1673). A takes the word from podman.restart (default always); B writes the constant always (render.go:1010).
- **quadlet.Unit [Install].Unit.Rootful** `bool` (in both, optional): M writes "WantedBy=default.target" when false (rootless, the default) and "WantedBy=multi-user.target" when true. That is B's rule (B internal/config/load.go:278-285, render.go:1013); A writes default.target in both modes (podmangen.go:230). The same flag is what the tool passes to podman.QuadletDir.

#### quadlet: extras

- B: [Container] ShmSize=2g. A fixed requirement of the broker image (B internal/config/config.go, ContainerShmSize). One key, no logic for M to own, and it needs podman 4.7 (M containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:197), above A's 4.5 floor. Setting{SectionContainer, "ShmSize", "2g"}.
- B: [Container] Ulimit=nofile=2448:1048576, Ulimit=memlock=-1, Ulimit=core=<limit>. Design note 8.2 names solace-util's ulimits as the example for the slot (docs/design/2026-11-15-shared-deploy-api.md:273-274). The key repeats, the values are broker requirements and the operator's core limit, and the key needs podman 4.7. Three Setting entries in B's order (B render.go:958-960).
- B: [Service] LimitNOFILE=, LimitMEMLOCK=, LimitCORE=. The systemd-side ceiling for the three Ulimit lines (B render.go:1001-1009). Only a workload that raises limits needs them, and the -1 to infinity rewrite is B's (SystemdCoreLimit). Setting entries with SectionService, written after M's Restart= line.
- A: [Container] Label=solace-connector/le-mode and Label=solace-connector/role. Leader election is the connector's own notion (A podmangen.go:115-129). They go through the generic Unit.Labels list, after M's ownership labels; the rule for when the role label is written stays in A.
- A: Environment JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS, JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS, LOGGING_SYSLOG_APPNAME, LOGGING_SYSLOG_HOST, LOGGING_SYSLOG_PORT. Application content. They go through Unit.Env; the merge of the MQ TLS flag with the operator's options and the syslog condition stay in A (podmangen.go:171-182).
- B: Environment broker settings (routername, nodetype, redundancy_*, configsync_*, system_scaling_*, username_*, *filepath). Application content, the same list B writes into its compose file (EnvPairs, B render.go:779-864). They go through Unit.Env in B's order; M only escapes them.
- A: Secret mounts for application.yml, the status script, logback-spring.xml, the TLS stores and the credentials. The names, targets and their order are the connector's layout (podmangen.go:183-198). They go through Unit.Secrets; rendering the files' content stays in A (design note 8.3).
- B: Secret mounts for the admin and user passwords, the pre-shared key, the certificate passphrase and the server certificate. Names and targets follow the broker's settings (B render.go:996-998). They go through Unit.Secrets in B's order, the server certificate last.
- B: /etc/sysctl.d/99-solace.conf (not a quadlet artifact). Host tuning for one workload, written by B through the same writeArtifact helper as the unit (B census). M renders no model for it; it stays in B and moves to the atomic write helper of todo item 7 with the unit.
- third tool: [Unit] After=, Requires= on another unit; [Service] TimeoutStartSec=, TimeoutStopSec=, RestartSec=. Neither tool writes them, so no named field is justified yet. The slot's allow-lists for [Unit] and [Service] carry them.
- third tool: [Container] Exec= (command arguments), Tmpfs=, AutoUpdate=. Neither tool writes them. Each needs its release added to M's table before the slot accepts it; a named field (for example Args []string) follows only if the example tool needs one.

#### quadlet: differences

- **Header comment**
  - A: One first line: "# Generated by solmq-conn-util -- podman quadlet unit for the Solace PubSub+ Connector for IBM MQ." (podmangen.go:139).
  - B: No comment line in the unit.
  - Proposal: M writes one first line from Identity.Tool: "# Generated by <tool>. Do not edit: a deploy overwrites this file." No version of the tool or the module, so an upgrade alone changes no unit.
- **[Unit] key order**
  - A: Description, After, Wants (podmangen.go:142-144).
  - B: Description, Wants, After (render.go:896-898).
  - Proposal: Description, Wants, After, then the slot's [Unit] lines.
- **Description text and escaping**
  - A: Constant product text plus podman.name, written raw.
  - B: Constant product text plus hostname and node type, written raw; the hostname can come from the OS in standalone.
  - Proposal: The tool supplies the whole text in Unit.Description. M doubles '%' and refuses control characters, a newline and a trailing backslash.
- **[Container] key order**
  - A: Image, ContainerName, Label, User, Group, posture, PublishPort, Environment, Secret, Volume, Health*.
  - B: Image, ContainerName, HostName, User, Group, posture, PodmanArgs, ShmSize, Ulimit, Health*, Network or PublishPort, Volume, Environment, Secret.
  - Proposal: Image, ContainerName, HostName, Label (M's five, the tool's, the digest label last), User, Group, NoNewPrivileges, DropCapability, ReadOnly, Network, PublishPort, PodmanArgs (cpuset then memory), Environment (TZ first), Secret, Volume, Health* (Cmd, Interval, Timeout, Retries, StartPeriod), then the slot's lines. Groups: identity, privileges, network, resources, content, probe, extras.
- **What is escaped**
  - A: Only Environment= (quadlet.Escape) and Secret= ('%' doubled). Image, ContainerName, Description, Label, PublishPort, Volume, HealthCmd and Restart are raw and rely on load-time gates, two of which admit '%'.
  - B: Environment=, Secret= and the Health lines ('%' only). Description, Image, ContainerName, HostName, User, Group, PodmanArgs, PublishPort, Volume and WantedBy are raw.
  - Proposal: M escapes every value by one rule: '%' and '$' doubled in [Container], '%' only in the other sections, quadlet.Escape inside a quoted assignment. A field with a grammar (image, names, ports, ids, words) is checked instead, so it has nothing to escape.
- **Image reference**
  - A: repo/name:tag, or @digest when the tag starts with sha256:; held to SafeToken, which admits '%'.
  - B: registry/repo:tag held to the OCI reference grammar; a digest cannot be expressed.
  - Proposal: One string field. M checks the reference grammar with a tag or a digest. How the string is built, and whether B's config can name a digest, stay in each tool.
- **Container name grammar**
  - A: DNS-1123 label, at most 63 lower-case characters (kube.ValidLabel); default "connector".
  - B: Letters, digits, '.', '_', '-' with an alphanumeric first character, at most 100 (validate.go:404, 410); default "solace".
  - Proposal: M checks containercli.ValidName and that the file name fits 255 bytes. A tool may keep a narrower rule; defaults stay in the tools.
- **Labels**
  - A: Two lines, raw and unquoted: Label=solace-connector/le-mode=<mode>, and Label=solace-connector/role=active for standalone and active_active.
  - B: No Label line.
  - Proposal: M writes the five ownership labels, then the tool's labels, then the digest label, every one as Label="key=value" quoted whole like an Environment line, so a value with a space stays one label.
- **User and Group**
  - A: Integers. Both lines, or neither for run-as: image. A bare uid means gid 0 (internal/spec/runas.go).
  - B: Strings that may be names (runUserRE, validate.go:415). User always; Group only when the value carries one (render.go:904-907).
  - Proposal: Numeric RunAs. M writes User= and Group= together, or neither for ImageUser(). A bare uid in B's config resolves to gid 0 in B before the call, as in A. The default ids (A 1000:0; B 1000:0 rootless and 1000001:0 rootful) stay in the tools.
- **Network line**
  - A: None: bridge is implied, never stated. No network key in the config.
  - B: Network=host only when podman.network.mode is host; nothing for the default, which B calls bridge (load.go:261).
  - Proposal: M always writes Network=<word>; the zero value writes Network=private; the accepted words are podman's six (todo item 6). B's default word changes from an unstated bridge to a stated private.
- **PublishPort grammar**
  - A: host:container from two integers 1 to 65535; a bare N becomes N:N.
  - B: [ip:]host:container[/tcp|/udp] with ranges, written as the operator typed it; skipped silently under host networking.
  - Proposal: One Port type and one ParsePort with B's grammar, which contains A's. M returns an error for ports under host or none; a tool that wants B's silent drop passes no ports.
- **Resource caps**
  - A: None.
  - B: PodmanArgs=--cpuset-cpus= and PodmanArgs=--memory=, raw (render.go:945-954).
  - Proposal: Named Resources fields; M writes the two PodmanArgs lines and checks both grammars. A PodmanArgs= line in the generic slot is refused.
- **Environment names and order**
  - A: Upper-case names; TZ, JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS, JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS, LOGGING_SYSLOG_*; before the Secret lines.
  - B: Lower-case broker settings, some with '-' or '.' from node and user names; TZ first; after the Volume line.
  - Proposal: Same line form as today (quadlet.Environment). Timezone is a named field written first; the rest keep the tool's order. The name grammar admits B's keys. A duplicate name is an error.
- **Secret lines**
  - A: Written before Volume and Health; an empty store name is skipped silently (podmangen.go:105-108).
  - B: Written last in [Container]; no skip.
  - Proposal: Same line form (name,type=mount,target=path), after Environment and before Volume. An empty name, a relative target, a comma or a duplicate target is an error. No mode=, uid= or gid=.
- **Volume line**
  - A: <libs dir resolved against env.yaml>:/app/external/libs:ro. No SELinux option. Only the value as written is checked; '%', ':' and backslash pass (validate.go:1150, 1164-1166).
  - B: <dataDir>:/var/lib/solace:Z, raw (render.go:985). The source must be absolute; '%' passes, and the colon is left to the renderer (hostpath.go:41-46). docs/todo.md:1104-1107 records that B escapes '%' and refuses ':', which the checkout I read does not do yet.
  - Proposal: Typed Mount: absolute source and target, options built from ReadOnly and Relabel ("ro", "Z", "ro,z"). M doubles '%' and '$' and refuses ':' and whitespace in the source it is handed, including the resolved prefix.
- **HealthCmd**
  - A: Constant "sh /app/external/.status-script --health", raw; written only when the status script is mounted.
  - B: The configured argv, or the built-in curl probe, joined with spaces and '%' doubled; written only when healthCheck.enabled.
  - Proposal: Health.Cmd is an argv. M joins with single spaces, doubles '%' and '$', and refuses a token that holds whitespace. The condition is the tool's: a nil Health writes nothing.
- **Health timings**
  - A: Integer constants written as 30s, 10s, 3, 60s.
  - B: Operator strings in Go duration form (defaults 5s, 5s, 3, 60s), '%' doubled.
  - Proposal: time.Duration fields written as whole seconds ("90s" for 1m30s); retries an int of 1 or more. Defaults stay in the tools.
- **[Service] section**
  - A: Only Restart=<podman.restart>; the whole section is dropped when the value is empty.
  - B: Always written: LimitNOFILE, LimitMEMLOCK, LimitCORE, then Restart=always.
  - Proposal: M always writes [Service] with Restart= first, then the slot's lines. B's three Limit lines move after Restart.
- **Restart word**
  - A: Operator key podman.restart held to systemd's seven words, default always.
  - B: Constant always; no key.
  - Proposal: Typed Restart with systemd's words; the zero value writes always. Whether B gains a key is todo item 19, not the model.
- **WantedBy**
  - A: default.target for rootful and rootless (podmangen.go:230).
  - B: default.target rootless, multi-user.target rootful (load.go:278-285).
  - Proposal: M derives it from Unit.Rootful, B's way. A's rootful units change to multi-user.target.
- **Renderer result and refusal**
  - A: Returns Unit{Filename, Content string}; never an error.
  - B: Returns []byte; never an error; the file name is built in the deploy code.
  - Proposal: Render returns File{Name, Service, Content, Digest} and an error. The load-time checks in each tool stay as the first gate; M's are the gate at the sink.
- **Installed file mode and write**
  - A: 0o644, written in place with os.WriteFile after MkdirAll 0o755 (main.go:993; runner.go:118-122).
  - B: 0o600, os.WriteFile after MkdirAll 0o755; the mode applies only when the file is created (manager.go:736-740).
  - Proposal: Both write through the atomic helper of todo item 7 with quadlet.FileMode 0o600 and a 0o755 directory. The helper sets the mode on the temporary file, so an older 0o644 file is replaced by a 0o600 one.
- **generate output file**
  - A: stdout, or -o with os.WriteFile 0o600 in place (main.go:1776).
  - B: stdout, or --out through a temporary file, chmod 0o600 and a rename; a symlink is refused and an existing file is confirmed (internal/cli/helpers.go:160-195).
  - Proposal: The same atomic helper and mode in both. The symlink refusal and the overwrite question are orchestration and stay in each tool.
- **Unit directory**
  - A: Always podman.QuadletDir; no config key.
  - B: podman.quadletDir key (config.go:1004), defaulted the same way.
  - Proposal: Outside the model: Render gives the file name, the tool joins the directory. Aligning the key is todo item 19.
- **Reading the unit back**
  - A: Parses the installed unit's Secret= lines to find stale TLS store secrets (gen.go:605-619).
  - B: Never parses its unit; compares bytes only.
  - Proposal: M offers no unit parser. A drops this read when the labels land (docs/todo.md:886-888), and the byte compare gives way to the digest label of todo item 10, which Render returns.
- **Podman floor**
  - A: 4.5 (AssertQuadletKeys floor in gen_runas_test.go, per A's census).
  - B: 4.7, because of ShmSize and Ulimit.
  - Proposal: The floor stays each tool's. M's own lines stay within 4.5; HostName (4.6) and the slot's keys raise it only for the tool that sets them. artifacttest.CheckQuadletKeys stays the check, and its table gains ReadOnly.
- **Empty values**
  - A: The renderer skips silently: empty timezone, empty restart, empty secret name, nil libs.
  - B: The renderer skips silently: empty gid, cpuset, mem, empty port list.
  - Proposal: An optional field left at its zero value writes nothing; a required field left empty and an empty entry inside a list are errors. No line is ever written with nothing after the '='.

#### quadlet: owner questions

1. Is the proposed key order accepted: in [Container] Image, ContainerName, HostName, Label, User, Group, the two posture lines, ReadOnly, Network, PublishPort, PodmanArgs, Environment, Secret, Volume, Health*, then the slot; and in every section M's keys first and the slot's lines last? Recommended: yes. Every golden changes once anyway (design note 8.4), and one rule per section is easy to document and to pin.
2. Should every unit start with a comment line, as A's does today? Recommended: yes, one line naming the tool from the identity and nothing else. No tool or module version, so the decision that an upgrade alone restarts nothing holds.
3. Should a Label= line be written quoted whole, Label="key=value", like an Environment line, or raw as A writes it? Recommended: quoted whole, so a value with a space cannot split into two labels. I recall that quadlet reads Label= the same way as Environment= but did not open podman's source here; confirm it at the 4.5 tag before the plan is fixed, and fall back to raw with a no-whitespace grammar if it does not hold.
4. For a third tool outside Solace Professional Services, are services.solace.com/solution and services.solace.com/developed-by parameters or constants? Recommended: two fields of the shared identity. A and B pass accelerator and apac-psg; a tool that leaves them empty gets the other three labels only. The product and instance keys stay as decided, because removal selects on them.
5. Which B-only keys get a named field? Recommended: HostName, the memory cap and the cpuset are named, because each is a generic container setting and M owns the spelling that fits the podman floor (PodmanArgs=--memory= and not Memory=). ShmSize, Ulimit and the [Service] Limit lines go through the slot, as design note 8.2 says.
6. What may the generic slot carry? Recommended: only keys from a list M holds per section (the podman release table for [Container], a short list for [Service] and [Unit]), never a key M writes itself, never a widening key, and never PodmanArgs=, which can carry any podman flag. Render refuses anything else with an error naming the key. artifacttest stays as the tool's own check.
7. Should the run user be numeric only, with User= and Group= always written together? Recommended: yes. B's runUser stops accepting names, and a bare uid there means gid 0, as in A. The identity is then stated in full and can be compared with the Kubernetes and compose models.
8. Should WantedBy follow the rootful flag (default.target rootless, multi-user.target rootful)? Recommended: yes, B's rule. A's rootful units change from default.target to multi-user.target.
9. Which mode does an installed unit get, A's 0644 or B's 0600? Recommended: 0600, as a constant in M used by both tools through the atomic write helper. Only the quadlet generator of the same account reads the file, and todo item 10 already notes that A writes it world-readable.
10. A's libs mount carries ':ro' and no SELinux option, while B's data mount carries ':Z'. Should A's mount be relabelled? Recommended: the model takes an explicit Relabel value and writes none unless asked; A decides in its own session, and I would choose the shared label ('ro,z'), since two instances may mount one libs directory. On an enforcing host a bind mount without a label is usually unreadable in the container; this was not run.
11. M will check the mount source it is handed, which for A is the path resolved against env.yaml's directory. An env.yaml under a directory with a space, ':' or a control character then fails generate and deploy on podman when libs is set. Accept? Recommended: yes, fail with an error naming the path. '%' and '$' stay legal and are doubled.
12. How should HealthCmd be written from an argv? Recommended for this tag: joined with single spaces, as both tools do, with a token that holds whitespace refused (B allows such a token at load and mis-renders it on podman). The JSON array form, which would carry the exact argv with no shell in the container, is a later change after one host check on the oldest podman.
13. Should the timezone be a named field of the container models, or one more entry the tool puts into the environment list? Recommended: a named field. Both tools write TZ first and only when set, and one field gives one grammar check on all three platforms.
14. When ports are given together with host or none networking, should Render refuse or drop them? Recommended: refuse. B keeps its documented behaviour by passing no ports under host; a third tool gets an error, not a silent loss.
15. Should Render stamp the deploy-digest label itself when the tool passes its key? Recommended: yes. Only the renderer knows where the line goes, so the two passes of todo item 10 belong there; File.Digest returns the value and the tool does the compare.
16. Should the Unit model know the tool's podman floor and refuse a key above it? Recommended: no. The floor stays in each tool's tests through artifacttest.CheckQuadletKeys; M's own lines stay within 4.5, and ReadOnly is added to the release table with the release read from podman's source.
17. For the read-only root decided for A in todo item 6: is ReadOnly written only when asked, and where do the writable paths go? Recommended: write ReadOnly=true only when the field is set (the 2026-11-15 decision leaves the root filesystem to each tool). Writable tmpfs paths wait for item 6's plan and use the slot until then.
18. Does the model need command arguments (Exec=) or a way to add one capability back for a third tool? Recommended: neither in this tag. Add an Args field only if the example tool needs it; the posture has no opt-out, and a tool that needs a capability raises it as its own change.
19. Should the compose Service model and the quadlet Unit model share their leaf types (Identity, Label, RunAs, Port, Mount, EnvVar, Health)? Recommended: yes, one set in containerart, each renderer checking its own sink's grammar. B's environment list is already the same for both engines (render.go:772-774). The secret type stays per sink, since compose also needs the variable it reads the value from.
20. Is the zero value of Restart 'always'? Recommended: yes, and the line is always written. Whether solace-util gains a restart key belongs to todo item 19.
21. Is containercli.ValidName the grammar M holds Unit.Name to? Recommended: yes, plus the file-name length. A keeps its DNS-1123 rule at its own boundary if it wants the same name on Kubernetes.
22. Should a secret mount state mode, uid or gid? Recommended: no. Both tools rely on podman's default (0444, root), which matches what compose gives; a stricter mode would need the same on docker, where compose does not apply it.

#### quadlet: census notes

- A: Scope. Tool A writes exactly one quadlet artifact: one .container unit, from podmangen.RenderQuadlet (A:internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:135-233), fed by gen.GeneratePodman (A:internal/gen/gen.go:693-775). No .network, .volume, .pod or .kube unit, no drop-in. Cites are relative to A unless marked M (M). The compose file and the Kubernetes documents are outside this task and not listed. Writer. Every line goes through A's own internal/yamlwriter Writer.Line(0, ...) (yamlwriter.go:18-22); the posture block alone goes through Writer.Raw (yamlwriter.go:27, podmangen.go:164). M pieces used today: quadlet.Environment, quadlet.Secret, quadlet.Posture (with Escape and EscapePercent underneath). Everything else is hand-written in A: 29 of 39 lines have no M piece. Ordering rules (all fixed by code order, pinned byte for byte by TestRenderQuadletFull and TestRenderQuadletMinimal, podmangen_test.go:69-114 and 116-148): - Comment line first, with no blank line before [Unit]. - Sections [Unit], [Container], [Service], [Install]; one blank line after each of the first three; no blank line after [Install]; file ends with one newline. - [Container] order: Image, ContainerName, Label (mode then role), User, Group, NoNewPrivileges, DropCapability, PublishPort (config order), Environment (TZ, JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS, JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS, LOGGING_SYSLOG_APPNAME, _HOST, _PORT), Secret (credentials in model order, application.yml, TLS stores in model order, status script, logback), Volume, Health* (Cmd, Interval, Timeout, Retries, StartPeriod). - JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS puts the tool's MQ TLS flag before the operator's options so the operator's wins (spec/javaoptions.go:100-102). - plan.Files is built in the same order as the Secret= lines so deploy creates what the unit mounts (gen.go:726-728, 558-561). Comments written into the artifact: one, the "# Generated by solmq-conn-util ..." header (podmangen.go:139). Nothing else; the long explanations are Go comments only. What the renderer refuses: nothing. RenderQuadlet is pure, returns no error and emits values verbatim (podmangen.go:2-7); it only skips: an empty secret name (106-108), User=/Group= for run-as image (160), TZ on empty timezone (168), the [Service] section on empty restart (223), Volume on nil libs (199), Health* on an empty status secret (214). All refusal is upstream in GeneratePodman, which returns no unit on any error: - no podman: section (gen.go:696-698); any validate.Run error (gen.go:699-707); a run-as ParseRunAs rejects (gen.go:720-723, spec/runas.go:47-63: "image" or digits, no leading zero, max 2147483647); status password or build failure (gen.go:709-717). - podman.name must be a DNS-1123 label, checked with M kube.ValidLabel (validate.go:1732-1734). - podman.restart must be a systemd Restart= word; unless-stopped and on-failure:N get their own messages (validate.go:1673, 1680-1701). - ports 1-65535 each side (validate.go:1627-1634). - image.name and image.tag required; repo, name, tag held to SafeToken (validate.go:1531-1555); timezone held to SafeToken (validate.go:1528-1530). - java-options: no '$', quotes, backslash, backtick, control or invisible characters, no " #", valid UTF-8; '%' is allowed and left to the renderer's doubling (validate.go:1486-1516). - logging.syslog: host required and ^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]+$, port 1-65535, protocol udp or tcp (validate.go:141, 999-1018). - libs: dir required when libs is set, mount-path refused, dir held to safeHostPath (validate.go:1747-1761). - tls.stores names: lowercase letters and digits joined by single hyphens, max 63 (validate.go:452, 462-463); a selected store held only in a Kubernetes Secret is refused for podman (validate.go:1219-1227). - Removed keys refused: podman.image, podman.timezone, podman.secrets, podman.stores (validate.go:1718-1744), podman.mode and podman.quadlet (validate.go:1840-1860). podman.base-dir is ignored with a lint warning (validate.go:1848-1851). - Leader-election mode must be standalone, active_active or active_standby (validate.go:676). Escaping gaps a module Unit model would close (observed, not run): only Environment= and Secret= are escaped. Image=, ContainerName=, Description=, Label=, PublishPort=, Volume=, HealthCmd= and Restart= are written raw and rely on the load-time gates. Two of those gates do not cover '%', which systemd expands as a specifier: SafeToken is built on execguard.UnsafeChars, which has no '%' (M execguard/execguard.go:92; validate.go:1871-1873), so image.repo/name/tag can carry one into Image=; and Volume= takes res.abs(libs.dir), where safeHostPath admits '%' and only the value as written is checked, never the env.yaml directory it is joined to (validate.go:1161-1166), so a working directory with a space or '%' reaches the unit unchecked. Not emitted at all (relevant to the decided Unit model): no Network= line (bridge is implied, never stated; design note 8.2 has M write the network line), no Privileged-style key (quadlet has none), no mode=/uid=/gid= on Secret= (podman default 0444 root, M quadlet.go:76-77), no HealthOnFailure (deliberate, podmangen.go:207-209), no Exec/Entrypoint, EnvironmentFile, PodmanArgs, UserNS, AutoUpdate, Pull, ReadOnly, no [Service] key other than Restart, no lib or tool-version label. The only labels are the two leader-election ones. Podman floor: User/Group/NoNewPrivileges/DropCapability need 4.4, Health* need 4.5 (podmangen.go:157-159, 213). Sinks and file mode: - generate --platform podman: plan.Unit.Content to stdout with fmt.Print, or to the -o file with os.WriteFile mode 0600 (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:499-510, 1771-1779). - deploy: path = filepath.Join(sc.Dir, plan.Unit.Filename); the installed file is read and compared byte for byte, and the unit is written only when it differs, with runner.WriteFile(unitPath, content, 0o644) (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:958-960, 992-996). runner.WriteFile creates parent directories 0o755 and then os.WriteFile (internal/runner/runner.go:116-126). sc.Dir comes from M podman.QuadletDir: the system quadlet directory for rootful, else $XDG_CONFIG_HOME or <home>/.config plus containers/systemd (internal/runner/runner.go:651-660; M podman/podman.go:140-162). No operator key chooses the directory. - Nothing else of a podman deployment is written to the host: application.yml, the status script, logback-spring.xml, the TLS stores and the credentials go into podman's secret store (main.go:933-939; gen.go:553-566). - The unit is also read back as data: gen.PodmanUnitStoreSecrets parses the installed unit's "Secret=<name>," lines by prefix <name>-tls- to find stale store secrets on deploy and remove (gen.go:605-619; main.go:1062-1068). A module renderer must keep that line shape, or M must offer the parser. - Because of the byte compare, any layout change from a module Unit model makes the first deploy see a changed unit and ask to restart (design note 8.4 accepts this). Test pins on the artifact: inline goldens in podmangen_test.go (above); internal/gen/gen_runas_test.go uses M artifacttest: AssertQuadletPosture (103), AssertAbsent with Widening() + HostNetwork() (135-147), AssertQuadletKeys with floor "4.5" (158), AssertSecretTargets (168-171).
- B: Scope. Cites are relative to tool B (B) unless prefixed "M" (M). Tool B renders exactly one quadlet artifact: one .container unit per host, from render.Quadlet (internal/render/render.go:888-1015). I found no .network, .volume, .pod or .kube unit, and no systemd drop-in written for the unit: the user@<uid>.service drop-in and the PAM limits file are only named in remedy text (internal/container/limits.go:36,41,258-260). The sysctl drop-in /etc/sysctl.d/99-solace.conf is written through the same writeArtifact helper (internal/container/limits.go:160-164) but is not a quadlet artifact; it is the one place a comment line is written ("# Written by solace-util: ..."). Module pieces used today (4 of M's quadlet exports): quadlet.Posture (render.go:924), quadlet.EscapePercent (render.go:970-972,974), quadlet.Environment (render.go:987), quadlet.Secret (render.go:997). quadlet.Escape is reached only through Environment. Everything else is fmt.Fprintf in the tool. What the renderer refuses. Nothing: Quadlet returns []byte with no error (render.go:888) and is pure. It only skips: Group= when no gid (905), the two PodmanArgs lines when the value is empty (949-954), the Health* block when disabled (961), PublishPort when the list is empty or mode is host (978-984), TZ when unset (784). All refusals are at config load, and the unescaped lines depend on them: - image.registry/repo/tag held to the OCI reference grammar; a digest cannot be expressed (internal/config/artifactvalues.go:86-105). - podman.container.name and the three redundancy names held to identRE; container.name also to engineContainerNameRE and a 100 character bound (internal/config/validate.go:1593-1624, 384, 404, 410). - runUser to uid[:gid] (validate.go:1625-1628, 415); mem to integer+b|k|m|g (validate.go:1629-1635, scaling.go:60); cpuset and monitorCpuset to the list/range grammar, not inverted, with the count matching the tier or exactly 1 for the monitor (validate.go:1636-1677). - network.ports entries (artifactvalues.go:132-181), health timings as Go durations and ulimits.core as -1 or a byte count (artifactvalues.go:187-221, 227-255); network.mode must be bridge or host (validate.go:1701-1705). - healthCheck.cmd tokens: no empty token, valid UTF-8, no control or invisible character; whitespace and shell syntax stay allowed (validate.go:351-372). The built-in probe is refused for an image tag older than 10.26 or unreadable (validate.go:1715-1736). - timezone charset (artifactvalues.go:56-81); additional usernames to the broker grammar, unique, not admin/monitor, access level from the enum (validate.go:1256-1298). - tls.cert and tls.certKey must be set together (validate.go:1568-1573); dataDir must be absolute and pass CheckHostPath (validate.go:1575-1580; hostpath.go:73-101); image.repo, image.tag, semp.adminPass, dataDir and in HA all six names/addresses plus the psk are required (validate.go:1490-1530). - render.SecretPreflight refuses an empty secret value before deploy; it is not part of rendering (render.go:1198-1223). Ordering rules. - Sections: [Unit], [Container], [Service], [Install], one blank line between sections, no trailing blank line; the file ends with the WantedBy line and its newline. - [Container] key order is fixed: Image, ContainerName, HostName, User, Group, the Posture block, PodmanArgs cpuset then memory, ShmSize, Ulimit nofile/memlock/core, Health* (5 lines), Network=host or PublishPort lines, Volume, Environment lines, Secret lines. - Environment order is EnvPairs order (render.go:779-864): TZ, routername, nodetype, the HA block or redundancy_enable=no, the cert filepath, eight scaling settings, admin access level, additional users' access levels in config order, then one *filepath line per secret spec. - Secret order is containerSecretSpecs order (admin, additional users in config order, psk, cert passphrase) with the server certificate appended last "so no existing index or golden moves" (render.go:725). - NoNewPrivileges and DropCapability must sit in [Container], not [Service] (render.go:908-923); M's artifacttest.AssertQuadletPosture checks that by position (internal/render/render_test.go:1236-1244). - Every [Container] key is held to the podman 4.7 floor by TestQuadletKeysAreWithinTheFloor (render_test.go:1632,1642-1682); that is why memory and cpuset ride PodmanArgs= and not Memory= (render.go:925-930). Comments in the artifact. None. The unit carries no '#' line; I read two goldens in full (internal/render/testdata/podman_quadlet_healthcheck.golden, podman_quadlet_monitor.golden) and the renderer has no comment write. Sink and mode. - Deploy: path is <podman.quadletDir>/<container.name>.container, forward slashes (internal/container/manager.go:792). The tool compares first with bytes.Equal (manager.go:711-717), asks if the service is running, then writeArtifact: MkdirAll of the directory at 0o755 and os.WriteFile at 0o600 (manager.go:722-745, 828). An unchanged unit is not rewritten. Under the Echo runner nothing is read or written and the unit counts as changed. Note os.WriteFile applies 0o600 only when it creates the file (the tool's own comment at internal/cli/helpers.go:166-169 says so). - Remove: os.Remove of the same path, then daemon-reload (manager.go:1053-1063). - Generate: emitOrWrite sends the same bytes to stdout, or to --out through a temp file chmod 0600 and a rename; a symlink target is refused and an existing file needs confirmation (internal/cli/ops_container.go:406-413; internal/cli/helpers.go:142-195). Points for the Unit model in M (found by reading; nothing was run). 1. The model needs more than Environment/Secret/Posture: [Unit] Description/Wants/After, HostName, User/Group, repeatable PodmanArgs, ShmSize, repeatable Ulimit, the five Health* keys, Network or repeatable PublishPort, Volume with a :Z option, a [Service] block (LimitNOFILE, LimitMEMLOCK, LimitCORE, Restart) and [Install] WantedBy. PodmanArgs, ShmSize, Ulimit and the [Service] Limit* lines are the "workload's extra settings" slot of design note 8.2. 2. Escaping is uneven today. Only Health* (EscapePercent), Environment (Escape) and Secret (EscapePercent) are escaped. Description, Image, ContainerName, HostName, User, Group, PodmanArgs, PublishPort, Volume and WantedBy are written raw and rely on the load-time grammars above. 3. Possible gap on Volume=: podman.container.dataDir is written raw (render.go:985), and CheckHostPath's refused set is execguard.UnsafeChars minus the backslash (hostpath.go:47-52), which holds no '%' and no ':' (M execguard/execguard.go:92). So a data directory containing '%' would reach systemd unescaped, and one containing ':' would add a field to the mount line. I did not confirm whether IsAbsHostPath or another check closes this; a module renderer that owns the Volume line should escape '%' and decide the ':' case. 4. redundancy.*.addr has only a not-empty check that I could find (validate.go:1515-1519); it is safe in the unit because quadlet.Environment escapes it. 5. In standalone, id.Hostname can come from the OS hostname (FillStandaloneNodeName, internal/config/roles.go:132-138) and is written raw into Description= and HostName=. I did not trace whether that fill happens before the identRE check. 6. The unit never carries a secret value: ContainerSecret.Value is used only by the deploy's secret-store writes, and the certificate secret's Value is empty at render time (render.go:745-750).

### quadlet: verifier

- **differences / 'Reading the unit back' (and File.Digest): the on-disk compare is dropped**
  - Was: "the byte compare gives way to the digest label of todo item 10, which Render returns"
  - Should be: Keep the compare of the installed file (bytes, or the same digest computed over the file on disk) and ADD the label compare; either mismatch counts as changed. With the label alone, a unit edited on disk whose Label line is left alone (an added capability, device or PodmanArgs line) and then restarted runs a container whose label still equals the tool's digest: the deploy reports unchanged and never rewrites the file. Both tools detect that today. It would also make the proposed header comment ('a deploy overwrites this file') untrue.
  - Evidence: A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:958-960 reads the installed unit and compares it with plan.Unit.Content, 992-996 writes only on a difference. B internal/container/manager.go:711-717 (unitChanged, bytes.Equal) and 794-796. M docs/todo.md:556-561: 'A mismatch of either kind counts as changed, and forces a daemon reload before the restart even when the unit and the secrets on disk already match' -- the decision keeps the on-disk compare beside the label.
- **model quadlet.Mount.ReadOnly (difference 'Volume line'), and Unit.ReadOnlyRoot**
  - Was: Mount.ReadOnly bool: the zero value writes a read-write mount with no option. ReadOnlyRoot bool: false writes nothing.
  - Should be: An access value with no usable zero (for example Access: ReadOnly or ReadWrite, zero is a Render error), the rule the model already applies to RunAs ('a forgotten field cannot mean root'). A's libs mount is read-only unconditionally today, so under the model a forgotten or zeroed field silently widens it to read-write. Same pattern for ReadOnlyRoot: A's decided default is a read-only root, so a bool whose zero is the writable root should be a required tri-state for the tool that decided it, or A's own test must pin it.
  - Evidence: A internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:199-201 writes the constant ':ro' whenever Libs is set; podmangen.go:55-60 defines Mount as 'one read-only bind mount'. Model row Unit.User: 'The zero value is an error, so a forgotten field cannot mean root'. M docs/todo.md:357-358 (A defaults to read-only and writes it into every artifact).
- **ownerQuestions 10: recommendation to relabel A's libs mount 'ro,z'**
  - Was: "I would choose the shared label ('ro,z'), since two instances may mount one libs directory"
  - Should be: Recommend no relabel for A (today's behaviour) and present 'z' as a widening for the owner to accept or refuse: a relabel changes the SELinux label of the operator's host directory, persistently, and the shared form drops the per-container separation for it, so every container on the host may read it. A's unit changes no host state today, and libs.dir is resolved against env.yaml's directory, which is often under a home directory. If a label is needed, the operator sets it, or A uses the private form per instance. (SELinux behaviour is from podman's documentation as I recall it; not opened here, not run.)
  - Evidence: A internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:200 (no relabel option today); internal/gen/gen.go:1003-1005 (source is res.abs(libs.Dir)); cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1710-1727 (resolved against the env file's directory).
- **model Unit.Render note: 'The model has no field that can hold a secret value'**
  - Was: Stated as a property of the model; the same note says Render's error lists '(field, value, rule)'.
  - Should be: Reword: no field is MEANT for a secret, but EnvVar.Value, Label.Value, Setting.Value and Description are free text and M cannot tell. Environment= and Label= lines become arguments of 'podman run' in the service quadlet generates, so their values are readable by any local user whatever the mode of the .container file; quadlet.FileMode 0600 does not protect them. So: (1) the guide and the type docs say secrets go only through Unit.Secrets; (2) Render's errors name an Env, Label or Setting entry by its name and never print its value; (3) artifacttest keeps a check a tool can run on its own shapes.
  - Evidence: M containerart/quadlet/quadlet.go:29-32 ('Quadlet copies the value into the ExecStart of the service it generates'); M docs/todo.md:544-545 ('Quadlet turns a Label= line into an argument of podman run, which any local user can read'); model rows EnvVar ('M refuses only NUL'), Label, Setting.
- **model quadlet.Setting (generic slot) and ownerQuestions 6: where the allow-list comes from and how values are checked**
  - Was: [Container] key allowed when it is 'a key of the podman release table that M does not write itself and that is not a widening key'; value 'escaped by the section rule, and refused when it holds a spelling of artifacttest.Widening() or HostNetwork()'.
  - Should be: (a) An explicit allow-list owned by package quadlet (today exactly ShmSize and Ulimit in [Container]), each key with its own value grammar and write form. Not derived from artifacttest's release table: that table is a version floor for tests, and CheckQuadletKeys tells a developer to add any unknown key to it, so the easy fix for a failing test would silently admit a new key to the renderer. (b) quadlet cannot import artifacttest: artifacttest imports quadlet, compose and 'testing', so it is an import cycle and would pull 'testing' into both binaries; the lists must move into quadlet or a leaf package and artifacttest read them from there. (c) Widening() is a token list for what the two tools render today, not a list of every widening quadlet key (examples recalled from podman's unit documentation, not opened here: Unmask, UserNS, GlobalArgs, Mount, EnvironmentHost, GroupAdd, SecurityLabelType, Pod, Rootfs), so 'not a widening key' has to be decided by allow-list only. (d) A key M writes in any shape is refused always, also when this unit does not carry it (ReadOnly, HostName, Health*, Volume, PublishPort): slot lines are written last, and for a single-valued key the last line wins. (e) [Unit] dependency keys (Requires=, Wants=, OnFailure=) start other units, so their values need a unit-name grammar, not only the widening-token check; and the [Unit] slot accepting After= contradicts 'never a key M writes itself'. (f) AutoUpdate= (extras, third tool) lets podman replace the image without the tool and its redeploy question; it should need an owner decision, not a table entry.
  - Evidence: M containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:18-21 (imports testing, compose, quadlet), 147-152 and 174 (the message that says to add a missing key), 182-198 (table holds only what the tools write, plus Memory), 33-46 (Widening list); M docs/developer.md:175-176 ('A new quadlet key goes into the release table'). Model rows Setting and extras.
- **differences 'What is escaped' and model Mount.Source / ownerQuestions 11: one escaping rule per section**
  - Was: "M escapes every value by one rule: '%' and '$' doubled in [Container]"; mount source: "'%' and '$' stay legal and are doubled".
  - Should be: Decide the write form per key, by how quadlet reads that key (raw value, word-split value, key=value words); M already has two forms for that reason. For Mount.Source use a grammar, not escaping, for '$', quotes and backslash: both tools already refuse '$' and quotes in the path as written, so nothing is lost, and it also covers A's unchecked resolved prefix. Reason for caution on '$': as I recall podman's quadlet source, an absolute Volume source is also copied into RequiresMountsFor= of the generated unit, where systemd expands '%' but not '$', so a doubled '$$' names a different path there. That needs a read of pkg/systemd/quadlet at the floor tags before the plan is fixed; I could not open podman's source here.
  - Evidence: M containerart/quadlet/quadlet.go:14-20 (EscapePercent, 'for a value written unquoted ... podman splits an unquoted command line itself') against 22-36 (Escape for a quoted value). A internal/validate/validate.go:1150 and 1164-1166 (pathShellMeta is UnsafeChars minus backslash, so '$' and quotes are refused, backslash and ':' pass). B internal/config/hostpath.go:47-61 and 73-101. M execguard/execguard.go:92.
- **model Health.Cmd and difference 'HealthCmd': typed as an argv**
  - Was: Health.Cmd []string, 'the probe argv', joined with single spaces, '%' and '$' doubled, a token with whitespace refused.
  - Should be: Name the sink for what it is. A HealthCmd that is not a JSON array is run by podman through a shell in the container (from podman-run's text on --health-cmd as I recall it; not opened here), so each 'token' is shell text: ';', '|', '&&', quotes, '$(...)' and globs are interpreted, and refusing whitespace alone does not make it an argv. Either hold each token to a grammar without shell metacharacters until the JSON-array form lands, or call the field a command line and say so in the guide. Not a regression against today (A writes constants, B joins the same way), but a []string type promises exec semantics the sink does not give. Also record the behaviour change for B: with '$' doubled, a '$NAME' in a probe reaches the container's shell, where today systemd expands it from the unit's environment first.
  - Evidence: B internal/render/render.go:962-970 (joined, only '%' escaped, 'a token containing a space is not representable here'); B internal/config/validate.go:351-372 ('Whitespace and shell syntax stay allowed (a probe may be sh -c ...)'); B docs/configuration.md:209; A internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:215.
- **model quadlet.RunAs and difference 'User and Group': B's User= without Group=**
  - Was: "M always writes both lines"; "A bare uid in B's config resolves to gid 0 in B before the call, as in A".
  - Should be: B's 'User=<uid>' with no Group= line is not representable in the model and is not listed as an extra. Either add a uid-only form, or record as a decided difference that B refuses a bare uid at load. Mapping it to gid 0 states the root group, where today podman takes the group from the image's entry for that user (0 only when the image has none; my recollection of podman, not checked against the broker image). B's gate is also wider than the census says: it admits names and a leading '-' or '.' in both halves, so the numeric-only rule turns values that load today into errors and belongs in the list of such changes.
  - Evidence: B internal/render/render.go:904-907 (Group= only when the gid part is not empty) and 1282 (splitUser); B internal/config/validate.go:415 (runUserRE ^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+(:[A-Za-z0-9._-]+)?$) and 1625-1628; B internal/config/load.go:313-314 (both defaults carry gid 0).
- **model quadlet.Health timings and difference 'Health timings'**
  - Was: time.Duration written as whole seconds; 'a value that is not a whole number of seconds is an error'; 'Retries 1 or more (B's rules, validHealthDuration)'.
  - Should be: B accepts any non-negative Go duration (500ms, 1.5s) and documents the keys as Go durations, so the whole-second rule refuses configs that load today: either M writes Go duration text, or the narrowing is listed as a difference B takes at load. The retries rule is not B's: validHealthDuration covers interval, timeout and startPeriod only; retries is an int that is defaulted to 3 when zero and written with %d, so a negative count reaches the unit today. '1 or more' is a new M rule.
  - Evidence: B internal/config/artifactvalues.go:187-201 (time.ParseDuration, d < 0 or zero refused) and 227-241 (the three names checked); B internal/config/config.go:1118 and load.go:351 (retries, default only); B internal/render/render.go:973; B docs/configuration.md:209.
- **model Unit.HostName: 'M checks an RFC 1123 host name'**
  - Was: Presented as a check on a value B writes raw; no difference entry.
  - Should be: List it as a difference and decide the rule: B holds the three node names to [A-Za-z0-9._-]+ with no length bound, so an underscore, a leading '-' or '.', or a name over 63 characters passes B's load and would fail in M. The same string is the routername value and part of the redundancy_group_node_<name>_ keys, so the rule has to be chosen for all three uses. In standalone the name can be the OS hostname, which B checks against nothing (see missed).
  - Evidence: B internal/config/validate.go:384 (identRE), 579-588 (validIdent, empty passes), 1597-1599; B internal/render/render.go:896, 903 and 788, 802-807.
- **model Unit.Timezone charset**
  - Was: "M checks a zone-name charset (letters, digits, '_', '+', '-', '/')"
  - Should be: Narrower than both tools' gates, which refuse only whitespace, control and invisible characters and execguard.UnsafeChars: '.', ',', ':' and '%' pass today (a POSIX TZ rule string, for one). The value is written through quadlet.Environment, which escapes it, so the narrow charset is a behaviour change and not an escaping need. Keep the tools' charset in M, or list the narrowing under differences.
  - Evidence: A internal/validate/validate.go:1528-1530 (timezone held to SafeToken) and 1871-1873; B internal/config/artifactvalues.go:56-81; M execguard/execguard.go:92 (no '.', ',', ':' or '%' in UnsafeChars).
- **model Unit.Render rules and difference 'Renderer result and refusal': what the sink gate refuses**
  - Was: Unquoted values: 'no control character or newline, no leading or trailing space, no trailing backslash'; EnvVar.Value: 'M refuses only NUL'; 'M's are the gate at the sink'.
  - Should be: Hold every operator-influenced value at the sink with execguard.IsControl (C0, DEL and C1) and execguard.IsInvisible, as both tools do at load, or state outright that M's gate does not replace a tool's load gate. quadlet.Escape passes C1 controls and format characters through as raw bytes, so a tool that relies on M alone (the third tool, or A or B after trimming its own checks) writes them into the unit, where a bidirectional override hides text from a review.
  - Evidence: M execguard/execguard.go:190-204; M containerart/quadlet/quadlet.go:53-58 (only bytes below 0x20 and 0x7f are escaped); B internal/config/validate.go:351-372, artifactvalues.go:64-71, hostpath.go:81-88; A internal/validate/validate.go:1486-1516; M docs/developer.md:155-159.

Missed:

- (B) The standalone routername taken from the OS hostname is never checked. config.Load runs Validate first; the CLI fills redundancy.primary.name from os.Hostname (trimmed only) afterwards, and validIdent passes an empty value. The name is then written raw into Description= and HostName= (and, escaped, into routername). The census left this untraced (its note 5). [B internal/cli/context.go:162-167 and 196-209; B internal/config/load.go:74; B internal/config/validate.go:579-582; B internal/config/roles.go:132-138; B internal/render/render.go:896, 903]
- (B) The Volume= gap the census called 'possible' is open in this checkout: dataDir is written raw, nothing escapes '%' and nothing refuses ':'. IsAbsHostPath also counts a drive-letter path (C:/x, bare C:) and a backslash-led path as absolute, and CheckHostPath admits the backslash, so those reach the Volume= line as well. B's own decision CR-0030 (escape '%', refuse ':' at load) is recorded but not built; M docs/todo.md:1104-1107 words it as done. [B internal/render/render.go:985; B internal/config/validate.go:1575; B internal/config/hostpath.go:47-61, 139-153; M execguard/execguard.go:92; B docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:57]
- (B) The gate on runUser is wider than 'uid[:gid]': letters, '.', '_' and '-' pass in both halves, so User= and Group= can carry names or start with '-'. Both lines are written raw. [B internal/config/validate.go:415, 1625-1628; B internal/render/render.go:892, 904-907, 1282]
- (B) HealthRetries has no load check. healthCheck.retries is an int, defaulted to 3 only when zero, and written with %d; a negative or very large count reaches the unit. [B internal/config/config.go:1118; B internal/config/load.go:351; B internal/render/render.go:973; B internal/config/artifactvalues.go:227-241]
- (A) The Volume= source is not always absolute. Resolver.abs returns the path as written when Abs is nil (a caller of gen without the CLI's resolver), and loadEnv keeps the raw, possibly relative, env directory when filepath.Abs fails. A relative source is read by podman as a named volume (A's own comment says so). The renderer does not check; the model's absolute-source rule would close it. [A internal/gen/gen.go:96-101, 1003-1005; A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1710-1727, 1758-1767; A internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:199-201]
- (A) The operator key podman.rootful is not named in the census. It selects the system or the user quadlet directory and the systemctl scope, and is the value the model's Unit.Rootful would take; the renderer ignores it and writes WantedBy=default.target either way. The census says 'No operator key chooses the directory', which holds only for the path, not for the choice between the two. [A internal/spec/targets.go:262-267; A internal/runner/runner.go:651-660; A cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:876; A internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:229-230]

Notes: Method: read-only; nothing was run. I read the design note (all sections), decisions.md and todo.md in full, A's podmangen.go and the parts of gen.go, main.go, runner.go, validate.go and spec that feed it, B's render.go 500-1030 with config/validate.go, artifactvalues.go, hostpath.go, load.go, config.go, roles.go, container/manager.go, cli/helpers.go and cli/context.go, and M's quadlet.go, artifacttest.go, containercli.go, kube.go, execguard.go and podman.go. 1. Lines. Every write in A's RenderQuadlet (podmangen.go:139-232) and in B's Quadlet (render.go:895-1013), EnvPairs (784-862) and ContainerSecrets (714-751) has a census row; I found no missing line or key, and both tools render exactly one .container file (B's SecretScript is gone, render.go:1225-1239; A's 'podman run script' survives only in a package comment). The goldens I opened (B podman_quadlet_rootful.golden, A podmangen_test.go:69-148) match the censuses. What the censuses missed are gates and sources, listed under missed. 2. Representability. Every census row maps to a model field, an M-written line or a listed extra, except: B's User= without Group=, B's sub-second health durations, B's node names against the RFC 1123 host rule, and the timezone charset (all under corrections). The differences the model already lists (comment text, key order, label quoting, names in runUser, ports under host networking, whitespace in a probe token, [Service] order, WantedBy for A rootful) I did not repeat. 3. Posture. One proposal weakens what both tools do today: dropping the on-disk unit compare. Two more can weaken it by construction or by recommendation: the read-write zero value of Mount.ReadOnly, and the 'ro,z' recommendation for A. The rest of the corrections are claims or rules that are weaker or looser than stated (the secret-value claim, the slot's allow-list, one escaping rule per section, the argv-typed probe, the sink gate). Proposals I challenged and found no loss in: header comment, [Unit] and [Container] order, Description escaping, image grammar, container name grammar (wider than A's, and A may keep its own), labels quoted whole (a wrong parse fails safe: the digest never matches and the unit restarts), numeric run user with an error on the zero value, the stated network word with 'private' as the zero value and the option forms refused, ports refused under host or none, named Resources with PodmanArgs refused in the slot, secret lines (type=mount only, absolute target, errors on empty or duplicate), [Service] always written, Restart words, WantedBy from Rootful, file mode 0600 through the atomic helper (an improvement for A, which installs at 0644 in place, main.go:993), and the generate output file. Podman facts I rely on from memory and did not open (the rules allow no decompression of /usr/share/man/man5/podman-systemd.unit.5.gz and there is no podman source on this host): a non-JSON HealthCmd runs through a shell; an absolute Volume source is copied into RequiresMountsFor=; Label= is read as quoted key=value words like Environment=; which group podman gives a bare --user uid; the other widening quadlet keys named in the slot correction; the SELinux meaning of z and Z. Each should be read at the 4.5 and 4.7 tags before the plan is fixed. Smaller points, no correction entry: - Identity: the model row says the fields are required and non-empty, while ownerQuestions 4 says a tool that leaves solution and developed-by empty gets three labels. One of the two has to go. decisions.md:100-102 fixes the values as accelerator and apac-psg. - Unit.Timezone: 'refuses an Env entry named TZ beside it' leaves TZ in Env with an empty Timezone unchecked; say whether that is refused too. - Unit.DigestLabel is a tool-supplied key: hold it to the Label.Key grammar and the duplicate and M-owned-key rules. - The header comment writes Identity.Tool raw; give it the unquoted-value rule, not only 'no control character'. - FileMode as os.FileMode makes a pure rendering package import os; io/fs has the same type. - 0600 on the .container file keeps the file private, not its content: the generated service and the podman process carry the same Environment and Label values (see the secret-value correction). - The model does not say what happens to the exported pieces quadlet.Environment, quadlet.Secret, quadlet.Posture, Escape and EscapePercent once Unit.Render exists; artifacttest reads quadlet.Posture (artifacttest.go:124), so that one at least stays. - A census cite check: all the lines I re-opened match (139-233, gen.go:693-775, main.go:958-996 and 1771-1779, runner.go:116-126, validate.go:1150-1166, 1528-1555, 1673-1701, 1732-1760). B census cite check: the same (render.go lines above, validate.go:384-425, 1575-1705, artifactvalues.go:56-255, load.go:261-285 and 313-351, manager.go:711-745 and 792-828, helpers.go:142-195).

### 3.3 kubernetes

A writes 168 fields or lines, B writes 169.

#### kubernetes: model

- **Writer (new package, working name yamlw).Str** `func Str(s string) Node` (in both, required): The one way a string from a tool is written, value or map key: a double-quoted scalar through strconv.Quote, the rule compose.Quote already holds (M:containerart/compose/compose.go:30-44), without compose's '$' doubling. Replaces A's four functions (strconv.Quote, consolidate.QuoteScalar, envValueQuote, quoteRes: A:internal/deploy/deploy.go:185,276-295,385) and B's three styles (bare, %q, hand-quoted constants: B:internal/render/render.go:87,110,319). Invalid UTF-8 is refused with an error naming the field, because strconv.Quote would write a byte escape that YAML reads as another code point (compose.go:38-43).
- **Writer (new package, working name yamlw).Int, Bool** `func Int(n int64) Node; func Bool(b bool) Node` (in both, required): Bare decimal and bare true/false. With Word, the only bare scalars that exist.
- **Writer (new package, working name yamlw).Word** `func Word(s string, allowed ...string) (Node, error)` (in both, required): A bare token from a closed list M checks (Opaque, ClusterIP, TCP, Retain, ReadWriteMany, IfNotPresent, Exists, NoSchedule, In, RuntimeDefault). An unknown word is an error, so nothing a tool supplies reaches the text both unquoted and unchecked. Used by M's documents and offered to a tool for the enums of a Custom body (B's pullPolicy and updateStrategy, render.go:95,143).
- **Writer (new package, working name yamlw).Block** `func Block(s string) Node` (in A, required): Literal block scalar for file content in a ConfigMap: '|' on the key's line, each line indented two under the key, an empty line as a bare newline (A:deploy.go:502-527; A:internal/yamlwriter/yamlwriter.go:35-41). New in M: the chomping indicator follows the content ('|' for exactly one trailing newline, '|-' for none, '|+' for more), where A today silently adds a newline to content that has none; an indentation indicator is written when the first line starts with a space; content a literal block cannot carry (CR, a control character other than tab, U+0085, U+2028, U+2029, a BOM) falls back to Str.
- **Writer (new package, working name yamlw).Map, Entry, Seq, EmptyMap** `type Map []Entry; type Entry struct{ Key string; Value Node }; type Seq []Node; var EmptyMap Node` (in both, required): Layout of the one writer. Two spaces per level. A Map keeps the order it is given; M sorts only where the input is a Go map (labels, annotations, data, selectors).
