A sequence under a key is indented two more than the key, the form M's compose.Posture (compose.go:66-70) and A already write; B and the upstream bundle put the dash at the key's own indent (render.go:97-98). A mapping inside a sequence item nests under its first key, which is exactly what A writes for '- secret:' at 14 with name at 18 (deploy.go:731-733): A's census lists that as a quirk, but 16 would make name a sibling of secret, so a tree writer produces 18 by construction. A key whose Map or Seq is empty is dropped, never written with a null under it; '{}' appears only through EmptyMap (emptyDir). Block sequences only: no flow form, no comment, no header line, as neither tool writes one.
- **Writer (new package, working name yamlw).Join** `func Join(docs ...[]byte) []byte` (in both, required): One joiner for A's strings.Join(docs, '---\n') (deploy.go:477) and B's two helpers (joinManifests, B:internal/k8s/prep.go:418-426; joinYAMLDocs, B:internal/k8s/operator.go:411-424). A blank document is dropped, trailing newlines are trimmed, documents are joined by a line holding '---', the stream ends in exactly one newline, no leading or trailing separator, nil for no documents.
- **Owner (identity, set once per run; shared with the compose, quadlet and podman-secret sinks).Tool** `string` (in neither, required): Value of app.kubernetes.io/managed-by and services.solace.com/product (decisions.md:100-102). Label-value grammar, at most 63.
- **Owner (identity, set once per run; shared with the compose, quadlet and podman-secret sinks).Instance** `string` (in neither, required): Value of deploy.solace.com/instance. Checked per sink: on Kubernetes at most 63 with alphanumeric ends; refused when it does not fit, never truncated or hashed (todo.md:108-110). A passes kubernetes.deployment.name, B kubernetes.name.
- **Owner (identity, set once per run; shared with the compose, quadlet and podman-secret sinks).Version** `string` (in neither, required): The tool's own version, for the deploy.solace.com/version annotation, Kubernetes only (decisions.md:103-105,158-160). Free text, written through Str.
- **Owner (identity, set once per run; shared with the compose, quadlet and podman-secret sinks).Solution, DevelopedBy** `string, string` (in neither, optional): Values of services.solace.com/solution and services.solace.com/developed-by. Both Solace tools pass accelerator and apac-psg (decisions.md:101-102). Proposed: the label is left out when the value is empty, since the design note says these values do not suit a tool outside Solace Professional Services (design note 9.2, lines 326-327). Owner question.
- **Manifest (a stream of documents).Owner** `Owner` (in neither, required): Set once, stamped by M into every document's metadata. A tool that applies in several steps (B: namespace, Secrets, custom resource) builds several Manifests with the same Owner.
- **Manifest (a stream of documents).Docs** `[]Document (sealed interface: Namespace, Secret, ConfigMap, Service, PersistentVolume, PersistentVolumeClaim, Deployment, Custom, Raw, and the RBAC kinds if built)` (in both, required): Kept in the order given, which is the apply order. A: Namespace, ConfigMap, credentials Secret, stores Secret, image-pull Secret, PV, PVC, Deployment, Service (deploy.go:343-470; its doc comment at 307-308 names PV/PVC before nothing else, the code puts the image-pull Secret first). B: Namespace, Secrets admin, TLS, image-pull, additional-users, then the custom resource (prep.go:186-235,398-411). Which documents exist, and their order, stay the tool's.
- **Manifest (a stream of documents).Render** `func (m Manifest) Render() ([]byte, error)` (in both, required): Pure. Each document is apiVersion, kind, metadata, then the kind's body, joined by Join. Every grammar failure is an error naming the kind, the object, the field, the value and the rule, never a secret value; nothing partial is returned. Today A's Render returns one error only and B's BrokerCR none (deploy.go:311-313; render.go:70).
- **Manifest (a stream of documents).Refs** `func (m Manifest) Refs() ([]byte, error)` (in both, required): The delete form, for kube.Client.Delete: each document as apiVersion, kind, metadata.name and metadata.namespace only, in reverse order (A's reason: the PVC is held by pvc-protection until the Deployment is gone, deploy.go:137-142). Refuses a Namespace document: a namespace goes only through nsgate and DeleteNamespace (M:kube/kube.go:107-108). A Raw document is passed whole. Replaces A's Teardown flag and nameOnlySecret (deploy.go:472-489) and B's full custom resource on delete (B:internal/k8s/deploy.go:187). Needs no secret value and no file, so a remove resolves nothing (decisions.md:126-127).
- **Manifest (a stream of documents).Objects** `func (m Manifest) Objects() []Object // Kind, Name, Namespace, ClusterScoped` (in both, required): What the manifest writes, for the rbac probes, the nsgate owned list and the leftover compare of gdu 18. Replaces A's deploy.Written and spec.CreatedNames (deploy.go:799-823; A:internal/spec/kubernetes.go:373-395) and B's ownedSecretNames, so the probes and the rendered set cannot drift. A cluster-scoped object is flagged, not hidden (A leaves its PV out today, deploy.go:804-805).
- **Meta (metadata of every object).Name** `string` (in both, required): Written through Str, first key of metadata. Grammar checked at render by kind: Namespace a DNS-1123 label (kube.ValidLabel, kube.go:263-275); Service a DNS-1035 label, which must start with a letter; every other kind a DNS-1123 subdomain of at most 253 (the grammar gdu 4 adds; B already holds its Secret names to it, B:internal/config/validate.go:667-681). Two latent defects this closes, read from the code and not run: (1) both tools and kube.NamespaceManifest write names bare (kube.go:60; deploy.go:380,499,537; B:internal/k8s/secrets.go:40), and ValidLabel accepts 123, true, null or 1e3, which a YAML 1.1 reader takes for a number, boolean or null; (2) A holds the name its Deployment and Service share only to ValidLabel (A:internal/validate/validate.go:903-905), so a digit-led name passes load and the API server refuses the Service.
- **Meta (metadata of every object).Namespace** `string` (in both, required): Written through Str, after name. DNS-1123 label. Required on every namespaced kind and refused on a cluster-scoped one (Namespace, PersistentVolume, ClusterRole, ClusterRoleBinding, a Custom marked ClusterScoped), so no object lands in the context's namespace by accident. A writes none on its PV (deploy.go:426-429).
- **Meta (metadata of every object).Labels** `map[string]string` (in B, optional): The tool's own extra labels on the object. Neither tool writes one on an object it authors; only B's upstream bundle does (control-plane on its Namespace, app.kubernetes.io/* on its Deployment: operator-1.4.2.yaml.tmpl:3-6,1898-1906). Key and value through Str, sorted bytewise together with M's own. Held to the label grammar (A's isLabelKey and labelNameRE, validate.go:1313-1332, lifted into M). A key M owns (app.kubernetes.io/managed-by, anything under services.solace.com/ or deploy.solace.com/) is refused.
- **Meta (metadata of every object).Annotations** `map[string]string` (in B, optional): The tool's own annotations. B's TLS Secret carries solace-util.sha256, the only annotation either tool writes (secrets.go:42-54,188). Key and value through Str (B quotes the value and leaves the key bare, secrets.go:52), sorted bytewise with M's own. A key under deploy.solace.com/ is refused. Also the home of load-balancer annotations on a Service.
- **Meta (metadata of every object).metadata.labels: ownership labels (written unasked)** `derived from Manifest.Owner` (in neither, required): On every object of every kind, the Namespace and the cluster-scoped PV included: app.kubernetes.io/managed-by=<Tool>, deploy.solace.com/instance=<Instance>, services.solace.com/developed-by, services.solace.com/product=<Tool>, services.solace.com/solution (decisions.md:100-102; todo.md:64-66,91-92). Order inside metadata: name, namespace, labels, annotations. Never in a selector, a pod template or a custom resource's podLabels (decisions.md:200): M refuses its own keys in Deployment.Selector, Service.Selector and Pod.Labels and has no other path to those places.
- **Meta (metadata of every object).metadata.annotations: lib and version (written unasked)** `derived from Manifest.Owner and M's own build info` (in neither, required): deploy.solace.com/lib=go-deploy-v<M's version>, go-deploy-devel on a workspace build, and deploy.solace.com/version=<Owner.Version>. Kubernetes only, for information, never read back (decisions.md:103-105,158-160; todo.md:123-125). Written by Render, so deploy and generate carry the same text with no second call.
- **Namespace.Meta** `Meta` (in both, required): apiVersion: v1, kind: Namespace, metadata with name, labels and annotations; no namespace key. Replaces kube.NamespaceManifest (kube.go:52-61), the one whole document M writes today, used by both tools (deploy.go:348; prep.go:403). Labelled, and the same text for generate and for a deploy that creates the namespace (decisions.md:161-163). Whether the document is in a stream stays the tool's choice (A drops it on deploy and teardown, deploy.go:343-354).
- **Secret.Meta** `Meta` (in both, required): apiVersion: v1, kind: Secret. Key order: apiVersion, kind, metadata, type, data (both tools: deploy.go:377-383; secrets.go:37-56).
- **Secret.Type** `SecretType // Opaque, kubernetes.io/tls, kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson` (in both, required): Always written, a bare constant of M's. The zero value is an error, not Opaque. A: Opaque twice and dockerconfigjson (deploy.go:382,397,414). B: Opaque, tls, dockerconfigjson (secrets.go:123,158,187,269).
- **Secret.Data** `map[string][]byte` (in both, optional): Written as data:, never stringData:. M base64-encodes (StdEncoding) and writes the text through Str; keys sorted bytewise (B's rule, secrets.go:57-64) and held to the Secret key grammar (letters, digits, '-', '_', '.', at most 253). An empty map drops the key, so an empty Secret is still a valid object (A writes 'stringData:' with nothing under it, deploy.go:383-386). A value never appears in an error.
- **Secret.TLSSecret** `func TLSSecret(meta Meta, chainPEM, keyPEM []byte) (Secret, error)` (in B, optional): Type kubernetes.io/tls with keys tls.crt and tls.key (secrets.go:184-193). Refuses an empty chain or key. Reading the files, joining the CA chain and computing the digest annotation stay in the tool (secrets.go:196-229): M reads no file (design note 3.3 rule 4).
- **Secret.RegistrySecret** `func RegistrySecret(meta Meta, registry, user, pass string) (Secret, error)` (in both, optional): Type kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson, key .dockerconfigjson, payload {auths:{<registry>:{username,password,auth}}} through encoding/json in that field order, auth = base64(user:pass). The two tools hold the same code twice (A:internal/gen/gen.go:504-520; B secrets.go:231-262). Refuses an empty registry, user or password. M exports the Docker Hub key https://index.docker.io/v1/ as a constant (A's default, A:internal/spec/image.go:9,83-88). B writes image.registry as given, the empty string included (secrets.go:255-256).
- **ConfigMap.Meta** `Meta` (in A, required): apiVersion: v1, kind: ConfigMap, metadata, data (deploy.go:496-501). A generic platform kind only A renders.
- **ConfigMap.Data** `map[string]string` (in A, optional): Keys sorted bytewise, which is A's order today (application.yml, logback-spring.xml, status: deploy.go:502-520); key grammar as for a Secret, written through Str; each value through Block. No binaryData: neither tool writes one. M exposes the document's size so a tool can refuse one over the client-side apply limit before it applies (A's kubeApplyMax and allowance, gen.go:1108-1135).
- **Service.Meta** `Meta` (in A, required): apiVersion: v1, kind: Service (deploy.go:776-780). Name held to DNS-1035. Load-balancer annotations go in Meta.Annotations.
- **Service.Type** `ServiceType // ClusterIP, NodePort, LoadBalancer` (in neither, optional): Always written, first key of spec; the zero value writes ClusterIP. A writes no type and inherits ClusterIP (deploy.go:781); B states LoadBalancer, but inside its custom resource (render.go:210).
- **Service.Selector** `map[string]string` (in A, required): spec.selector, sorted by key, key and value through Str, label grammar. A: app=<deployment name> (deploy.go:782-783). M's ownership keys are refused here.
- **Service.Ports[].Name** `string` (in A, optional): Required when there is more than one port. A DNS label of at most 15 characters, through Str. A: management (deploy.go:785).
- **Service.Ports[].Port** `int` (in A, required): 1 to 65535, bare (deploy.go:786). At least one port. Order as given.
- **Service.Ports[].TargetPort** `int` (in A, optional): Always written; 0 means the same as Port (A resolves that default in its spec, A:internal/spec/kubernetes.go:447-449; deploy.go:787).
- **Service.Ports[].Protocol** `Protocol // TCP, UDP, SCTP` (in B, optional): Written only when set, as an upper-case Word. A writes none. B writes a protocol on the ports of its custom resource and keeps the spelling as typed although load accepts any case (render.go:221; census note), which this enum would fix if B reuses it in its body.
- **PersistentVolume.Meta** `Meta` (in A, required): apiVersion: v1, kind: PersistentVolume, cluster-scoped: a namespace is refused (deploy.go:426-429). Carries the ownership labels like every object. The derived name (<namespace>-<claim>-pv, A:internal/spec/kubernetes.go:303) stays the tool's.
- **PersistentVolume.ReclaimPolicy** `ReclaimPolicy // Retain, Delete` (in A, optional): persistentVolumeReclaimPolicy, always written, first key of spec; the zero value writes Retain, A's stated constant (deploy.go:431-435).
- **PersistentVolume.Capacity** `string` (in A, required): capacity.storage, a quantity checked by a grammar lifted into M (A's quantityRE, validate.go:1088-1090; B has its own k8sQuantityRE) and written through Str. A writes it bare (deploy.go:436-437).
- **PersistentVolume.AccessModes** `[]AccessMode // ReadWriteOnce, ReadOnlyMany, ReadWriteMany, ReadWriteOncePod` (in A, required): Block sequence of Words, at least one. A: ReadWriteMany (deploy.go:438-439).
- **PersistentVolume.StorageClass** `string` (in neither, optional): storageClassName, written through Str only when set. A's PV has none.
- **PersistentVolume.NFS** `*NFSSource{ Server, Path string; Writable bool }` (in A, optional): nfs: server, path, readOnly (deploy.go:440-443). server held to a host grammar and path to an absolute path, both through Str (A writes both bare and relies on validate, validate.go:1061-1066). readOnly is always written and is true unless Writable, so the zero value is the read-only one A writes.
- **PersistentVolume.Source** `yamlw.Map (generic slot)` (in neither, optional): A volume source M does not model (csi, local). Exactly one of NFS and Source. The key hostPath is refused. Key order in spec: persistentVolumeReclaimPolicy, capacity, accessModes, storageClassName, then the source (A's order, deploy.go:435-443).
- **PersistentVolumeClaim.Meta** `Meta` (in A, required): apiVersion: v1, kind: PersistentVolumeClaim (deploy.go:445-449).
- **PersistentVolumeClaim.StorageClass** `*string` (in A, optional): nil leaves the key out, so the cluster's default class applies; a pointer to the empty string writes storageClassName: "", which is how A binds its claim to its own PV (deploy.go:451).
- **PersistentVolumeClaim.VolumeName** `string` (in A, optional): The PV to bind, through Str (deploy.go:452).
- **PersistentVolumeClaim.AccessModes** `[]AccessMode` (in A, required): As on the PV (deploy.go:453-454).
- **PersistentVolumeClaim.Storage** `string` (in A, required): resources.requests.storage, quantity grammar, through Str (deploy.go:455-457). Key order in spec: storageClassName, volumeName, accessModes, resources.
- **Deployment.Meta** `Meta` (in both, required): apiVersion: apps/v1, kind: Deployment (deploy.go:534-538). In B only inside the upstream operator bundle (tmpl:1897-1906), which stays text. The ownership labels go here and never into the template.
- **Deployment.Replicas** `int` (in both, required): spec.replicas, always written as given, negative refused. M holds no default: A's 1 is applied in its spec (A:internal/spec/kubernetes.go:441-443).
- **Deployment.Selector** `map[string]string` (in both, required): spec.selector.matchLabels, sorted by key, through Str. Must be a subset of Pod.Labels, which M checks. M's ownership keys are refused. A: app only, on purpose, since a selector is immutable (deploy.go:541-546). No strategy key: neither tool writes one.
- **Deployment.Pod** `Pod` (in both, required): spec.template. Pod is also exported as a fragment (a Node), so a workload kind M does not model yet can embed the same hardened template in a Custom body.
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).Labels** `map[string]string` (in both, required): spec.template.metadata.labels, sorted bytewise, through Str. A: app, solace-connector/le-mode, solace-connector/role, already in that order (deploy.go:549-560). M adds nothing here (decisions.md:200).
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).Annotations** `map[string]string` (in B, optional): spec.template.metadata.annotations. Only the upstream bundle has one (kubectl.kubernetes.io/default-container, tmpl:1912-1915). Sorted, through Str.
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).ServiceAccountName** `string` (in B, optional): serviceAccountName, first key of the pod spec when set, through Str. Only the upstream bundle sets one (tmpl:1966).
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).AutomountToken** `bool` (in A, optional): automountServiceAccountToken is always written; the zero value writes false, A's constant (deploy.go:562-565). A hardening line M adds unasked.
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).ImagePullSecrets** `[]string` (in both, optional): imagePullSecrets, one '- name:' each through Str, dropped when empty (deploy.go:566-572; bundle tmpl:1958-1961; B's custom resource carries the same list as image.pullSecrets, render.go:96-99). Subdomain grammar.
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).NodeSelector** `map[string]string` (in both, optional): nodeSelector, keys sorted, key and value through Str, label grammar (A: deploy.go:573-587, sorted, QuoteScalar; B: inside its custom resource, a list of 'key: value' entries in config order, %q, render.go:327-331,504-507). Offered as a fragment for B's body.
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).Tolerations** `[]Toleration` (in both, optional): See Toleration. Order as given. Offered as a fragment for B's body (render.go:313-326).
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).Affinity** `Affinity` (in both, optional): See Affinity. The key is dropped when nothing is set. Offered as a fragment for B's body (render.go:333-356).
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).RunAs** `RunAs{ UID, GID int64; FSGroup *int64; FromImage bool }` (in A, required): Pod securityContext: runAsUser, runAsGroup, fsGroup, then runAsNonRoot: true when UID is not 0 (deploy.go:249-258). fsGroup is GID unless FSGroup is set: it is what lets the uid read the 0400 secret files M mounts (deploy.go:240-246). FromImage writes no id, for an image user or an OpenShift SCC. The tool must choose ids or FromImage; the bare zero value is refused, so a forgotten field never means root. Range 0 to 2147483647 (A:internal/spec/runas.go:23-26). A's default 1000:0 stays in A.
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).securityContext.seccompProfile.type (written unasked)** `none: no field` (in both, required): RuntimeDefault, always, last in the pod securityContext (deploy.go:259-260; the upstream bundle writes the same, tmpl:1962-1965). Nothing in the model changes it.
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).InitContainers** `[]Container` (in A, optional): Same model and the same hardening as a container. A: libs-download (deploy.go:591-606). Dropped when empty.
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).Containers** `[]Container` (in both, required): At least one; names unique in the pod.
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).Volumes** `[]Volume` (in A, optional): See Volume. Order as given (A: config, secrets, stores, libs: deploy.go:711-755). Every volume must be mounted by some container, and every mount must name a volume.
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).Extra** `yamlw.Map (generic slot)` (in B, optional): Pod-spec keys M does not model, written last. Only the upstream bundle has one (terminationGracePeriodSeconds, tmpl:1967). A key M writes at this level is refused, and so are hostNetwork, hostPID, hostIPC, hostUsers, shareProcessNamespace and securityContext. Whether the slot ships at all is an owner question.
- **Pod (Deployment.spec.template).network line: none (no field)** `none` (in neither, optional): Kubernetes gets no network key (decisions.md:187-189). The model has no field for hostNetwork, hostPID, hostIPC or a hostPath volume, so a rendered pod cannot ask for them. Pod-spec key order: serviceAccountName, automountServiceAccountToken, imagePullSecrets, nodeSelector, tolerations, affinity, securityContext, initContainers, containers, volumes, Extra (A's order with the new keys added, deploy.go:561-756).
- **Container.Name** `string` (in both, required): DNS label, through Str. A: connector and libs-download (deploy.go:599,608).
- **Container.Image** `string` (in both, required): Through Str. A writes the reference bare (deploy.go:600,609); B quotes repository and tag because a bare tag such as 10.10 is a float (render.go:85-88). Refused when empty or when it holds whitespace or a control character. Building the reference, digest form included, stays the tool's (A:internal/spec/image.go:61-73).
- **Container.PullPolicy** `PullPolicy // Always, IfNotPresent, Never` (in B, optional): imagePullPolicy, always written; the zero value writes IfNotPresent, the default B's renderer already states in its custom resource (render.go:89-95). The upstream bundle writes Always (tmpl:1932). A writes none and inherits the API default, which is Always for a latest tag. Owner question.
- **Container.Command** `[]string` (in both, optional): Block sequence, each item through Str with every '$' doubled: Kubernetes expands $(VAR) in command and args and reads '$$' as one '$' (the rule A states for env values, deploy.go:287-292). A writes its init command as a flow sequence (deploy.go:601); the bundle has '/manager'. The shell text itself (A's wget lines, deploy.go:594-597) stays the tool's.
- **Container.Args** `[]string` (in B, optional): Written as Command is. Only the upstream bundle has args.
- **Container.securityContext (written unasked)** `none: no field` (in A, required): allowPrivilegeEscalation: false, privileged: false, capabilities.drop with the one item ALL, on every container and init container (deploy.go:263-274,605,610). The upstream bundle states two of the three (tmpl:1953-1957). No field adds a capability, sets privileged or changes a line; artifacttest gains a Kubernetes posture check that forbids the widening tokens in any rendered text, as it does for compose and quadlet.
- **Container.ReadOnlyRootFilesystem** `bool` (in neither, optional): readOnlyRootFilesystem: true is written only when set. Neither tool writes it, and B refuses a read-only root on every platform (render.go:283-285); decisions.md:168-169 leaves that check in each tool.
- **Container.Ports** `[]ContainerPort{ Name string; Port int; Protocol Protocol }` (in A, optional): '- name:' then 'containerPort:', protocol only when set (deploy.go:611-613). Name a DNS label of at most 15; port 1 to 65535.
- **Container.Env** `[]EnvVar{ Name, Value string }` (in both, optional): 'env:' is dropped when the list is empty (A guards it by hand, deploy.go:614-621). name held to envref.ValidName (M:envref/envref.go:71), through Str; value always through Str with every '$' doubled. That gives one rule where A has three: TZ, the syslog host and the app name bare (deploy.go:624,636,638), the Java options through envValueQuote (628,632), the syslog port quoted by hand (640). Order as given. No valueFrom and no envFrom: secrets arrive as files.
- **Container.Mounts** `[]Mount{ Volume, Path, SubPath string; Writable bool }` (in A, optional): volumeMounts: '- name:', 'mountPath:', 'subPath:' when set, 'readOnly:' always, true unless Writable (deploy.go:643-678; A leaves the line out for its one writable mount, 602-604). Path must be absolute; Volume must name one of Pod.Volumes; a Secret or ConfigMap volume cannot be Writable. Order as given.
- **Container.Liveness, Readiness, Startup** `*Probe` (in both, optional): See Probe. A: tcpSocket liveness and exec readiness (deploy.go:679-707); the upstream bundle: httpGet for both (tmpl:1933-1945). Startup is in neither.
- **Container.Resources** `Resources{ Requests, Limits ResourceList{ CPU, Memory string } }` (in both, optional): 'resources:' dropped when all four are empty; requests before limits, cpu before memory; quantity grammar; through Str, so a bare integer cpu needs no special case (A's quoteRes, deploy.go:276-285). A fills both blocks from one pair for guaranteed QoS (deploy.go:758-772), which stays A's choice; the upstream bundle sets them apart (tmpl:1946-1952).
- **Container.Extra** `yamlw.Map (generic slot)` (in neither, optional): Container keys M does not model (lifecycle, workingDir). A key M writes is refused, securityContext included. Key order: name, image, imagePullPolicy, command, args, securityContext, ports, env, volumeMounts, livenessProbe, readinessProbe, startupProbe, resources, Extra (A's order with the new keys added, deploy.go:608-709).
- **Probe.TCPPort** `int` (in A, optional): tcpSocket.port (deploy.go:682-684). Exactly one of TCPPort, Exec and HTTPGet, else an error.
- **Probe.Exec** `[]string` (in A, optional): exec.command as a block sequence, each item through Str (A writes sh, the script path and --health bare, deploy.go:698-703). No '$' doubling here if probe commands are not expanded by Kubernetes: to confirm against the API reference before this is built.
- **Probe.HTTPGet** `*HTTPGet{ Path string; Port int; Scheme Scheme }` (in B, optional): httpGet: path, port, scheme when set. Only the upstream bundle uses it (tmpl:1933-1936,1940-1943); the form a third tool will most likely need.
- **Probe.InitialDelaySeconds, PeriodSeconds, TimeoutSeconds, FailureThreshold** `int (each)` (in A, optional): Each written when above zero, in that order after the action (deploy.go:685-686,704-707). The numbers are the tool's.
- **Volume.Name** `string` (in A, required): DNS label, unique in the pod, through Str. Exactly one source per volume.
- **Volume.ConfigMap** `string` (in A, optional): configMap.name (deploy.go:712-714).
- **Volume.Secrets** `[]SecretSource{ Name string; Items []KeyPath{ Key, Path string } }` (in A, optional): The secret mount, written by M. One source without items becomes 'secret:' with secretName and defaultMode (deploy.go:715-720). Anything else becomes 'projected:' with defaultMode and one '- secret:' source each, name then items (deploy.go:721-738). Key and path both through Str (A quotes the key and not the path, 735-736); a path is relative and holds no '..'. Sources in the order given (A: its own Secret first, then each operator Secret, deploy.go:76-87).
- **Volume.Mode** `int` (in A, optional): defaultMode of a Secret volume, always written, 0400 when unset (A's constant, deploy.go:719,728). Written as a four-digit octal literal, which kubectl reads as octal; above 0777 refused. Left out for a ConfigMap volume unless set, as A does.
- **Volume.Claim** `string` (in A, optional): persistentVolumeClaim.claimName (deploy.go:742-751). Which of A's three claim sources applies stays in A.
- **Volume.EmptyDir** `bool` (in A, optional): 'emptyDir: {}' (deploy.go:752-754). There is no hostPath source.
- **Toleration (fragment, also usable in a Custom body).Key** `string` (in both, optional): Through Str, label-key grammar. Required unless Operator is Exists (A's validate rule, validate.go:1341-1344). A: QuoteScalar (deploy.go:184-186); B: %q on a key parsed out of 'key[=value]:effect' (render.go:316-317).
- **Toleration (fragment, also usable in a Custom body).Operator** `TolerationOperator // Equal, Exists` (in both, optional): Always written as a Word; the zero value writes Equal. A leaves the key out when unset (deploy.go:187-189); B always writes it, in hand-written quotes (render.go:318-323).
- **Toleration (fragment, also usable in a Custom body).Value** `string` (in both, optional): Through Str, only with Equal; refused with Exists (validate.go:1349-1352). A: deploy.go:190-192; B: render.go:320.
- **Toleration (fragment, also usable in a Custom body).Effect** `TaintEffect // NoSchedule, PreferNoSchedule, NoExecute` (in both, optional): A Word, left out when unset, which matches every effect (deploy.go:193-195). B always writes the key, as effect: "" when the entry names none (render.go:324).
- **Toleration (fragment, also usable in a Custom body).Seconds** `*int64` (in A, optional): tolerationSeconds, needs NoExecute (deploy.go:196-198; validate.go:1362-1364). Key order: key, operator, value, effect, tolerationSeconds. A toleration with nothing set is refused, where A writes '- {}' (deploy.go:199-201).
- **Affinity (fragment, also usable in a Custom body).NodeRequired** `[]NodeTerm{ Match []MatchExpr }` (in B, optional): nodeAffinity.requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution.nodeSelectorTerms. B writes one ANDed term (render.go:371-376).
- **Affinity (fragment, also usable in a Custom body).NodePreferred** `[]WeightedNodeTerm{ Weight int; Match []MatchExpr }` (in B, optional): weight 1 to 100, then preference.matchExpressions (render.go:362-369).
- **Affinity (fragment, also usable in a Custom body).MatchExpr** `MatchExpr{ Key string; Operator MatchOperator; Values []string }` (in B, optional): key through Str, operator a Word of In, NotIn, Exists, DoesNotExist, Gt, Lt, values through Str (render.go:379-390).
- **Affinity (fragment, also usable in a Custom body).PodAffinity, PodAntiAffinity** `PodAffinity{ Required []PodTerm; Preferred []WeightedPodTerm }; PodTerm{ TopologyKey string; MatchLabels map[string]string; Namespaces []string }` (in both, optional): A's spread rule is one PodAntiAffinity term the tool builds: weight 100 or required, topologyKey kubernetes.io/hostname, matchLabels app (deploy.go:220-237). B: a fixed broker-spread term first, then configured terms, where weight 0 means required (render.go:393-453). M has two lists and no weight-0 convention. topologyKey through Str (B quotes it for a configured term and leaves it bare for its fixed one, render.go:412,424); matchLabels sorted, through Str; namespaces through Str.
- **Affinity (fragment, also usable in a Custom body).Extra** `yamlw.Map (generic slot)` (in A, optional): A's verbatim kubernetes.deployment.affinity block, written before the typed terms as A does (deploy.go:217-219). A kind present both typed and in Extra is refused (A's rule against a podAntiAffinity in both, validate.go:1397-1399). Key order: Extra, then nodeAffinity, podAffinity, podAntiAffinity (B's order, render.go:347-355); inside each, preferred then required (render.go:362-376,407-434); inside a term, topologyKey, labelSelector.matchLabels, namespaces.
- **Custom (an operator resource whose body the tool writes).APIVersion** `string` (in B, required): Written bare after a group/version grammar check. B: pubsubplus.solace.com/v1beta1 (render.go:78).
- **Custom (an operator resource whose body the tool writes).Kind** `string` (in B, required): Written bare after a grammar check. B: PubSubPlusEventBroker (render.go:79). Proposed: refused when M has a typed document for the kind, so the hardening of Deployment cannot be bypassed. Owner question.
- **Custom (an operator resource whose body the tool writes).Meta** `Meta` (in B, required): M's metadata with labels and annotations, in M's order; B writes namespace before name today (render.go:80-82). The labels stay in metadata and never enter the body's podLabels (decisions.md:200).
- **Custom (an operator resource whose body the tool writes).ClusterScoped** `bool` (in neither, optional): Makes a namespace an error instead of a requirement.
- **Custom (an operator resource whose body the tool writes).Body** `yamlw.Map` (in B, required): Every top-level key after metadata, normally spec (B's body is render.go:83-226). The tool decides every key, order and value; M's writer does the quoting and layout, so the values B writes bare with no check found (serviceAccount, storage class, node memory, claim names, load-balancer ip and pool, anti-affinity namespaces: render.go:102,167,171,250,418,485-490, as B's census lists) can no longer restructure the document. apiVersion, kind and metadata are refused as keys. The fragments Tolerations, NodeSelector and Affinity are there for a body that embeds core shapes. Tree against raw text is an owner question.
- **Raw (a pre-rendered upstream document).Text** `[]byte` (in B, required): One document passed through unchanged: no Meta, no labels, no quoting. For B's embedded operator bundle, 1967 lines of upstream text with six substitution points (B:internal/k8s/operator.go:65-84; tmpl grep of kind lines and template actions). Refs passes it whole, which kubectl delete accepts.
- **Raw (a pre-rendered upstream document).Split, Kind** `func Split(stream []byte) []Raw; func (r Raw) Kind() string` (in B, optional): Splits a stream on a line holding only '---' and reads the column-0 kind, replacing B's two regular expressions and its split helpers (operator.go:86-117). The one imperative command B also writes, kubectl set env for WATCH_NAMESPACE, is not an artifact; the tool builds it itself (owner rule 3).
- **ServiceAccount.Meta** `Meta` (in B, required): apiVersion: v1, kind: ServiceAccount, metadata only. In B only in the upstream bundle (tmpl:1725-1729). A generic platform kind, so it gets this model; whether it is built in the breaking tag is an owner question.
- **Role, ClusterRole.Meta, Rules** `Meta; []PolicyRule{ APIGroups, Resources, ResourceNames, Verbs []string }` (in B, required): apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1; rules as block sequences of Str, in the order given. ClusterRole is cluster-scoped. In B only in the upstream bundle (tmpl:1731-1790). Proposed, in line with least privilege: a '*' verb or resource is refused unless the tool sets a named allow field.
- **RoleBinding, ClusterRoleBinding.Meta, RoleRef, Subjects** `Meta; RoleRef{ Kind, Name string }; []Subject{ Kind, Name, Namespace string }` (in B, required): roleRef with apiGroup rbac.authorization.k8s.io written by M, then subjects. In B only in the upstream bundle (tmpl:1870-1895).
- **CustomResourceDefinition.no typed model** `Raw` (in B, optional): An operator's schema, about 1700 lines of upstream text in B's bundle (tmpl:8-1724). It is the operator's artifact, not a deployment's, so M carries it as Raw and never models it.

#### kubernetes: extras

- A: kubernetes.deployment.affinity, the verbatim affinity block (deploy.go:205-219). Free-form operator YAML that may hold any affinity field, and A reads it as a yaml.v3 node, a library M must not import (design note 8.2: standard library only). A converts the node to M's value tree and passes it in Affinity.Extra; M writes it and refuses a kind that is also set typed.
- A: kubernetes.deployment.spread (preferred or required). The word, the fixed weight 100 and the hostname topology are A's config, not a platform concept (deploy.go:220-237). A builds one PodAntiAffinity term from it; M gets no Spread field.
- A: pod template labels solace-connector/le-mode and solace-connector/role. Workload labels whose values come from leader election (deploy.go:551-560). They go through the generic Pod.Labels map; M knows no leader mode.
- A: initContainer libs-download and its wget script. One workload's way to fetch jars (deploy.go:591-606). It is an ordinary entry of InitContainers with a Command the tool writes; M adds the hardening lines and knows nothing about libs.
- A: ConfigMap keys application.yml, logback-spring.xml and status. The application's own files (design note 8.3). They are entries of ConfigMap.Data; M writes the block scalars.
- A: env TZ, JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS, JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS, LOGGING_SYSLOG_APPNAME, _HOST, _PORT. Product settings (deploy.go:622-641). They are entries of Container.Env in the tool's order; M quotes and escapes.
- A: the stores volume projected over the tool's Secret and operator-held Secrets. Which Secret holds which store is A's model (deploy.go:51-89). It is a Volume with several SecretSources and items; M has no notion of a store.
- A: liveness on the management port and readiness through the status script. The probe actions and their timings are the workload's (deploy.go:679-707). They fill the generic Probe.
- A: libs volume: an existing claim, a created claim, a download claim, or emptyDir. The four-way choice is A's config (deploy.go:740-755). It resolves to Volume.Claim or Volume.EmptyDir before M is called.
- A: derived names: -config, -credentials, -stores, -image-pull, -libs, <namespace>-<claim>-pv. Naming is the tool's (A:internal/spec/kubernetes.go:303-314). M checks the grammar of the result and nothing else.
- B: TLS Secret annotation solace-util.sha256. B's own change marker, read back by its server-certs flow (secrets.go:188,221-229). It goes through Meta.Annotations; M computes no digest for a Secret.
- B: Secret keys username_admin_password, username_monitor_password, preshared_auth_key, username_<user>_password, username_<user>_globalaccesslevel. The Solace operator's spellings (secrets.go:68-76,152-153). They are keys of Secret.Data.
- B: the PubSubPlusEventBroker spec. Only the operator understands it (design note 8.3). It is Custom.Body, built by B with M's value tree and M's fragments for tolerations, node selector and affinity.
- B: per-role nodeAssignment and the fixed broker-spread anti-affinity term. The roles Primary, Backup and Monitor and the app.kubernetes.io/name=pubsubpluseventbroker selector are the operator's (render.go:289-356,409-420). B assembles them in its body from M's fragments.
- B: metallb annotations and free load-balancer annotations under spec.service.annotations. They sit inside the custom resource, not on a Service M renders (render.go:479-495). B writes them in its body as a map through Str.
- B: the operator bundle: CustomResourceDefinition, ServiceAccount, Role, ClusterRole, RoleBinding, ClusterRoleBinding, Deployment, and its Namespace with control-plane: controller-manager. Upstream vendor text with six substitution points (operator.go:65-84). Re-typing it would turn every operator upgrade into a re-modelling task, so it travels as Raw documents.
- B: the fixed Secret name regcred in the operator namespace. Dictated by the bundle's own imagePullSecrets reference (secrets.go:282-296; tmpl:1958-1961). It is a name passed to RegistrySecret.
- B: terminationGracePeriodSeconds and the pod template annotation of the upstream operator Deployment. Present only in upstream text (tmpl:1912-1915,1967). If a tool ever needs them they go through Pod.Extra and Pod.Annotations, not through named fields.
- third tool: a PersistentVolume source other than NFS. Only NFS is written today (deploy.go:440-443). Another source goes through PersistentVolume.Source until two tools need the same one.
- third tool: a workload kind M does not model (StatefulSet, DaemonSet, Job). Neither tool authors one. It goes through Custom with the exported Pod fragment in its body, so it still gets M's hardening lines.

#### kubernetes: differences

- **Order of keys in metadata**
  - A: name, then namespace, and nothing else on any object (deploy.go:379-381,498-500,536-538).
  - B: Secrets: name, namespace, annotations (secrets.go:39-54). The custom resource: namespace, then name (render.go:80-82). The upstream bundle: labels, name, namespace (tmpl:3-6).
  - Proposal: Meta writes name, namespace, labels, annotations for every kind; labels and annotations sorted bytewise by key, M's own and the tool's together.
- **Scalar quoting**
  - A: Four functions plus bare text: strconv.Quote for credential values, the init command and projected keys; QuoteScalar, which quotes only when needed, for node selector and toleration text; envValueQuote for the Java options; quoteRes for a bare-integer cpu or memory; every name, the image, TZ, hosts and storage sizes bare, guarded by validate (deploy.go:185,280-295,385,437,585,601,609,624,735).
  - B: Three styles: bare for names, enums, ids, quantities and ports; Go %q for image, cpu, timezone, toleration and affinity text and every free map; hand-written quotes for Equal and Exists (render.go:87-88,95,110,165-171,188,317-324,465).
  - Proposal: One rule in the writer: every string a tool supplies, key or value, goes through Str (strconv.Quote); bare text is only M's own keys and constants, integers, booleans and Words from a list M checks. '$' is doubled only in env values, command and args.
- **Secret payload**
  - A: The credentials Secret uses stringData with plaintext values through strconv.Quote; the stores and image-pull Secrets use data (deploy.go:383-386,398-401,415-416).
  - B: Always data with base64 values (secrets.go:56-64).
  - Proposal: data only. One form for every Secret, and kubectl apply then tracks each key under the field it prunes; a key dropped from stringData can stay behind in data on the cluster (to confirm on a real cluster).
- **Who encodes base64**
  - A: The caller: the renderer receives base64 text for the stores and for the registry payload (deploy.go:35-39,94-101; gen.go:515-519).
  - B: The renderer, from raw bytes (secrets.go:30,63).
  - Proposal: The model takes bytes; M encodes with StdEncoding. A value can then never be written double-encoded or unencoded.
- **Order of data keys**
  - A: The caller's order: credentials in model order, stores in model order (deploy.go:384-386,399-401).
  - B: Sorted bytewise, across all users for the additional-users Secret (secrets.go:57-62).
  - Proposal: Sorted bytewise for Secret and ConfigMap data. A's ConfigMap keys are already in that order.
- **Registry payload key**
  - A: The registry host from image.repo with a trailing slash trimmed, or https://index.docker.io/v1/ when unset (image.go:83-88; gen.go:494).
  - B: image.registry as given, the empty string when it is unset (secrets.go:255-256).
  - Proposal: RegistrySecret refuses an empty registry and M exports the Docker Hub key as a constant; each tool decides its key. JSON shape and field order are already identical.
- **Indentation of a sequence under a key**
  - A: Items two further in than the key (deploy.go:438-439,570-571,611-612).
  - B: The dash at the key's own indent (render.go:97-98,216-219,302-311), as in the upstream bundle.
  - Proposal: Two further in, the form M's compose.Posture already has (compose.go:66-70). B's custom resource moves if its body goes through the writer.
- **Joining documents**
  - A: strings.Join with '---' and a newline; each document ends in a newline; no handling of a blank document (deploy.go:477).
  - B: Two helpers: joinManifests trims the right side, drops blank documents and ends in one newline (prep.go:418-426); joinYAMLDocs trims both sides and returns nil for none (operator.go:411-424).
  - Proposal: One yamlw.Join: blank dropped, trimmed, one '---' line between, one final newline, nil for none.
- **A key with nothing under it**
  - A: Writes 'stringData:' even with no entry, and guards 'env:' by hand (deploy.go:383-386,614-621).
  - B: Writes 'storage:' and a role's 'spec:' even when nothing follows, and leaves ports out on purpose (render.go:169,312,212-216).
  - Proposal: The writer drops a key whose mapping or sequence is empty; an explicit empty mapping exists only for emptyDir.
- **Tolerations**
  - A: A struct in Kubernetes' own shape; each field only when set; operator left out when unset; effect left out when unset; tolerationSeconds; an empty item becomes '- {}' (deploy.go:167-203).
  - B: A string 'key[=value]:effect' parsed at render; operator always written, in quotes; effect always written, empty or not; no tolerationSeconds (render.go:313-326).
  - Proposal: One Toleration type: key, operator always stated (Equal by default), value only with Equal, effect left out when unset, tolerationSeconds; an empty toleration is refused. B's string form stays B's config and is parsed into the type.
- **Node selector**
  - A: A map, keys sorted, key and value quoted when needed (deploy.go:577-587).
  - B: A list of 'key: value' entries in config order, trimmed, both halves %q (render.go:327-331,504-507).
  - Proposal: map[string]string, keys sorted, both through Str, label grammar checked.
- **Affinity**
  - A: The operator's block passed through verbatim, then a fixed spread term with bare values (deploy.go:210-237).
  - B: Typed terms: node affinity with match expressions, pod terms with weight, topologyKey, matchLabels and namespaces; quoted for configured terms and bare for the fixed first term; preferred before required (render.go:358-453).
  - Proposal: One typed Affinity fragment with separate Required and Preferred lists and B's key order, plus an Extra slot for A's verbatim block.
- **Resource quantities**
  - A: cpu and memory quoted only when a bare integer; PV and PVC storage bare (deploy.go:276-285,437,457).
  - B: messagingNodeCpu %q, messagingNodeMemory bare with no check found, storage sizes bare (render.go:164-167,179-184).
  - Proposal: Every quantity through Str after one grammar check in M.
- **Image reference and pull policy**
  - A: One bare reference; no imagePullPolicy, so the API default applies (deploy.go:600,609).
  - B: repository and tag apart, both quoted, and pullPolicy always stated, IfNotPresent by default (render.go:84-95).
  - Proposal: Deployment: image through Str and imagePullPolicy always written, IfNotPresent by default. How a reference is assembled stays in each tool.
- **Name grammar**
  - A: Every name, existing Secret and claim names included, held to a DNS-1123 label (validate.go:903-920,1042,1079).
  - B: Namespace and broker name a DNS-1123 label; Secret names a DNS-1123 subdomain (B:internal/config/validate.go:645-681).
  - Proposal: M checks the API's own grammar per kind at render: label for a Namespace, DNS-1035 for a Service, subdomain for the rest. A tool may stay stricter at load.
- **The delete manifest**
  - A: Re-renders in reverse with Secrets by name only, but ConfigMap, PV, PVC, Deployment and Service in full, so the stream carries application.yml with the status password (deploy.go:144-148,361-372,472-476; census note).
  - B: Pipes the full custom resource to delete (B:internal/k8s/deploy.go:187); operator remove pipes bundle text and the regcred.
  - Proposal: Manifest.Refs: every typed document by apiVersion, kind, name and namespace only, reversed; a Namespace refused; Raw passed whole.
- **Render errors**
  - A: Render returns one error, the namespace grammar, and relies on validate for everything else (deploy.go:307-313).
  - B: BrokerCR returns none (render.go:70); the Secret builders return errors for missing material (secrets.go:93-103,145-147,169-183,250-252).
  - Proposal: Every M render returns an error naming kind, object, field, value and rule. Tools keep their load-time checks for a friendlier message.
- **Writing generate output to a file**
  - A: os.WriteFile with 0600, which applies only when the file is created, follows a symlink and asks nothing (A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1771-1780).
  - B: A temp file beside the target, chmod 0600, rename; a symlink refused; an existing file needs a yes (B:internal/cli/helpers.go:143-199). A dead branch writes .broker.yaml as 0644 (census note).
  - Proposal: Both call M's one atomic write (gdu 7) with mode 0600: temp file, chmod, rename. The overwrite question stays in each tool.
- **Apply size limit**
  - A: Refuses a ConfigMap or stores Secret over 256 KiB less 4096 bytes, the limit of kubectl's last-applied annotation (gen.go:1108-1135).
  - B: No check.
  - Proposal: M offers the limit and a pure size check per document; each tool calls it before a deploy.
- **Where defaults live**
  - A: In its spec: replicas 1, run-as 1000:0, storage 1Gi, busybox:1.37, service port (A:internal/spec/kubernetes.go:437-458).
  - B: Partly in the renderer: pullPolicy IfNotPresent (render.go:91-94); the rest at load.
  - Proposal: M holds no product default. Its defaults are posture only: read-only mounts, mode 0400, automount off, type and pull policy stated.
- **Namespace document**
  - A: kube.NamespaceManifest, name bare, no label (deploy.go:347-354).
  - B: The same function for the broker (prep.go:403); the operator's Namespace comes from upstream text with its own label (tmpl:1-6).
  - Proposal: kube.Namespace for a namespace a tool authors, labelled; the bundle's Namespace stays Raw. Owner question on labelling it.
- **Tool-specific label and annotation keys**
  - A: Slash form under solace-connector/ (deploy.go:552,559).
  - B: Dotted form, solace-util.sha256 (secrets.go:188).
  - Proposal: M's vocabulary covers ownership; a tool's own keys pass through Meta and Pod.Labels unchanged. Aligning their style belongs to gdu 19.
- **Timezone**
  - A: Container env TZ, bare (deploy.go:622-625).
  - B: spec.timezone in the custom resource, quoted (render.go:187-189).
  - Proposal: No timezone field in M. A passes an Env entry, which M quotes; B writes it in its body.
- **Image-pull Secret: when it is built**
  - A: Built only with create: true, named <deployment>-image-pull; otherwise referenced by the operator's name (A:internal/spec/kubernetes.go:344-354).
  - B: Built whenever user and password are set, under a configured name or <name>-image-pull (B:internal/config/config.go:198-221).
  - Proposal: Stays in each tool: it is config, not rendering. One for the key-by-key comparison of gdu 19.

#### kubernetes: owner questions

1. Does M write every string a tool supplies on Kubernetes (names, map keys, values, base64 text) as a double-quoted scalar, and keep bare only its own keys, integers, booleans and checked enums? Recommended: yes. It is the rule compose.Quote already holds and the design note names (8.2, layer 1); one rule replaces seven; a name such as 123, true or 1e3, which kube.ValidLabel accepts, can no longer be read as a number or boolean. The cost is 'name: "broker"' in every golden. The alternative, quoting only when a plain scalar would be misread, needs a complete list of YAML 1.1 cases inside M.
2. Does M write every Secret with data and base64, never stringData? Recommended: yes. A's credentials Secret then stops showing plaintext in generate output, and one form holds for both tools.
3. Is the body of a Custom document a value tree written by M's writer, or text the tool hands over? Recommended: a value tree. The tool still decides every key and value, and M's quoting closes the bare, unchecked values in solace-util's custom resource (service account, storage class, node memory, claim names, load-balancer address and pool, anti-affinity namespaces). Its goldens change once either way.
4. Does Custom refuse a kind M has a typed document for, and accept any other kind? Recommended: yes to both. A Deployment can then not be written around M's hardening, and a third tool can still write a StatefulSet or Job by embedding M's exported pod template in a Custom body.
5. Does solace-util's operator bundle stay embedded upstream text, carried as Raw documents with no ownership labels, with only the regcred Secret rendered by M? Recommended: yes. The bundle is the vendor's artifact, operator remove never deletes its namespace, and the leftover removal of gdu 18 does not cover it. The regcred then needs an instance value of its own, since it belongs to the operator install and not to one broker: recommended the operator's deployment name.
6. Are the generic kinds only the upstream bundle contains (ServiceAccount, Role, ClusterRole, RoleBinding, ClusterRoleBinding) built as typed documents in the breaking tag? Recommended: design them now, as modelled here, and build them only if the example tool uses them; otherwise they wait for the first tool that authors one. CustomResourceDefinition never gets a model.
7. For a tool outside Solace Professional Services, are services.solace.com/solution and services.solace.com/developed-by supplied by the tool and left out when empty? Recommended: yes. Both Solace tools pass accelerator and apac-psg; product and instance, the two keys a destructive step selects on, stay required.
8. Does a remove pipe name-only references for every kind, through one Manifest.Refs, in place of full bodies? Recommended: yes. solmq-conn-util's delete stream then no longer carries application.yml with the status password, solace-util's no longer carries the full custom resource, and a remove needs no credential and no file.
9. Should a rendered pod state hostNetwork: false, or state nothing? Recommended: state nothing, as confirmed on 2026-11-15 (Kubernetes gets no network key); the model has no field for host networking, host PID, host IPC or hostPath, and artifacttest gains a Kubernetes posture check that forbids them and the privilege tokens in any rendered text.
10. Which API defaults does M state without being asked? Recommended: the ones with a security or behaviour meaning: Service type (ClusterIP), imagePullPolicy (IfNotPresent), automountServiceAccountToken (false), readOnly on every mount, defaultMode 0400 on a Secret volume, the PV reclaim policy (Retain) and the toleration operator. Port protocol and probe counters are written only when set. Stating IfNotPresent changes solmq-conn-util's behaviour for a latest tag, which the API pulls always today.
11. Must a tool state the pod identity, and may it be root? Recommended: the tool passes ids or 'from the image' explicitly and the zero value is an error; uid 0 is accepted when stated, without runAsNonRoot, as solmq-conn-util allows today. Refusing root outright would be the stricter reading of the container posture rule.
12. Does the container model offer secret-backed environment variables (secretKeyRef, envFrom)? Recommended: no. Secrets arrive as files at absolute targets; solace-util's one exception lives inside its custom resource. A third tool that needs it brings its case.
13. Do Pod and Container get a generic Extra slot for keys M does not model? Recommended: yes, with a refusal of every key M writes and of the widening keys, since the design already gives compose and quadlet such a slot. Without it a third tool is blocked by the first missing field.
14. Does M own the check that one document fits kubectl's client-side apply limit (256 KiB in the last-applied annotation)? Recommended: M offers the constant and a pure size check; each tool decides when to call it, as solmq-conn-util skips it on teardown.
15. Is Manifest.Objects the single source for the permission probes, the namespace gate's owned list and the leftover compare, replacing solmq-conn-util's Written and CreatedNames and solace-util's ownedSecretNames? Recommended: yes, so what is probed and what is rendered cannot drift.
16. Is a Secret volume's defaultMode written as the octal literal 0400, or as decimal 256? Recommended: 0400. kubectl is the only reader and takes it as octal, and generate output is read by people. Decimal is the choice if the output must also survive a strict YAML 1.2 tool.
17. Does solmq-conn-util keep its verbatim affinity block? Recommended: yes, through Affinity.Extra, converted by the tool from its yaml.v3 node, because M stays on the standard library. The alternative is to narrow the block to M's typed terms, which drops fields operators may already use.
18. What registry key does solace-util write when image.registry is unset? M will refuse an empty key, where the tool writes one today. Recommended: the Docker Hub key when image.repo has no host of its own, and that host otherwise; decided in solace-util's session.

#### kubernetes: census notes

- A: Scope: tool A's kubernetes artifact only (one multi-document manifest from deploy.Render, A:internal/deploy/deploy.go:313-478). Compose and quadlet were not in this task's reading list and are not covered. Paths below are relative to A unless marked M. WRITER - internal/yamlwriter/yamlwriter.go: Writer.Line(indent, s) = spaces + s + newline (18-22); Raw verbatim (27); SplitLines drops one trailing empty element (35-41). No quoting, no key helper: every "key: value" is string concatenation in deploy.go. Indent is absolute spaces chosen at each call site. - Quoting functions in use, four different ones: strconv.Quote (credential values 385, init command 601, projected item key 735); consolidate.QuoteScalar, quote-only-when-needed (nodeSelector 585, toleration key/value 185,191; internal/consolidate/consolidate.go:607-635); envValueQuote, YAML escape plus $ -> $$ (deploy.go:293-295; JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS, JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS only); quoteRes, quote bare integers (280-285; cpu/memory only, not PV/PVC storage). The affinity passthrough uses consolidate.FormatScalar (source style kept; consolidate.go:574-588). Everything else is written unquoted and depends on validate. - gopkg.in/yaml.v3 is imported only for the yaml.Node type of the affinity passthrough (deploy.go:15,210); no marshalling. THE ONLY M PIECE IN THE RENDER - kube.NamespaceManifest (M kube/kube.go:55-61) writes the whole Namespace document; kube.ValidLabel guards it (kube.go:263). nsgate.Object is used by deploy.Written (806-823), which renders nothing. No metadata.labels or metadata.annotations is written on any object today (no managed-by, version or lib annotation); the only labels are the pod template's app, solace-connector/le-mode and solace-connector/role. WHAT THE RENDERER REFUSES - deploy.Render itself returns one error only: a namespace that is not a DNS-1123 label, from kube.NamespaceManifest, and only on the path that emits the Namespace document (deploy.go:347-351). With Teardown or OmitNamespace it cannot fail. - gen.GenerateKubernetes (internal/gen/gen.go:233-330) refuses before Render: no kubernetes: section (236-238); any validate.Run error (239-255); status password or model build errors (259-267); an unparsable kubernetes.run-as (269-272, 781-787); a credential whose -env variable is unset or empty (282-285, 797-812); a store that cannot be read or built, no file access, or a stores Secret over the kubectl apply limit of 256 KiB less a 4096 byte allowance (288-292, 1112-1124, 1154-1200); image-pull create without image.user and image.pass (480-482); a ConfigMap over the same size limit, skipped on teardown (315-319, 1129-1135). - validate guards for the values written unquoted (internal/validate/validate.go): deployment.name and derived names DNS-1123 and <= 63 (899-915); namespace (916-920); cpu/memory quantity grammar (929-930, 1043-1058); credentials create XOR existing and existing name (958-973); libs exactly one mode, pvc create XOR existing, pvc.existing and download.pvc labels, PV name length, storage quantity, nfs.server hostRE, nfs.path safeHostPath, urls http(s) and no quote/space/$ characters, download image SafeToken (1015-1088); syslog host/port/protocol (994-1012); timezone SafeToken (1528); standalone needs replicas 1 (943-946). checkNodeSelector, checkTolerations, checkSpreadAndAffinity exist at 1320, 1338, 1382 (bodies not opened). - Not guarded in the renderer: renderDeployment dereferences inst.Model (deploy.go:618) though Render nil-guards Model at 323 and storeSources at 61; toleration operator/effect, image ref, syslog host and the credential key (kv.Key, the stable name) are written with no transform. ORDERING RULES - Document order (deploy.go:343-470): Namespace; ConfigMap; [teardown only: name-only Secrets credentials, stores, image-pull]; credentials Secret; stores Secret; image-pull Secret; PersistentVolume; PersistentVolumeClaim; Deployment; Service. The doc comment at 307-308 lists "Secrets, libs PV/PVC"; the code puts image-pull (3c) before the PV/PVC (3b). - Each document is rendered into its own buffer and the set is joined with "---\n" (477): no leading "---", no trailing separator, each document ends with one newline. - Teardown reverses the whole list (472-476) so the workload is deleted before the PVC it holds (reason at 137-142), drops the Namespace, and renders every tool-owned Secret by name only; the stores Secret is always named on teardown even when this spec renders none (366-367). ConfigMap, PV, PVC, Deployment and Service are still rendered in full on teardown, so the delete manifest carries the whole application.yml (with the status password) and the pod spec. - Within documents: ConfigMap keys application.yml, logback-spring.xml (syslog only), status (502-527). Credentials in model.Secrets order (gen.go:797-812). Stores data in Model.Stores order minus cluster-held (gen.go:1155-1160). nodeSelector keys sorted (deploy.go:582). Tolerations in config order with fixed field order key, operator, value, effect, tolerationSeconds (184-198). Affinity: operator's verbatim block first, then the spread podAntiAffinity (217-237); validate refuses a podAntiAffinity in both (comment 209). Pod spec order: automountServiceAccountToken, imagePullSecrets, nodeSelector, tolerations, affinity, securityContext, initContainers, containers, volumes. Container order: name, image, securityContext, ports, env, volumeMounts, livenessProbe, readinessProbe, resources. Env order: TZ, JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS, JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS, LOGGING_SYSLOG_APPNAME, _HOST, _PORT. volumeMounts order: secrets, config (application.yml), config (logback), stores, libs, config (status). volumes order: config, secrets, stores, libs. Projected sources: the tool's own stores Secret first, then operator Secrets in first-use order, one source per distinct Secret (60-89). - Layout quirks a shared model must reproduce or deliberately change (goldens are byte for byte): "- secret:" at indent 14 with name/items at indent 18, not 16 (731-733); defaultMode written as bare 0400 (719, 728); init command as a flow sequence (601) while the readiness command is a block sequence (700-703); block scalars use plain "|" with empty lines as bare newlines (502-527); storageClassName: "" (451); LOGGING_SYSLOG_PORT quoted by hand (640); an empty toleration renders "- {}" (200); stringData: is written even with no entries (383-386). COMMENTS WRITTEN INTO THE ARTIFACT - None. The renderer emits no "#" line and no header; every explanation is a Go comment. The only comment-like text is inside the ConfigMap payloads (the logback XML's own <!-- --> comments, internal/logback/logback.go:35-40, and whatever application.yml and the status script contain), which are data in block scalars. SINK AND FILE MODE - `generate kubernetes` (cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:473-484): KubeOpts{} so the Namespace document is included; emit prints to stdout when -out is empty, else os.WriteFile(out, 0o600) (main.go:1771-1780). - deploy (main.go:660-713): rendered with OmitNamespace true and never written to disk; piped on stdin to kube.Apply, `apply -f -` (internal/runner/runner.go:136-150, 144). The Namespace is applied separately by ensureNamespace, only when absent and creatable, using kube.NamespaceManifest again through the same apply path (cmd/solmq-conn-util/namespace.go:56-69). - remove: rendered with Teardown true and piped on stdin to kube.Delete, `delete -f - --ignore-not-found` (runner.go:146); the namespace is handled afterwards by removeNamespace (main.go:710-711). - deploy.Written (deploy.go:806-823) narrows spec.CreatedNames (internal/spec/kubernetes.go:373-395) to what one render writes, for the RBAC probes and the namespace gate; the cluster-scoped PV is not listed. VALUE RESOLUTION OUTSIDE THE RENDERER (the renderer is pure, deploy.go:1-4) - Credentials: gen.ResolveCredentials, literal or M envref.Ref.Resolve (gen.go:797-812). Stores: gen.resolveStores, base64 of built or read PKCS12 bytes (gen.go:1154-1200). Image-pull payload: gen.dockerConfigJSON, json.Marshal then base64, registry from image.repo or the default (gen.go:471-520; internal/spec/image.go:83-88); this is the tool's own registry payload that design section 8.2 moves into M's Secret. Status script: statusscript.Render(management port, spec.StatusUserName) (gen.go:311). Status password on kubernetes is random per render (gen.go:257-262), so the ConfigMap text differs on every render. - Defaults applied before render (internal/spec/kubernetes.go:437-458): replicas 1, run-as 1000:0 (internal/spec/runas.go:16), service.port = management port both sides, libs storage 1Gi, download image busybox:1.37. Design note read: M:docs/design/2026-11-15-shared-deploy-api.md sections 1, 3, 4, 7, 8. Its 8.1 table cites deploy.go:374-418 for the inline Secrets, which matches the lines opened here.
- B: Scope. This census covers tool B's Kubernetes artifacts only (the task's read list). B's compose file (render.Compose, internal/render/render.go:1043-1188) and quadlet unit (render.Quadlet, render.go:888-1015) were read but are not listed here; they belong to the container census. Cites are relative to B unless prefixed "M:" (= M). Documents B can emit on Kubernetes: 1 Namespace (M kube.NamespaceManifest), up to 4 broker-namespace Secrets (admin Opaque, TLS, image-pull dockerconfigjson, additional-users Opaque), 1 operator-namespace Secret (regcred), 1 PubSubPlusEventBroker CR, and the upstream operator bundle (8 kinds: Namespace, CustomResourceDefinition, ServiceAccount, Role, ClusterRole, RoleBinding, ClusterRoleBinding, Deployment; six substitution points Namespace x7 lines, WatchNamespace, Image, CPU, Mem, PullSecret). B writes no ConfigMap, Service, PV, PVC or Deployment of its own. One non-manifest write exists: `kubectl set env deployment/pubsubplus-eventbroker-operator -n <opNS> WATCH_NAMESPACE=<list>` when narrowing the watch list on remove (internal/k8s/watch.go:445-458), built by the tool, not by M. What M writes today: only the Namespace document (M:kube/kube.go:55-60) and the apply/delete stdin commands. Everything else is hand-built: Secrets by string concatenation (internal/k8s/secrets.go:35-66), the CR by fmt.Fprintf (render.go:70-227), the bundle by text/template (operator.go:65-84), the joins by two separate helpers (joinManifests prep.go:418-426, joinYAMLDocs operator.go:411-424) that differ slightly (TrimRight vs Trim; nil vs "\n" for empty input). Facts that matter for the planned M Meta/Secret/Custom: - No object B renders carries any label or annotation except the TLS Secret's single annotation solace-util.sha256 (secrets.go:188). No lib/version annotation exists anywhere. The Namespace has no labels (the bundle's own Namespace has control-plane: controller-manager, upstream). - Metadata key order differs between kinds: Secrets write name then namespace (secrets.go:40-41); the CR writes namespace then name (render.go:81-82). A shared Meta changes one of them (goldens move). - Secret layout: apiVersion, kind, metadata(name, namespace, annotations), type, data. data keys sorted bytewise; values base64.StdEncoding; no stringData; annotation keys sorted, values strconv.Quote, annotation KEYS and all names bare. - Registry payload: struct-ordered JSON username, password, auth; auths key is image.registry verbatim (empty string key when image.registry is unset; I found no refusal of that case in dockerRegistrySecret, secrets.go:249-262, only name == "" is refused). - The CR body uses three scalar styles: bare (names, enums, ids, quantities, ports), Go %q (image repo/tag, cpu, timezone, toleration key/value/effect, match keys/values, topologyKey, all free-text map keys and values), and literal quoted constants ("Equal", "Exists"). %q is Go string-literal quoting, not compose.Quote. - CR list indentation is the "- at parent indent" style (e.g. " nodeAssignment:" then " - name: Primary"; " ports:" then " - containerPort:"). Ordering rules: - broker generate/deploy: Namespace, then Secrets in the fixed order admin, TLS, image-pull, additional-users (ownedSecretNames, prep.go:162-174; GenSecrets prep.go:186-235), then the CR (prep.go:407-411; cli/ops_k8s.go:93-101). Deploy applies them as three separate applies (namespace only if absent), generate as one stream. - operator: bundle Namespace first, regcred second, rest of bundle third (operator.go:262-288, 149-157); remove deletes rest+regcred, keeps the namespace, deletes CRDs last and only with --delete-crd (operator.go:528-574). - Inside the CR: customVolumeMount in fixed role order Primary, Backup, Monitor (render.go:244); nodeAssignment Primary, then Backup and Monitor only in HA (render.go:303-307); map-valued inputs sorted by key (podAnnotations, podLabels, matchLabels; render.go:470-477); list-valued inputs in config order; the legacy anti-affinity term always precedes configured podAntiAffinity terms (render.go:409-427); metallb annotations precede free annotations (render.go:484-494). - Secret data keys of the additional-users Secret are sorted across all users, not grouped in config order. What the renderers refuse (render time): - kube.NamespaceManifest: a namespace that is not a DNS-1123 label (M:kube/kube.go:56-59). - AdminSecret: empty semp.adminPass; empty resolved name (secrets.go:94-103). - AdditionalUsersSecret: a user with an empty password (secrets.go:145-147); returns nil (no document) with no users. - TLSSecret: tls.cert or tls.certKey unset; empty resolved name; any unreadable cert, CA or key file (secrets.go:169-183, 201-217). secretPreflight also stats the files before any manifest is built (prep.go:140-154). - dockerRegistrySecret: empty name; json marshal failure (secrets.go:250-265). - GenOperator/OperatorApply: a bundle with no Namespace document (operator.go:150-152, 263-269); template parse/execute errors (operator.go:75-82). - render.BrokerCR refuses nothing and returns no error: it is a pure function and relies entirely on config.Validate. It never emits readOnlyRootFilesystem (render.go:283-285). What load refuses for these artifacts (config.Validate, K8s branch): kubernetes.namespace/name/operator.namespace not DNS labels, name too long once suffixed (validate.go:645-667); the three Secret names not DNS-1123 subdomains (672-680); cert without key or the reverse (685-690); monitorPass or psk without adminPass, adminSecret inside the operator's own name prefix, two Secret roles resolving to one name (1399-1451); msgNode.cpu and containerSecurity.readOnlyRootFilesystem set at all (701-714); updateStrategy/imagePullPolicy outside their enums (716-725); security ids not plain decimals 0..2^31-1 (726-733, 916-930); operator image/cpu/mem, storage sizes, watch entries (artifactvalues.go:273-302); ports shape, names, numbers, duplicates (844-905); loadBalancer.annotations and placement.labels* without a key (601-611, 744-751); storage class together with customVolumeMount, unknown role keys, blank claims, partial HA coverage (764-816); additional users: name grammar, built-ins, duplicates, access level, empty password, non-env-var name on Kubernetes (1256-1327); affinity operators/keys/values/topologyKey (1340-1381); image.* grammar and timezone charset (artifactvalues.go:56-105). Values written bare into the CR for which I found NO charset or grammar check in internal/config: kubernetes.serviceAccount (render.go:102), kubernetes.storage.class (171), kubernetes.msgNode.mem (167), kubernetes.storage.customVolumeMount claims (250, only non-blank), kubernetes.loadBalancer.ip and ipPool (485-490), kubernetes.placement.antiAffinityNamespaces entries (418), and the parsed toleration strings have no shape check (they are %q-quoted, so only wrong, not injectable). A grep of internal/config for those key names found no validator; treat as a finding to confirm, since a newline in one of the bare ones would restructure the CR. An M writer that quotes or validates every scalar would close this. Two guard mismatches inside B: spec.preSharedAuthKeySecret is keyed on PSKConfigured() (psk OR pskEnv, render.go:140) while the Secret key is written only when the resolved redundancy.psk is non-empty (secrets.go:117); equal after Load, different for a Config built in code. kubernetes.ports protocol is validated case-insensitively but rendered as written (render.go:221), so "tcp" reaches the CR lower-case. Comments written into the artifacts: none. BrokerCR, secretManifest.render and kube.NamespaceManifest write no '#' lines, and the embedded bundle has zero comment lines (grep count 0). No header, no generated-by line, no version stamp. File mode and sink: - Deploy: every manifest goes to `kubectl apply -f -` on stdin through kube.Apply; nothing is written to disk (cluster.go:187-191). Removal re-renders the CR / bundle and pipes it to kube.Delete (cluster.go:193-197). - `broker generate` and `operator generate`: stdout via os.Stdout.Write (cli/helpers.go:19-22), or with --out a temp file created beside the target, chmod 0600, then renamed over it; a symlink target is refused; an existing file needs an overwrite confirmation (cli/helpers.go:143-199). The broker stream carries base64 secret values, the operator stream the registry credential. - DeployBroker has a keepYAML branch that writes the CR alone to ./.broker.yaml with mode 0644 (deploy.go:21, 38-44); its only non-test caller passes false (cli/ops_k8s.go:99), so it is dead from the CLI. - TLS material is read from disk at render (tls.cert, tls.cas, tls.certKey); every other value comes from the loaded Config. ServerCertChanged reads back only the annotation via jsonpath, never the Secret data (prep.go:349-364). For the design's Custom wrapper: the CR's metadata is two lines and the body (spec) is everything from render.go:83 to 226; the body's only cross-references to other documents are Secret names (pullSecrets, adminCredentialsSecret, monitoringCredentialsSecret, extraEnvVarsSecret, preSharedAuthKeySecret, tls.serverTlsConfigSecret), all resolved through config.Config name helpers (config.go:99-221) shared with internal/k8s.

### kubernetes: verifier

- **model: Container.PullPolicy; difference 'Image reference and pull policy'; owner question 10**
  - Was: imagePullPolicy is always written and the zero value writes IfNotPresent.
  - Should be: The zero value writes no imagePullPolicy key, so the API default applies as it does for A today (Always for a latest or tag-less reference, IfNotPresent otherwise). M states a policy only when the tool sets one. If the owner wants the key always stated, the stated default must not be weaker than the API's: Always when the reference has no tag or the tag latest and no digest.
  - Evidence: A writes no imagePullPolicy on either container (A internal/deploy/deploy.go:600,609). A refuses only an empty image.tag, so tag: latest loads (A internal/validate/validate.go:1541-1543), and kubernetes.libs.download.image is held to SafeToken only, so an untagged init image loads (validate.go:1082-1083). For both the API pulls Always today. A stated IfNotPresent runs whatever the node has cached under that name, with no registry check of the pull secret and no pick-up of a re-pushed fix. B's IfNotPresent sits in a custom resource for the operator (B internal/render/render.go:91-95) and is no precedent for A's Deployment. The model's own question 10 names the behaviour change but recommends it.
- **model: Pod.RunAs**
  - Was: RunAs{ UID, GID int64; FSGroup *int64; FromImage bool }; the bare zero value is refused so a forgotten field never means root; uid 0 is accepted when stated.
  - Should be: A shape where stated and forgotten differ: pointer ids, or constructors (ids, from-image) with an unexported set flag. As modelled, uid 0 with gid 0 IS the zero value: either it is refused and A's run-as 0 and 0:0 stop rendering, or it is accepted and a forgotten field renders root.
  - Evidence: A accepts run-as '0' and '0:0': ParseRunAs returns RunAsID{UID:0, GID:0} (A internal/spec/runas.go:50-66), validate only warns on uid 0 (A internal/validate/validate.go:331-337), and the renderer writes runAsUser, runAsGroup and fsGroup 0 without runAsNonRoot (deploy.go:251-257). A's own comment states the hazard: 'Its zero value is root, so the caller always sets it' (deploy.go:115-117).
- **model: Manifest.Refs and Manifest.Objects, with Secret.Type, TLSSecret and RegistrySecret**
  - Was: Refs needs no secret value and no file, so a remove resolves nothing; it replaces A's Teardown flag and nameOnlySecret; Objects replaces deploy.Written, CreatedNames and ownedSecretNames.
  - Should be: Add a name-only reference document (apiVersion, kind, name, namespace; no body, no Type) that Refs and Objects accept, and build the remove set from those. As modelled a Secret document cannot exist without material: Type's zero value is an error, TLSSecret refuses an empty chain or key, RegistrySecret refuses an empty user or password. A remove would have to read certificates and credentials to learn names, or invent placeholder bytes. The reference form is also the only way to name an object the current config does not render.
  - Evidence: A's teardown resolves nothing: 'Delete needs names only: no credential is resolved, no store opened' (A internal/gen/gen.go:276-280). It always names the stores Secret, even when this spec renders none, so one an earlier deploy left is removed (deploy.go:144-147, 366-367); a Refs derived from the deploy manifest loses that Secret, which holds key stores. B's remove set is ownedSecretNames, computed from config flags with no file read (B internal/k8s/prep.go:162-174) and deleted by name (prep.go:273-287). Owner rule: remove resolves no credential (decisions.md:125-127).
- **model: Manifest.Refs with Raw documents; difference 'The delete manifest'**
  - Was: Refs refuses a Namespace document; a Raw document is passed whole.
  - Should be: The Namespace refusal applies to a Raw document too, by Raw.Kind(), and fails closed: a Raw whose kind line cannot be read is refused. A Raw CustomResourceDefinition is refused as well unless the tool hands it over in a separate, explicitly named call, since deleting it cascades to every custom resource in the cluster.
  - Evidence: B's bundle starts with its own Namespace document (B internal/k8s/assets/operator-1.4.2.yaml.tmpl:1-6). B keeps Namespace and CRDs out of every delete stream: splitOperatorBundle and its reasons (B internal/k8s/operator.go:377-406), the namespace is kept (operator.go:528, 554-555), CRDs go only with --delete-crd after the cascade gate (operator.go:529-533, 556-572). A Manifest of Raw bundle documents handed to Refs as modelled pipes the Namespace and the CRD to kubectl delete.
- **model: Probe.Exec; difference 'Scalar quoting' ('$' doubled only in env values, command and args)**
  - Was: exec.command items through Str, with no '$' doubling if probe commands are not expanded by Kubernetes: to confirm.
  - Should be: Double '$' in Probe.Exec items exactly as in Command, Args and Env values. The kubelet expands $(VAR) in an exec probe's command from the container's literal env values, and every Env entry of this model is a literal, so every reference would resolve.
  - Evidence: From the kubelet source as I know it, not opened in this run (prober runProbe passes p.Exec.Command through ExpandContainerCommandOnlyStatic with container.Env): confirm when built. Doubling is the safe default either way, because '$$' reads back as one '$' in that expansion whether or not a reference follows.
