  - Should be: One name with two grammars is a trap: the engine form 6898m is also a valid Kubernetes quantity (m is the milli suffix) under A's quantityRE and B's k8sQuantityRE, so a value copied from docker.resources.memory to kubernetes.resources.memory validates and asks for about 7 bytes. B already calls copying between the two forms the likeliest mistake, and B's kubernetes memory value has no check at all today. Either M converts one grammar per sink, or the kubernetes memory check refuses sub-unit suffixes, or the names stay different.
  - Evidence: A:internal/validate/validate.go:1089-1093; B:internal/config/artifactvalues.go:263-267,281-286; B:internal/config/scaling.go:56-60; B:internal/config/validate.go:1629-1635; B:internal/render/render.go:167,952-954,1115-1117
- **Alignment row 'Read-only root filesystem' marked inherent**
  - Was: 'The broker needs a writable root and B refuses the key by name'
  - Should be: Not forced. B's own rule says the broker accepts a read-only root from 10.9 and that the tool does not support it on any platform yet; the bundled operator CRD carries brokerContainerSecurity.readOnlyRootFilesystem. So this is a common key (A's proposed read-only-root) that B refuses until it supports it: one-sided and not forced, not an inherent difference.

  - Should be: Only the namespace list and the weight are forced by the broker. B's built-in term is A's 'spread: preferred' on kubernetes.io/hostname, always on because the namespace list defaults to [kubernetes.namespace] and there is no off value. spread can be the common key (B default preferred, A default none) with B's two keys beside it; that also gives B the off switch the affinity row needs.

But the refusal does not transfer: B's built-in term is always on, so refusing podAntiAffinity beside it removes a feature B has today (it appends the operator's own terms after the built-in one). B needs an off switch first, or keeps appending. Passing a verbatim block also drops B's load-time checks on operators and topologyKey, and the block must be written once per role.

The CRD annotations field is a map, as recommended. One uid:gid pair can fill all four CR fields (the operator's own defaults are the same pair in both blocks) and the word image maps to leaving both blocks out, which is what A's image means on OpenShift. What is lost is a pod id that differs from the container id, and the meaning of 0. Record it as B's choice or align it; it is not an inherent difference.

Notes: Read-only; nothing was run, edited or created. What was opened. A: internal/spec targets.go, kubernetes.go, defaults.go, env.go, image.go, runas.go, syslog.go, javaoptions.go, expand.go in full, spec.go 1-200; internal/validate validate.go and stores.go in full, extra.go 1-80; examples env.yaml 1-40 and 180-312; docs/configuration.md 33-52 and 560-1042; parts of deploy.go, dockergen.go, podmangen.go, runner.go, main.go, statusscript.go, troubleshooting.md, the A decisions file. B: internal/config config.go, load.go, validate.go, artifactvalues.go, scaling.go, execguard.go, secretref.go, platform.go in full, hostpath.go 60-160 and 200-420, roles.go 100-156; render.go 40-520 and 520-1215, 1240-1295; env/sample.yaml in full; the three starter files; docs/configuration.md 60-330 and 691-784; parts of k8s/secrets.go, k8s/operator.go, container/manager.go, container/euid.go, container-security.md and the bundled operator CRD. M: the design note in full, decisions 100-230, todo items 2, 6, 9, 19, containercli.go 90-118, podman.go 15-30 and 120-163, paritydoc/body.md 140-152 and 190-210, envref Check/Resolve. Not opened: A internal/consolidate and gen.go beyond grep hits; B domaincerts.go, duration.go, README beyond grep hits. Result on the two listings. Every key path, default and rule in A's rows and in B's rows was confirmed against the struct fields, the defaulting functions and the validators, except the items under corrections. Every field of A's Docker, Podman, Kubernetes, Deployment, Service, Libs and Secrets structs and of B's K8sConfig, DockerConfig, PodmanConfig, Network, Container, HealthCheck, Ulimits, Storage, Operator, Placement and LoadBalancer structs has a row; no platform key is missing from the listings. B's reviewer's 'no rule found' items are confirmed: image.user, redundancy.*.addr, kubernetes.serviceAccount, storage.class, msgNode.mem, customVolumeMount claim names, loadBalancer.ip and ipPool, placement.antiAffinityNamespaces, tolerations format, weights, healthCheck.retries sign. A's reviewer's 'no check' items are confirmed: management.port, the lower bound of replicas. Recommendations I checked and agree with: strict decode in both; expansion (decided); docker.project-name with -p (decided; M's ComposeUp and ComposeDown take no project today); scalar <platform>.network (matches the 2026-11-14 and 2026-11-15 decisions; B's bridge today means 'write no line', per its own docs line 240); flat docker and podman sections; kubernetes.name and kubernetes.namespace; DNS-1123 container name (a narrowing of B, not forced by the label grammar); restart keys for B; B's port grammar in M; docker.compose in A; node-selector as a map and tolerations in Kubernetes' shape per role (CRD confirms both); credentials create|existing for B subject to the gap above; image.user-env in B; dropping baseDir. Inherent rows I agree are forced: image digest (the CRD takes repository and tag as two strings, tag default latest, CRD lines 150-158), JVM options and syslog, TLS material, application credentials, HA model, scaling tier, replication access, libs against data directory, cpuset, process limits, the health check switch and command, replicas, storage, update strategy, operator keys. Stated from knowledge, not run: where docker login and podman login store credentials; which compose features nerdctl and a standalone compose binary support; the quadlet generator's other search directories. The milli meaning of the m suffix is in A's own comment (validate.go:1089-1091). Other findings. (1) B writes kubernetes.serviceAccount, storage.class, msgNode.mem, claim names, loadBalancer.ip/ipPool and antiAffinityNamespaces unquoted into the CR with no load check; these stay B's text after rendering moves, so B must hold them to a grammar itself. (2) B's doc says an unset serviceAccount uses the namespace default; the CRD says a new ServiceAccount is created (docs/configuration.md:204 against CRD 1505-1508). (3) A's stale /run/secrets comment is also at internal/spec/spec.go:38-39, not only kubernetes.go:121-122. (4) A's pending CR-0041 changes a libs.download entry from a string to a URL with an optional sha256; no listing row shows that shape yet. (5) B's HA group keys are built from redundancy.<role>.name, which may hold '.' and '-', into Environment= names (render.go:802-807, 883-885); B's own comment says such names are alphanumeric and underscore only (render.go:836-838). Not a key issue, but M's quadlet Environment line builder will meet it.

## 5. Critic

### Contradictions

1. The output hook against the decision. Design 3.2 says 'the tool still sees every byte' (M:docs/design/2026-11-15-shared-deploy-api.md:98-99). The runner group's proposed helper 'feeds every chunk to the hook'. The operations map, its verifier, and the containercli, podman-host and kube verifiers all require that stdout a method parses or returns never reach the hook, name at least five secret-bearing reads (podman secret inspect --showsecret, both get-secret forms, the namespace listing, raw container inspect with Config.Env) and add a third class, probes whose stdout is dropped today. This needs the owner, because it reverses a point recorded as decided.
2. One Exec, or several. The catalogue has one Exec per client that 'returns the child's exit code' (design note :125, :128). Surface: one method covering three to five run modes, else the builder stays exported. Operations map: three methods (Exec, ExecOutput, Attach) plus one interface over a bound pod and a bound container. Its verifier: A needs a fourth form, both streams merged and returned. The surface review knows neither Attach nor ExecOutput as methods, nor the bound-target types.
3. Exit status of a session. exitcode group: the client returns the code in Result and no error. Operations map: Attach returns a nil error once the session ran, while Exec returns an error that carries the code. Guards verifier: Exec returns a typed error implementing ExitCode() and B keeps ChildExit(err). Result.ExitCode is -1 for never started (design note :87) while Exec.Attach reports a signalled child as exitcode.Failure (runner group note). Three shapes for one contract.
4. How a client is set up. Decided: a struct literal with Base []string (design note :62-66). Guards group: a sealed Tokens type from Rules.Guard that Base requires (its verifier lists four costs and calls it a change to a decided shape). Operations map question 10: a constructor per client that takes execguard.Rules and runs Check. The constructor cannot apply to systemd.Client, whose programs are literals that never pass execguard (M:systemd/systemd.go:99-106; ops note A.6). Value or pointer is also unsettled: podman.Client must keep the version, and the map's 'copy the client for another hook' assumes a value that shares state.
5. podman.Version. Surface: unexported, covered by podman.Client.Version, which the catalogue lists (design note :126). Operations map, unusedMethods: no tool calls it once the gate is inside the client, keep the read internal. The surface's coveredBy points at a method the map removes.
6. systemd.Systemctl has three answers: keep exported (reviewer), unexport and add Status, Cat and a probe or Show (verifier), keep nothing exported and add Show plus a generic systemd.Client.Run (operations map). The generic Run is unknown to the surface review.
7. Generic run. The operations map adds Run and Output to all four clients over an exported runner.Do. The surface review asks for a generic run only in kube (as an owner call) and an unnamed helper in runner; for containercli and systemd it keeps or debates exported builders instead. kube.Forbidden's verdict flips with this (keep as classifier for tool-built commands, or unexport once Run returns ErrForbidden).
8. Container inspect. Surface reviewer: Containers and Decode unexported behind Client.Inspect. Verifier: keep Decode, and keep Containers unless a raw Client.Describe is added. Operations map: Inspect returns the raw bytes too, or B runs it through the generic Run; its own note says the raw answer holds the container environment and must not reach the hook, which the Run route does.
9. podman unshare. Surface: an exported builder podman.Unshare with no client method. Operations map: a client method podman.Client.Unshare returning the child's exit code.
10. IDMap. Surface: podman.Client.IDMap returns both maps from one call as a typed value and rootless.CheckIDMap changes signature. Operations map: two commands, the mapping text handed to the unchanged rootless.CheckIDMap.
11. Return shapes of the systemd reads. Surface and its verifier: a typed state with a typed unanswered error, never the word (State), a typed not-found (NRestarts), a bool or typed state (Linger). Operations map: (state word, answered bool), (count, known), and 'yes, no, or whatever logind said'.
12. Host probes. Surface (rootless group) proposes lifting the subid file read, id -un and the runtime-directory probe into M as builders, saying rule 5 reopens M:docs/developer.md:183-184. Operations map question 13 recommends the opposite: they stay in the tools.
13. Home and style of typed errors. Design: kube.ErrForbidden, a sentinel (design note :148). kube group: sentinels in kube. Runner, containercli and kube verifiers: one definition in runner shared by all clients. Style: sentinels (cli verifier, for confirm), struct types found with errors.As (kube verifier notes this is M's habit; rootless asks for *IDMapError), and *rbac.RefusedError beside ErrForbidden. confirm.Unanswered would share a word with the clients' unanswered.
14. Session environment. Catalogue: systemd.Client carries it (design note :127). Operations map question 9: only compose and the systemd client ever set Env. Its verifier: B's podman children inherit the repaired session today, so they lose it unless podman.Client carries it too. Runner group: lift B's NoEnv into M, which its verifier shows collides with a systemd.Client that sets Cmd.Env.
15. Methods the map adds that conflict with text on record. DeleteNamed replaces the catalogue's DeletePod (design note :128) and moves B's own deletes into M, where gdu 12 bounds them 'in the tool' (M:docs/todo.md:673-674). InspectImage, Stats and Top move commands the design names as tool-built (design note :169; M:docs/developer.md:64-65 still lists image, stats, top, cp, registry login and delete-pod as staying in the tools). The kube verifier's kubectl wait reverses the reason of gdu 17 (M:docs/todo.md:917-918). SecretValue has no counterpart in the surface review, which covers that read only as a capture form of Get.
16. compose.Quote and compose.Escape. Surface: unexport both; verifier: move the '$' doubling into the shared writer. Compose model, owner question 17: both stay exported because 'a tool's tests and its own content use them', and Posture stays because 'both tools' posture tests read it'. grep finds no tool test that names any of them: Quote and Escape appear only in the two renderers and one comment-level hit in B's hostpath files, Posture only at A:internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:194, A:internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:164 and B:internal/render/render.go:924, :1090.
17. One writer, two rules. The Kubernetes model's writer has 'block sequences only: no flow form' and a Str that never doubles '$'. The compose model writes entrypoint, command and healthcheck.test as one-line flow sequences and needs every Str doubled; its Extra slot reuses 'the writer's Node' with Str 'always through Quote, so a $ is doubled'. The Kubernetes model itself needs the doubled form for env, command and args. Design 8.2 layer 1 is one writer; as modelled it is two.
18. Lists the renderers need sit where they cannot be imported. The quadlet Setting check refuses values holding artifacttest.Widening() or HostNetwork() and the compose model cites HostNetwork; artifacttest imports both renderer packages and testing (M:containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:18-21). The surface review meanwhile keeps Widening in artifacttest and its verifier removes HostNetwork.
19. The three renderer models disagree on the shared container description (each was written alone). Identity: labels.Owner, Identity, Owner; Solution and DevelopedBy required in the quadlet row, optional in compose and kube. Run-as: compose a string with names allowed plus UserFromImage; quadlet numeric only through constructors; kube a struct with FromImage; the env table gives B A's grammar including the word image, which its verifier says B cannot take. Mounts: ReadOnly bool (compose, quadlet) against Writable bool (kube); the two verifiers then propose two different fixes. Timezone: no special case (compose), a named field (quadlet, recommended for all models), no field (kube). Health: Healthcheck.Command against Health.Cmd.
20. Renderer defaults and refusals disagree. Restart: required with no default (compose) against zero value 'always' (quadlet). Root filesystem: zero value writes read_only: true (compose), writes nothing (quadlet), written only when set (kube); gdu 6 leaves it to each tool (M:docs/todo.md:379-380). Ports under host or none: left out (compose), an error (quadlet), a load error in both tools (env table). Deploy digest: required (compose), optional (quadlet). cpuset: Extra slot (compose) against a named field (quadlet; the containerart verifier wants resources typed). Extra slot policy: deny list (compose, kube) against allow list (quadlet; both compose and kube verifiers ask for allow lists).
21. Grammars for the same value differ between models. Hostname: letters, digits, '.', '_', '-' (compose) against an RFC 1123 host name (quadlet), for the one B node name. Image: tag and digest both optional (compose) against 'a tag or a digest' (quadlet). Env names: the containerart verifier wants envref.ValidName on compose environment keys and the quadlet Environment key, but that grammar has no '.' or '-' (M:envref/envref.go:69-84) and B builds keys from node names (B:internal/render/render.go:801-807), which is why both models define a wider grammar.
22. Kubernetes documents: package and object type. The surface review wants a documents package under kube (name left to the owner) and nsgate.Object{Kind, Name} moved into kube. The Kubernetes model writes kube.Namespace in one place, names no package, and returns Manifest.Objects as {Kind, Name, Namespace, ClusterScoped}. The kube verifier's import rule (kube imports neither rbac, nsgate nor the documents package) is not checked against Manifest feeding the rbac probes.
23. podman.SystemQuadletDir. Surface: keep, because B needs the rootful default at config load. Env table question 11: B drops podman.quadletDir. B's only non-test use of the constant is that default (B:internal/config/load.go:282); A has none (grep). If the key goes, the reason for the export goes with it. The same proposal changes a row the parity body records as aligned (M:paritydoc/body.md:148).
24. Release order against what the streams need. gdu 2 and 19 'follow' the breaking tag (M:docs/todo.md:28-30; M:docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:44), while design point 11 says the next tag ships everything in the plan. Stream proposals need both inside it: the M host-path gate 'must land with or after gdu 2's tilde lift in A' (guards verifier); gdu 9's -p needs B's project key and gdu 6 a network key in both tools, whose names the env table sets; the env table then renames 75 of B's tags and reshapes both files. If gdu 19 follows, each tool maps config to M's models twice and B's operators meet two breaking env-file changes.
25. Pure renderer against a renderer that reads build info. Design 8.2: 'a pure function, a typed value in and bytes out' with output 'byte for byte stable for golden tests' (design note :250-252, :264-266). gdu 1: the module reads its own version when it renders and a workspace build writes another value (M:docs/todo.md:123-125).
26. Design rule 6 against M's parsers. The rule: under Echo 'a read answers nothing, and its parser reports unknown' (design note :116-117). ParseNamespace reads an empty answer as Absent (M:kube/kube.go:89-96) and B relies on that; 'unknown' is a real state word that Stopped accepts as confirmed down (M:systemd/systemd.go:67-72). Both verifiers saw one each; together the rule cannot be applied as written.
27. Two verifiers read B's frame writer oppositely. cli verifier: os/exec serialises when both streams are one writer, so no race is established today. Operations verifier, note (c): B's lockedWriter is evidence that one command's two streams already arrive on two goroutines. Source: B hands one writer to both streams (B:internal/cli/watch.go:89-96; B:internal/engine/runner.go:133-136), M passes them to os/exec unchanged (M:runner/runner.go:226) and documents the merge (M:runner/runner.go:123-126). Concurrent delivery starts only with a stream-marked hook; B's comment (B:internal/cli/watch.go:99-100) is not evidence of it.
28. A decided item rests on a stale premise. gdu 13 says A's 'refresh reports its own errors and returns none, so nothing changes for it' (M:docs/todo.md:708-710). A's refresh returns the collection error to the loop (A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/status.go:1110-1115), so the usage-error rule changes A too. Found by the cli verifier, confirmed here.
29. Decided items that no longer agree with each other. gdu 1: 'neither tool renders a top-level network' (M:docs/todo.md:104-105); gdu 6: with no key the compose file states the project network with its driver (M:docs/todo.md:377-378). The compose model adds that block and asks whether it is labelled. gdu 6's scope still lists hostNetwork: true for a rendered pod (M:docs/todo.md:390-391) while the confirmed reading is 'Kubernetes gets no network key' (M:docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:189) and the Kubernetes model has no such field. The env table's DNS-1123 container name (63) narrows a value gdu 1 assumes may be 100 long (M:docs/todo.md:108-110).

### Gaps

1. Legend and name coverage. M: = M:, A: = A:, B: = B:. A file:line is a line opened in this run; a point without one quotes a stream. I listed every exported func, type, const and method in M's non-test files and matched them to the eight surface groups: none is missing (245 checked = the 243 of M:docs/review/2026-11-13-plan-review.md:372 plus Runner.Run and Runner.Attach). Not reviewed as names: exported struct fields. Concrete case: rbac.Probe.Subresource and Probe.AllNamespaces (M:kube/rbac/rbac.go:22-25, used at rbac.go:83-88). The operations verifier found no non-test code sets them and handed it 'for the surface review'; the surface review has no entry. My grep agrees: no Subresource hit in either tool, and every AllNamespaces hit is kube.GetOptions (A:internal/runner/runner.go:841; B:internal/k8s/statusreport.go:452,460; B:internal/k8s/watch.go:131; B:internal/k8s/operator.go:439).
2. internal/build and the internal layout. M:internal/build/build.go:16-37 (Cmd, Stdin, ShQuote, UploadScript) was outside the surface review, yet five separate proposals send new code to internal/: the free-operand check (guards), the charset or UnsafeChars (guards), the two Posture constants (containerart), the clients' shared run logic (kube), the inspect builder and decoder (containercli). No stream says which internal packages exist after the tag and who imports each.
3. No package inventory and no import graph. The streams propose at least 14 new packages or homes: tilde, atomic write, label vocabulary, YAML writer, Kubernetes documents, a neutral charset, terminal helpers, platform vocabulary, name grammars, network and restart word lists, shared containerart leaf types, test support (diff, catalogue, ASCII), watchtest, a kube test package, plus the internal ones. M:docs/developer.md:28 requires leaf-first order and no cycles. Today's graph is small: runner -> execguard and exitcode (M:runner/echo.go:13, M:runner/runner.go:46), artifacttest -> compose and quadlet (M:containerart/artifacttest/artifacttest.go:20-21), nsgate -> rbac (M:kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:25). Pressure points were raised one by one (rbac.Run and nsgate.Read importing kube, documents against kube.Object, podman.Client on containercli.Client, renderers needing lists that artifacttest holds) but nobody drew the whole graph.
4. The lib annotation has no home and no test seam. Decided: the module 'reads its own' version and a workspace build writes go-deploy-devel (M:docs/todo.md:123-125). No code reads build info anywhere in M, A or B (grep for ReadBuildInfo and debug.BuildInfo: no hit). The kube model only says 'derived from M's own build info ... written by Render'. Not covered by any stream: which package holds the reader; what it returns when M is the main module (its own tests, the example tool); how a tool's golden test gets a fixed value. By the decision's own words the text differs between a go.work build and a pinned build, and M:docs/consuming.md:110-111 and :119-120 run the same tool goldens both ways (dev-all with go.work, then GOWORK=off), and every repin changes the value. The reader's branches also bear on the 99.7% floor (M:docs/design/2026-11-15-shared-deploy-api.md:194-195; M:docs/test.md:546).
5. Ownership labels and annotations on the PersistentVolume meet a warning A already has. A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1807-1818 (warnIfPVUnchanged) warns whenever the apply output holds 'persistentvolume/<x> configured'. With M writing labels and the lib and version annotations into every object's metadata, the cluster-scoped PV included (kube model, Meta rows), the first deploy after adoption and every later tool or module upgrade changes the PV's metadata, so the warning ('any change to libs.pvc.create is not in effect') fires with nothing wrong. The operations stream noted the scan and the renderer stream the annotations; neither joined them.
6. No label design. M:docs/review/2026-11-13-plan-review.md:324 asks for 'a short design note first: keys, shape' before the label work; M:docs/design/ holds only the deploy API note. The three renderer models each invent the identity type (compose: labels.Owner; quadlet: Identity; kube: Owner with Version), and no stream defines the rest of the vocabulary the decided items need (M:docs/todo.md:89-90, :106-110, :864-868, :1018-1022): the key constants, the per-sink value check, the kubectl selector string for ListLabelled, the --label arguments for podman secret create, and the match used on SecretLabels output and in the nsgate discount.
7. The --delete-namespace flag has no owner. gdu 1 gives both tools the flag (M:docs/todo.md:102-103) and the refusal must name it beside --no-prompt (cli group, Gate challenge). No stream says whether M holds the name, as it does for execguard.ApproveFlag (CR-0001, M:docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:68) and confirm.DefaultNoPromptFlag (M:confirm/prompter.go:12). grep finds the string in no Go file of M, A or B.
8. gdu 4's grammar table does not exist. M:docs/todo.md:304-307 leaves 'the grammar each builder holds its name to' to the step's plan. The streams add per-method remarks but no table of client method x argument x grammar. Missing outright: a systemd unit-name grammar (only implied by the quadlet model's File.Service = <Name>.service), the grammar for the kind operand of Scale and the rollouts, the form and type of the timeout argument (kube.RolloutTimeout is the string "300s", M:kube/kube.go:127), and which arguments are free operands.
9. gdu 7 (atomic write) has no design, and Windows is a gate. Package name and Windows behaviour are still undecided (M:docs/todo.md:430-433), while CI runs every gate on windows-2025 (M:.github/workflows/ci.yml:49, :66). The streams only add requirements to it in passing: a symlink refusal (kube verify), a 0600 FileMode constant (quadlet model), generate output as a new consumer (compose verify notes gdu 7's scope names units only, M:docs/todo.md:426-428), B's sysctl drop-in. Creating the directory, reading the installed unit for the compare and removing the unit stay unowned (ops map, localInBoth).
10. gdu 8 (test catalogue gate) is in the breaking tag (M:docs/todo.md:28-29) and got only passing mentions. Its catalogue format is undecided (M:docs/todo.md:459-461). Related and also unlisted: the effect of the unexports and removals on M:docs/test.md (550 lines) under the rule that a lifted test keeps its name (M:docs/developer.md:47). The streams name single tests (TestVerdict, TestKind, TestBodyIsACopy, TestComposeRoundTrip, TestPromptYesNo, TestPostureBytes); nobody listed all rows that move, go or change source.

The tail-count check of gdu 14 (M:docs/todo.md:750, :755-757; the kube verifier says it belongs in neither LogOptions package), the Since check, the podman secret delta and the ID compare of gdu 16 and 10 (M:docs/todo.md:867-868, :558-559), the Kubernetes leftover delta of gdu 18 (M:docs/todo.md:1025-1026). Occupants decodes kind, the top-level type, and metadata name, uid, deletionTimestamp, annotations and ownerReferences (M:kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:65-73) and needs type and one annotation for the token-Secret discount (nsgate.go:147-149). It decodes no labels, which the gdu 1 discount needs. Two streams recommend the listing ask for 'metadata only' so Secret data stays out; that drops the type field and nobody says what output format replaces -o json (M:kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:52-56) or how Occupants' parser changes. Also open with no proposal: the discount list for the wider listing (M:docs/todo.md:96-100, :127-130), the read of the namespace's own label, and the rewrite of parity rule 9 (M:paritydoc/body.md:71-83).
13. The example tool and the guide (M:docs/design/2026-11-15-shared-deploy-api.md:221-222, :334-337) were scoped by no stream. Points that can be read from the repo: a nested module is outside every gate, since build, vet, test, cov and scan all run on ./... from the root (M:scripts/dev.sh:62, :71, :95-97, :106, :119) and CI calls only those tasks (M:.github/workflows/ci.yml:61, :66); go.work is ignored (M:.gitignore), so the example needs a replace line or a pin to the very tag it ships in; dev-all knows exactly two tools (M:scripts/dev-all.sh:39-42); the notice gate does not descend into subfolders (M:docs_test.go:10-19; M:docs/developer.md:87-89). graphify-out/go.mod is the only precedent for a nested module here.

Reading M:paritydoc/body.md, the decided work touches at least rule 1 (delete timeout), 2 (rbac.Run), 4 and 5 (they name PodmanDeploy, PodmanSecretCreate and createPodmanSecrets, body.md:34-48), 6 (tail grammar), 9, 11 (who writes the hardening lines), 13 (usage errors end the loop), 16, the rows at body.md:145-158, and new rows for labels, network mode, context, step order, leftover removal and shared keys. paritydoc.Check is byte for byte, so all of it must land in one adoption round. ### Unverified

1. Nothing has run on a real engine or cluster. Smoke S7, S8, S9 and the v0.1.3 checks are all marked 'not yet run' (M:docs/smoke.md:25, :49, :60, :83) and CR-0009 is deferred (M:docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:76). The plan review says the same of itself (M:docs/review/2026-11-13-plan-review.md:357) and asked for the smoke run before the label tag (plan-review.md:327), which is now the one breaking tag. Every typed error the clients are to return (refused, not found, unanswered, timed out) is derived from exit codes and text nobody has observed.
2. A's Kubernetes path has never run on a real cluster (M:docs/developer.md:314). The Kubernetes document model (Deployment, Service, ConfigMap, PV, PVC) is built from that renderer's output; the kube verifier found a latent defect by reading alone (a digit-led name passes A's load and fails the Service).
3. Owner checks still open that the models already assume (M:docs/review/2026-11-13-decisions.md:217-230): Config.Secrets IDs before and after a replace (podman.Client.SecretIDs and the Inspect change rest on it); the discount list under the wider listing; Network=private on the oldest podman (the quadlet model makes it the zero value); the time of one real broker remove (the 300s delete default); the OnDelete check of gdu 17.
4. Podman and quadlet facts the quadlet verifier states from memory, with no source opened: Label= parsed like Environment= (decides the form of all five ownership lines and the digest line); an absolute Volume source copied into RequiresMountsFor (decides whether '$' may be doubled); a non-JSON HealthCmd run through a shell (decides whether Health.Cmd can be an argv); the group podman gives a bare uid; the list of widening quadlet keys; the release of ReadOnly=. The verifier itself asks for a read at the 4.5 and 4.7 tags.
5. Compose facts not run or read: that environment secrets and inline configs are copied into the container and fail under read_only: true (decides the zero value of the root-filesystem field and A's decided default); which line breaks compose's YAML loader honours; a key-only environment entry; that -p outranks COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME (stated at M:docs/todo.md:496); what 'down' does to a project and network shared by two instances (gdu 19's shared default project); whether nerdctl and standalone docker-compose take -p, environment secrets and inline configs. M:docs/developer.md:310-314 records that even compose-from-stdin was checked from documentation only.
6. Kubernetes facts not run: kubelet expansion of $(VAR) in an exec probe command; whether a key dropped from stringData stays in data after apply (the reason given for data-only Secrets); defaultMode 0400 read as octal; a partial listing printing what it could and exiting 1 (M:docs/todo.md:1021-1022 is the only source); auth whoami and the 1.28 floor; the DeleteProbe claim that asking 'delete namespaces' cluster-wide refuses an identity that may delete its own namespace. The code half of that last claim is confirmed (M:kube/nsgate/nsgate.go:61; M:kube/rbac/rbac.go:62-68, :86-88); the cluster half is not.
7. systemd exit codes 5, 3 and 4 are 'from systemd's source' and the host check is only a planned smoke row (M:docs/todo.md:476-482, :509-510). systemd.Client's not-found and LoadState design rests on them.
8. Go build-info behaviour. Nobody checked what a dependency's version reads as under go test, under go.work, or with M as the main module. The lib annotation decision (M:docs/todo.md:123-125) and every Kubernetes golden in both tools rest on it.
9. The cost of a hook writer. The runner verifier says a non-file writer makes every run a piped run, so compose and pull progress and colour change. No stream measured it and no smoke row covers it; the design's 'each tool keeps its output model' (M:docs/design/2026-11-15-shared-deploy-api.md:99-100) rests on it for B.
10. The preview mode. Design rule 6 (M:docs/design/2026-11-15-shared-deploy-api.md:116-117) keeps the preview 'as today', and several client behaviours are specified for runner.Echo. The runner verifier reports that no tool ships a preview and B installs Echo from tests only. No stream confirmed with the owner that Echo is a product mode before treating its behaviour as a requirement.
11. The operations map's 'common' verdicts for one-tool operations (Login, Unshare, CopyTo and CopyFrom, Stats, Top, InspectImage, SecretKeys, SecretValue, ServerVersion, ComposeVersion, systemd Show) are one stream's judgement. The verifier confirmed which tool runs each, not the verdict, and noted that two verdicts depart from text on record.
12. The second verifiers' own additions (roughly 150 'additions' entries across the surface groups) were checked by nobody. Sampled here and confirmed in source: kube.ValidLabel accepts 123 and true (M:kube/kube.go:263-275); envref.ValidName is letters, digits and '_' only (M:envref/envref.go:69-84); ParseNamespace reads an empty answer as Absent (M:kube/kube.go:89-96); Stopped accepts 'unknown' (M:systemd/systemd.go:67-72); A's watch refresh returns its error (A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/status.go:1110-1115); no tool test names the Posture constants (grep: only A:internal/dockergen/dockergen.go:194, A:internal/podmangen/podmangen.go:164, B:internal/render/render.go:924, :1090). The rest are unsampled.
13. Usage counts are not on one scale. The kube group counts every mention, comments included (its own signatureNotes); the runner group's raw counts needed eight corrections; the other groups count code references. A keep or unexport that leans on 'A0 B0' in the kube group should use the code-reference figures in each reason, not the count columns.
14. The env table's reasons marked 'inherent' were re-argued by its verifier in six rows (read-only root, spread, pod identity, service account, second image, TLS Secret) on reading alone; whether the broker and the operator really accept the aligned form (for example one uid:gid pair filling all four custom-resource fields) was not tried.

### Next work

1. Get the owner's ruling on the output contract, then write it as one table: per client method, where stdout goes (hook, returned, dropped), where stderr goes, what the error may carry, and whether the call may take its own hook. Settle with it: merged order (A's Combined), concurrent hook calls, the Result of a multi-command operation, and what Echo prints. Every method signature waits on this, and it reverses a sentence recorded as decided (design note :98-99).
2. Merge the surface review and the operations map into one catalogue. One row per method: signature, grammar of each argument (the gdu 4 table), typed errors, the builder or parser that goes private. Resolve in that pass: the Exec family and session exit status; generic Run against exported builders (Systemctl, Forbidden, Containers); Version; Unshare; IDMap; the systemd return shapes; host probes; client construction and where execguard runs; the session env on podman.Client. Record each departure from design 3.4, design :169 and todo item 12 as an owner question.
3. Reconcile the three renderer models into one shared container description and one writer before any is built: identity type, run-as, mount, port, health, env-name grammar, timezone, restart default, root filesystem, ports under host, resources, the Extra slot policy, hostname and image grammars; flow sequences and the two quoting forms in the writer; the package and object type for Kubernetes documents. Give every security-relevant field a zero value that is an error or the hardened choice, as the verifiers asked model by model.
4. Write the label design note the plan review asked for (plan-review.md:324): keys, per-sink value checks, the selector and --label builders, the match function, the identity type. In the same note fix the lib-version reader (package, injectable value for goldens, value when M is the main module), A's PV warning (A:cmd/solmq-conn-util/main.go:1807-1818), and the owner of the --delete-namespace flag name.
5. Decide the release order of gdu 2 and gdu 19 against the breaking tag, and with it how much of the env table (key spelling, flattening, network, project, restart, run-as, image keys) rides that tag, so each tool maps config to the models once.
6. Collect real-engine evidence before the shapes are frozen: run smoke S7 to S9 (CR-0009, a standing reminder), the pending owner checks (decisions.md:217-230), and a one-page list of the from-memory facts with the decision each carries: quadlet Label=, RequiresMountsFor and HealthCmd read from podman source at the 4.5 and 4.7 tags; compose read_only with environment secrets; shared-project down; -p precedence; kubelet probe expansion; partial kubectl listing; systemd exit codes; build-info values; compose output through a pipe.
7. Draw the package inventory and import graph for everything proposed (about 14 new packages plus internal ones), check it against developer.md:28, and fix the test-support packages (diff, catalogue of gdu 8, ASCII, watchtest, can-i default, recording hook, Kubernetes posture) in one decision.
8. Redesign the namespace gate as a whole for gdu 1: the listing command and output format that keeps Secret data out yet still yields type, annotations, ownerReferences and labels; the Occupants input; the discount list (owner check); the namespace's own label read; the question decision as a pure function; DeleteProbe's scope; Protected inside DeleteNamespace.
9. Scope the guide and the example tool: where the nested module sits, how it is gated (dev.sh tasks, CI, the dev-script contract in init-devops), replace or pin, what it deploys, which checks a third tool runs, what paritydoc and the Solace label values mean for it, and what dev-all does with a third consumer.
10. Produce the three edit lists nobody owns: the parity body (rules 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 9, 11, 13, 16, the divergence rows, new rows), developer.md:70-289 with README and package docs, and docs/test.md rows under the lifted-name rule; plus the smoke rows for every new executor and artifact.
11. Design gdu 7 and check Windows for every new package (atomic write, file mode, host-path absoluteness, terminal helpers), since CI gates on windows-2025.
12. Sweep both tools once for parallel code outside the four lenses, starting with B:internal/output (developer.md:67-68), the two examples packages, version printing, the generate output writer and the doc and catalogue gates, and decide each under rule 5.
13. Check the client and env-key design against the recorded later step 'root deploying on behalf of a rootless user' (developer.md:314-316), so it does not need a second break.
